Even though he was simply walking, there were many eyes glancing at him. They were whispering among themselves, too.

“What is this?”

“The man you beat yesterday was a favorite to win the Hwangja Division. Thanks to that, you’ve become one of the people drawing attention in this tournament as well.”

He had taken all the attention from the opponent he had barely defeated yesterday.

“Don’t look, but the one in blue on the right over there. She looked at me twice. Ah, she’s looking again.”

“Even so, couldn’t you walk a little faster?”

Seo Daeryong was moving slowly, savoring everyone’s gazes.

“Ah! I think the women at the far left over there are whispering while looking at me.”

“To my eyes, it looks like they’re discussing what to eat.”

Just then, a young woman wearing a sword trotted over and greeted Seo Daeryong.

“I enjoyed watching your match yesterday. Fight well today, too.”

She bowed and left.

Seo Daeryong froze, staring blankly at her back.

“Pavilion Master.”

“What?”

“You didn’t perhaps pay people off to cheer me up, did you?”

“Considering how smug you are, I’d like to say I did, but no.”

“You really didn’t, right? This really feels like a dream.”

Seo Daeryong was truly delighted. It seemed this was the first time a woman had come up to him first and shown him such favor.

I hoped he would fully enjoy this joy and excitement. He would never forget the duels here for the rest of his life.

I hoped Seo Daeryong would someday sit his grandson on his knee and tell him about these days. A story that began with, “It may be hard to believe, but when your grandfather was young…”

“Let’s go! To win!”

Was it thanks to the woman’s encouragement?

Seo Daeryong kept winning, and he easily took first place in the Hwangja Division, advancing to the main tournament. Now the main tournament would be a series of duels between the first-place winners of each division.

“I’m going all the way!”

Seo Daeryong was burning with passion, separate from the mission. Yes, this was a moment that came only a few times in life. More thrilling, more exciting, and more precious than becoming the greatest under heaven in his old age. Yes, burn bright, Daeryong.

Chapter 156: A Nonsense Dream or a Prophetic Dream.
---------------------------------------------------
Jin Haryeong was being chased by someone.

Unable to shake off her pursuer no matter what she did, she finally used her lightness skill to climb up into a tree. Hidden among the branches, she did not so much as let out a breath.

The pursuer who had been chasing her ran past the tree, then suddenly came to a stop.

Like a beast scenting its prey, the pursuer slowly turned around, then lifted his head and looked up into the tree. The man’s face was empty, like that of a monster.

Aagh! She screamed, but no sound came out of her mouth. She launched herself away again and fled.

Just as the distance between them was growing narrower and narrower, someone came running from ahead and kicked the face of the man pursuing her without mercy.

With a thud, the faceless pursuer rolled over and over, then vanished.

The person who had helped her had hair hanging down in a tangled mass and a face full of beard. Where had she seen this man before?

The next moment, she cried out.

“Are you that servant?”

The instant she realized he was the man she had met at the inn, Jin Haryeong’s eyes flew open and she woke from the dream. The last words had actually left her lips as she woke.

She was not someone who usually dreamed. This was the first time she had ever had such a strange dream.

Perhaps because she had spent the whole night being chased, she was drenched in sweat.

Leaving aside the dream of being pursued, why had that man, of all people, appeared and saved her? Was it because she had mistaken the servant for the master that day, and it had lingered in her mind?

She recalled the face of the bearded servant. She had only seen it once, yet she remembered it clearly. It was the face she had seen in her dream. Since she was normally poor at remembering people’s faces, the vividness with which his face came to mind was a novel experience.

“Are you all right?”

From outside came the voice of her guard, Chuho.

“I’m fine. I had a nightmare.”

“It must be because your nerves are on edge due to the Little Dragon Tournament.”

“I suppose so.”

“Please do not worry too much. There is no one who can defeat you, my lady.”

“I’m going to rest more, so don’t disturb me today.”

“Yes.”

But a short while later, having slipped out of her quarters without Chuho knowing, she was entering the inn where she had eaten noodles.

When she stepped inside, the place was packed with customers. Because of this tournament, everywhere was crowded no matter where one went.

Her eyes caught sight of a man drinking in a corner. It was the servant from her dream. She had come thinking he might possibly be here, and he really was.

Today, she had not come for the noodles. She wanted to meet that servant once. She wanted to confirm whether he really was the sort of person who would appear in her dreams or not.

But soon, she shook her head and let out a wry laugh.

How ridiculous was it to come meet a servant from some rural sect just because he had appeared in her dream? She was about to turn and leave when someone called to her from behind.

“Have you come to eat noodles?”

When she turned around, the servant had risen and was looking at her.

Because of that, the gazes of the people around them focused on her. A few who recognized her were startled. Thinking people would find it strange if she simply turned and walked out now, she calmly walked over to the servant’s table and sat across from him.

After sitting opposite the servant, she ordered noodles and wine.

“I did not think you would share a table with me.”

“Why not?”

“Because last time, you learned that my status was that of a servant.”

“Because I’m of noble birth, I wouldn’t eat at the same table as a servant? Is that what you mean?”

“Is that not the case?”

At the sight of him asking so bluntly, the corners of Jin Haryeong’s lips lifted slightly.

“That’s right.”

Annoyed by the man’s expression, which seemed to say he had known as much, she added,

“But the reason isn’t because the other person is a servant. It’s because the other person feels uncomfortable. When I’m in front of them, they can’t eat properly.”

“Ah, then that is a relief. I will not feel uncomfortable at all.”

A servant this unabashed before the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Leader?

‘By now, he should know who I am.’

He was a type she had never once encountered before. He truly knew nothing to an absurd degree. Even the heirs of prestigious orthodox sects acted with the utmost caution before her. They could not even conceive of asking such sensitive questions.

“You always go around alone.”

In truth, regardless of whether the other party was a servant or not, she did not mingle well with people.

She liked being alone, so she did not even enjoy having conversations like this with someone. That was why even the fact that she had come all the way here felt unfamiliar to her.

Perhaps she had come because she thought there might be something special about this man that her unconscious mind had sensed, causing her to have such a dream.

She glanced at the man. His hair was unkempt, his beard had grown scraggly, and there was truly nothing worth looking at.

‘It was just a nonsense dream. A nonsense dream.’

Whether he knew her thoughts or not, the man treated her comfortably.

“Ah, our young master has also advanced to the main round.”

She had heard the rumors about him as well. Her guard, Chuho, had told her that he had caused an upset and advanced to the main round. It must have been a sect from the countryside that knew nothing of the world and had only trained in martial arts. That was probably why they had been able to advance to the main round. Just by looking at this man, who seemed almost guileless in his ignorance of worldly affairs, she could guess the atmosphere of that sect.

“Your young master may end up facing you, miss. Ah, do you know? They say there have been people who died in the main round. Please, take good care of our young master!”

“For a martial artist to die on the dueling stage would be an honorable death.”

“Would it not be an unjust death, rather than an honorable one?”

“What do you mean?”

“For a martial artist, isn’t there no place safer than a dueling stage? There are fixed rules, judges, and people watching, so no one can launch a sneak attack. And yet someone dies on that dueling stage? Would that not be far too unjust a death?”

She felt that it was some kind of sophistry, but at the same time, no particular rebuttal came to mind. She had never once thought about it in that way.

“You speak as though you are a martial artist.”

“I have learned swordsmanship too.”

“You met a good master, then. Teaching swordsmanship even to a servant.”

“Yes, I learned from a truly frightening person. He is probably the most frightening person in the world.”

Jin Haryeong inwardly snorted. How many people had he even met, to say such a thing? Had he ever even met someone truly frightening? Then again, in the village he came from, perhaps his own master really had been the most frightening.

Her gaze went to the sword at the servant’s waist.

“Why did you wrap the scabbard in cloth?”

It was tightly wrapped in common cotton cloth that could be found anywhere. Usually, there were two reasons for something like this. Either wandering swordsmen who traveled through rough places wrapped their scabbards to keep them from being damaged, or someone was trying to conceal a precious sword.

“I did it to look stylish.”

“Then shouldn’t you have wrapped it in fine silk?”

“It’s stylish because it isn’t silk. The key is to look dressed up without looking dressed up.”

Seeing her look at him in disbelief, the man went one step further.

“They say East Lake here in Wuhan is very beautiful. Would you show it to me sometime?”

Unless he was insane, he could not possibly say something like this to her. This was the point where she should shout at him, asking who a mere servant thought he was speaking to in such a way.

Why was she staying silent?

Because he had appeared in her dream? No. It must be because she did not want to become some ill-bred woman who treated those of low status carelessly. Her grandfather detested it when his children or grandchildren became the subject of such gossip. Yes, that had to be it.

“Then I will be sure to show you another magnificent view I know.”

Did this person truly believe such a moment would come between them? They had shared a table twice, and he seemed ready to go sightseeing together.

A sigh escaped her on its own. Coming here had been her mistake.

“What is your name?”

She was asking because she was angry. The question carried the feeling of, You bastard, what’s your name?

“Just call me ‘Servant.’”

This man, really, to the very end!

“That would make me look far too ill-mannered, wouldn’t it?”

“It is Geomyeon. Not the yeon of ‘fate,’ but the yeon of ‘smoke.’ I will remain like this for a while, then disappear like smoke.”

As he said that, the man grinned. Jin Haryeong thought that for a mere servant, he truly did all sorts of things.

Just then, someone approached while calling her name.

“Haryeong.”

The man around her age was Song Taegun, the son of the Blue Dragon Corps Commander. He was the only friend who had grown up with her since childhood. The moment Jin Haryeong saw him, she was inwardly flustered.

“You came to eat noodles.”

Song Taegun knew very well that she liked the noodles here.

“How did you know I was here?”

“I was passing by in front, and some customers coming out were talking about how you were here. You ran away after avoiding Warrior Chu again, didn’t you?”

Song Taegun looked at the man with her and asked,

“But who is this?”

Not knowing how to introduce him, Jin Haryeong was flustered.

At that moment, the man cupped his fist toward him and greeted him.

“I am Geomyeon of the Seodo Sect in Gansu. My senior brother has advanced to the main round this time.”

“Ah! If it is the Seodo Sect, is your senior brother’s name not Seoryong?”

“That is correct.”

“I heard he has been causing quite a stir in this tournament. It is an honor to meet a disciple of such an impressive sect.”

“My senior brother is the impressive one. As for me, I merely cheer from outside the dueling stage.”

“You seem far more impressive than your senior brother, Young Hero Geom.”

“What do you mean?”

“Are you not sitting here together with this friend? Do you know how difficult that is? Even for me, an old friend, it is difficult.”

At that, Jin Haryeong said to him,

“Stop saying useless things and go. I’ll see you later.”

He looked as if he wanted to stay longer, but because Jin Haryeong was exuding an icy air, Song Taegun smiled and bid them farewell.

“Let us have a drink together next time.”

“By all means.”

After Song Taegun left, Jin Haryeong questioned him coldly.

“Why did you lie and say you were a junior disciple? Why? Did you think I would be ashamed if it was revealed that I was sitting with a servant?”

To be honest, she had felt that way. When Song Taegun asked who he was, she had been flustered for a moment. She had hesitated to introduce him as a servant. He had struck a nerve, and she felt ashamed.

“Strictly speaking, it was not a lie. I am a servant, but I also learned martial arts in the same sect, so I am also a junior disciple, am I not?”

“Once is enough for sophistry.”

Jin Haryeong sprang up from her seat and left the place.

And that night, she had another nightmare.

This time, she was hanging from a cliff, and once again that servant appeared, reached out his hand, and saved her.

Jin Haryeong, who woke at dawn, looked down at her hand. She vividly remembered the sensation of holding Geomyeon’s hand. It was as vivid as a prophetic dream.

“You crazy woman, why do you keep acting like this?”

She knew the answer herself. Why else? Because she was doing things she had never done in her life.

She pulled the blanket back over herself and lay down again. But sleep did not come easily.

* * *

Seo Daeryong’s first opponent in the main round was the heir of the renowned Zhangping Sword Family of Fujian.

“Shall we practice together?”

“With you, Pavilion Master?”

When I suggested practicing for the first time, Seo Daeryong was startled.

“It would be an honor for me.”

“Follow me.”

Taking Seo Daeryong with me, I brought him to the mountain forest where I had been training.

“Even when it comes to finding a place to practice, you are certainly different from me, Pavilion Master. How did you manage to find a place like this?”

There was a broad open space, blocked on all sides, with only one path by which one could enter. Since one only needed to pay attention to a single direction, it was an ideal place for martial arts training.

“Come here to train from now on.”

“No. You should be the one training here, Pavilion Master. The quality of our training is as different as heaven and earth.”

“Is that really so?”

“Pardon?”

“I think your training, challenging this martial arts tournament with your life and future on the line, is far more valuable. If you are heaven and I am earth, then I will acknowledge it.”

“Thank you for saying that, but that does not mean heaven and earth will change places. Even if they were overturned, it would not feel all that good either.”

Yes, this was the sort of person one should have as a right-hand man. It was not a matter of whether his martial arts were stronger or weaker. Someone with whom even exchanging a single line felt pleasant, someone who spoke the thoughts he held just as they were—this was the sort of person who ought to become a right-hand man. At least, for the position of right-hand man.

“Did you see the heir of the Zhangping Sword Family fight?”

“Yes. He fought really well.”

“Objectively speaking, you are at a disadvantage.”

“Since you put it that way, Pavilion Master, I suppose I will definitely lose.”

I honestly nodded. He could not overcome the difference in the amount of time spent properly training in martial arts. Moreover, he had not fully inherited the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s martial arts, and he could not openly reveal his demonic energy as he pleased. In many ways, it would be a difficult fight.

“Now, I will imitate the Zhangping Sword Family’s martial arts, so think of me as your opponent in the duel!”

“Yes!”

I imitated the swordsmanship of the Zhangping Sword Family’s heir and faced Seo Daeryong as though that man were fighting him.

Seo Daeryong lost again and again. I did not go easy on him, and each time a bout ended, another bruise was added to Seo Daeryong’s body. There were moments when his life was in danger, but he did not give up.

“Please, just one more time.”

Seo Daeryong tried various methods he could think of. This way and that way. He tried every possible method.

I silently repeated the duels, waiting for him to find the answer on his own. I did not tell him the solution that would let him win.

Even if it meant losing the duel, he had to find it himself. The mission was important, but this moment, which would become a turning point in the life of “Seo Daeryong the martial artist,” was even more important.

We practiced dueling through the night. Seo Daeryong was utterly exhausted, but his eyes held a look I had never seen from him before.

And four hours before the main round duel.

I forced Seo Daeryong, who insisted on training until the end, to sleep.

Seo Daeryong, who had said his nerves were too sharp for him to sleep, began snoring as soon as he lay down.

I woke him half an hour before the duel.

“Feel refreshed?”

“Yes. I feel like I can live again. But what should I do?”

“Why?”

“I dreamed that I lost the duel.”

Looking at Seo Daeryong’s tearful face, I smiled and said,

“Now then, shall we go find out whether that dream was nonsense or prophetic?”

Chapter 157: So What If I'm Misunderstood?
------------------------------------------
The fervor of the Little Dragon Tournament grew even hotter.

Once the main round began, all the small sparring stages in the Murim Alliance’s outer compound were dismantled, and a large arena was set up at the center.

Around the arena, tiered seating was arranged so that countless people could sit. It had been made so that thousands could watch the matches at the same time.

The martial tournament in which the first-place winners of the thirty-two groups would compete for victory was to begin in earnest today.

If one made it into the final eight, entry into one of the Murim Alliance’s elite organizations was guaranteed. Even if not, those who had advanced to the main round would be given a chance to join the Alliance if they caught the eye of its experts. That was why this main round was truly important to everyone.

Of course, Seo Daeryong was burning for a different reason.

Something was awakening in his heart as a martial artist. Until now, Seo Daeryong had merely been someone who had learned martial arts, but through this tournament, he was being reborn as a true warrior.

Even seeing the heroes packed tightly all around him, Seo Daeryong was not intimidated in the slightest.

He was looking only at the opponent he would fight. I said nothing. Everything I needed to say to him, I had already said through training. All I could do was hope that Seo Daeryong had realized something from that training. I trusted Seo Daeryong’s intelligence.

“Zhaotang of the Zhangping Sword Family of Fujian Province!”

At the referee’s shout, infused with inner force, cheers erupted.

Zhaotang slowly climbed onto the arena. His expression and his steps carried ease and confidence. He was a skilled fighter for whom it would not be strange at all to say that his goal in this tournament was to win.

“Seo Ryong of the Seodo Sect of Gansu Province!”

Cheers erupted again. If anything, the cheers directed at Seo Daeryong were even louder than those for Zhaotang. Zhaotang had been a famous rising talent from the start, but Seo Daeryong was a new expert from an unknown sect who had caused a sensation and advanced to the main round. As much as people wished to cheer for the strong, they also longed to see an unexpected upset from a new master.

Listening to the cheers, Seo Daeryong turned to look at me below the arena.

He sent me a sound transmission.

—I’ll go up there and make last night’s dream a dog dream before I come back down.

What words were needed in a situation like this?

Instead of cheering him on with words, I gave him a firm nod with a look that said I believed in him.

At the referee’s signal, the match between the two began.

Zhaotang was a martial artist who had learned swordsmanship properly from childhood until now.

His attacks were sharp, and his defense was solid. His defense in particular was nearly perfect; like an impregnable fortress, it did not budge against any attack.

I knew very well what kind of frustration Seo Daeryong must be feeling. The fortunate thing for Seo Daeryong was that it was a frustration he had experienced more than enough before climbing onto this arena.

‘Don’t get drawn in!’

Most people could not endure that frustration and attacked recklessly, only to be counterattacked. The essence of Zhaotang’s swordsmanship lay in counters.

If he could instead make his opponent feel frustrated, he would surely be able to find an opening. But I had not given Seo Daeryong that answer. I wanted him to find it on his own.

Sword and saber struck sparks.

Those watching held their breath. The techniques exchanged were far too high-level to be called a clash between rising talents.

Seo Daeryong fought aggressively, but he never rushed. He was painting his own grand picture. The title of that picture was Patience. He had found the answer himself.

Hundreds of exchanges passed like that, and at last, when he succeeded in making his opponent impatient, the picture was complete. He had made it seem that if the match continued like this and was decided by the referee, the more aggressive Seo Daeryong would likely have the advantage.

The impatience he had created in reverse revealed the tiniest of openings, and Seo Daeryong did not miss that moment.

Puaaak!

Seo Daeryong’s saber cut deeply into Zhaotang’s arm. Blood sprayed, and Zhaotang dropped the sword he had been holding.

Though Zhaotang tried to continue fighting despite his serious injury, the referee stepped in and declared Seo Daeryong the victor.

Cheers burst out for both men. Win or lose, it had been a splendid match worthy of the main round.

After coming down from the arena, Seo Daeryong could not stand properly and collapsed into a sitting position.

Many people were watching him, but he paid them no mind. Rather, perhaps the sight of him sitting there on the ground in a daze made him seem all the more human; everyone who passed by cupped their fists and showed their respect, and some even applauded from afar.

I said nothing. It was not so much because of the people watching, but because I was letting Seo Daeryong savor this moment fully on his own.

Among those congratulating him was the woman who had cheered for Seo Daeryong before.

“Congratulations on your victory!”

Seeing her smile brightly, Seo Daeryong sprang to his feet.

“Thank you.”

The woman left with a smile, and Seo Daeryong sat down beside me again.

“Until just a moment ago, you looked like you were dying, but now you look ready to fight another match.”

After confirming that the woman had gone far away, Seo Daeryong shook his head and flopped back down.

“No, I couldn’t do it even if it killed me. That was truly exhausting. My next opponent will be even stronger, won’t he?”

“Probably. Why? Do you want to give up?”

“Yes, please send me home!”

“What are you going to say at our drinking gatherings? That you came back because, at the last moment, you were too scared and too tired? Well, Lord Jang and Ian are so kind that they’ll comfort you. They’ll tell you that you did well enough. But deep down, they’ll think this: if it were me, I would have challenged it to the end. Every time they see you, if it were me, I would have gone to the end…”

“Stop.”

I grinned and held out my hand. Seo Daeryong gripped my hand tightly and stood up.

“Thank you. If you hadn’t been my sparring partner, Pavilion Master, I never would have won.”

“He wasn’t someone you could beat just because someone practiced with you. You did well.”

“This is really enough to drive a man insane. When I beat that bastard and cheers erupted from every direction, it felt so good I thought I’d go mad.”

“If you truly go mad, you become a madman. If you go just mad enough, no one will be able to handle you. Go mad in moderation.”

After chatting about this and that, we returned to the inn, where an unexpected person was waiting for me.

Jin Haryeong was eating noodles at the inn.

Seo Daeryong and I stopped briefly at the entrance. Seo Daeryong spoke worriedly.

“I hope noodles are the only thing that young lady likes.”

“What are you talking about all of a sudden?”

“Why are you pretending not to know? A woman of such noble status came here again just to eat noodles?”

“She probably came today to be treated.”

“What treatment?”

“Her pride was wounded.”

“You wounded it on purpose, didn’t you?”

I could not deny that question. At the very least, I was doing my best to grow closer to her.

“This is all because of the Cheonmyeong Society.”

“The problem is that young lady doesn’t know that.”

Seo Daeryong went straight up to the guest room, and I walked toward her.

* * *

Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk’s hand as he walked toward her. She thought it looked smoother and whiter than the hand that had reached out to her when she was hanging from a cliff in her dream.

Geom Mugeuk came to her table and greeted her.

“Your match is in three days, isn’t it? Our young master’s was today.”

Truly, she had to acknowledge this attendant’s ability to be sociable. They had only met a couple of times, yet he spoke to her as comfortably as if they had known each other for ages.

“May I sit for a moment?”

She nodded.

“Today, please make sure to pay for your noodles before you leave, miss.”

“What do you mean?”

“The first day we met, and the second time as well, you left just like that, so I paid for the wine and food.”

For a moment, she was flustered. Now that she thought about it, the second time had definitely been like that. She had been so angry that she had sprung up and stormed out. Had she done that the first time too?

Had she ever, in her entire life, failed to pay someone what she owed? It had clearly been a mistake. Her face flushed faintly. This man kept making people flustered like this.

Is that why you appeared in my dream? To tell me to pay you back?

She had intended to seize the initiative from the very beginning, but instead, she had been put on the back foot.

“How much was it? I forgot.”

“No. Please buy me noodles and wine twice from now on.”

Was he using that as an excuse to see her again? She began to think that this seemingly naive fellow might actually be a womanizer.

‘It won’t be easy. Do you think there were only one or two men like you?’

If one lined up all the men who wanted to marry her and change their lives, then with a bit of exaggeration, they could encircle the walls of the Murim Alliance. Listening to and watching the process by which they coveted her background and appearance was enough to make her develop a loathing for men she had never even had before.

“Let’s settle it all today. Order whatever dishes you want, not just noodles.”

“No, I don’t want to today.”

To think an attendant would refuse so firmly! Feeling as though she truly had become a maid, she ordered wine.

“I need a drink.”

She ordered wine. Only after drinking two cups in succession did she speak of what had happened that day.

“To be honest, I was angry that day. Just as I was about to introduce you honestly to my friend as an attendant, you said first that you were my junior martial brother. So I felt as though I had deceived my friend, and I became angry.”

What would change by saying this?

Even knowing that, it was because of her damned personality, which insisted on saying what needed to be said.

She met Geom Mugeuk’s gaze.

They were eyes that truly did not suit an attendant. Calm and deep, yet impossible to fathom—those eyes resembled those of her late father, and also those of her grandfather, the Alliance Leader.

With those eyes, Geom Mugeuk asked calmly.

“Why do you keep worrying about that matter?”

At that moment, she said inwardly, I really hate this personality of mine too.

“Because I don’t want to be misunderstood.”

“Because you might look like someone who looks down on attendants?”

“Yes. Because I am not that kind of person.”

“So what if you’re misunderstood a little?”

“I don’t like it.”

“Have you never considered that such feelings might make you look down on the other person even more?”

For a moment, she bristled.

“What do you mean by that?”

“An attendant is a person too. We think the same way our masters do; we merely refrain from speaking. If someone looks down on attendants from the start, then from our side as well, we can simply think, Ah, that’s the kind of person they are, and disregard them.”

“Then are you saying my consideration is worse than those people who look down on attendants?”

“That is not what I mean, but let me ask you one thing. Is that consideration for the other person? Or is it for yourself, miss?”

“!”

In that instant, Jin Haryeong flinched.

“Ah, it is fine even if it is consideration for yourself. But in that case, you must not let it show. If we notice it, then we have to become just as serious about it. Ah, so eating at the same table with us and having a conversation with us is something she has to agonize over that much. She has to try that hard not to hurt us. She is worrying about it that much. We end up thinking thoughts we never needed to think. All so that your heart can feel at ease, miss.”

Jin Haryeong could not say a word. Her face had flushed red. It was something she had truly never once considered, so she did not know what to say.

She felt wronged and furious. No doubt only after returning home would she regret, thinking, I should have answered like this back then, and kick at her blankets.

Was that why he appeared in my dream?

That thought led to self-reproach again.

‘Ah! I really am selfish.’

Even in this moment, she was only worrying about herself. Her pride, wounded because an attendant had appeared in her dream, was being desperately bandaged with the excuse that this attendant was the sort of person who said things she had never expected.

“You are far too insolent for an attendant. You should consider it an honor to dine at the same table as me.”

After saying this coldly, she added,

“Like that?”

“Yes. How nice is that? If I were of your status, miss, I would think that way too. Most of the people eating here would do the same. So you should do that as well. Do not think too deeply. If you do, you will wound yourself.”

After staring at the man for a moment, she sprang to her feet.

“No! Who are you to lecture me? You’re just a green young attendant!”

As if to show she would do exactly as she had just learned, she spoke her mind without restraint.

“I acknowledge it. And it suits you well. Take this opportunity to remove your mask and live comfortably.”

“Shut up! You attendant bastard!”

She shouted in anger and left the place just like that.

Soon after, as if he had been waiting, Seo Daeryong scurried over and sat across from him. It seemed he had been secretly watching from the end of the stairs leading to the second floor.

Sipping the wine she had left behind, Seo Daeryong said,

“A fated love always ends in tragedy.”

How could Geom Mugeuk not know that he was saying it out of concern for him? Still, it was not an atmosphere that called for seriousness, so Geom Mugeuk teased him lightly.

“When has love ever come about so easily? Even when people are in the same organization, it doesn’t work out well. You know that better than anyone, don’t you?”

It was because Seo Daeryong had once eaten a meal with a junior from the Yellow Springs Pavilion whom he liked, and nothing had progressed afterward.

“Must you always poke where it hurts? I have young ladies cheering for me too, you know!”

Looking at Seo Daeryong pouting, Geom Mugeuk smiled.

Daeryong, a fated love is something one cannot have even if one wants it. The moment I begin to love, the fate named Hwa Mugi will surely say this: How dare you play at love when I exist? As long as that fate is approaching me, love is nothing more than a luxury to me, just like the tune that drunkard over there is singing. That is why, right now, I do not even have the time to feel my own heart, not even its beating.

“Investigator Seo, let’s go train in martial arts.”

“Yes! For loser monkeys like us, training is the only path to survival!”

“What do you mean, us?”

And when they stood up, he realized it.

Jin Haryeong had left without paying again today.

Chapter 158: Even a Passing Attendant.
--------------------------------------
Jin Haryeong was ascending the dueling platform.

Today was the day her first final-round duel was held. The cheers of the heroes were tremendous. Because they respected and liked the Martial Alliance Leader, everyone liked his granddaughter, Jin Haryeong, as well. Moreover, she was a beauty renowned as the greatest beauty of Hubei, so her popularity was only natural.

“Please win!”

“Jin Haryeong is the best!”

“You’re pretty, Jin Haryeong!”

“Buy us noodles!”

“The champion is Jin Haryeong!”

Jin Haryeong flinched on the dueling platform.

She had heard something that shouldn’t have been mixed in among the cheering voices.

She looked around. Because she had never looked at the heroes from the dueling platform until now, cheers erupted from all directions. Among these countless people, she found the person she had to find. Geom Mugeuk, sitting among the heroes, had shouted.

“You just left again!”

Even amid the crowd’s cheers, his words flew clearly to her ears. At that moment, she realized. That she had come out without settling the bill again that day.

She had even been asked to pay, yet she had gone out without doing it.

‘Ah! To think I’m so scatterbrained!’

That day too, she had gotten entangled with that man, lost her temper, and just stormed out.

Even so. So what? You’re going to come all the way to the dueling arena and shout for me to buy noodles?

A hollow laugh escaped her because it was so absurd.

Her opponent in the duel, seeing that look, hardened his expression. He misunderstood that she was looking down on him and laughing.

The misunderstanding certainly firmed his will to win, but unfortunately, he could not narrow the gap in skill with her. Not even against Jin Haryeong, who was thinking of noodles throughout the fight.

As she descended the dueling platform amid applause, she glanced at the spot where Geom Mugeuk had been earlier. But Geom Mugeuk was no longer there.

To think he left without watching the duel to the end. This guy! Did he really come just to collect the noodle money?

At that moment, the bodyguard martial artist Chuho came and reported.

“The Alliance Leader asks you to come to his quarters.”

“Grandfather?”

Half a shichen later, Jin Haryeong passed through countless heavy guards and entered the Martial Alliance Leader’s residence.

Located deep within the Martial Alliance, that place had splendidly carved rocks and thousand-year-old trees harmonizing elegantly together, truly resembling a place where immortals dwelled. A man stood there with his hands behind his back, lost in thought. Blending with the beautiful scenery, he looked like a part of a painting.

“Grandfather, I’m here!”

“You’ve come?”

A man with a robust physique turned toward her.

Looking twenty years younger than his age, he possessed a perfectly muscular body that even young people could not match.

More than his body, what dominated his impression were his eyes. With his two large, intense eyes that seemed like they could make even a tiger flinch if met directly, he looked like a divine general descended from the heavens.

Rather than this place where immortals dwelled, the dueling platform she had just been on suited him more.

Martial Alliance Leader Qin Batian.

As the foremost orthodox expert, he was the pillar of the righteous martial arts world and received the respect of all. There was even a saying that the reason the Demonic Cult could not act recklessly was because Qin Batian lived; he was the symbol of the righteous martial arts world.

Even in Jin Haryeong’s eyes, her grandfather was an impressive figure.

He had a kind heart, strong will, and above all, a high sense of chivalry. He was someone who deserved to be respected by anyone.

However, that was martial artist Jin Haryeong’s assessment, and granddaughter Jin Haryeong’s assessment was different. He was a rigid, suffocating grandfather who oppressed her—nothing more, nothing less.

Honestly, that made her angrier. He was so perfect and kind to others, yet he couldn’t do the same for his family. Rather than treating those who should be treated well properly, he devoted his body and life to people he had never met.

“I met the Zhenlong Manor Lord some time ago.”

On the day she won her duel, her grandfather brought up another subject first.

What’s the rush? Let’s talk about your granddaughter winning the duel first. Or at least the weather today.

Of course, she couldn’t voice such complaints. He wasn’t scary, but he wasn’t comfortable either.

“Is the Manor Lord doing well?”

“He complained about having no strength, but that old man is still as stubborn as ever.”

Really, if it weren’t for Grandfather, this was someone she wouldn’t think of for the rest of her life.

But there was a different reason Grandfather had brought up the Zhenlong Manor Lord.

“His grandson who went away for martial arts training has returned. He subtly brought up the idea of matching him with you. What do you think?”

Ah! Here we go again.

“Why is our grandfather so anxious to marry off this cute granddaughter? It’s not like you need an in-law from a famous family, right?”

She tried acting cute, but Qin Batian didn’t accept it.

“I am asking what you think.”

“I don’t like it. Grandfather, I don’t like it.”

At her firm answer, Qin Batian’s expression slightly furrowed.

“Why don’t you like it?”

“Would Grandfather want to marry someone whose face you don’t even know?”

“He is a trustworthy person.”

“Who says so? You haven’t even seen him.”

“You’ll know when you see the Zhenlong Manor Lord.”

“How exactly? Everyone looks at the granddaughter of the respected Grandfather and thinks what? Is it the same as how I am outside? Refined, proud, and polite? No, right? I’m rebellious like this. So how can you know his grandson just by looking at the Zhenlong Manor Lord?”

Qin Batian’s expression hardened, but Jin Haryeong didn’t back down.

“As I told you before, when it comes to marriage, I will meet someone I like and do it when the time is right.”

“That is childish talk! Do you even know how treacherous and dangerous this martial world is? All sorts of strange and weird fellows roam around.”

At the mention of weird fellows, she suddenly thought of an impudent servant. That was true, he was.

“The Zhenlong Manor has produced excellent martial artists for generations, so it is a place you can trust.”

“That trust is only valid for Grandfather.”

“What do you mean?”

“No one would dare betray Grandfather’s trust. That is why you have always had an accurate eye for people. But I am different. Just as people treat Grandfather differently from how they treat me.”

It was an irreverent remark. The surrounding air grew cold with the anger-laden aura of her grandfather.

Still, Jin Haryeong didn’t back down. If she broke under this anger, she might really have to marry someone she didn’t want.

“If you keep forcing me like this, I will marry just anyone.”

“Anyone? Who exactly are you talking about?”

“Anyone on the street. Even if he is a servant, I don’t care.”

After saying it carelessly, she inwardly flinched. A servant?

“If you can do it, go ahead.”

“Grandfather!”

“You absolutely cannot do that. Do I not know you? You couldn’t do that even for the sake of your pride. Go and bring a servant, a street merchant, or anyone. I will hold a grand wedding ceremony for you.”

Jin Haryeong couldn’t deny it. Because that was who she was.

“I know best that Grandfather is truly an excellent person. I know you are a noble person who would gladly give your life for the martial world. I respect Grandfather the most. But I don’t want to turn such a wonderful grandfather into an uncool Martial Alliance Leader who forces his granddaughter into marriage.”

She knew. The reason Grandfather was intervening so actively in her marriage. It was because of her deceased father.

He thought her father had died because he married poorly. That he had ruined his life by meeting the wrong wife.

Her father had been swept up in her mother’s family grudges and eventually died from an enemy ambush. Her mother had died with him then.

Though all revenge had been concluded afterward, regret remained in Grandfather’s heart.

Grandfather, how is that Mother’s fault? It was Father’s chosen life. Father loved Mother until the moment he died.

Knowing that Grandfather was trying to send her to a family he chose for this reason, she hated it even more.

“You still don’t know the world. You don’t know how treacherous and dangerous this martial world is.”

“I will learn it myself.”

“You will regret it.”

“I will regret it.”

But no matter how stubborn she was, it didn’t work on Qin Batian. Though he was known as a generous and broad-minded person in the world, that heart did not apply to his granddaughter’s marriage.

“The Zhenlong Manor Lord’s grandson will come to tomorrow evening’s dinner. You must attend.”

She suddenly remembered what Geom Mugeuk had said to her. Whether that consideration had been for her or for the other party. Could it be that Grandfather was making the same mistake?

“Do you want to marry me off for my sake? Or to ease your own heart?”

Grandfather did not fluster like her. Qin Batian answered without hesitation.

“Both.”

At Qin Batian’s will to never back down, Jin Haryeong retreated for now.

“I’ll see you tomorrow.”

I understand Grandfather’s feelings, but that is absolutely not a heart for me.

* * *

“Hoo, hoo.”

Seo Daeryong catching his breath after training was clearly a different person from before.

When he learned from Hyeolcheon Doma, he had trained diligently without question, but now his training was different. He had enemies he had to fight, and his training was about how to defeat those enemies. Through this practical training, Seo Daeryong was entering the true path of a martial artist.

“Pavilion Lord, I want to become stronger!”

Moments like this came. Not just vaguely wanting to become stronger while playing around, but when you were working so hard you could barely breathe, you really wanted to do more. This was true resolve.

“Very well, become stronger. I shall watch over you.”

“You must watch.”

“Yes, I shall watch.”

Seo Daeryong was now a little giant. You may be grateful to me, but I am the one who is grateful. Because of you at that moment who said the main cult wouldn’t change, my regression could find its direction.

After finishing martial arts training with Seo Daeryong and returning, I found Jin Haryeong had come to the inn.

“That appearance isn’t unfamiliar now. What brings you here today?”

She had a slightly darker expression than usual.

“A face that needs counseling for its worries?”

“The granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Leader receiving counseling from the son of the Demonic Cult Leader. You figure everything out in the end. Where is the Martial Alliance’s forbidden area? To open the secret vault…”

“Stop right there!”

“Yes!”

Seo Daeryong went up to the guest room, and I walked to the seat where she had been sitting.

As soon as she saw me, Jin Haryeong asked outright.

“You shouted for me to buy noodles when you were cheering, didn’t you?”

“You heard?”

“Your words just flew into my ears and stuck there.”

“I’ve always had a loud voice.”

“Sit down.”

I sat across from her.

She filled my cup to the brim with alcohol.

“Do you drink well?”

“It depends on the occasion.”

“When do you drink well? When you’re with good people?”

“No, when I’m with good people, I actually get drunk quickly.”

“Then when?”

“I drink well when I’m angry. I talk a lot when excited, so the alcohol wears off quickly. When emotions run high, alcohol is like water. Though I regret it the next day with a hangover.”

“‘If I drink again, I’m a dog!’ Like that?”

“That’s right. You know me well.”

We emptied our cups together. Jin Haryeong set down her empty cup and said.

“The alcohol goes down smoothly for me today too.”

“Were you angry today as well?”

“Today too?”

“You usually come because you’re angry, don’t you?”

“I don’t come because I’m angry, I leave because I’m angry. Because of someone.”

At her words, I laughed.

“Do you drink alcohol poured by a servant too?”

“It depends on what kind of servant.”

I filled her cup with alcohol. She stared at me intently and suddenly asked.

“Do you have any intention of shaving that beard?”

“No, I don’t.”

“Then do you have any intention of letting yourself be used by me? Just for one day.”

“I do.”

She looked at me with a surprised expression.

“You can’t shave your beard but you’ll let yourself be used?”

“It’s just one day, isn’t it? Growing a beard takes a long time.”

“Won’t you ask what it’s about?”

“It must be something very troubling for you, lady.”

“You might end up in trouble too. Someone might hold a grudge. You might be humiliated.”

“Having grudges held against me, being humiliated—that is the life of a servant. What could be more troublesome than that?”

“Then attend the Martial Alliance banquet with me tomorrow. You’ll get to meet my grandfather too. Can you do it?”

I blinked for a moment before answering.

“That’s a bit difficult.”

“Coward.”

“Servants are all like that, I suppose.”

Jin Haryeong laughed at me.

“I didn’t have any intention of asking for your help from the start. You wouldn’t end up well if you got swept up in my affairs. Still, I’m grateful you said that.”

She rose from her seat.

“See? One out of three.”

At that moment, a young man walked toward us.

“Miss Jin.”

The moment I saw him, I was inwardly surprised. It was someone I knew. Someone who had a direct connection with me before the regression.

“I am Cao Xin of Zhenlong Manor. We met once before. You’ve become more beautiful.”

Cao Xin.

The Cao Xin I knew was not the Cao Xin of Zhenlong Manor.

After Huawuqi went into seclusion, he became the head of Shenlong family, a newly risen orthodox family. The current Zhenlong Manor would later change its name to Shenlong family and rise to prominence under Cao Xin’s leadership. After the Martial Alliance’s decline, it would grow into a sect representing the righteous faction.

It was that family I had gone to in order to retrieve the Shenwu Incense Burner, the second ingredient for the Regression Technique.

‘So at this time in his youth, you approached Jin Haryeong.’

I knew well what kind of person Cao Xin was.

He was a typical hypocrite. He hid his cruel and ruthless nature deeply and lived the life of a great hero. He didn’t hesitate to use any means necessary for his success and felt no guilt about it.

If the Cao Xin of that time and the present Cao Xin had the same nature?

My gaze turned to Jin Haryeong.

This woman is in danger now.

Could it be that her life turned out that way not because of the Cheonmyeong Society, but because of this man?

Cao Xin looked at me and asked.

“Who is this person?”

Her gaze and mine met. She smiled faintly and said.

“Don’t mind him, he’s just a servant.”

Chapter 159: How a Frog Survives.
---------------------------------
At the word “attendant,” Jo Sin’s eyes changed.

Had he not known about him, it might have been one thing, but Geom Mugeuk could see it—the contempt lodged in his gaze. That unbearable, frivolous anger, as if to say, “Why is someone like you drinking with the woman who will be mine?”

‘So he was like this from this time on.’

No—if anything, while young, he was unable to hide his true nature. The older Jo Sin had been truly exceptional at concealing himself.

“To think you would share a table and drink with an attendant. You truly are as broad-minded as the rumors say.”

Jin Haryeong answered the words he had meant as a compliment with a question.

“Does one need to be broad-minded just to sit at the same table as an attendant?”

Hadn’t the matter of sitting with an attendant been the topic weighing on her mind recently? Beyond simply sharing a table, the word taking up the most space in her head over the past few days was not “Soryong Tournament,” but “attendant.”

“I only meant that I do not care in the least about something like eating with an attendant.”

“Ah, I see. Treating even those beneath you fairly—how admirable. Well then, tomorrow’s banquet will surely have an obvious atmosphere. Let us young people hold our own banquet here.”

It was an easygoing proposal, even accompanied by praise, but Jin Haryeong refused flatly.

“I’m sorry, but I’ll decline.”

To be honest, Jo Sin was handsome. So she did feel some desire to sit with him and share a cup of wine.

But what came before that feeling was Geom Yeon.

If she drank with him, she would have to send Geom Yeon away. And if they drank together instead, she would earn Jo Sin’s displeasure.

Neither option appealed to her. In particular, she did not want to say, “We’re going to drink now, so could you leave?”

“This is a first. Being rejected after asking a woman to drink with me.”

“We’ll see each other tomorrow anyway, won’t we?”

Jin Haryeong did not see it, but Geom Mugeuk did. The moment she refused him, Jo Sin’s eyes changed. You dare reject me? A dark, cold emotion beyond mere irritation blossomed there.

Just as Jin Haryeong was about to turn away, Jo Sin sat down across from Geom Mugeuk.

“I’ll have a drink with this attendant, then.”

To the startled Jin Haryeong, Jo Sin said,

“I am curious to find out what charm this attendant possesses that allows him to sit with you when even I cannot.”

The instant she heard those words, irritation surged within Jin Haryeong. Jo Sin’s behavior now clearly meant that he intended to harass Geom Yeon. He would act like this just because she refused to drink with him? The favorable impression she had felt because of his good looks vanished in an instant.

‘Does he have no intention of making a good impression on me?’

She could not understand it. If his grandfather had brought up marriage, then Jo Sin must know about it as well.

She sat back down.

She sat because she was worried about Geom Yeon, but that only provoked Jo Sin further.

“He truly is an attractive attendant. To make Lady Jin, who was about to leave, sit back down.”

Jo Sin asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What is your name?”

“It is Geom Yeon.”

“For an attendant, you have quite a fine name.”

“Thank you.”

“Do you know who I am?”

“Earlier, you said you were Young Hero Jo of Jillyong Manor.”

“You have a good memory too. Where did you find such an impressive servant cur?”

At the words servant cur, her expression twisted slightly. Jo Sin was looking into that emotion she could not hide, and Geom Mugeuk was looking into Jo Sin’s changing emotions.

“Lady Jin is beautiful, isn’t she?”

“She is beautiful.”

“Beautiful enough to think of at night? What do you say? With her…”

Because the words that would follow were obvious, Jin Haryeong cut him off.

“Watch your mouth! You keep crossing the line.”

“I thought you were broad-minded, seeing as you even drink with servant curs, but it seems your generosity chooses its people.”

Their eyes met.

At that moment, an image appeared in Jin Haryeong’s mind. It was a pitch-black venomous snake, head raised, flicking its tongue. It was the first time she had ever felt such a thing while looking at a person, and inwardly, she was shaken.

Then Jo Sin smiled with the face of a good-natured man.

“My joke went too far, didn’t it?”

He tried to pass it off as a joke, but Jin Haryeong’s feelings had completely cooled.

‘Is he trying to show off that he can treat the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter however he pleases?’

Jo Sin asked Geom Mugeuk again,

“I truly cannot understand. What kind of person is our Lady Jin?”

“I do not know very well either.”

“You don’t know?”

“I may be an attendant, but I am not Lady Jin’s attendant.”

“You’re not?”

Jo Sin was astonished.

“Then whose attendant are you?”

“I am the attendant of Young Master Seoryong of the Seodo Sect, who is participating in this Soryong Tournament.”

“Then why were you drinking with Lady Jin? That is even stranger, isn’t it?”

Geom Mugeuk also sensed that something was strange.

‘Why is this man so agitated?’

Even if he was still in the prime of his youth, Jo Sin was a cautious man. To say this was simply due to jealousy was not enough to explain how excessive his actions were today.

‘What is making you run so wild?’

Like a child praised by his schoolteacher, like a merchant who had made a fortune, like a martial artist who had achieved great mastery in martial arts, he seemed swept up in an uncontrollable heat.

Tak.

At that moment, Jin Haryeong set her cup down with a clear sound. It was a wordless warning to stop.

Then Jo Sin’s attitude changed from what it had been until now. As if he were an old friend, he asked her gently,

“Do you not find it strange, Lady Jin?”

“What do you mean?”

“The granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader is sitting and drinking with the servant of a martial artist participating in this martial arts competition. It is something everyone would find shocking, yet the granddaughter herself, the person at the center of it all, does not find it strange in the least. What do you think?”

“So? What is it that you want to say?”

“They say that if you put a frog in lukewarm water and slowly boil it, it dies without even realizing it is dying.”

The corner of Jin Haryeong’s mouth rose. Before she could say anything in anger, Jo Sin continued.

“What if this man approached Lady Jin with some hidden purpose? What if he approached you before the finals in order to put San Gong Poison in your wine? What will you do then? Will you say you were deceived after the fact?”

Jo Sin brought his face close to Geom Mugeuk’s.

“What is your purpose?”

Geom Mugeuk said nothing. At Jo Sin’s ferocious air, as if he might strike out with a palm at any moment, Jin Haryeong stepped in.

“Young Hero Jo.”

Her cold, sunken tone made her feelings clear.

“I’ll see you at the banquet hall tomorrow. There’s still a long while before the water boils.”

Their gazes met. Jin Haryeong boldly glared at the snake with its head raised. Right now, she was not afraid of that snake.

Jo Sin smiled.

“It seems I made a mistake upon our first meeting. My apologies, Lady Jin. I shall see you tomorrow at the banquet hall.”

He rose from his seat and smiled at me. Not only Geom Mugeuk, but Jin Haryeong as well felt it—the dense killing intent contained in that smile.

After Jo Sin left the inn, Jin Haryeong drank in silence. She looked as if she were chewing over what Jo Sin had said earlier.

Geom Mugeuk silently waited for her to speak.

After a while, she let out a sigh and said, no longer with the coldness she had shown Jo Sin earlier, but returned to her usual self.

“It’s difficult. So difficult. Why is everything so difficult?”

“They say life is difficult by nature. If you asked someone from a hundred years ago, they would say it was difficult. If you asked someone from five hundred years ago, they would say it was difficult. The Alliance Leader would say it is difficult, and that waiter over there would say it is difficult. Even a child born tomorrow will say it is difficult once he grows sensible.”

“This is exactly why. How could anyone not suspect you when you’re an attendant and yet you say things like that?”

“Do you suspect me as well, Lady Jin?”

She answered honestly.

“To be honest, the moment I heard the story about the frog, I flinched.”

“If you fall for those words, you will be moved from a warm hot spring into a cauldron where the water is truly being boiled.”

Jin Haryeong shook her head.

“You’re too good with words. You win.”

“What do you mean?”

“I don’t want to trust that man even if I die, so I have to trust you.”

“At times like this, you should trust neither of us. Why choose between the two?”

Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk. The thought occurred to her that it would be a serious problem if this person were truly evil. It was because she had a premonition that stopping him would be even harder than stopping Jo Sin, who was terrifying like a snake. Even so, she decided to trust her instincts.

“This isn’t the time to worry about me. You’re in serious trouble. This frog may still be swimming in warm water, but that frog over there has just been set down alone on a rock in the middle of lava.”

She had an innate sense for reading people. She had realized it. A man like Jo Sin was not the sort to let an attendant who had sat and drunk with the woman he wanted to marry remain alive. All the more so if that woman had taken the attendant’s side.

“Young Master will protect me.”

“No. Even if your young master is skilled enough to advance to the main tournament, he won’t be able to protect you too. That man’s family is no ordinary one. Go back to your hometown right away.”

“I cannot leave my young master behind. If, as you say, he tries to harm me, would returning to my hometown allow me to avoid him? Instead, it would bring harm to my young master’s sect.”

“You’re right.”

She sprang up from her seat.

“Wait here. I’ll go find a pole long enough to pull you out of that lava.”

She left the inn.

Soon after, Seo Daeryong sat down in the place she had left.

“This side is more entertaining than the duel.”

“I thought you were tired?”

“No matter how tired I am, how could I endure the curiosity? What was that bastard earlier? Is this one of those things where an obstacle must appear in a fated love?”

“To him, I would be the obstacle.”

“He looked perfectly fine on the outside.”

“Those kinds of bastards are more dangerous, aren’t they?”

“Exactly. It’s only because he hasn’t seen you shave the Pavilion Master’s beard.”

“You’re saying I’m more dangerous?”

“Can there even be a comparison? In looks or in danger.”

As if he thought his own joke was amusing, Seo Daeryong laughed by himself.

“I’ll watch this fated love and win the tournament while I’m at it.”

“Do that. I’ll watch your fated duel and take care of this matter while I’m at it.”

I was curious to see what Jin Haryeong would do.

And the solution she brought the next day was one I had not expected.

“Come with me to the banquet.”

She was asking me to go with her to the banquet being held today. It was a banquet where the Murim Alliance Leader and Jo Sin would be present.

“I believe I told you I couldn’t go, even after being called a coward.”

“The situation is different now from then. I will take responsibility for your life.”

“How?”

“You’ll know when we get there.”

Meet the Murim Alliance Leader?

Could I truly hide my existence from him? My realm had already reached Banbakguijin, and I could appear as though I had never learned martial arts at all.

But would that work on the Murim Alliance Leader as well? Could I truly hide the presence that emanated from the very person that was me?

Probably not. I might be able to disguise a nine-star martial realm as eight stars, but I would not be able to disguise myself as an attendant.

To meet the Murim Alliance Leader meant I had to be prepared for my identity to be revealed.

“What are you thinking so hard about?”

“I am thinking about how your grandfather will deal with me after he learns about me.”

Of course, she was worrying about something entirely different.

“My grandfather won’t harm you because of your status. Even if he gets angry, he’ll be angry at me.”

“He is an admirable man, so he must treat those beneath him with dignity as well, yes?”

“Of course.”

‘Then how would he treat the blood kin of the Heavenly Demon?’

It was a question I could not bring myself to ask her.

Of course, even if it were exposed that I was of the Heavenly Demon’s blood, I would not be killed in the Murim Alliance. If that happened, war might break out. In that sense, the Murim Alliance was safer than our cult.

Instead, I would be expelled, and while taking responsibility for causing trouble with the Murim Alliance, this successor trial would end in failure.

I pondered for a long time. Jin Haryeong understood and waited for me.

At last, I finished deeply considering the various circumstances.

“Very well. Let us go.”

“Ah, you made the right choice.”

“Let us meet at the entrance of the Murim Alliance in half an hour. Since we are going to a banquet, I need to prepare a little.”

“Good.”

After sending her off, I went up to the guest room and informed Seo Daeryong that I would be attending the banquet hosted by the Murim Alliance Leader.

Seo Daeryong asked worriedly,

“Will you be all right?”

“Do I look like I will?”

“Until now, you have said you were going to meet all sorts of dangerous people. Starting with the Demon Army Lord, then the Demon Supremes. And now you are going to meet the Murim Alliance Leader.”

“What do you think? Of a leader like this?”

“In the past, I would have hated it.”

Whenever I said I was going to meet the Demon Supremes, Seo Daeryong had recoiled in horror.

“And now?”

“Even if I back away in fear, I end up on the dueling platform.”

We laughed together. The fact that the two of us were alone in enemy territory was making him and me even closer.

“Have a safe trip. I’ll be training.”

I looked at my reflection in the bronze mirror. Long, disheveled hair and cheap clothes. If anything, I tousled my hair even more and did not change my clothes.

When Jin Haryeong saw me at the entrance of the Murim Alliance, she shouted,

“You said you were going to prepare for the banquet! What on earth did you prepare?”

“Don’t I look better?”

“Not at all!”

“Should I go change?”

“No, then we’ll be late. Let’s go!”

When Jin Haryeong and I entered the banquet hall, the gazes around us focused on us.

Among a crowd of heroes, Father’s presence looked like blood, like red, standing alone in a black-and-white painting.

The presence of the Murim Alliance Leader was the same. Though there were countless people in the banquet hall, I recognized at a glance who the Murim Alliance Leader was.

It felt as though everyone else appeared in black and white, while only the Murim Alliance Leader appeared in blue.

Truly, the presence given off by the strongest masters of murim was astonishing.

Far away, Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon, who had been conversing with guests, slowly turned his head toward us.

Chapter 160: Son-in-law, Come Here and Accept a Cup of My Wine.
---------------------------------------------------------------
As the Alliance Leader’s gaze turned toward us, the noisy hall began to fall silent.

His eyes passed over his granddaughter and came to rest on me, standing beside her. I kept my head slightly lowered, not meeting his gaze.

Even without looking, I could tell he was examining me. It was a tense moment, and I made no attempt to hide that tension. After all, it would have been stranger for an ordinary attendant not to be nervous in a place like this.

Even if I was found out, I did my utmost not to reveal myself.

Jin Paecheon, the Murim Alliance Leader, asked me,

“Who might you be, young hero?”

At that very moment, Jin Haryeong said loudly,

“He’s the man I’m seeing.”

This was the plan she had come up with to save me.

I had chosen a frontal breakthrough, but so had she. I had expected it to some extent, but seeing it in person, I was grateful for her courage and consideration. How could it be easy, as the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader, to say such a thing in front of all these people?

At her words, murmurs began to spread around us. Among them were Jo Sin and his father, the Lord of Jinryong Manor.

The Lord of Jinryong Manor’s face twisted into a deep frown. He was in the middle of arranging his son’s marriage, and the woman who was to become his daughter-in-law had brought another man with her.

Jo Sin, standing beside him, smiled as he looked at us. But he was surely feeling deeply humiliated.

When Jin Paecheon raised his hand, the surroundings went quiet at once.

“I did not ask you.”

After saying that to Jin Haryeong, Jin Paecheon asked me again,

“Who are you, young hero?”

“My name is Geom Yeon.”

“From which sect do you come?”

“I am an attendant who came serving Young Master Seoryong of the Seodo Sect in Gansu.”

At the word attendant, the surroundings began to murmur again. This time, the murmur was even louder than before.

Jin Paecheon was not one to easily show emotion before the assembled heroes, but in this moment, even he could not hide his bewilderment.

Jin Paecheon looked at Jin Haryeong. His expression said, Are you truly going to do this? Jin Haryeong’s expression answered firmly, Yes.

Jin Paecheon’s gaze returned to me. Apart from her bombshell declaration, how much had the Alliance Leader grasped about me?

“Young Hero Geom.”

Even though I had said I was an attendant, he still addressed me as young hero.

“Haryeong has been fond of mischief since she was young. She was especially skilled at startling people. Once, she even circulated a sword art mnemonic backward and nearly fell into qi deviation. Today, she has startled us by bringing you here.”

Jin Paecheon spoke as though today’s affair, too, was merely one of her pranks.

“Everyone has been sufficiently startled, so I believe you have played your role well enough. You must have suffered much after being dragged here by that girl’s pestering. I will treat you properly another time, so for today, you may return.”

The Alliance Leader settled the situation in that manner. Some would think Jin Haryeong had played a prank, while others would think the Alliance Leader was trying to brush off her rebellion in this way.

At least one thing was clear: no one truly believed I was the man she was seeing.

Of course, Jin Haryeong did not back down meekly.

“You were very startled back then, weren’t you? If Grandfather hadn’t been there, I wouldn’t even be alive today. That’s why I don’t intend to play pranks anymore. This man is truly someone I like.”

Everyone’s gaze focused on me once more. Some clicked their tongues at my appearance, while others let out sighs.

Their expressions all said the same sort of thing, ranging from, “A child in one’s arms is still one’s child,” to, “So even the Alliance Leader is no exception.” They simply could not bring themselves to say it in front of the Alliance Leader.

“You said you like that man?”

“Yes.”

Her answer to her grandfather’s question came without hesitation.

Tension swirled between the two of them. It was even tauter than what had filled the dueling stage. Except for Jo Sin and the Lord of Jinryong Manor, everyone watched the situation with great interest.

“Then you could marry that man.”

At the mention of marriage, Jin Haryeong flinched.

“Still, marriage is one of the great matters of human life, so we should see each other a little longer first.”

“You have gone so far as to declare it before so many people. What is there to hesitate over? Very well. Since he is the person you want, I will have you married.”

“Grandfather!”

“I will have you married right here and now, so do it. Is this not what you wished for? You do not want a political marriage. You want to marry someone you like. Very well, I shall do as you wish!”

Jin Paecheon did not stop there and went one step further.

“Prepare the wedding at once.”

When the Alliance Leader’s order fell, his subordinates truly came and began preparing for a wedding.

As the situation flowed in this direction, Jin Haryeong was visibly flustered.

She seemed to know that her grandfather’s personality was far from ordinary, but she had not expected him to push a marriage through on the spot like this.

“I haven’t even greeted that person’s family yet.”

“Murim people are not bound by formalities. They will all understand. Go and greet them politely later.”

Jin Haryeong looked at me with a flustered expression. Truly, even I had not expected this situation.

“Our future son-in-law, come here and accept a cup of wine from me.”

Future son-in-law? That was truly a phrase I had never heard in my life, including my life before regression. And I was hearing it from the Murim Alliance Leader? Father, I’m being called son-in-law by the Murim Alliance Leader and receiving wine from him!

I accepted the wine the Alliance Leader poured for me. It was the first cup I had ever received from him.

“Thank you.”

“Take good care of my granddaughter from now on.”

From the deep and cold look in his eyes as he gazed at me, I could tell. He had already discerned my true nature. For now, he intended to first resolve the matter of his granddaughter before the assembled heroes, then deal with me afterward.

Looking at me, he smiled faintly. This was not a smile of pleasure, but a warning that said, I will see you shortly.

Meanwhile, the wedding preparations proceeded smoothly. Since warriors often held weddings in the Murim Alliance, preparing one was not particularly difficult.

In the end, Jin Haryeong raised the white flag.

“Stop! I lost. We’re not going to get married.”

Only then did Jin Paecheon smile.

How dare you act up before this grandfather of yours?

I admit defeat, Grandfather. But I do need to explain this.

After such expressions passed between them, Jin Haryeong explained today’s events to everyone.

“Elders, seniors, I apologize for ruining this precious gathering. There is a reason I caused this commotion today. Simply because he was with me, someone is trying to kill this man out of jealousy. So I arranged this, even if clumsily, in order to protect him.”

At that, someone among the attendees clapped and shouted,

“How admirable.”

It was Jo Sin.

“To go this far for that shabby, ugly attendant! Truly remarkable!”

When he clapped, others joined in and applauded as well.

I could feel that Jo Sin was thoroughly enraged. All the better. People made mistakes when their emotions ran high. Go on, then. Go even more mad.

Jin Haryeong said loudly to everyone,

“From now on, if anyone lays a hand on this man, I will surely take revenge in my own name. My grandfather has taught me all my life to refrain from tormenting the weak, so I hope you will keep that in mind.”

Mentioning the Alliance Leader was a very clever choice.

After finishing her words, Jin Haryeong tugged at my sleeve.

“Now, let’s go. Grandfather, I’m sorry for what happened today. I’ll come visit again and apologize properly.”

She and I bowed respectfully to the Alliance Leader, and then I followed Jin Haryeong out of the place.

After leaving the banquet hall, we walked silently through the inner grounds of the Murim Alliance.

Then she stopped.

“I’m sorry I didn’t tell you in advance.”

“It’s all right.”

“You said that to me back then, didn’t you? That the other person gets hurt because of my selfishness.”

“I didn’t say those words directly.”

“That was what your words meant in the end. You’re right. The way I handled this matter was also completely self-centered. But no matter how much I thought about it, this was the only way I could protect you.”

Whether the method was right or wrong, she had sincerely tried to protect me.

“Since I said it in front of Grandfather, they won’t dare try anything to harm you. If they still try something even after this, then that will be good for me. It will give me an excuse to refuse a political marriage forever. I can say, ‘Look, Grandfather said he was a good person, but wasn’t he wrong?’”

“Then I will gladly die for you, my lady.”

Jin Haryeong looked at me with a slightly angry face.

“I know it’s a joke, but don’t make that sort of joke. That is what I’ve heard from Grandfather countless times since I was young. Protecting good people from the wicked—that is the sole reason we of the orthodox faction exist. I may not be such an outstanding person, but I will never stand by and watch someone else become unfortunate because of me.”

“You are admirable.”

“I didn’t say it to be praised.”

“But will you be all right? By tomorrow, rumors of what happened today will spread.”

“Bad rumors won’t spread. Rather, the rumor will be that the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader caused a commotion to save an attendant. It’ll spread in a good way. The Alliance manages things like that too. You didn’t know, did you?”

She stared straight at me.

“At least until the Soryongjeon ends, they won’t be able to do anything to you. Find an opportunity and leave far away. Don’t go to your hometown. Go somewhere else, somewhere far.”

She took money from her robes and gave it to me.

“I’m sorry I couldn’t keep my promise to buy you noodles and wine three times.”

It was more money than the cost of noodles and wine. It seemed to be money for me to use while fleeing.

Regret flowed in her gaze as she looked at me. She believed this moment was our last. Even if I did not leave immediately, she had no intention of approaching me again. So that I would not fall into danger once more.

“Then I hope you find happiness.”

After bidding farewell, she turned and walked away. I watched her back, then looked down at the money in my hand.

Then I looked up at the bright, full moon.

Father, the unification of murim that you dream of can only be achieved by killing all people like this.

As I looked up at the night sky like that, I turned around and said,

“There is something I must tell you.”

At some point, the Murim Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon, had arrived silently behind me. He must have heard the entire conversation between her and me.

“Of course you must speak. And you had best speak well. Your life depends on each and every word.”

Jin Paecheon, who had unleashed his aura, was not the same person I had seen earlier in the banquet hall.

His aura felt like the sea. From far away came a deep resonance like the cry of a whale, and the wind carried the scent of the ocean. I felt as though I were floating alone on an endless sea.

The next moment.

Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud!

Dozens of warriors dropped down in every direction, as if bolts of lightning had struck straight down. They wore white masks over their faces and white martial robes. They were the masters of the Cheonryong Guard (天龍守護隊), said to follow the Alliance Leader even into hell wherever he went.

Clang!

Dozens of people drew their swords at the same time, yet it sounded as though only one person had drawn a blade. Just from that sword draw, one could tell how well trained they were, and how skilled. The force emanating from their swords tightened around me like a spiderweb.

“I would like to be alone with you, Alliance Leader.”

“Is there any reason I should do that?”

“What I am about to tell you is something only you, Alliance Leader, must know.”

The Alliance Leader did not dismiss those around him. Nor would his guards easily withdraw while leaving a strange master near him.

“If I had come here today to ambush you, Alliance Leader, there would have been other opportunities or methods. At the very least, I would not attempt it in a situation this tense.”

At that, the Alliance Leader said to them,

“All of you, withdraw.”

In an instant, they launched themselves away and disappeared. Though they vanished from sight, I knew they had not disappeared completely. I also knew he could not make them withdraw completely.

They were not people who simply obeyed the Alliance Leader’s commands; they were those who protected him. No matter what happened, they would never leave the Alliance Leader’s side.

They would carry out every order, but they would never accept an order not to protect him.

That was why, in order to kill the Alliance Leader, one had to kill them all.

I sent a sound transmission to Jin Paecheon.

—From this point on, I will speak through sound transmission.

—Reveal your identity first.

I revealed my identity to him.

—I am of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult…

Before I could even finish speaking.

Fwaaaang!

A stream of palm force emitted from Jin Paecheon’s hand shot toward me like lightning and struck.

Kwaaang!

An explosion rang out around my body, and dust rose all around.

When the dust settled, I was standing in place. I had crossed both hands in front of me and raised my protective astral qi to block the palm force.

Beyond my crossed arms, I looked at Jin Paecheon and continued the words I had begun.

—…the second son of the Cult Leader.

Shwaaaaaang!

Kwaaaaang!

This time, an even stronger palm force flew in and struck me.

I did not get pushed back. I remained standing in the same place, both arms crossed to block it. Even though my protective astral qi had been brought to its limit, my arms hurt as though they might be torn off.

—…Geom Mugeuk.

By blocking those two palm forces, I proved my identity with my strength.

The Alliance Leader’s tiger-like eyes were shining. The man who had asked, “Who might you be, young hero?” and the man now emitting this ferocious momentum were completely different people. And I could feel it. This burning state was his true self.

—Why has the Demon Cult Leader’s child approached my granddaughter?

I uncrossed my arms and straightened my lowered body.

—To save your granddaughter.

Jin Paecheon’s aura grew savage. The overwhelming force whirling around me made it feel as though my entire body were being torn apart.

An aura capable of ripping a person to shreds through sheer pressure alone—this was the presence of the current Murim Alliance Leader.

The next palm force that came would be incomparably stronger than the previous ones. If he drew his sword, each and every move would decide life or death.

This was where it began. The battle of whether I could persuade him or not.

—Only I can save your granddaughter.

Jin Paecheon’s aura changed from savage to terrifying. Dark clouds gathered, the world grew dim, and the sea began to overturn.

Chapter 161: Sometimes Tragedy Is Close By.
-------------------------------------------
The two auras were different.

If Father’s aura was one that made my body grow cold, my true qi slow, and seized me with the terror of suffocation, as though I were sinking into an endless abyss, then the aura of the Murim Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon, was like a typhoon encountered upon a dark sea.

I stood firm against the Alliance Leader’s aura. The storm waves thrust me up into the air, then plunged me down to the depths of the sea, but I did not lose consciousness.

Reflexively, my martial arts began to react. When faced with a powerful foe, the Wind God Four Steps stirred me like this.

—Let’s fight. Let’s tear that aura to pieces! Let’s break through the typhoon and charge in!
