Suppressing the temptation of that supreme martial art with great effort, I endured the Murim Alliance Leader’s aura to the end. Just as the spar with Father at the tavern had helped me greatly, merely enduring this aura would become a great lesson in itself.

When I withstood his aura, an emotion appeared on the Murim Alliance Leader’s face that seemed to say, “Well, look at this little bastard?”

At the same time, the pressure grew even fiercer. Now, lightning began to strike over the typhoon-lashed sea. Huge whirlwinds rose from all across the ocean, dragging seawater up with them. Truly, the Alliance Leader’s aura instilled overwhelming terror.

So the Murim Alliance Leader was this tyrannical a man. Perhaps the reason Father dreamed of unifying the murim was because of this very Murim Alliance Leader. There was no way the existence of such a powerful adversary had not provoked Father.

I had reached the limit of what I could endure. It was time to either collapse or flee.

And at that moment, I saw it again.

Two eyes.

Gigantic pupils staring at me.

Some immense being was looking down at me. I knew that it was the Heavenly Demon Soul I had once seen in the painting.

I had not even learned the Nine Transformations Demonic Art yet, so why did I keep seeing visions of the Heavenly Demon Soul?

The colossal Heavenly Demon Soul stood towering in the sea, blocking the oncoming typhoon as it looked down at me. Its gaze seemed to regard me as laughable, and yet it seemed to miss me, and also to pity me. Then again, it seemed to contain no emotion at all.

The vision of the Heavenly Demon Soul slowly disappeared.

The Murim Alliance Leader’s aura, which had surged toward me as if to kill me, had already vanished. Standing far away, the Murim Alliance Leader was astonished that I had endured to the very end.

I was on the verge of collapse. If the Heavenly Demon Soul had not blocked it at the final moment, I would have lost consciousness and fallen. The Heavenly Demon Soul, too, was likely an illusion created by my own mental strength, but it had been so vivid that it felt as if the real Heavenly Demon Soul had come and gone.

I repeated to the Alliance Leader what I had said before.

—Only I can save your granddaughter.

Now Jin Paecheon did not fly into blind rage. After all, these were the words of someone who had withstood his aura.

—What does that mean?

Meeting the Murim Alliance Leader and revealing my identity.

Originally, it was not a choice I would have made. Even so, the reason I ultimately chose it was because of the two weapons I possessed.

The first was that I trusted my experience. My experience from before the regression, and the experience I had accumulated dealing with the Demon Venerables after it. I trusted myself.

But if not for the second weapon, I would not have made the reckless choice of meeting the Murim Alliance Leader based on experience alone.

My second weapon was none other than his granddaughter, Jin Haryeong.

As long as she was deeply involved in this matter, I was certain that the Murim Alliance Leader would ultimately have no choice but to help me. I felt sorry for her, but I intended to use her completely. Since it was for her sake anyway, she would understand.

—Have you heard of the Cheonmyeong Society?

Jin Paecheon nodded. Recently, they had been committing all sorts of evil deeds while impersonating our sect, so naturally the Murim Alliance would know of it as well.

—I was tracking them under Father’s orders. But I obtained information that they were plotting something related to this Little Dragon Tournament.

—Your source?

—The Lord of Villains’ Valley.

Jin Paecheon was not surprised. Just as with the Cheonmyeong Society, he must have received information about the recent death of the Lord of Villains’ Valley. He simply did not yet know that the two incidents were connected.

—I heard that the master of Villains’ Valley had changed.

—He revealed it in order to survive, so the information is certain. That is why I had my subordinate enter the Little Dragon Tournament and came here to catch them.

—If such a thing had happened, you should have informed us.

—There are circumstances that prevented me from doing so.

—What circumstances?

—Because eliminating the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society is the successor’s trial that has been given to me.

Jin Paecheon’s eyes grew cold and deep, and he emitted his aura again. This aura was different from the typhoon-lashed sea he had shown before. It was a gentle aura that wrapped around my body like a spring breeze, trying to read the changes in my emotions, an attempt to confirm whether my words were true.

—Tell me about Haryeong.

—I am certain that the Cheonmyeong Society is targeting Miss Jin.

—Why are you certain?

—I learned that the Cheonmyeong Society is plotting something related to the Little Dragon Tournament. But when I arrived, I found that Miss Jin had entered this very tournament, and she is even a strong contender for victory. You think that is a coincidence? Absolutely not. They will undoubtedly target Miss Jin. No, they devised this plan from the beginning knowing that Miss Jin would participate.

—Even if that is true, it would be enough for me to step in and resolve it myself. Why would I entrust my granddaughter’s affairs to you?

—Because if anyone other than me intervenes right now, Miss Jin will be put in danger.

The aura that had been gently surrounding me turned cold in an instant. Even the mere mention of his granddaughter’s danger caused his aura to spew killing intent.

—The Cheonmyeong Society is a far larger and more powerful organization than we originally thought. Perhaps there are already people among the upper ranks of our sect, the Murim Alliance, or the Evil Dao Alliance who have been recruited by them. That is also why we must be speaking through voice transmission right now.

I told him that the Lord of Villains’ Valley and the martial uncle of the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon had already been recruited by them. If figures of that caliber had been won over, this was not something to take lightly.

—If you investigate, you will learn that my words are true. Fortunately, they still do not know that I have come all the way here to track them. If you trust me, I will root them out completely.

Jin Paecheon stared at me without a word. Normally, he would have regarded it as a scheme by our sect and never believed me. But because Jin Haryeong was involved, he could not dismiss it easily.

Right now, I am the only one who can save your granddaughter.

My persuasion, with my strongest weapon placed at the forefront, continued.

—I believe you must have heard various pieces of information about me. I do not know what kind of information it was, but this much is certain. I am working to establish a demonic path different from the one before. At the very least, I do not scheme by using the young granddaughter of an opposing faction.

The Heavenly Demon Divine Sect, the Murim Alliance, and the Evil Dao Alliance all send countless spies to one another. Because of that, they all know the basic information. I assume they have also grasped, to some extent, what I have done as the Pavilion Master of the Yellow Springs Pavilion.

—Do you know why I resolved that I must save your granddaughter?

—Why?

—Because she is the Murim Alliance Leader’s granddaughter, yet she is someone who would even offer marriage in order to protect the life of a mere attendant. You raised her truly splendidly.

It was both sincerity and the Flattery Divine Art.

Jin Paecheon strode toward me.

In a flash, Jin Paecheon’s sword was pointed at my neck.

“Go back and wait. I will give you an answer soon.”

“Yes, I will wait.”

I bowed politely to him and turned around.

The gaze I felt on my back was powerful enough to pierce through me.

It was only natural that he would suspect me, but even so, I could only hope that he would make a wise judgment.

* * *

When I returned to the inn, Seo Daeryong had packed everything so that we could leave at any moment.

“Ah! You’ve returned!”

“What is all this?”

“What else? I packed so we can run if things go south.”

“What about me?”

“Don’t worry. I packed your luggage too, Pavilion Master.”

“No. If something happens, that means something happened to me, doesn’t it?”

“That doesn’t mean I have to get caught too, does it?”

“……”

“……”

“Ah! You have no idea how much I wanted to hear these chilly jokes of yours.”

“It wasn’t a joke. Anyway, how was it? You met the Murim Alliance Leader, didn’t you?”

“I thought I was going to die.”

“He’s strong, as expected?”

I nodded.

“You did well to come back.”

Seo Daeryong began unpacking again. His face, now free of mischief, was full of relief.

By now, I knew Seo Daeryong well enough. It was not a joke; if something went wrong with me, he would run away at once. He was not the type to charge in emotionally, saying he would take revenge.

Only after he returned and reported the entire situation would his revenge truly begin. Just as he had memorized the personal details of all the Demon Lords for the sake of avenging his senior of the Yellow Springs Pavilion, he might memorize every single warrior of the Murim Alliance. Whether it took ten years or a hundred, he would dream of revenge until the day he died. That was the kind of person Seo Daeryong was.

“What about your identity, Pavilion Master?”

“He knows it’s me now.”

“Then he knows about me too.”

“That’s right.”

“And I’m still allowed to win?”

His expression was far too serious for me to ask, Can you even do it?

“Now you absolutely have to.”

“Why?”

“The Murim Alliance Leader now knows of the Cheonmyeong Society’s existence, doesn’t he? Then do you think he would want his own granddaughter to win and become their target?”

“He’ll want me to win and become their target instead.”

“That’s right.”

“Ah, how is a person without a grandfather supposed to live, feeling this wronged? Grandfather! Are you watching?”

At Seo Daeryong’s joke, I laughed and said,

“Think positively. This is the moment in your life when you receive the Murim Alliance Leader’s support.”

“Ah! You’re right. I’ve gained another thing to brag about at drinking gatherings. But is it all right to reveal everything like this to the Murim Alliance Leader?”

“I should abandon the idea that I can accomplish something while deceiving the Murim Alliance Leader. I will continue to deal with him honestly. I’ll show him ninety-nine truths.”

“Then what is the remaining one lie?”

“Why do you think it’s a lie?”

“Pardon?”

“It could be a truth I simply haven’t told him yet.”

“What truth?”

I looked at the Murim Alliance buildings visible far beyond the window and said,

“You will find out later. Much, much later.”

* * *

At dawn the next day, I woke from sleep.

The Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art had awakened me.

When I left the guest room and went down to the first floor, someone was sitting alone in the closed inn. He revealed no aura at all, but even his silhouette in the darkness exuded presence. Merely seeing him seated with his back turned was enough to make me tense. It was none other than the Murim Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon.

“You have come?”

I sat across from him. The Heavenly Dragon Little Tiger Unit was nowhere to be seen nearby. But they were surely not far away, concealed in various places and guarding the surroundings like an iron wall.

Jin Paecheon abruptly brought up Father.

“I met your father about twice in my life.”

I quietly listened to him.

“Each time I met him, he was someone who made me think I had to work harder. The one who kept me from stopping was your father.”

I felt as though I understood the Alliance Leader’s heart. Father was a man who yearned to become stronger more than anyone else.

“I had forgotten that for a while, but yesterday, you rekindled the dying ember in my heart.”

It meant that I, who had withstood his aura, had left that strong an impression on him. If I was already like this while young, what would I be like later? He must have felt that sense of crisis.

“Very well. I will entrust this matter to you.”

He must have agonized greatly before arriving at this decision. Just who had he consulted? Who was the person he trusted most?

“However, I have one condition.”

“Please speak.”

“I must know the entire course of events from here on. From this moment, one of my people will be attached to you. That person will watch your every move.”

Even now, I sensed that someone was hidden around us. He was as skilled in concealment as Hwi, Father’s guard. Of course, I did not reveal that I had detected him.

The Murim Alliance Leader was not entrusting this matter to me solely because there might be a traitor.

He also intended to test me. It stemmed from a desire to see what kind of person I was, someone who might become the next cult leader. In the end, I had ended up taking a test from both the Demonic Cult Leader and the Murim Alliance Leader.

“In that case, I also have a condition.”

“What is it?”

“We will deal with the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society on our side. In return, we will handle it as discreetly as possible and leave.”

Jin Paecheon nodded and rose from his seat.

“Do only what you came to do and leave.”

I could understand what Jin Paecheon was worried about. He meant not to shake Jin Haryeong’s heart.

“The subordinate who came with me teases me. He says the end of fated love is always tragedy. But young people these days do not like tragedies. I am sure that is true not only for me, but for your granddaughter as well.”

I knew very well why Jin Paecheon was so worried. Because his son had met a tragic end together with his daughter-in-law, he wished for his granddaughter, at least, not to be swept up in a tragedy caused by matters between men and women.

“Sometimes, tragedy may be close at hand.”

“What do you mean?”

“The reason your granddaughter said in front of all those people that she was dating me was probably not merely because of affection or goodwill toward me.”

“You mean because of the man who says he will kill you?”

“Miss Jin resembles you, Alliance Leader. She is someone who can never tolerate injustice.”

“That is why I worry all the more at times.”

“Then find out who that person is, and how he is pressuring your granddaughter. That single investigation will do more to improve your relationship with her than a hundred worries.”

Jin Paecheon stared at me for a moment as if glaring, then left with a single parting remark.

“Don’t concern yourself with needless worries. Just do the job entrusted to you properly.”

I watched the Alliance Leader’s back disappear into the distance, then went up to the second-floor guest room. Before entering my room, I spoke into the empty air.

“When I sleep, please sleep as well. If I go anywhere, I will tell you.”

Even at my needless attempt to act friendly, the person in hiding gave no answer.

Chapter 162: Time to Take Off the Rose-Colored Glasses.
-------------------------------------------------------
The second round of the main tournament began.

As the thirty-two were reduced to sixteen, the fervor of the Little Dragon Tournament burned even hotter. Now, only the promising young talents who had made names for themselves not only in their own hometowns but in the surrounding regions remained.

“Well then, I’ll win the championship for the Martial Alliance Leader!”

Seo Daeryong said it as if in jest, but his resolve was contained in those words as well.

His opponent this time was Li Xiu of the Shandong Li Clan.

Saber art against saber art.

If anything, Seo Daeryong was more confident in this match than he had been in the previous one.

“Strangely, I feel more at ease facing saber arts than other martial arts. Why is that?”

Since he was in the midst of receiving the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s saber art, his understanding of saber techniques was naturally high. That was why saber arts felt comparatively more comfortable to face.

“Our young master is letting his guard down, that’s why.”

“No. Absolutely not.”

Seo Daeryong waved his hands in denial. He had every right to feel wronged by the accusation of carelessness. He was working as if he had been born for this tournament.

“You saw Li Xiu fight, didn’t you? He’s not an easy opponent. Don’t let your guard down.”

“Understood.”

Though I said that, I expected Seo Daeryong to win this match. That was because his opponent’s saber art was disadvantageous in compatibility compared to the saber art Seo Daeryong had learned.

Sure enough, just as I had expected, Seo Daeryong won without much difficulty.

Waving to the cheering heroes, Seo Daeryong came down from the arena.

“I finally made it into the top eight!”

Now Seo Daeryong would be selected for one of the Martial Alliance’s elite organizations.

“What a shame. You’ve gained a chance to live a new life now.”

“Want to stay? If you say you’ll remain as a spy, Tongcheon Pavilion will welcome you with both arms raised.”

“A spy?”

“You’d conceal your identity and operate in enemy territory. The genius mind that entered Hwangcheon Pavilion at the top of the class! And on top of that, the finest spy, equipped with martial arts as well! How does that sound?”

“You said the Martial Alliance Leader already knows your identity and mine, didn’t you?”

“You remembered?”

“Don’t even dream of abandoning me and leaving! Being your right-hand man is better than winning the championship. Even if I won the greatest martial arts tournament under heaven instead of the Little Dragon Tournament, I’d still prefer the right-hand seat.”

“Would you really?”

“Of course.”

“Then imagine this! You’ve become the greatest in the world. All the martial artists under heaven respect you. Even then, you’d prefer being my right-hand man?”

“Yes.”

“Even the Demon Venerables you fear so much smile when they see you. You accidentally step on the foot of the Extreme Evil Lesser Demon, and he says, ‘That can happen. Would you like to step on this foot as well?’ Now, even then?”

“Yes! Even then, I’d prefer being your right-hand man.”

“The beauties of the jianghu line up begging you to meet them just once. Even then?”

“You would still look impressive even as a one-armed martial artist, Pavilion Lord.”

I burst into laughter, and Seo Daeryong laughed along with me. Since he had lately become completely absorbed in martial arts rather than women, the joke was all the more amusing. How could I send away such an endearing Seo Daeryong?

“Just try leaving! If you’re gone, I’ll put Commander Jang in that right-hand seat immediately.”

“Ah! Now that I think of giving it to someone else, I’m hesitating again.”

With the brilliant feat of Seo Daeryong advancing to the top eight, we left the arena.

* * *

The next day, as Jin Haryeong was about to step onto the arena, her guard, Chuho, asked her,

“Are you feeling unwell anywhere?”

At his concern, Jin Haryeong turned her head.

“No. Why?”

“You look as though you’re in a bad mood.”

“Don’t worry. It’s nothing.”

In truth, her mood had sunk low.

Since the banquet hosted by her grandfather, she had met neither Geomyeon nor Jo Sin.

If someone like Jo Sin held a grudge, killing a person would be nothing to him. If he killed without leaving evidence, then even if suspicion fell upon him, he could not be held accountable. And in this world, no one would care about the death of a servant.

In all honesty, she wanted to smash Jo Sin to pieces until he begged for his life. The reason she could not was because of her grandfather. As long as her grandfather remained Alliance Leader, if she caused trouble, he would have to bear the burden of dealing with it. Yes, endure it. Endure.

She could endure Jo Sin, but there was something she could not endure quite as well. The human heart was truly strange; when she could see him comfortably, she had not thought much of it, but once she found herself unable to meet him, she found herself pointlessly wanting to see him. What was so good about that insolent servant who constantly flustered people?

When she stepped onto the arena, cheers rang out from all directions.

“Hubei’s Fairest! Jin Haryeong! Hubei’s Fairest! Jin Haryeong!”

“Do your best, Miss Jin!”

“I adore you, Miss Jin!”

“You must win!”

“Let’s eat noodles!”

“Win the championship, Miss Jin!”

She heard it clearly. The strange yet welcome words mixed in among the cheers.

Her gaze turned toward the crowd of heroes. Wherever her eyes fell, cheers erupted. Those moved by Hubei’s Fairest looking their way shouted her name until their throats nearly burst.

Among them, she found Geom Mugeuk. She looked at him and gave a single nod. It was a signal that they should eat noodles together.

That day, contrary to Chuho’s worries, she displayed more splendid skill than ever before. Her sword was swift and precise, and her footwork could not have been more graceful.

With a dazzling victory that drove the spectators wild, she, too, advanced to the top eight.

* * *

Geom Mugeuk had arrived at the inn early and was waiting.

“Congratulations on your victory.”

Instead of accepting the congratulations, Jin Haryeong spoke firmly.

“We shouldn’t meet.”

“Why not?”

“Are you asking because you don’t know? Because you’ll be in danger.”

“Then why did you come?”

“That’s…”

After hesitating briefly, she said something unexpected.

“Because of a promise.”

“What promise?”

“You’ll know when we get there.”

She took Geom Mugeuk and left the inn.

“Do you perhaps know how to use qinggong?”

Did she ask whether he knew qinggong? Geom Mugeuk smiled and answered.

“I do.”

“No. Let’s get horses and go.”

As if she had asked something pointless, she procured horses. Geom Mugeuk did as she wished.

“You know how to ride, don’t you?”

“An ordinary servant is someone who leads horses, but the servant you see before you is special, isn’t he? I ride well.”

And so they borrowed two horses and rode off.

The place she took Geom Mugeuk was East Lake, east of the Martial Alliance.

“You asked me before to show you around here, didn’t you?”

“You remembered that?”

“It’s not as though we’ve had so many conversations that I’d forget.”

After saying it herself, Jin Haryeong had this thought inwardly.

‘Exactly. How many conversations did we even have for me to be making such a fuss? Making a fuss in front of Grandfather, making a fuss in front of Jo Sin. Even coming all the way to East Lake.’

She looked at Geom Mugeuk. His hair still hung down in a disheveled mess, and with the beard covering his face, she could hardly see his features; his clothes were old and dirty. What on earth was there to like about this man?

Just then, Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“Shall we go over there?”

“What? Why?”

“I think the view will be better from that hill.”

At the place Geom Mugeuk brought her to, Jin Haryeong was genuinely amazed.

“It’s truly beautiful!”

It was a breathtaking spot where the beauty of East Lake could be taken in at a glance.

“How did you know? About this place?”

“Servants are good at finding the right spot by nature.”

Jin Haryeong truly felt that this man was impossible to understand.

For a while, the two of them stood silently, falling into the beauty of East Lake.

It was Jin Haryeong who broke the silence.

“Your young master will be admitted into the Martial Alliance. Ordinarily, you would stay too, wouldn’t you?”

“Why do you keep trying to send me away? I’ll remain together with my young master.”

Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk. As if he had already made up his mind, Geom Mugeuk said,

“By saying in front of everyone that day that I was the person you were seeing, Miss Jin, you did everything you needed to do. What remains is my choice.”

“It will be dangerous.”

“How did you feel when your grandfather tried to force you into marriage?”

“I hated it.”

“I’m the same. I dislike people interfering in my life.”

“I may dislike it, but I won’t die.”

“A married life you hate might perhaps be more painful than death.”

“!”

After a short pause, Jin Haryeong said,

“You haven’t even been married.”

“I once saw someone truly suffer from it by their side. Don’t worry about me.”

“I don’t understand why you’re a servant.”

“Then what do I seem like?”

After a brief pause, she extended her hand and said,

“The Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter’s friend. Let’s be friends.”

She did not know her own heart. Why was she forming such a deep connection with a mere servant?

“Wouldn’t that be too much of a loss for you, Miss?”

“Why would friends weigh gains and losses? Then they wouldn’t be friends.”

Geom Mugeuk firmly clasped the hand she held out.

“To think I’d be friends with the Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter!”

“To think I’d be friends with a servant!”

After they shook hands vigorously, she said,

“If someone bullies you somewhere, tell them my friend is the Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter! Got it?”

“I will.”

“I’ll boast about it too, wherever I go. That the most special servant in the world is my friend.”

“Someday, when you need that servant friend, call for him. Perhaps that special friend might do something you never expected.”

Jin Haryeong looked at Geom Mugeuk.

“You’re strange.”

This man did not seem as if he would die. This man did not seem as if he would fall into peril. She did not know how much she would regret it later if, after all this, he ended up being killed by Jo Sin. What would she do then…

After returning from East Lake, the two parted at the entrance to Wuhan, avoiding notice.

Geom Mugeuk was returning alone when someone blocked his path.

A venomous snake, seething with poison—it was Jo Sin.

“Was your outing enjoyable?”

Geom Mugeuk faced him with an expression full of fear. Until he found out whether Jo Sin had come solely for the marriage proposal or had some connection to the Cheonmyeong Society, he intended to watch how the bastard behaved.

“You were so confident when you were with her. Why are you trembling now that you’re alone?”

“If you lay a hand on me, Miss Jin won’t stay still.”

When Geom Mugeuk acted frightened, Jo Sin’s face filled with a sneer.

“And all you can do is hide behind a woman.”

Jo Sin strode over and grabbed the collar of Geom Mugeuk, who was backing away.

“Because of a worthless bastard like you, Miss Jin has been tainted.”

Jo Sin raised his hand high, about to strike him with his palm. Geom Mugeuk squeezed his eyes shut and deliberately looked afraid.

“If you thought I wouldn’t be able to kill you just because you did that in front of the Alliance Leader, you judged me very wrongly.”

“Please spare me!”

Jo Sin lowered the hand he had raised.

“I won’t kill you. It wouldn’t be any fun if I just killed you. I need to see Miss Jin regret it. I need to see the scales fall from her eyes and see her disappointed in you. I’ll make her come clinging to me too late.”

Geom Mugeuk knew that was not the only reason. If he died or disappeared, Jin Haryeong would never agree to a marriage with him.

Using an expression like “tainted” while still trying to marry her—he did not need to see it to know what that married life would be like.

“If you don’t do as I say from now on, I’ll flay all your skin off and throw you into a barrel of salt. Understood?”

“Yes!”

“Tonight, I’m holding a banquet and inviting the young talents. I’ve invited Miss Jin as well, so you come there too. And come with your hair neatly arranged and your beard shaved. You must use this money to buy and wear the most expensive clothes you can.”

Jo Sin believed that this servant’s unadorned appearance had played a part in Jin Haryeong liking him. It was the kind of fantasy young women had. An unadorned man appeared more confident. But once opened up, there was nothing inside. Today, he intended to shatter that first.

If this servant bastard wore expensive clothes that did not suit him and came dressed up in his own idea of finery, it would instead have the opposite effect. In the end, he was no different from everyone else trying to curry favor with her. Today, he would completely shatter the girl’s fantasy.

“If you cooperate as I tell you, I’ll let you live. I’ll give you a large sum of money later too.”

“How much will you give me?”

“One hundred thousand taels.”

At the unexpected amount, Geom Mugeuk opened his eyes wide with greed.

“You must give it to me.”

“I always keep my promises. So you do as you’re told.”

Jo Sin felt pleased inwardly.

‘Trash-like bastard!’

That would make him far easier to use.

When Jo Sin left with light steps, Geom Mugeuk, who had been pretending to be a coward, returned to his original self.

“Seeing as he says he’ll give me as much as one hundred thousand taels, he intends to kill me once this is over.”

He spoke as if to himself, but it was meant for the hidden martial artist to hear.

“Report the conversation that took place in this meeting to the Alliance Leader exactly, without leaving out a single word. It is a very important matter for Miss Jin and the Alliance Leader.”

Just from hearing this report, the Martial Alliance Leader would be able to discern Jo Sin’s true intentions.

“And please convey this as well. When this matter is brought to a close, I am willing to take care of Jo Sin too. He is someone who will be harmful to Miss Jin for a long time to come. That said, the Martial Alliance cannot simply deal with the heir of a prestigious orthodox sect as it pleases. In the end, interests will become tangled, and he will be let off. Rather than do that, tell him to simply leave it to me.”

The hidden martial artist still gave no answer.

* * *

That night, Jin Haryeong entered the manor.

The young talents who had been invited had already all gathered at the manor. She absolutely had not wanted to come, but Jo Sin had said this when inviting her.

“I invited that servant as well, so Miss Jin, you must come too.”

If Geomyeon came, she had to come too. He was no longer a servant but her friend. She could not send him alone into a pit of vipers.

Jo Sin greeted her with a pleasant expression.

“The reason I called the two of you here today is because I wish to apologize.”

“Apologize?”

“That’s right. That day, my unnecessary pride got the better of me, and I unintentionally made a mistake toward the two of you. I’m sorry.”

Jin Haryeong looked at him with suspicious eyes, but Jo Sin faced her with a gentle expression. Today, he did not bring a snake to mind.

‘Yes, if possible, it would be better to resolve things amicably.’

For Geomyeon’s sake as well, there was no need to deliberately provoke this man.

It was just then.

A murmur began near the entrance, and Jin Haryeong and Jo Sin’s gazes naturally turned that way.

Chapter 163: One More Layer of Rose-Colored Glasses.
----------------------------------------------------
Geom Mugeuk entered the great hall.

With his beard cleanly shaved and his hair neatly arranged, he was dressed in a fine new martial uniform and long robe. With each and every step, he captured everyone's gaze.

It wasn't merely because he was handsome. There was a strange atmosphere about him that drew people in. An air of ease that could only be felt from someone approaching the pinnacle of the Martial Way, and a mysterious yet noble aura that no one could imitate, emanated from him.

Jin Ha-ryeong didn't recognize Geom Mugeuk. The moment she saw him, pure admiration sprang to mind.

*He's magnificent!*

He was truly magnificent. If someone were to ask her what kind of face she liked, she felt she could say this: That person right there.

She wasn't the only one having such thoughts. The women stared at Geom Mugeuk, spellbound. It wasn't just the women—the men were equally captivated.

"Who is he?"

"I don't know either. Who is he?"

"A young hero I've never seen before?"

"He's handsome!"

Such remarks flowed openly from here and there.

Drawing everyone's attention, Geom Mugeuk walked toward where Jin Ha-ryeong and Jo Sin were standing.

*Why is he coming toward us? Ah, is he someone Jo Sin knows?*

While she was thinking that, Geom Mugeuk greeted her first.

"So the young lady Jin has come as well."

Jin Ha-ryeong was startled.

*He knows me? Who is he, pretending to know me?*

The voice was familiar. But even after hearing it, she couldn't place him. Such was the vast difference between the Geom Mugeuk she had seen until now and the one before her.

"Surely you don't mean you don't recognize me?"

Even at this very moment, she truly didn't recognize him. She thought he might be someone with a similar voice, but it never occurred to her that he was the same person.

"Who are you?"

"Didn't you tell me to say something if someone bullied me? At this rate, will you even be able to help me? You might not recognize me and bully me along with them."

In that instant, Jin Ha-ryeong's eyes went wide. They were exactly words she had spoken. *If someone bullies you, tell me—the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Lord is your friend.*

"No way? You!"

"That's right. It's me."

Without realizing it, Jin Ha-ryeong let out a sharp scream.

"I'm not sure if this is something to scream about, but I am indeed Geom Yeon."

Those clear, profound eyes that were unbecoming of a servant—looking again, he was indeed Geom Yeon.

In that instant, a thrill ran through her body. The hair all over her skin stood on end.

It was already thrilling to have such a magnificently handsome man standing near her, but for this person to be that Geom Yeon who had been a servant?

She asked with a trembling voice.

"What happened?"

"I shaved my beard and tidied my hair. I also bought and wore new clothes."

Jo Sin, who was beside her, was equally flustered.

He was so surprised and taken aback that he couldn't react at all. He merely blinked his eyes and stood there blankly.

At first, he too hadn't recognized Geom Mugeuk. Because he took great pride in his own appearance, he felt resistance the moment he saw a man more handsome than himself.

That was why he had already been in a foul mood from the moment Geom Mugeuk appeared.

It was unpleasant enough that a better-looking bastard was attending the banquet, but when he walked toward him, who was with Jin Ha-ryeong, his nerves were on edge. *What the hell, this bastard. Why is he coming this way?*

*But what? This guy is that guy? That dirty servant bastard is this guy? Impossible. Truly impossible!*

Jo Sin had nearly screamed and spewed curses.

*That bastard was this handsome?*

This completely threw his plans off track. He had intended to erase the sense of mystery and bring out ordinariness, but now true mystery had burst forth. What angered him more was that he had paid money to bring it out.

Geom Mugeuk greeted Jo Sin, who was standing behind him.

"Young Master Jo gave me money to buy clothes."

Jin Ha-ryeong looked at Jo Sin with a surprised expression.

"Is that true?"

"Ah, yes. It is."

Jin Ha-ryeong misunderstood the situation.

"Oh, you really decided to apologize. Honestly, I didn't believe your words. I'm sorry."

"No."

Jo Sin was truly in a state where he could neither laugh nor cry.

He immediately sent a sound transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—You bastard! Get lost! Get the hell out of here!

He had meant to get out of the banquet hall, but Geom Mugeuk walked over to the junior martial artists as if he had been told to withdraw from that spot.

"Oh! There are many expensive and precious dishes. May I eat to my heart's content? Ah, there's alcohol here too. The aroma is very fine."

Everyone watched his every move. Geom Mugeuk felt not the slightest burden from people's gazes. He ate with evident enjoyment and drank his fill. He acted so naturally, as though he were the protagonist of this place.

Jin Ha-ryeong approached him as if mesmerized. *Damn it,* Jo Sin thought, following behind.

"You truly seem like a different person."

"It's because I usually go around so unkempt."

"Why didn't you go around like this before?"

"What business does a servant have dressing up?"

Seeing the way Jin Ha-ryeong's eyes sparkled toward Geom Mugeuk, Jo Sin's heart grew urgent.

*This isn't right. This isn't it!*

He had to change this situation somehow. He had tried to lift the veil of infatuation from her eyes, only for an even thicker one to fall into place.

Jo Sin sent a neurotic sound transmission to Geom Mugeuk.

—You bastard! Why did you dress up so handsomely!

At that, Geom Mugeuk looked at him. In those eyes that said, *You're the one who told me to dress up like this,* Jo Sin could not retort.

Moreover, he hadn't really dressed up that much. It was just shaving his beard, tidying his hair, and wearing new clothes. But even so, Geom Mugeuk shone.

Jo Sin had to drag Geom Mugeuk down somehow.

"You dressed up to the nines to impress our young lady Jin."

"Yes, I deliberately put on some airs."

As if it sounded like he had put on airs just for her, Jin Ha-ryeong's cheeks flushed slightly.

At that sight, Jo Sin burned with anguish.

*Damn it! This isn't it either.*

He should have looked awkward, like someone forcibly dressed up. But that servant bastard looked like someone who naturally suited such luxurious clothes.

And his actions displayed *I dressed up because I'm attending this banquet* without a hint of shame. That rather made him look like an even more confident person.

"By coincidence, your martial uniform is the same color as young lady Jin's, as if you matched."

"So it is."

Unable to watch that scene any longer, Jo Sin sent a sound transmission to one of his men to have him strike up a conversation with Jin Ha-ryeong.

While Jin Ha-ryeong briefly accepted the man's greeting, Jo Sin took Geom Mugeuk to a corner.

"You crazy bastard! Do you want to die?"

"Why do you ask?"

"Do you want to earn one hundred thousand nyang for opening young lady Jin's eyes today? Or lose one hundred thousand nyang and die by my hand? It's up to you."

"Then what should I do?"

"Do something that makes a woman lose all affection. Put on airs, pick fights with others, cause a drunken scene! You got it?"

Jo Sin truly hadn't expected such words to come out of his own mouth. Had he ever tried to degrade someone so vulgarly? He would have just killed them and been done with it.

But right now, he was impatient. He had to somehow humiliate Geom Mugeuk before a new blind infatuation stuck to her eyes. His irritation had truly reached the top of his head.

"Then give me the money first."

"What?"

"Give me fifty thousand nyang and I'll do it. And another fifty thousand nyang when it's finished."

"You bastard! Do you want to die?"

Jo Sin raised his hand high to strike, but Geom Mugeuk wasn't frightened.

"That's how deals work. I have to make a living too."

"You worn-out piece of trash!"

Just then, Jin Ha-ryeong walked over from that direction. Jo Sin lowered his raised hand and adjusted Geom Mugeuk's clothes.

"Our servant young hero truly looks like a different person."

At that, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

"As I said before, it's all thanks to the young master."

"Honestly, the money was given for our young lady Jin."

"You are truly broad-minded and magnificent."

Geom Mugeuk did not spare his praise for Jo Sin.

Jo Sin felt an uneasy sensation, as if he had opened something that shouldn't have been opened.

But he couldn't back down like this.

Jo Sin changed the subject.

"We plan to make a large investment in the Martial Alliance from our main branch this time."

When Jo Sin brought up work matters, Geom Mugeuk stepped aside.

Jo Sin enthusiastically boasted about his clan and his wealth, but Jin Ha-ryeong's gaze kept turning toward Geom Mugeuk, who was talking with other junior martial artists. Especially when a female martial artist listening to Geom Mugeuk giggled at something amusing, Jin Ha-ryeong clenched her fist.

How could Jo Sin not know her heart?

Even though he had stopped speaking, Jin Ha-ryeong was only looking at Geom Mugeuk.

Jo Sin's expression hardened.

Jin Ha-ryeong suddenly felt a chill and turned her head. Jo Sin was silently staring at Geom Mugeuk. The snake that had disappeared from her heart rose again.

"It's truly a waste for someone like that to be a servant. Don't you think?"

"What can one do about an innate fate?"

Regret was felt in her answer. Jo Sin was truly furious.

*Damn wench! She ignores me this much?*

Needless rage surged up.

He again sent a sound transmission to the junior martial artist he had ordered to talk to Jin Ha-ryeong earlier.

—Provoke a martial arts duel and fight that bastard. Beat him mercilessly when you do.

The junior martial artist who received the sound transmission was Ho Yeong.

Ho Yeong was a person with the ambition of somehow getting into Jo Sin's good graces to expand his clan. Therefore, he accepted the order without hesitation.

Ho Yeong asked Geom Mugeuk.

"I see you wear a sword. Have you learned martial arts?"

"Yes."

"How did a servant learn martial arts?"

"It's because my master is an excellent person."

"Then may I learn a move or two of the martial arts passed down by that excellent person?"

Everyone made curious expressions.

As Jin Ha-ryeong tried to stop them, Jo Sin spoke to her.

"Aren't you curious, young lady Jin? What kind of martial arts and how much that servant has learned?"

Of course, Jin Ha-ryeong was curious too, but her worry took precedence.

"Even so, he wouldn't be at the level to compete with junior martial artists."

"Warrior Ho is a scion of a prestigious clan. He won't injure that servant."

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke to Ho Yeong.

"If we use swords, we might get hurt, so let's compete with fist techniques."

"Very well."

Ho Yeong was internally dumbfounded. He hadn't expected him to agree to fight so readily.

*I'll break at least one place thoroughly.*

If he pretended it was an accident and broke an arm or a leg, Jo Sin would be sufficiently satisfied.

"Then please go easy on me."

"Fine. To think I'd experience a martial arts duel with a servant in my lifetime. Truly a wonderful day."

Ho Yeong was letting his guard down. Showing a nervous demeanor was itself a shameful thing.

Geom Mugeuk dodged Ho Yeong's fist. He didn't dodge it lightly, but narrowly, as if he were about to stumble and fall. Laughter burst out here and there.

Ho Yeong's fist flew again, and this time too, Geom Mugeuk dodged by rolling on the floor. Ho Yeong, pressing Geom Mugeuk as if hunting prey, looked around and laughed.

"This is really dull……"

"Watch out!"

The moment he turned his head at someone's shout.

*Pow!*

Ho Yeong was sent flying by Geom Mugeuk's fist. He had let his guard down and taken a clean blow. Moreover, as he fell, he crashed through a table, and the food there splattered, dirtying Jo Sin's clothes.

Jo Sin, whose anger was already at its peak, couldn't hold back and exploded.

"Goddamn it!"

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk ran over and examined the fallen Ho Yeong.

"Fortunately, he isn't seriously injured."

Compared to Geom Mugeuk, who first checked his opponent's injuries, and his own behavior of spewing curses because food had gotten on his clothes, Jo Sin felt awkward. Though no one spoke, contempt was contained in the gazes directed at him.

But what angered him more was that Jin Ha-ryeong didn't even pay him any mind and was only looking at Geom Mugeuk.

"I apologize. I got too excited and—"

Geom Mugeuk apologized to the junior martial artists watching.

Jin Ha-ryeong stepped forward and spoke on their behalf.

"It's nothing to apologize for. The one who didn't concentrate during the duel is at fault."

Everyone nodded at Jin Ha-ryeong's words. At least in this duel, Ho Yeong had let his guard down and been caught, so they couldn't blame Geom Mugeuk for a surprise attack.

Jin Ha-ryeong bid farewell to Jo Sin.

"Well, I'll be leaving now. Come, you come too."

Jin Ha-ryeong took Geom Mugeuk and left the banquet hall. From behind came the sound of something shattering. She didn't know what had broken, but she knew who had broken it.

Geom Mugeuk walked back with Jin Ha-ryeong along a moonlit road.

She was again worried about Geom Mugeuk.

"You've become even more dangerous because of this."

Then Geom Mugeuk suddenly spoke.

"Friend."

Jin Ha-ryeong was startled for a moment.

"Why are you so surprised? Didn't we agree to be friends?"

"No, that's right."

"Friend. You are the granddaughter of the Martial Alliance Lord, the Hobuk Ilmi, and a candidate to win the Soryongjeon. You are most true to yourself and most magnificent when you are proud and elegant. This appearance of worrying about someone every time doesn't suit you."

After a brief pause, Jin Ha-ryeong spoke.

"Then can I trust you? Do you have the confidence not to die miserably at the hands of someone like that for no reason, and not to make me feel guilty for the rest of my life?"

"I do."

"Really? How can I believe that?"

"Because we're friends. Believe a friend's words."

"You said I suit a proud and elegant appearance, but I'm not that kind of woman. I have lots of thoughts, lots of worries, and lots of regrets. I'm timid by nature. I don't like meeting people, and that's why I don't even know how to shake off my wounds. So don't you startle me later."

"Yes, I won't."

It was something absurd, but she found herself believing Geom Mugeuk's words. There was no basis for her belief, yet she believed.

This time, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

"In exchange, promise me one thing."

"What promise?"

"If something strange and inexplicable happens to you as you live your life from now on, promise that you will definitely come find me and tell me."

He hadn't said this solely regarding the matter of this Cheonmyeong Hoe. It was also something he said with the time in mind when signs of that incident would one day reach the Martial Alliance as well.

"A moment like that in my life……"

No matter how much she thought.

"It's right now?"

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke with a faint smile.

"Yes, if something as strange and inexplicable as right now happens, make sure you do. Now, let's head back."

Jin Ha-ryeong silently watched Geom Mugeuk's back as he walked ahead. Suddenly, this thought came to her. As he had said, perhaps it was fine now to show the proud, elegant bearing of the Hobuk Ilmi, rather than needless worries.

She began to walk toward Geom Mugeuk, who was waiting up ahead as if asking why she wasn't coming.

Chapter 164: Once You Set Foot on That Path.
--------------------------------------------
That night, an unexpected visitor came to Jin Haryeong’s quarters while she lay sleepless. It was her grandfather, Jin Paecheon.

“You’re still awake?”

“Yes.”

“Shall we go for a walk with Grandpa for the first time in a while?”

“I’d like that.”

She walked with Jin Paecheon through the inner gardens. The martial artists guarding various spots stepped aside of their own accord and concealed themselves.

“You’ve been worried sick because of me lately, haven’t you? I’m sorry, Grandfather.”

Jin Paecheon didn’t mention it, but neither did he look angry.

“You attended a banquet today?”

“Yes. It was a banquet hosted by Young Master Jo of the Jinryong Estate.”

“How was it?”

Her grandfather had never asked such a question before. If he knew she had attended, then he would also know that Geom Mugeuk had been there.

“Grandfather. Have you ever looked at someone and had a mental image come to mind? Like an animal, or anything else.”

“I have. Recently as well.”

The person Jin Paecheon recalled was Geom Mugeuk. The mental image that had surfaced amid the surprise and shock of that moment, when he blocked Jin Paecheon’s momentum, still had not faded.

“I experienced it for the first time this time. When I looked at someone, a snake came to mind.”

Even without explicitly naming Jo Sin, her grandfather would know whom she meant.

As expected, Jin Paecheon did not ask who it was.

She had thought he might defend Jo Sin, but her grandfather showed no particular reaction.

Since they had a private moment, she revealed her honest feelings about Jo Sin.

“Even if I end up in a strategic marriage, it won’t be with Young Master Jo.”

Then Jin Paecheon said something unexpected.

“I have no intention of arranging a strategic marriage between you and Young Master Jo.”

“Really?”

“I am already a man who has everything. What would I lack to do such a thing?”

At Jin Paecheon’s firm words, Jin Haryeong broke into a bright smile. Her grandfather truly looked magnificent.

“I knew you’d say that.”

Jin Haryeong linked her arm through her grandfather’s.

“What is a fully grown lass doing?”

“Let’s walk like this for just a little while.”

Jin Paecheon looked at his granddaughter with a benevolent smile. She was the only joy in the hard life of an Alliance Lord. It felt like just yesterday that she had been toddling about, yet she had already grown so much.

“Were you upset with this old man?”

“No. I understand, Grandfather. What grandfather would like to see his granddaughter associate with a servant?”

“It is not because that child is a servant.”

“Then why?”

Jin Paecheon did not explain the reason. He saw no need to speak of Geom Mugeuk’s true identity.

Jin Paecheon changed the subject.

“How is the Little Dragon Tournament going?”

“I’m doing my best.”

“Do you have confidence you can win?”

“You know how hard I’ve worked for this tournament. I will definitely win!”

Because he knew that all too well, Jin Paecheon hesitated. Honestly, he felt the urge to advise her to forfeit. Even though he knew the Heavenly Fate Association had intervened, it was because he had wished from the start that Jin Haryeong would not become involved.

But because he knew the effort she had poured into this tournament, he could not bring himself to tell his granddaughter to give up.

“Do your best.”

“Yes, Grandfather.”

And so the two walked for a long while beneath the moonlight. Jin Paecheon realized that at every moment of their walk, his granddaughter was thinking of someone.

Because he could guess who it was… he only hoped it would pass as a fleeting fever of youth.

* * *

Seo Daeryong, returning from training, was greatly excited.

“Do you know how many people recognized me on the way back?”

“You mean how many women pretended to know you, don’t you?”

“How quick-witted you are. How many was it?”

“That would depend on how slowly you walked.”

On roads crowded with people, especially where there seemed to be many women, Seo Daeryong would display his Turtle Divine Skill. Then, if someone happened to recognize him, he would come running like an excited rabbit.

“Five?”

Seo Daeryong flinched.

“How did you know?”

“I analyzed it from your current state of excitement.”

“That can’t be it. Were you watching over me? In case I fell into danger?”

“Ah! Now that I think of it, that might be possible. I had never once considered the possibility that our young master might fall into danger.”

“…It’s because I’m reliable, right?”

“…Of course.”

“That’s not it! You forgot, didn’t you? How could you leave me unattended when I could have fallen into danger!”

“Well, you fall into danger, get taken hostage, get injured—that’s how you become a true martial artist. What’s wrong? You know this well.”

“I’ll pass! I’m going to live quietly as an inspector of the Hwangcheon Pavilion, not getting hurt, not getting swept up in conspiracies, just training hard in martial arts. I’m training for my health!”

“Hurt? Because I don’t pay attention to you?”

“No.”

“Rather, not paying attention to you is probably helping you.”

“What do you mean?”

“Let’s say someone has noticed us. If I don’t pay you any attention at all, would they think you have value to exploit? They’d think that touching you for no reason would likely expose their own identities instead.”

“Ah! You thought that far ahead.”

“Of course. It’s good to act like strangers.”

Then Seo Daeryong looked out beyond the inn and spoke.

“Here comes our Pavilion Lord’s sponsor.”

The person walking toward them was Jo Sin. Seo Daeryong had teased him endlessly about having his beard shaved and being bought new clothes.

“Well then, let’s scatter like strangers.”

Seo Daeryong hastily went up to the upper floor.

Shortly after, Jo Sin entered the inn and sat facing me.

“The noodles here are delicious. Would you like some?”

At my words, Jo Sin snorted with an expression that said now was not the time for noodles.

Looking at Jo Sin, I thought this: even a villain needs a process to ripen. The difference between Jo Sin, who had been the head of the Shinryong Clan, and the current Jo Sin was as great as whether I shaved his beard or not.

“Should you have won that duel yesterday? Or should you have gotten beaten?”

“I couldn’t help it. I’m sorry.”

“It’s not something that ends with an apology. Miss Jin has fallen even harder for you.”

Jo Sin pulled out a dagger.

“Now I have no choice but to turn that handsome face of yours into a go board.”

If it were middle-aged Jo Sin, instead of pulling a dagger, he would have taken me to the place with the best cuisine in this region and treated me with a bright smile. He would have fed even more delicious food to someone he hated while praising them all the more. Subtly and persistently, he would never have revealed his own emotions until he obtained what he wanted. Had he been like that, he would have been as difficult to handle as the Demon Lords, but the current Jo Sin was in the palm of my hand.

“Rather, if you use me, you can win Miss Jin’s heart. I know women’s hearts well. If I help you, you will be able to marry Miss Jin.”

I knew Jo Sin was determined to marry Jin Haryeong. Therefore, it would be hard for him to refuse this offer.

“What could someone like you know?”

“If I knew nothing, how could I have made Miss Jin fall for me?”

“It was all intentional?”

“Of course. Every word, every action must go according to plan. Matters between men and women cannot be rushed.”

There had been a wandering martial artist who was a womanizer. Whenever he rested, he would tell everyone his experiences, and even recounting what I had heard from him would be enough to pique Jo Sin’s interest.

“What is your motive for helping me?”

“What motive could there be? It’s money.”

“Now I see, this fellow is quite something.”

I had to keep this Jo Sin from acting up until the winner emerged. When the Heavenly Fate Association approached the winner in earnest, I would see how Jo Sin acted then. Whether he was truly connected to the Heavenly Fate Association or not.

Until then, I had to become the type of person who scammed women for money. That way, Jo Sin could tell me his secrets. Not because he trusted me, but because I was someone he would kill once the job was done.

“Do you know why Miss Jin is avoiding Young Master Jo right now?”

“Why?”

“Miss Jin is someone who pursues freedom. But when people try to force a strategic marriage on her, she naturally rebels. The reason she rejected Young Master Jo and felt goodwill toward me was also because of that rebelliousness.”

Jo Sin’s expression relaxed slightly. It meant the reason she disliked him wasn’t because of him personally.

“Did she say that?”

“Must she say it outright? You can tell by reading the room.”

“So?”

“Right now she is devoted to the Little Dragon Tournament. If you push for marriage at this time, would that work? At times like this, you should cheer for her to win the tournament.”

Jo Sin nodded as if he hadn’t thought of that. People like Jo Sin were always like that. Thinking only of themselves, they had no understanding or consideration for the other party.

“Go and congratulate her on the day of her duel.”

“Congratulate her? Then should I buy flowers or gifts?”

“Winning the entire tournament might be different, but if she receives a gift for winning the Round of Eight, it would only burden her. Just go and congratulate her with words.”

“Then I should prepare a gift in advance for when she wins the tournament.”

“As expected, you’re clever! Martial arts, clan, and even looks. Young Master Jo is perfect in every way. If you just choose the right method, Miss Jin will have no reason to refuse.”

Jo Sin smiled at me, pleased by my praise. But the gaze directed at me still contained unmistakable killing intent. He was likely imagining the moment he would kill me after using me to the fullest.

“You sly bastard! If she sees your true self, she’ll flee in horror.”

I said to him as he rose from his seat.

“Make sure to prepare one hundred thousand nyang!”

When I disappear like smoke from the Martial Arts Alliance, this little villain who would become a future petty lord would also disappear from this world.

The moment Jin Haryeong reached out her hand to the servant and suggested they be friends, she had not taken my hand, but had grasped her new life.

* * *

Two days later, Seo Daeryong won his duel in the Round of Eight.

But in the process, he suffered a grievous wound, his shoulder pierced by his opponent’s sword.

The officiating martial artists rushed over, hoisted him up, and ran toward the tent where the physician was waiting.

I ran after them as well.

While being carried, Seo Daeryong sent me a voice transmission.

*If I die, please tell our drinking comrades! That this Seo Daeryong fought valiantly and left without regrets!*

*Leave your last words quickly. I suppose I’ll give the right-hand man position to Jang Ho?*

*No!*

*Then who should I give it to? I-an?*

*No!*

*Then who?*

*Even if I become a ghost, I won’t give up that position.*

The physician’s treatment was finished.

Fortunately, his life was not in danger, but the wound was quite deep.

The Martial Arts Alliance physician applied Golden Wound Medicine and prescribed internal medicine as well, advising him to rest for a while.

I returned to the inn and sat Seo Daeryong down.

“Sit.”

“Yes.”

I infused a stream of internal energy into Seo Daeryong’s back. My true qi slowly circulated through his meridians and soothed the injured area. Depending on whether this process was done or not, the speed of injury recovery differed greatly.

“Thank you. I feel much better.”

“Don’t worry. I’ve examined you, and fortunately, you avoided vital meridians and your bones are unharmed.”

Originally, the Round of Eight was the limit of Seo Daeryong’s ability. However, barely winning this time was the result of pouring out every drop of his luck, talent, and effort.

But it seemed Seo Daeryong wanted to go all the way to the end.

“Can I participate in the Round of Four duel with this body?”

Because I well knew that he wanted to go, I deliberately did not dissuade him.

“You have to go. Bind it tight with bandages and go win. Even if you lose the use of one arm, you have to go. That’s how important this is.”

Then Seo Daeryong’s reaction was unexpected.

“Yes.”

At his meek answer, I jumped up instead.

“Yes? You should say you can’t go. What kind of duel is worth risking your arm for? You should argue!”

“But there’s too important a matter at stake.”

“What matter? What’s so important about a Martial Arts Alliance tournament?”

“It’s not that. It’s the Pavilion Lord successor exam. If I win, we pursue them.”

I quietly stared intently at Seo Daeryong’s face. Seo Daeryong’s eyes, which had been filled with loyalty and passion, finally avoided my gaze. Caught him.

“Are you trying to back out by making me feel guilty?”

“Did it show?”

“Of course. That passion was so unlike you. You’re the type who wouldn’t give up an arm even if the sky fell.”

“Of course. Even if murim falls to ruin, I’m not giving up my arm. Well done, Seo Daeryong! Looking good, Seo Daeryong! Making it to the semifinals is more than enough! Praise!”

His clowning made me laugh, and Seo Daeryong laughed along before screaming that it hurt.

“Aaah! It hurts. Don’t make me laugh.”

“You were the one being funny. Rest.”

I was about to leave the room when Seo Daeryong asked from behind me.

“Is it really impossible?”

Unlike before, his tone was serious. I could feel how badly he wanted to continue in the tournament.

“No.”

“Your successor trial is one thing, Pavilion Lord, but it’s also for my own sake. I felt many things during this martial arts tournament. I want to see it through to the end.”

A brief silence passed.

“Inspector Seo, once you set foot on that path, it’s hard to leave it.”

“What kind of path is it?”

“The path where you bind up an injured arm and step onto the sparring stage. The kind of path where, even with blood streaming down your whole body, even while limping, even if you can’t see out of one eye, you still walk forward.”

It was the way I had looked when I was gathering the final ingredient for the Great Art.

Because I had lived such a life, I wanted to stop him. And because I knew better than anyone how much such a life could make a martial artist grow, I could not stop him.

But once he crossed that line and tasted that world, he would no longer be able to live as an investigator of Hwangcheon Pavilion who practiced martial arts merely for his health.

“At least this time, I want to go as far as I can.”

“Your next opponent will be hard to beat even if you weren’t injured.”

“Please show me the way.”

“If you push yourself too hard, you may never be able to use that injured arm again.”

“Then I’ll get a right arm too. Ah, for me it would be a left arm.”

“You could suffer an even worse injury.”

“I’ll take that chance to get some rest.”

The resolve in Seo Daeryong’s eyes was no different from my own in the past.

“Then you shouldn’t be sitting there like that.”

Seo Daeryong sprang to his feet.

“Your fate always changes at an inn.”

“And you are always there, Pavilion Lord.”

How could it be because of me, or because of the place? It was surely because of the decision not to give up made by that small, stubborn man.

I took him and left the inn.

Burning with fervor, Seo Daeryong shouted.

“Now then, from this moment on, it’s hell training!”

As Seo Daeryong strode toward the mountain where we usually trained, I said to him,

“Not there. This way.”

I turned and walked in the opposite direction.

“There’s somewhere we need to stop by first.”

The place I stopped at with him was the Central Plains Money House. There, I withdrew five hundred thousand nyang.

“Why are you withdrawing so much money?”

“Because we’re not going to hell. If you try hell training with an injured body, you’ll only ruin it even more.”

“Then where are we going?”

“Paradise.”

To the startled Seo Daeryong, I gave a meaningful smile.

“We’re going to paradise. A paradise with a very expensive admission fee.”

Chapter 165: Is This Paradise?
------------------------------
Jin Haryeong’s sword brought forth one variation after another.

Unable to withstand her dazzling sword forms, her opponent at last admitted defeat.

“I have lost.”

As the sword aimed at his throat was withdrawn, Jin Haryeong raised a hand high to everyone.

Thunderous cheers erupted.

“Jin Haryeong is the best!”

“You’re amazing, Jin Haryeong!”

“The champion will be Jin Haryeong!”

“Jin Haryeong, the foremost beauty of Hubei!”

But today, the words she had been waiting for were nowhere to be heard among them. She looked around at the assembled heroes, but Geomyeon was nowhere in sight.

‘Could she be badly hurt?’

She had heard that the young master Geomyeon served had been injured in the quarterfinals. She knew that was probably why Geomyeon had not come, but somehow she still felt disappointed. She had hoped Geomyeon might come, if only for a moment, while she was fighting.

‘Her young master is hurt, so of course she can’t.’

With that lingering regret, she stepped down from the dueling platform, only to find an unexpected person waiting for her.

“Congratulations on your victory.”

It was Jo Sin. Normally, her expression would have hardened at once, but for some reason today she treated him gently.

“Thank you.”

“If Miss Jin wins the championship, I will host a separate banquet to celebrate.”

“Your words alone are enough. Then, I must prepare for my next bout.”

After giving a light cupped-fist salute, Jin Haryeong left the arena.

Jo Sin somehow felt that she had treated him more warmly than usual.

‘Was that fellow right?’

He had claimed to know women well, and sure enough, a single word of congratulation had changed the way she treated him.

‘That bastard is more useful than he looks.’

But that was his misunderstanding.

The reason Jin Haryeong had been at ease was because, during her walk with her grandfather the day before, she had received a firm answer that there would be no political marriage.

In that case, there was no longer any need to stay uncomfortable by clashing with Jo Sin. Once the Little Dragon Tournament ended, she would not have to see his face anyway. She would be joining the Murim Alliance’s elite organization and beginning a new life of activity.

‘My friend, I hope your young master recovers soon.’

Only then would she be able to hear that welcome voice among the cheers of encouragement.

* * *

At that hour, Geom Mugeuk was taking his young master somewhere.

“Aaaaaah! This is too fast!”

Geom Mugeuk was running with Seo Daeryong on his back, using lightness skill. Seo Daeryong, carried on his back, could do nothing but scream.

It was far too fast. Even though he was using lightness skill, Seo Daeryong was shocked that a person could run this quickly. And while carrying him, at that.

Seo Daeryong could not even open his eyes. Even when he barely managed to, the surroundings whipped past so rapidly that it only made him dizzy. What on earth could Geom Mugeuk see that allowed him to run like this? The fear that they would slam into a tree or a rock at any moment nearly made him faint.

What was even more astonishing was that, even in the midst of all this, his own body was utterly comfortable. Psychologically, he was terrified and uneasy, but his injured body was in fact wrapped in Geom Mugeuk’s energy so that it would not be jostled as they ran.

‘This is possible? He’s running this fast and protecting me at the same time?’

It was something he would never have believed if he had not experienced it himself. Seo Daeryong had been amazed by Geom Mugeuk many times, but what was even more amazing was that there were still things left to amaze him like this. No, there was something else to be amazed by. He did not even rest while running. Just how much inner force did he have?

‘Maybe this is a dream. Did I pass out on the dueling platform and start dreaming? Huh! Don’t tell me I died?’

As he was carried on Geom Mugeuk’s back, lost in all sorts of wild imaginings, they finally arrived at their destination. The place the two of them reached was a remote village far away from the Murim Alliance.

“Aaaaah! Is this paradise?”

After getting down from his back, Seo Daeryong staggered and then sank to the ground where he stood. He truly thought he would die from dizziness. He took a deep breath.

“Ah! Now I feel alive. It really is paradise. People are meant to live with their feet on the ground.”

Seo Daeryong swept back his disheveled hair and looked around. Inwardly, he was tense. He had set out prepared to endure hellish training, yet Geom Mugeuk had said they were going to paradise and brought him to a rustic village like this.

“Let’s rest over there for a bit before we go.”

At the entrance to the village stood a flat wooden platform, and a child was lying on it reading a book.

Seo Daeryong felt something strange. They had run all the way here in such a hurry, and now they were going to rest on a platform like this? And one at the village entrance where a child was reading?

Then something even stranger followed. Geom Mugeuk spoke to the child lying on the platform.

“Child, go bring out a bottle of liquor brewed from insects.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the child who had been lying on his stomach reading got up and asked with a yawn.

“Who introduced you?”

From that exchange, Seo Daeryong realized that what Geom Mugeuk had just said was some kind of secret code.

“We came from the Divine Cult.”

When Geom Mugeuk revealed that they were from the Demon Cult, Seo Daeryong was startled. Since this matter had begun, Geom Mugeuk had not once revealed his identity.

The child, on the other hand, did not seem surprised at all, as if he did not know where the Divine Cult was.

“Tell them we came privately, not on official business, so they may handle it as usual.”

“Yes. Please wait.”

The child disappeared somewhere. Only then did Seo Daeryong ask Geom Mugeuk.

“Where is this place?”

“The entrance to paradise.”

“It doesn’t look like paradise at all.”

“What kind of place is the paradise you’re thinking of?”

“Well…”

“You just imagined yourself in a room full of beauties, drinking wine over a feast of delicacies, didn’t you?”

Seo Daeryong flinched.

“No, I didn’t.”

Seo Daeryong’s face flushed red as he tried hard to deny it. He had probably imagined an even more indecent paradise.

“Hear me, sentient beings! Most places in this world that look like paradise are all hell. Hohoho.”

“Great monk who has attained enlightenment, please go back inside, and call our Pavilion Master for us. Ah! I asked where this place is!”

“You’ll know once you go in.”

A short while later, the child returned.

“They said to bring you in. Follow me.”

Geom Mugeuk and Seo Daeryong followed the child.

A formation had been installed along the winding path. It was the sort of formation that, if one did not know the precise location of the Gate of Life, would make a person wander endlessly before emerging somewhere else.

After they passed through the formation, there was a vast manor.

The signboard hanging there read:

Paradise Wish (極樂願).

It was not the “won” for manor, but the character meaning “wish.” It meant a wish to be reborn in paradise.

“It really is paradise?”

“I told you we were going to paradise.”

In the place they entered after following the child, an old woman was standing.

At a glance, she possessed an extraordinary bearing, and her back was so straight that the term old woman did not suit her. However, perhaps because she had been badly injured somewhere, bandages were wrapped around her head and arm.

“You came from the Divine Cult?”

“That is correct.”

“Proof that you are from the Divine Cult?”

Geom Mugeuk released demonic energy. It was demonic energy of a force sufficient for her to believe.

“Enough! Why are you spewing out such unpleasant energy? Ptui! Ptui!”

The old woman brought salt and sprinkled it over her body. She showed no sign whatsoever of fearing the Demon Cult.

“For your age, your realm is very profound.”

“Thank you.”

“Why did you bother revealing your identity?”

“Because no matter how much I tried to hide it, you would have found out anyway.”

“You know enough about me, do you? Then this will be simple. What do you want?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at Seo Daeryong and said,

“Please make this friend win the Little Dragon Tournament.”

Seo Daeryong stared at Geom Mugeuk in shock.

“If you ask someone to make me win, I can win?”

“Probably?”

“What?”

The old woman approached Seo Daeryong, whose eyes had gone wide. Perhaps her leg was injured as well, for she was limping.

The old woman held out her hand to Seo Daeryong.

“Hand!”

Since her right arm was injured, she extended her left.

“Ah, my left arm is injured.”

“Then should this old woman hold out her injured arm?”

“No. I mean, can we just not shake hands?”

But the old woman still held out her hand. The moment Seo Daeryong reluctantly took her arm—

The old woman suddenly yanked Seo Daeryong’s injured arm upward.

“Aaaaaagh!”

Despite Seo Daeryong’s scream, the old woman moved his arm this way and that without mercy.

“The arm is attached just fine.”

“Aaaaaaaah! I’m dying!”

“What an exaggeration!”

“I thought I was going to die from the pain!”

“You have a face that looks like it was made for exaggerating.”

Seo Daeryong pouted and looked at Geom Mugeuk. His expression asked who on earth this damned old woman was, but Geom Mugeuk merely smiled.

“Swing your saber once.”

“I don’t want to.”

The old woman seemed to glide toward Seo Daeryong, and then once again grabbed his arm and fluttered it here and there. Her movements were so fast that Seo Daeryong could not even stop her.

“You still don’t want to? Still?”

“Aaaaaaaah! No, I want to! Please spare me! I’ll do it!”

Only then did the old woman let go of his arm.

Wiping away the tears streaming down from the pain, Seo Daeryong drew his saber.

After watching Seo Daeryong’s forms for a while, the old woman shook her head.

“It will be difficult. As you are, you cannot defeat Jin Haryeong.”

The old woman knew well how the Little Dragon Tournament was currently unfolding. She knew the ability of Jin Haryeong, the favorite to win, precisely, and from the sight of Seo Daeryong swinging his saber, she had grasped his skill as well.

“That is why we came, because it is difficult. Are you not someone who makes the impossible possible?”

“That depends on how much money you pay. How much can you offer?”

Geom Mugeuk did not bargain over the price.

He held out the five hundred thousand nyang he had brought to the old woman.

“Five hundred thousand nyang.”

At that, Seo Daeryong was shocked.

“Wait! Don’t tell me that money you went looking for was meant to be spent on me?”

“That’s right.”

“No. Absolutely not!”

Seo Daeryong had thought, up until the point Geom Mugeuk found the money, that it would probably be used for him in some way, but he had never imagined all of that money would be spent.

“It’s my money, so why are you saying no?”

“In any case, no. I give up on the path I said I wanted to take. It was all nonsense. I’m happier as I am. Five hundred thousand nyang?”

Seo Daeryong sent a voice transmission.

-And that old woman is strange, isn’t she? One look and you can tell she’s a quack pretending to be mysterious. Her own body is that injured, so who is she going to heal? She should heal herself first… Huh! She just ate a bug!

While chewing something thoroughly, the old woman sat on a rock and massaged her own limbs.

“Investigator Seo. Money can always be earned again, but an opportunity like this will not come twice.”

“I don’t want it. Do not spend such a fortune on me. Even fifty thousand nyang would be a waste.”

“It is money with that much value. It is the cost of turning one man into a true martial artist.”

“What a man needs to become a martial artist is not five hundred thousand nyang, but will and courage.”

“Don’t feel burdened. From now on, you only need to work diligently as an investigator. And if a situation comes where you can occasionally save our cult from crisis, then save it. That will be enough.”

“That is a burdensome thing to say!”

“If you think too much, life becomes exhausting. You simply live following your heart as each moment comes. You said you wanted to win, didn’t you? I want to make you win. If our hearts line up perfectly, that is enough.”

At that moment, the old woman, who had arrived without them noticing, snatched the promissory note from Geom Mugeuk’s hand.

“I accept the treatment.”

“No!”

Seo Daeryong stepped forward to take the promissory note back from her, then flinched and stopped.

The old woman had only glanced at him, yet he was overwhelmed by the sharp force of it and his body froze.

“How many days remain before this exaggerator’s next bout?”

“Seven days.”

“Seven days. Time is tight. We begin at once.”

The old woman limped into the building.

Once the two of them were alone, Seo Daeryong quickly asked,

“Who on earth is that person? Please tell her to treat her own body first!”

“How is your arm?”

“What do you mean, how? It hurts even more. Huh?”

Seo Daeryong moved his injured arm. It moved better than when he had first arrived. The old woman had not been flailing his arm around at random after all.

“Huh? It hurts less?”

Then an astonishing name came from Geom Mugeuk’s mouth.

“That person is none other than the Insect Physician.”

Seo Daeryong was startled. It was a name he had heard before.

“She was a real person?”

“Yes.”

There was a rumor like a legend that drifted through the murim. It said there was a divine physician skilled in handling insects, one who used insects to cure all kinds of diseases and whose medical arts were so great she could even bring the dead back to life.

There were even rumors that her skill surpassed the Demonic Physician of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult and the Divine Physician of the Murim Alliance. Whether that was true had not been proven, but at least one thing was certain. Because the Insect Physician herself was an expert who had reached the pinnacle, it could be said that she knew a martial artist’s body better than the other two.

“But isn’t she the person called a money grub because she’s so obsessed with money?”

“If not for this person, no one can restore you in such a short time.”

“How do you even know someone like this? Ah, forget it. It’s too expensive.”

“Strictly speaking, not all of that money is going into you, so don’t feel burdened.”

“What? What do you mean by that?”

Just then, the door to the building opened and the Insect Physician shouted.

“What are you doing not coming in? Time is money too, money!”

Geom Mugeuk and Seo Daeryong hurried into the building.

Seo Daeryong’s expression naturally twisted as he stepped inside. Everywhere, there were jars filled with insects, and all manner of insects had been mounted on the walls. The smell was so terrible that he had to pinch his nose shut.

Seo Daeryong had no choice but to drink the medicine he was given, though he had no idea what ingredients were in it.

“What if it’s poison? No, this is definitely poison, poison!”

With a desperate expression, Seo Daeryong refused to drink.

“Then you will have paid the most expensive price in the world and died in the greatest luxury. It will be a death recorded in murim history.”

The Insect Physician washed off all the Golden Wound Medicine that had been applied and smeared on a new medicine. He could not know for sure, but it was undoubtedly a medicine extracted from all sorts of insects.

She wrapped a new bandage over it, and even the way she wrapped it was completely different from before.

Then she laid him on a stone bed radiating heat.

“Sleep deeply for a while.”

Whether because of the medicine or the hot bed, Seo Daeryong could not resist the drowsiness pouring over him.

“Ah, this isn’t right… It isn’t… Pavilion Master, we’re being decei…”

And so Seo Daeryong fell asleep as if entranced.

While the treatment proceeded, Geom Mugeuk went to the backyard and looked around at all the insects she raised. Hundreds of containers were stacked there, and inside them were all kinds of bizarre insects.

The Insect Physician came to stand beside Geom Mugeuk and, looking at the insect in front of them, said,

“That one may not look like much, but if it bites you, its poison will kill you instantly within half a quarter-hour. I have developed resistance to poison, so I am fine, but it would be dangerous for you.”

Saying it was dangerous, the Insect Physician opened the box and took out the insect. As if eager to fly out at any moment, the poisonous insect beat its wings rapidly. Holding the poisonous insect in front of Geom Mugeuk’s face, she asked coldly,

“If you came knowing about me, then you must have had a rough idea of my fee as well. Why did you pay so generously?”

Chapter 166: One Word Would Be Enough.
--------------------------------------
The sound of the poisonous insect’s wings beating in Chungui’s hand heightened the sense of danger.

The poisonous insect looked as though it might fly over and bite me at any moment, but I gazed at her calmly and said,
