“There is a subordinate I cherish as dearly as my own heart. She was abandoned by her parents when she was young. Even so, she grew up so bright and strong.”

Perhaps Chungui’s fingers tightened, for the poisonous insect’s wingbeats grew even faster.

“She must have had a turning point as well. If she had taken a different path then, she would have lived an unhappy life filled with resentment and vengeance toward her parents.”

“Why are you telling me that story?”

“Because, Doctor, you are serving as a signpost at that turning point.”

“!”

Chungui was startled.

“You knew?”

“Yes. I know about the kindness you secretly bestow.”

I knew why she was so obsessed with money.

She was sponsoring countless abandoned children. She provided them with food, sent them to schools, sent them to martial academies. In that way, she prepared paths for them to live and sent them out into the world. Though she was called by the contemptuous name “money grubber,” she was in truth the godmother of orphans, having lived her entire life for children.

This truth would be revealed only after her death. The reason I said I would give her five hundred thousand nyang was that, after deducting the treatment fee, I was donating the rest for those children.

She stared at me, then crouched down where she stood, tore the wings off the poisonous insect, and plucked off its legs one by one, popping them into her mouth.

“I’ve built up resistance to poison, so ordinary medicine can’t control my poisoning. I’m barely holding on by fighting poison with poison (以毒制毒).”

Perhaps the pain from that was severe, for she kneaded her shoulders, arms, and legs.

“How did you find out about my work?”

I sat down beside her.

“I am the second son of the leader of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult.”

There were advantages to being Father’s son. If you were about the successor of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, people tended to accept it even if you knew most secrets.

“Then why did you give me more money?”

Even after I revealed my identity, she was not surprised, nor did she try to curry favor. It was precisely because of that nature that she must have been able to devote herself steadfastly to children all her life while enduring abuse.

“Yes. I, too, wished to add my heart to your good deeds. To me, Elder, you are not Chungui. You are a Great Divine Physician.”

At the words Great Divine Physician, Chungui’s eyes trembled slightly. Great Divine Physician was the highest praise that could be given to a doctor.

“I hid it because I did not want to receive thanks like this.”

Chungui chewed up the poisonous insect she was holding, body and all. It must have been terribly bitter, for she grimaced, but she forced it down.

She did not seek praise, but I thought her true feelings were different.

Wouldn’t she wish, at least once, for someone to recognize her good deeds? Wouldn’t she want, at least once, to receive such sincere respect and praise? Just once.

There was something I had learned in my life before regression. That a single word could sometimes release deep wounds and emotions that had piled up over a very long time.

If only someone would say that one word. If only they would say it just once. Yet we do not easily hear that one word.

Thank you, you worked hard, I’m sorry, it was thanks to you… Just one word would be enough.

Because they cannot hear that one word that seems about to come but never does, people fall ill in their hearts. They live their whole lives carrying wounds that could have been healed with a single word.

I hoped that what I had just said to her would serve that purpose. That it would become the one phrase that unraveled her weary life. Your life was admirable.

Chungui turned to look at me sitting beside her.

I, too, stared straight at her, then abruptly said,

“You are holding my wrist right now.”

At some point, she had taken hold of my wrist. Of course, I could have avoided it, but I did not.

“Ah, forgive me. It has become a habit to take the pulse whenever I see a person.”

Though she said that, she was examining me. She wanted to know whether my words were true. She intended to judge not by my words, but through my body’s reactions. Through the smallest changes in my body, by reading my pulse, she could determine whether the other person was telling the truth or lying.

A martial artist did not carelessly allow others access to their pulse. The fact that I did not stop her from taking my pulse now was no different from saying, I sincerely trust you.

Then Chungui, who had been examining my body, was startled and asked,

“Could it be? Have you achieved great completion in the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art?”

As expected, a Divine Physician was a Divine Physician. With this brief pulse examination, she had realized that I had achieved great completion in the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art. In the past, thanks to the Blade Ghosts, subordinates of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, I had been able to attain great completion in the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art.

“That is correct.”

For the first time since we met, I saw her gaze tremble with genuine emotion. I knew where her surprise came from.

* * *

Seo Daeryong had a dream.

It was a dream in which, let alone the finals, he was stabbed by a sword and died in the semifinal bout.

Looking down at him as he suffered a mortal wound and lay dying, Geom Mugeuk said,

“I spent five hundred thousand nyang, and you die this pointlessly? Isn’t that too much?”

At his cold response, Seo Daeryong felt a surge of resentment.

“You’re the one who’s too much, Pavilion Lord. I’m dying. I’m dying, I said.”

“Everyone dies. Though no one dies right after receiving treatment worth five hundred thousand nyang.”

Feeling wronged, Seo Daeryong shouted,

“That’s why I said I wouldn’t do it!”

As he shouted, Seo Daeryong woke from his sleep. He flinched at the sight before him. Just like in the dream, Geom Mugeuk was looking down at him.

“I told you not to do it too.”

Unlike in the dream, Geom Mugeuk’s gaze was gentle, and Seo Daeryong let out a sigh of relief.

“I dreamed I died in the semifinals.”

“Dreams mean the opposite, so it’s fine.”

“You said it was a waste of five hundred thousand nyang while watching me die!”

“Then I guess it’s not the opposite? Looks like you’re going to lose in the semifinals.”

“Please don’t say anything unlucky.”

Seo Daeryong tried to get up, but his body would not move.

“Huh? My body won’t move.”

Seo Daeryong lifted his head and looked at his body. He was shocked. His entire body was densely covered in needles.

“She must have placed the needles wrong and paralyzed me. I can’t move at all!”

“They’re important needles. Chungui suppressed your paralysis acupoints.”

Only then did Seo Daeryong feel relieved.

“I’m sorry. For getting hurt for no reason. For making you spend so much money.”

“Listen, Investigator Seo. At times like this, you’re supposed to say this: ‘No, Pavilion Lord. You made me enter the martial arts tournament for no reason and got me hurt like this. This is too much. Even if it costs not five hundred thousand nyang but five million nyang, please cure me!’”

“Ugh, don’t make me feel even more sorry. Please leave.”

Geom Mugeuk turned to go out, but Seo Daeryong spoke from behind him.

“To be honest, I wanted to receive treatment. I said it was too much money and that I couldn’t, but in my heart, I thought it would be nice if I could be treated and go up onto the dueling platform. So I pretended to be unable to resist and accepted the treatment. If it had been my own money, would I have received it?”

“You would have.”

“Why are you so sure?”

“Because someone who spends another person’s money but refuses to spend his own wouldn’t have agonized over this in the first place.”

“Couldn’t it be a feint? Me pretending to be good until the very end.”

“What would you gain by pretending to be good in front of me? Give me just one reason.”

“……”

“See? It’s not that.”

“I suppose you’re right.”

I left him and came outside.

Beneath the window of the room I had just left, Chungui was sitting in the sunlight. I sat down beside her. Perhaps she had heard the conversation inside, for Chungui asked me,

“Then why are you pretending to be good in front of me? Is the successor of the Demonic Cult worried about orphans of the Central Plains?”

As she asked the question, Chungui took my wrist pulse again.

At the moment that would become another turning point in my life, I revealed my honest feelings.

“As you said, since I am a successor of the Demonic Cult, I am walking a path where I might die at any time. So is it not only natural that I would want to make a good impression on someone with medical arts said to revive even the dead?”

“Is that all?”

“What else could there be?”

She released my wrist.

After staring at me for a moment, she suddenly held something out. It was one of the poisonous insects she had eaten earlier.

“Would you like to try one too? It’s delicious.”

“If I won’t die from eating it, I will.”

“Shall I make sure you don’t die?”

At that moment, I knew I stood before another turning point in my life.

“There is one thing I have wanted to accomplish all my life as a doctor. But it is something I cannot even attempt unless the person’s blood vessels and meridians are as strong as yours. It may be dangerous, but if it succeeds, it will be a test that greatly benefits you.”

After thinking for a while, I nodded.

“I will try it.”

“Why don’t you ask what kind of test it is?”

Because I already know what kind of test it is, and I already know the result.

As I brought Seo Daeryong here, I had wondered whether this fortuitous encounter would truly reach me. It was not a fortunate chance that could be obtained by force. So I had decided that if I obtained it, I would, and if I did not, I would simply let it pass. Yet now, that fortunate chance was reaching me. In that sense, the one who led me to this opportunity was Seo Daeryong.

“Because I trust that a test given by someone who has lived her whole life for orphans will at least not be one that harms me.”

“Are you a fool? It could be a scheme to kill the successor of the Demonic Cult.”

“It is fine. There are others besides me who can inherit the position. A line of them, even after that.”

Chungui chewed up the poisonous insect she was holding and tapped her shoulder.

“It hurts so much I could die. Massage it for me.”

I massaged Chungui’s shoulders.

“It seems you have a great deal of work. Why not take in a disciple? You could have them massage your shoulders too.”

“What young ones these days would like poisonous insects? They all endure a month or two, then run away.”

It seemed there really had been disciples who had fled that way.

“Shall I become your disciple?”

“Do not say things you do not mean.”

“Are you all right with this, Medical Immortal?”

“With what?”

“If the test succeeds, will the person who may become the successor of the Demonic Cult not grow stronger?”

“I’ve lived my whole life being cursed anyway, so what is there to worry about? Harder. A little to the side. Ah, that feels good.”

* * *

When Seo Daeryong woke again, all the needles that had been stuck in his body had been removed. He felt refreshed, as if he had just woken from a good sleep.

Seo Daeryong sat up and moved his arm, but it did not hurt at all. It felt as though he had never been injured in the first place. No, it felt as though his body was in even better condition than before he was hurt.

‘How can this be?’

When Seo Daeryong came outside, Chungui was sitting in the yard, kneading her arms and legs.

He walked over hesitantly and sat down some distance away from her.

“Aren’t you going to say, ‘Thank you for treating me’?”

“You received five hundred thousand nyang.”

“You impudent brat. If someone is paid, does that mean you don’t have to thank them?”

“I’ll do it in a little while.”

“You look so kind, so why are you so spiteful?”

“You said I looked like a whiner.”

“What use are you if you’re a whiner, spiteful, and narrow-minded to boot?”

As she said that, she rubbed her arms and legs.

“Why is your body injured?”

“I got hurt catching bugs.”

“Why didn’t you treat yourself?”

“What you see now is after treatment.”

“What kind of bugs were you catching, and how did you end up this injured?”

“Stop grumbling and come massage my shoulders.”

“We gave you five hundred thousand nyang, so why do I have to massage your shoulders too?”

Though he grumbled with his mouth, Seo Daeryong went to her and massaged her shoulders.

“Ah! That feels good. You’re better than your master.”

“Since I was young, I’ve often been told I have strong hands. I used to massage my grandmother’s shoulders a lot too.”

“Is your shoulder all right?”

“How did that wound heal so completely? You are amazing.”

“Your inner force should have increased a bit too.”

“What?”

Startled, Seo Daeryong checked his inner force. His inner force had increased substantially. Compared to other rising talents of his generation, what he lacked relatively was inner force. But now, as he possessed inner force no less than theirs, his greatest weakness had disappeared.

Deeply moved, Seo Daeryong prostrated himself.

“You are my benefactor!”

“Not a quack?”

“How could that be? You are a Divine Physician!”

“Save your thanks for your master, who paid the money.”

“Since we’re talking about money, please give me a discount.”

“Are you worried your master will become a beggar?”

“My master has plenty of money, so I’m not worried.”

“Then?”

“It’s because it’s far too much money for me to pay back.”

“You intend to repay all that money?”

“I have to.”

As Chungui looked at Seo Daeryong, a faint smile formed at the corners of her mouth.

“If you massage my shoulders well, I may knock off ten thousand nyang.”

“Yes, Divine Physician! I will massage your arms too.”

Seo Daeryong once again earnestly massaged her shoulders and arms.

“By the way, where is the Pavilion Lord?”

“He is inside that vat over there.”

Seo Daeryong flinched. What she pointed to was a vat in the middle of the yard, filled with a strange liquid. From the moment he had come out, he had been barely enduring the foul smell coming from that vat.

“You don’t seem worried?”

“Who’s worrying about whom? Even if I worry about everyone in the world, I don’t have to worry about our Pavilion Lord. If I worry for no reason, I’ll be the strange one. He must have gone in because it was worth going in.”

“There was a reason he was willing to spend five hundred thousand nyang on you.”

Since it was a compliment about him, Seo Daeryong felt pleased.

“Go over there and bring that purple vat.”

“Yes.”

Seo Daeryong brought the vat over with a grimace. That vat, too, was filled with a liquid that gave off a terrible stench.

“You brat, do you know how precious that is, and yet you’re plugging your nose?”

“What is all this?”

“I became a ragged mess catching those things.”

Chungui took it and poured it into the vat where Geom Mugeuk was submerged. As the liquids mixed, bubbles churned and frothed.

“How does he breathe in there?”

“If you’re that worried, go in for him.”

“I decline.”

Seo Daeryong took several steps back.

“By the way, what kind of experiment is it?”

“While researching poisonous insects all my life, there was one desire I absolutely wanted to fulfill. Your master willingly became the test subject for that research. He will either come out dead, or come out transformed.”

In Seo Daeryong’s mind, Geom Mugeuk’s death was not a possibility.

“What will he transform into?”

Then, from Chungui’s mouth came words he had never expected.

“The Ten-Thousand-Poison-Impervious Body (萬毒不侵之體).”

Chapter 167: You Grow Old the Moment You Stop Working.
------------------------------------------------------
Seo Dae-ryong was startled by the words “Ten Thousand Poisons Impervious Body.”

If one became impervious to all poisons, no poison in the world would work on them. It meant they could not be killed by poison. In the martial world, poison was the thing one had to be most wary of, so his greatest weakness would disappear.

“You can achieve something like that this easily?”

“Your master will achieve it easily. As for the life of mine that makes it possible, it has been a long and arduous road to get here.”

Seo Dae-ryong found himself admiring Geom Mugeuk all over again. How could one explain the fate of being brought here for treatment and ending up with a Ten Thousand Poisons Impervious Body?

“You’re envious, aren’t you?”

“Not at all.”

“Why not? You look like a greedy fellow.”

“That greedy fellow is one who knows his place. I believe everyone has a vessel of fate given to them. The vessel of my fate is only big enough for me to struggle with everything I have just to win the Little Dragon Tournament. And even that has grown enormously because I met the Pavilion Master. In the past, it was truly no bigger than a tiny soy sauce dish.”

When he thought back to the days when his dark and gloomy nature had been filled with a desire for revenge against his senior in the Yellow Springs Pavilion and resentment toward the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, even a soy sauce dish felt like an overstatement.

“I’ve seen many times how large the Pavilion Master’s vessel of fate is. I’m seeing it now as well. Rather than jealousy, I’m grateful. After all, I have one more story to tell someday with my grandchildren sitting on my knees.”

“Then why do you look so anxious?”

“I’m wondering just what sort of deeds he is destined to accomplish in the future for him to walk such a path. Since, whether I like it or not, I’m his right arm, I can’t avoid that fate either.”

“If you think your vessel is about to shatter, you can just run away.”

“Even if everyone else runs, I can’t. He carried me on his back and ran all the way here without resting for even a moment, then showed me with my own eyes that he would put down five hundred thousand nyang. Thanks to that, I’m completely ruined. If I can’t run away anyway, is there any other grand method to make him stronger? Please make him even stronger.”

“All the ingredients I’ve saved and treasured are going into that over there. Even if I wanted to, I can’t do any more for him now.”

As another wave of pain seemed to come over her, Chungui rubbed her own shoulder.

Seo Dae-ryong massaged her shoulder for her.

“No, wait. You can make someone else’s body impervious to all poisons, but you can’t fix your own aching shoulder?”

Chungui answered with a bitter smile.

“That’s how everyone lives. You brat, do you think you’ll be any different? Ah, to the side. A little more to the right. Yes, there. Ah, that feels good.”

Geom Mugeuk remained submerged in the vat for two more days from that day onward.

Chungui continued adding various ingredients to the vat, and the smell grew more and more foul.

Then, when the final ingredient was added, something astonishing happened.

A fragrant scent began to rise from the vat that had been filled with that terrible stench.

It was truly unbelievable. It was not as though the final ingredient she mixed in had a fragrance capable of devouring all the foul odors either.

It felt as though the harsh smells had combined and combined again until, with the addition of the last thing, they had transformed into something completely new.

Seo Dae-ryong placed his hand on the vat and prayed fervently in his heart.

‘Pavilion Master, please hold on!’

* * *

At first, I truly felt as though I would die.

I would rather have fallen into the filthiest, foulest-smelling place in the world than enter that vat. Ah, no, that would not do. Because the foulest-smelling place in the world was probably this vat.

“Take this medicine before you go in.”

“What kind of medicine is it?”

“A medicine that will kill you for two days. If you’re afraid, you can give up even now.”

“Give up? This is a welcome medicine. There is no way I could go in there alive.”

Without hesitation, I swallowed the medicine Chungui gave me.

When I swallowed it, closed my eyes once, and opened them again, I was lying on a bed.

Only then could I feel it. Two days had passed in the meantime. I had not dreamed, nor did I remember anything. For those two days, it seemed I had truly died and awakened again.

When I opened my eyes like that, this time Seo Dae-ryong was looking down at me. After staring at me silently, he spoke with a serious expression.

“After careful consideration, I’ve decided not to repay the five hundred thousand nyang.”

Seo Dae-ryong was probably the only person who could make me laugh the moment I woke up.

“I don’t recall ever lending you five hundred thousand nyang.”

At that moment, Chungui entered the room and said,

“Leave that overly dramatic, overthinking fellow here. I ought to take him as my disciple.”

Seo Dae-ryong recoiled and stepped back in horror.

“I would like that too, but I have something important ahead of me.”

“You can come after you finish it. What do you say?”

“I’m sorry, but I already have a master. And the most frightening one in the world at that.”

“Not the bugs?”

“Of course, the bugs are frightening too.”

I could sense that there was sincerity mixed into Chungui’s jest.

Seo Dae-ryong shot me a look. What are you doing? Hurry up and stop her.

Of course, I did not stop her.

“It wouldn’t be bad to live a new life, would it?”

“You’re trying to cut off your right arm the moment you open your eyes. You can’t cut off that right arm. When you paid five hundred thousand nyang, my arm turned into ten-thousand-year cold iron.”

I laughed and said to Chungui,

“I will contact you around the time that ten-thousand-year cold iron rusts, wears out, and falls off.”

Seo Dae-ryong would not do, but if I found someone else suitable, I thought I must certainly recommend them as Chungui’s disciple.

“You, wait outside for a moment.”

“Yes.”

Chungui sent Seo Dae-ryong outside.

“You may get up now.”

I rose from the bed. Even though I had been unconscious for two days, my body felt light. Truly, such was the power of profound medicine.

“There’s a pleasant scent coming from my body.”

“It will continue for several days.”

“I wish this fragrance would last forever.”

“If it did, you’d never be able to infiltrate anywhere for the rest of your life.”

“Wouldn’t it be enough to live a life where I never need to do things like infiltration?”

“I suppose so. If you become the Heavenly Demon, you might be able to live such a life.”

Chungui brought wine and poured it for me.

“Now, receive a cup of wine from me.”

I drank the wine she poured in one refreshing gulp. She did not drink herself, only filled my cup. Only after I drank three cups in succession did she stop filling it. I was the one who drank, yet she was the one who revealed what was in her heart.

“To tell the truth, I was very exhausted. Both my body and mind were worn down. Perhaps helping orphans was a kind of desperate struggle to keep myself together. It was like a support that kept me from collapsing from exhaustion, that kept me from running wild.”

I understood what she meant. I, too, had experienced something similar. During my wandering swordsman days, Seojin had protected me, and during the days I sought ingredients for the grand method, my longing for revenge had played that role.

Chungui continued speaking.

“When I saw you, I felt something like destiny. It was an emotion I had never felt in my life. Ah, to be honest, it was when I learned that you had mastered the Heavenly Meridian Strengthening Art. At last, the time had come. The indifferent heavens were finally giving me a chance as well.”

She turned her head toward the window and looked up at the sky. My gaze naturally followed hers outside.

“That damned sky, at least when it came to my research, had been a truly cursed one.”

Her gaze returned to me.

“Do you know what was in the wine you just drank?”

“I did not sense anything at all.”

“It contained Formless Poison.”

I was startled.

Formless Poison was a poison that was colorless, odorless, and tasteless, and its power was so great that it was called the King of Poisons. It was the Heavenly Demon of the world of poison, the number one under heaven among poisons. Naturally, it was difficult to make and even more difficult to detoxify.

“I’m perfectly fine.”

“Congratulations. From now on, no poison in the world will be able to kill you.”

Her words meant that the test had succeeded, and that I had become a Ten Thousand Poisons Impervious Body.

“Formless Poison? Wasn’t that too strong a thing to confirm it with?”

“This way, there’s no need to confirm it twice.”

Creating a Ten Thousand Poisons Impervious Body with her own hands and then conducting the final test with Formless Poison was the crowning touch to the life she had dreamed of.

I was now sharing in the moment when a master artisan fulfilled the lifelong wish of a lifetime. I could feel all the emotions she felt: joy, relief, satisfaction mingled with regret, and lingering wistfulness. Perhaps they were the emotions I would feel someday at the moment I killed Hwa Mugi.

I performed a deep bow to her.

“Thank you.”

“I am grateful as well. This was my lifelong wish. Now I no longer have to scour the world searching for poisonous insects.”

The reason her body ached so much was also an illness she had gained in the process of obtaining poisonous insects.

I went to her and massaged her shoulders.

“Oh my, that feels good.”

“Come to think of it, I still haven’t even massaged my father’s shoulders.”

“What an unfilial son.”

“You may not know this, but he is someone I would have to risk my life just to massage his shoulders once.”

Chungui laughed as though she found it amusing.

As I massaged her shoulders from behind, I felt that her energy had ebbed compared to when we first met. It felt as though she had burst forth in her final climax and was now beginning to descend.

“What goal will you live for from now on?”

“What goal? I should just live comfortably.”

“You must not.”

“I must not?”

Because the moment someone who had devoted themselves so passionately to something stopped working, they would begin aging at a terrifying pace from then on. They would begin hurting even more.

“You must research and develop something new for the martial world again. And then give it to me again.”

“So the real greedy fellow was here.”

I moved from massaging her shoulders to stand in front of her and took Chungui’s wrist. When I held her wrist as if taking her pulse, she stared at me.

After pretending to take her pulse for a moment, I said to her,

“This is a body that can comfortably live another fifty years. Work!”

Her expression showed that she understood and knew exactly why I was saying this.

“Bah, you. You want me to live fifty more years saying it hurts, it hurts? Don’t curse me.”

“I will come back to see you and make sure to cure you.”

“Even if those are empty words, I’m grateful.”

“I am the one who should be saying thank you for the rest of my life. Thanks to you, Elder, I will live my entire life without worrying about being poisoned.”

“It was something that could have succeeded or failed. But you bravely risked your life. Think of it as compensation for that courage.”

“I will repay this grace again and again in the future. If you ever need my help, send word to the West Lake Inn in the Central Plains, and I will certainly come to see you.”

“I shall.”

I went outside with her.

Seo Dae-ryong greeted us with a joyful face.

“Congratulations to you both.”

Just as we finished saying our farewells and were about to leave, Chungui held out a poisonous insect.

“Eat one of these before you go! It’s delicious.”

“I’m sorry, but I dislike bugs too.”

I ran first, and Seo Dae-ryong sprinted after me.

Chungui watched the backs of the two men receding into the distance, then looked up at the sky.

“I have fulfilled my duty as a physician.”

After gazing up at the sky for a while like that, she habitually rubbed her shoulder and went inside.

“Oh my, my arm.”

* * *

I carried Seo Dae-ryong on my back and dashed with swift footwork.

Seo Dae-ryong struggled less than he had on the way here. He had already experienced it once, and his internal energy had also increased.

Carried on my back, he worried about everything under the sun.

“Can I win? Who will my opponent be? What if we arrive and I’m disqualified because we’re late? Or what if, like in the dream, I die in the semifinals?”

“Stop worrying about useless things!”

“This is my own little trick. I keep imagining even the worst-case scenarios until they lose their steam, and then in reality those things don’t happen.”

“Has it worked?”

“I don’t know. Those worst-case scenarios haven’t happened yet.”

“That’s why I’m saying they’re useless worries!”

When we barely made it back in time, a situation neither of us had expected was waiting for us.

“Huh?”

Seo Dae-ryong stared in surprise at the notice posted on the wall of the Martial Alliance. It was a notice conveying news related to this Little Dragon Tournament.

“I advanced to the finals?”

An incident had occurred in the quarterfinal bout of the group after Seo Dae-ryong’s.

During that bout, one side had suffered a critical injury, and it had been revealed that the opponent had acted intentionally. As a result, the opponent Seo Dae-ryong was supposed to face in the semifinals was disqualified, and Seo Dae-ryong had automatically advanced to the finals.

“I told you dreams mean the opposite, didn’t I? I said all those worries were useless, didn’t I?”

At my words, Seo Dae-ryong still could not take his eyes off the notice posted on the wall.

“Can I… really go to the finals like this?”

“You didn’t use a trick, and you didn’t cheat. Why wouldn’t you be able to? Since ancient times, they say neither the talented nor the hardworking can beat a lucky bastard.”

“I was never this lucky a person.”

“As you live, even luck can change.”

Seo Dae-ryong turned to look at me. His emotions were contained in his eyes. That luck is all thanks to you, Pavilion Master.

“When I decided to participate in the Little Dragon Tournament, I wanted to win no matter what and be of help to you, Pavilion Master. Now there’s only one step left.”

“That last step is the problem.”

An opponent against whom luck would not work had advanced to the finals. Allowing no upset whatsoever, Martial Alliance Leader’s granddaughter Jin Ha-ryeong had made it to the final match.

And so, this final match became a contest between Seo Dae-ryong, the embodiment of upsets, and Jin Ha-ryeong, the unchanging favorite to win.

“What do I do? I’m so nervous I don’t think I’ll even be able to draw my saber.”

“Even if you can’t draw your saber, you can read, can’t you?”

“Pardon? What do you mean?”

I took a lucky pouch from my robe. It was the strategy pouch that the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had given for Seo Dae-ryong.

“I mean the time has finally come to use this.”

Chapter 168: Not Ordinary, Thus Not Angered.
--------------------------------------------
“I forgot!”

Seo Daeryong shouted. He truly had completely forgotten the stratagem pouch his master had given him.

“How could I forget this?”

“That must be why the elder gave it to me instead of you, no?”

“Ah! I’m saved. I’m finally saved!”

“Do you really think so? Normally, you might have simply lost without incident, but because we open this pouch, you might overreach yourself and die.”

Seo Daeryong flinched.

“Are you using my tactic of killing the mood by imagining the worst-case scenario?”

“No. This time, I’m talking about a very real possibility. Are you still going to open it?”

“I have to. Win or lose, it’s the first stratagem Master has ever left behind for my sake. I have to see it.”

I opened the pouch. Inside was a piece of paper rolled into a tube.

I handed it to Seo Daeryong. He took a deep breath, then slowly unrolled it and read.

After confirming its contents, Seo Daeryong was startled. Before he could even finish reading it, he handed it back to me with trembling hands.

“L-Look at t-this!”

Written there was the mnemonic formula for a single blade art.

It was the formula for the first stance of the Myeolcheon Mado-sik (滅天魔刀式), the exclusive blade art of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Not the entire formula, but only that of the first stance.

[……Though it is the first of the nine stances of the Myeolcheon Mado-sik, it will be of great help in a battle between young talents. If there are parts you do not understand, learn as the Second Young Master teaches you, and the Second Young Master will also tell you how to conceal the fact that it is my martial art.]

Seo Daeryong looked half out of his wits.

“What’s wrong?”

“The Myeolcheon Mado-sik is a martial art passed down only to the formal successor.”

“Of course it would be.”

“Then why aren’t you surprised, Pavilion Lord?”

“Why should I be surprised?”

“This means he thinks of me not merely as his disciple, but as his successor.”

Seo Daeryong’s voice trembled. He had never trembled so much since I had met him.

“Isn’t that only natural?”

“Natural? Master thinks of me as the next Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and you call that natural?”

“Among the elder’s disciples, who else besides you is fit to become a Demon Venerable?”

Seo Daeryong was so moved he did not know what to do with himself.

In truth, I, too, only learned this time that the Blood Heaven Blade Demon considered Seo Daeryong his successor. I had expected it, but the decision was faster than I had thought. It meant he liked Seo Daeryong that much.

“But he’s transmitting such an important martial art by writing it on a scrap of paper like this?”

“That’s why he gave it to me. I won’t lose it.”

Though only in part, it meant he had revealed the mnemonic formula of his exclusive martial art to me as well. It was also a moment that showed plainly just how much the Blood Heaven Blade Demon trusted me.

“Ahhh!”

Overwhelmed with emotion, Seo Daeryong paced back and forth, unable to get a hold of himself.

Just then, I showed him the very end of the paper.

“Did you read this part too?”

“What part?”

At the end of the formula, one separate line had been written.

[If you lose, you die by my hand.]

“Gah!”

Seo Daeryong jumped in fright.

I, on the other hand, burst into laughter. Yes, with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s personality, there was no way he would not leave words like that.

“Ah! Maybe because he pressed down hard while writing, these letters look especially dark. Surely, just because I lose… he won’t really kill me, right?”

There was no way I would miss such a fine opportunity to tease him.

“Will he really not? You fight a young talent using his martial art, and then lose? Do you think that prickly pride of his will let it pass?”

“Ah, he really might kill me.”

Seo Daeryong clutched his head.

“Even if he spares you on my account, you’ll be in hellish training for a while.”

“It’ll be hellish training for the rest of my life. Pavilion Lord! I absolutely have to win! Please make me win!”

“Don’t get excited. If you make a mistake and injure or kill Miss Jin, the Murim Alliance Leader will kill you. In that case, you really will die.”

Since he already knew my identity and Seo Daeryong’s, we absolutely could not injure Miss Jin. The reason he had let us go as far as the final duel was because we shared the common goal of eliminating the Cheonmyeong Society.

“Pavilion Lord! Please make me win without injuring Miss Jin! Make me win using the first stance of the Myeolcheon Mado-sik, and make it look to everyone as if it isn’t Master’s martial art.”

“You do know how impossible what you’re asking is, right?”

“If it weren’t you, Pavilion Lord, I wouldn’t make such a request.”

There were five days left until the final match.

Fortunately, the first stance did not seem that difficult, and for the clever Seo Daeryong, it was worth attempting. In any case, the goal was not to achieve great mastery, but to use the profundity of the technique to defeat Jin Haryeong. One move would be enough.

“It’s a request from my right-hand man. I suppose I have to grant it. Let’s go!”

We headed straight up to the training ground in the mountains. We paid no mind to Jin Haryeong, the Alliance Leader, or the Cheonmyeong Society.

Rather, I thought this was the path that would keep the Cheonmyeong Society from suspecting us. Who would imagine that people who disappeared to train before the finals were plotting some conspiracy? They would think we were simply honest martial artists who had come up from the countryside.

We focused purely on the martial arts tournament, and I considered that the best way to approach the Cheonmyeong Society.

Several days passed that way, and the day before the final match arrived.

On the eve of the decisive battle, the Murim Alliance was truly in a festive mood. The surroundings were crowded with countless martial artists who had come from every part of the Central Plains. There were many merchants who had come to sell goods to the martial artists, and there were also those who demonstrated martial arts in the streets to make their sects’ techniques known.

Swindlers pounced, trying to deceive newcomers to the murim, and there were many thieves aiming for money pouches.

The Murim Alliance dispatched warriors from several elite organizations, including the Azure Dragon Corps, to keep any unsavory incidents from occurring.

Amid that great bustle, Jin Haryeong entered an inn with her bamboo hat pulled low. When she asked the innkeeper about Geom Yeon, she was told that he and his master had not come in for several days.

‘Where on earth did he go?’

She had wanted to see Geom Yeon before fighting the final match. She wanted to joke with him like this: Should I go easy on your young master, or not? She had really wanted to make that joke with him.

She was also curious what would happen to her heart if Geom Yeon begged her to go easy. At the moment, she had no intention whatsoever of doing so, but would she perhaps waver even a little?

With regret, she left the inn. Since it had come to this, she had no choice but to change the joke like this. After winning, she would say this to him.

If you had come in advance and asked me to go easy, I would have. Friend, where on earth did you go?

* * *

At last, the day of the final match dawned.

A countless crowd gathered, and befitting the finals, all the important figures were present.

First, the Murim Alliance Leader, who had hosted this tournament, was seated, along with the heads of the Murim Alliance’s organizations and renowned murim elders from all over the Central Plains.

The talk of this tournament was unquestionably Seo Daeryong. Everyone’s attention was focused on whether he, hailing from a small sect, could write a new history.

In particular, because his opponent was Jin Haryeong, the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader, the moment Seo Daeryong defeated her, he would truly become a hero.

Of course, far more people predicted Jin Haryeong’s victory than his.

At the referee’s introduction, Seo Daeryong stepped onto the dueling platform first.

Even his gaze was different from during his other duels.

For the past five days, he had burned himself white. Never in his life had he worked so hard at anything, and he likely never would again. Even if he lost, he would have no regrets. Ah, no. He could not lose. Just thinking about lifelong hell training was horrifying.

Cheers calling his name erupted from all sides. Seo Daeryong closed his eyes for a moment and savored it. It was a gift life had given him. Once this matter was over and he returned, he might have to live the rest of his life in the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult as an investigator of the Hwangcheongak. The final duel of the Soryongjeon—this was a moment that would never come again in his life.

Seo Daeryong looked back at Geom Mugeuk, who stood below the dueling platform.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been looking up at the stage with his arms crossed, met his gaze and nodded. Seo Daeryong nodded once as well. That was enough communion between the two before the match.

This time, the referee introduced Jin Haryeong. A cheer even louder than the one that had flown toward Seo Daeryong burst forth.

Standing on the dueling platform, her gaze passed beyond Seo Daeryong and turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

He was watching Seo Daeryong and her with indifferent eyes. She did not know why she felt this way, but in this moment, Geom Mugeuk seemed like a different person. Not an attendant, not a friend—someone else entirely.

“Now, the final duel will begin!”

At the referee’s shout, the duel began amid cheers that shook heaven and earth.

The first to move was Seo Daeryong. His blade swiftly cut through the air.

He had only one chance to win. He had practiced and practiced solely for that one moment. Until he created that opportunity, he absolutely could not be struck down first.

Seo Daeryong pressed forward fiercely, but Jin Haryeong blocked his attacks with ease. In martial skill, Jin Haryeong was clearly a level above him.

The one fortunate thing was that, thanks to his increased inner force this time, he was not pushed back in terms of strength.

A close fight continued.

But the duel gradually began to tilt toward Jin Haryeong.

Seo Daeryong’s attacks grew slower and slower, while Jin Haryeong’s sword grew faster and faster. Seo Daeryong had to exert all his strength just to block her attacks.

That he could endure even this much was thanks to Seo Daeryong’s effort and talent. But nothing could overcome this clear gap in skill.

Seeing him gradually being pushed back, the spectators expected the duel to end soon. The Murim Alliance Leader, too, watched his granddaughter’s duel with a relaxed heart.

Just as Seo Daeryong was driven to the very edge of the dueling platform—

That moment arrived.

The distance between the two of them, the position of her feet, the position of her sword, his own feet and body, the sword and the blade—at the exact moment they all arrived at the positions he had been waiting for.

Seo Daeryong did not miss that one chance.

Shwaaaak!

Seo Daeryong’s blade created a variation, and the modified first stance of the Myeolcheon Mado-sik was unleashed.

It was the first variation he had used since the duel began, and it was a move that properly struck at her blind spot.

Startled, she swung her sword to block, but even that had been within the scope of their practice.

Seo Daeryong’s blade twisted at a bizarre angle, striking her sword again and again as it flew in to cut the fingers gripping the hilt.

At the very moment the watching Murim Alliance Leader sprang to his feet—

Taaang!

The sword, torn from Jin Haryeong’s grasp, flew into the air, drew a curve as it fell, and landed on the floor with a ringing clang. If she had held on, her fingers would have been cut off, so she had no choice but to give up the sword.

Before anyone knew it, Seo Daeryong’s blade was aimed at Jin Haryeong’s neck.

Jin Haryeong could not believe this situation. The final technique that had sent her sword flying was so exquisite that there had been no way for her to deal with it.

Words she truly did not want to say flowed from her mouth.

“……I lost.”

At that moment, the referee declared Seo Daeryong’s victory.

“The winner of the Soryongjeon is Seo Ryong of the Seo Blade Sect of Gansu!”

At the same time, cheers erupted from every direction. Like a child, Seo Daeryong leaped down from the dueling platform and threw his arms around Geom Mugeuk.

“Thank you. Truly, thank you.”

Tears flowed from Seo Daeryong’s eyes. It was the first time seeing his tears. He seemed like the sort of man who would not cry easily, but he could not hold them back.

Although he could not have won if not for Geom Mugeuk, Seo Daeryong had truly put in great effort. Put another way, it was because he was Seo Daeryong that Geom Mugeuk’s teaching had worked.

Geom Mugeuk hugged him tightly and whispered softly.

“You worked hard.”

From an investigator of the Hwangcheongak, he had become the disciple of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, and now he had won the tournament to select the young talents of the Murim Alliance by defeating the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader. His joy was not merely because of his victory. This feeling, as though he had now truly stepped onto the path of a martial artist, was unbearably wonderful.

“Stop crying and go back up onto the dueling platform.”

“Yes.”

Wiping away his tears, Seo Daeryong climbed onto the platform.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk felt a gaze. Far away, the Murim Alliance Leader was looking at him. It was a distance from which no ordinary master could possibly send a voice transmission, but Geom Mugeuk’s transmission reached the Alliance Leader clearly.

—I apologize. The friend who came with me was so earnest that I helped him train.

—What was that final technique?

—It was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s martial art.

At my honest words, the Murim Alliance Leader was startled.

—It was the Blade Demon’s martial art?

—Yes. This friend is the Blade Demon’s chief disciple.

I told him deliberately. When Seo Daeryong was not an ordinary demonic martial artist, the Murim Alliance Leader’s anger would instead subside. And indeed, the Murim Alliance Leader no longer took issue with this result.

—At times, defeat may be more helpful than victory.

It seemed he thought that for his granddaughter, who had never known defeat, this loss might be of even greater help.

—I can only be moved by the Alliance Leader’s great magnanimity.

Geom Mugeuk’s gaze turned back to the dueling platform.

And then another moment arrived.

Beyond Seo Daeryong, who was beside himself with joy, a person caught his eye on the far side. It was the woman who had come to congratulate him every time Seo Daeryong won.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk saw it. Her eyes and expression were so indifferent as to be cold. Since he had only ever seen her face smiling shyly, that expression felt terribly unfamiliar.

She practiced relaxing her face. Smiling, frowning, then smiling again. Then she looked up at the dueling platform, waved her hand, and called Seo Daeryong.

The moment her eyes met Seo Daeryong’s, she smiled brightly. She was an entirely different person from the one Geom Mugeuk had seen just moments before. The reason he could clearly discern this stark change in expression even from afar was thanks to the Divine Eye Art.

‘It’s the Cheonmyeong Society!’

They had been setting their work in motion from long ago.

Perhaps they had approached not only Seo Daeryong, but also the other martial artists who had advanced to the main tournament in similar ways.

For those who liked women, with women. For those who needed friends, as friends. For those who needed money, with money. As expected, the Cheonmyeong Society was no ordinary organization.

In any case, he had finally succeeded in catching the Cheonmyeong Society by the tail.

But his joy was brief. Geom Mugeuk looked at Seo Daeryong with pity in his heart. He was smiling broadly as he waved to the woman from the Cheonmyeong Society.

‘Ah, Daeryong. What am I going to do with you?’

He intended not to tell Seo Daeryong until the woman revealed her true colors. It would be better for his words and actions to remain natural because he knew nothing.

Sorry, Investigator Seo. The more sincerely you like her, the more surely we’ll be able to catch them. It can’t be helped. Let’s mature through the pain!

Chapter 169: There Is a Woman More Beautiful Than You
-----------------------------------------------------
The awards ceremony was as splendid as the martial arts tournament itself.

The winner was given a gold plaque and prize money. What made it an even greater honor was that the Murim Alliance Leader personally presented the award.

The gathered heroes chanted Seo Daeryong’s name. In all his life, this was the first time Seo Daeryong had ever heard his name called so many times. It likely would never happen again.

As he handed Seo Daeryong the gold plaque, the Murim Alliance Leader said,

“Your skills were outstanding. I hope you will devote yourself to the peace of murim in the future.”

“Thank you.”

But what the Alliance Leader truly wanted to say was sent by sound transmission.

—If you injure a martial artist of the orthodox faction with that saber art, you will certainly die by my hand.

Seo Daeryong did not dare argue and meekly took only the gold plaque and prize money before coming down. After all, he was grateful enough that they had given him the award despite knowing he was a demonic practitioner.

Jin Haryeong, the runner-up, was likewise given a plaque and prize money.

“Well done.”

“I’m sorry.”

“Do you feel it now? Just how vast this gangho is?”

“Yes. I thought that even if I couldn’t be a dragon, I could at least be a snake, but I realized I was only a frog in a well. I’ll devote myself even more from now on, Grandfather.”

That was enough. The Murim Alliance Leader patted her shoulder with a satisfied smile.

As Jin Haryeong received her award and descended from the stage, she glanced at Geom Mugeuk, who was standing far away.

Geom Mugeuk looked at her and waved. The feeling that came through was, You did well, friend.

The moment she saw that, tears suddenly welled up in Jin Haryeong’s eyes. She had not thought much of it until now, but seeing Geom Mugeuk made a needless sense of sorrow surge within her.

Since she could not shed tears in front of the gathered heroes, she strode forward bravely. They say Jin Haryeong cried at the awards ceremony. They say she cried out of frustration. The thought of such rumors spreading was truly dreadful.

Someone beside her said something to her, but without even looking, she quickly walked away from the place.

The person who had spoken to her was none other than Jo Sin. Looking utterly embarrassed, he could not follow after her. In his arms was a gift commemorating her victory.

He could not give it to her, since he had engraved the words Congratulations on your victory onto a dagger. He truly had never imagined she would lose. He was supposed to give this to her in grand fashion after she won.

“Damn it! Curse it all!”

His gaze turned toward Seo Daeryong. Seo Daeryong was still overwhelmed by all the congratulations.

‘That country bumpkin actually won? Does that make any sense?’

The master bastard had won, and the attendant bastard was seducing the woman he was to marry. They were truly a pair of bastards who deserved to be beaten to death. There was no way this matter could end well.

In that state, brimming with anger, Jo Sin returned to his quarters.

When Jo Sin entered his room, he flinched in surprise. A man was waiting for him inside.

He was Cheol Gon (鐵坤), the man who delivered the orders of the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society.

At first, Jo Sin had once thought that this man, Cheol Gon, might be the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society disguised as an errand runner. That was how extraordinary the aura Cheol Gon exuded was.

‘A man like this is just a subordinate? Then just how incredible is the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society?’

He had yet to see the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society in person, but it had been promised that he would meet him if he safely concluded this matter.

In any case, Jo Sin was displeased that the man had entered his room without permission, but he could not show his dissatisfaction.

“You’ve come?”

“Wasn’t it Young Master Jo who called for me?”

“I called you?”

“Did you not say you were confident about the marriage with Jin Haryeong?”

“Of course.”

Just a year ago, Jo Sin had been falling behind in the succession struggle of Jinryong Manor.

That was when these people, the Cheonmyeong Society, appeared before him. He could not refuse their offer to make him the heir if he joined hands with them.

It was good that he had become the heir with the Cheonmyeong Society’s help.

Once he was confirmed as the successor, they made one demand. Marry the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader. It was not an impossible demand. Jinryong Manor was a family with more than enough standing to become in-laws with the Murim Alliance Leader.

Jo Sin persuaded his father and even provided an enormous sum of money to the Murim Alliance in an attempt to bring this marriage about.

The reason he chose the timing of Soryongjeon was that he would be able to see her often. With his family background and his own appearance, praised as handsome wherever he went, he was confident he could win Jin Haryeong’s heart without much difficulty. Until he learned of the existence of that damned attendant, that was.

“From the rumors I hear, you’re standing in line behind an attendant.”

“Where did you hear such nonsense? That man is my subordinate.”

When Jo Sin spoke confidently, Cheol Gon stared intently at him. The moment their eyes met, Jo Sin felt cowed, just as he had when they first met. If this killing intent that made him shrink back had been deliberate, Jo Sin would never have lost his nerve.

But Jo Sin could feel it. This killing intent came from the man’s innate nature. It was a primal killing intent that could only be exuded by someone who attached no meaning whatsoever to killing people. A killing intent that made him feel that if he challenged him recklessly, he would be hacked to pieces and tossed into some filthy gutter.

“That man is your subordinate?”

“I recruited him for the sake of my marriage with Jin Haryeong. I’m going to use him to make the marriage succeed.”

It was not a lie. He had promised the attendant bastard a hundred thousand nyang and made him one of his own. Of course, that had not been his plan from the beginning.

Cheol Gon stared at Jo Sin once again.

If that gaze had belonged to some clever man trying to read him, it would have been easier to endure.

But the gaze now was closer to indifference. The blunt gaze of a murderous fiend to whom truth did not matter, for whom only the result needed to be good.

As one could tell just by looking at Cheol Gon before him, these people were not ordinary. They were not the sort of mediocre force behind the common rumors that said some incident had occurred in some region and so-and-so had been behind it.

They were special. If this side kept its promise, they would certainly grant the promised reward in return. They were villains who moved only for their own interests, but precisely because of that, they were the sort of people from whom one could reliably obtain what one wanted. That was why he had joined hands with them.

Cheol Gon rose from his seat and walked to the window.

“Come here and look at that.”

Jo Sin walked over carefully and stood beside him.

“That broken sapling over there is one I stepped on and snapped on my way in. Whoever planted them planted them too close together. Like that, neither tree can grow into a great tree.”

Cheol Gon slowly looked at Jo Sin.

“We hope you will grow into a great tree.”

Jo Sin knew the words contained no sincerity.

Cheol Gon did not care in the slightest. Whether he became a great tree or not. If the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society told him to kill Jo Sin, he would kill him; if told to leave him alive, he would leave him alive. To him, Jo Sin was not even worth as much as that broken sapling.

If he could, he wanted to shout that he should go say that great tree nonsense to his own children. Jo Sin absolutely detested receiving this kind of interference and pressure from anyone. Showing courtesy to villains would only make them look down on you more.

But Jo Sin did not dare.

“I will not disappoint you.”

“I’ll prepare the wedding gift money and wait.”

Cheol Gon quietly left the room.

Once he disappeared, Jo Sin let out the breath he had been holding. It was a sigh in which tension, fear, irritation, and anger were all mixed together.

The marriage was one thing, the attendant another, and the Cheonmyeong Society yet another. None of the problems were as easy as he wished. It felt as though the life he had lived so comfortably until now had entered a new level of difficulty. Of course, that did not mean Jo Sin would give up.

‘I’ll pay them back properly someday.’

He did not yet speak those words aloud. That was something for after he had raised his family into the strongest power in murim.

* * *

“The prize money is a full ten thousand nyang!”

Seo Daeryong was beside himself with joy at the unexpected windfall.

Seo Daeryong handed me five thousand nyang.

“Here, since we won this together, let’s split it in half.”

“Planning to regret it later?”

“Five thousand nyang is more than enough for me. With this, I’ll treat Commander Jang and Martial Artist Lee. And also…”

“Also?”

“I’ll buy something delicious for the woman who cheered for me.”

“Did you arrange to meet?”

“No, I don’t even know her name yet.”

He would know soon enough. She would certainly approach Seo Daeryong in some way. I wondered what method she would use.

“She’s someone who cheered for me long before I won. In that sense, she’s special.”

Seo Daeryong was already completely smitten with that woman.

“Oh, right. You’ll come to the banquet with me, won’t you?”

The Murim Alliance had invited the winner and all those who advanced to the main tournament to hold a celebratory banquet.

“I should.”

“I’ll go buy some clothes to wear to the banquet for a bit. Come with me, won’t you? I’ll buy you a set too.”

“I’m fine. You go buy yourself something nice.”

“I will. I really am going to buy an expensive set of clothes this time!”

Once the excited Seo Daeryong left, I spoke into the air.

“I’m sorry I couldn’t tell you in advance. The situation was too urgent at the time.”

I was speaking to the martial artist sent by the Alliance Leader, the one hidden around me. When I took Seo Daeryong to Chungui, I had dashed off using Quick Step, so he had not been able to follow me.

After returning to the Murim Alliance, I sensed that he had hidden himself near me again, but because I was training with Seo Daeryong, I had no leisure to speak to him separately. From his perspective, he must have felt displeased in various ways.

But as always, the hidden martial artist showed no reaction at all. He was truly a man faithful to his orders.

Being sorry was one thing. I did not tell him that the woman who approached Seo Daeryong was someone from the Cheonmyeong Society. I judged that there was no need to tell him in advance.

* * *

I did not enter the banquet hall.

The main character of today had to be Seo Daeryong, after all. As if I had truly become an attendant, I stood outside the banquet hall, observing the situation within.

But somehow, Jin Haryeong found out and came out to where I was.

“Why aren’t you going in?”

“It just feels stuffy.”

“Shall we walk for a bit?”

“Sounds good.”

I strolled through the inner garden with her.

“To tell you the truth, before the final match, I went looking for you. Do you know what I was going to say? If you asked me, I could lose on purpose. I was going to tease you like that.”

It seemed she was still embarrassed and bothered by having lost the duel. She was talking more than usual, and faster too.

“Without even knowing I’d lose like this. Isn’t it ridiculous? When I think of that, I don’t know if I’ll even be able to sleep from embarrassment. By the way, where did you go? Oh, right, which organization will your young master be entering this time? And you…”

I cut off the words pouring out of her.

“Friend, stop.”

Only then did Jin Haryeong exhale deeply. After calming herself for a moment, she revealed what was in her heart.

“Yesterday, after I lost and saw you, I felt like I was going to cry.”

“It’s all right to lose once in a while.”

In truth, it was all right to lose many times. As long as one had the will to keep moving forward. When seen in old age, failures like this were truly nothing, so why had they felt then as though the heavens had collapsed?

Perhaps that was why one missed one’s youth even more. Those days, that time when even the smallest thing made one’s entire body and mind respond with a lively throb.

“I don’t want to have such a weak mindset already.”

“First, put down the burden of being the Alliance Leader’s granddaughter. The Alliance Leader is the Alliance Leader, and you are you. Because of that burden, you can’t even feel happy. You placed second in this huge tournament.”

Jin Haryeong smiled brightly.

“What? You’re already feeling better?”

“Yes. Seeing you trying so hard to comfort me made me feel better.”

“That was easy. Making you feel good.”

“Do it often from now on.”

She firmly believed that Seo Daeryong would enter the Murim Alliance. Naturally, she assumed that I would live here with him as well.

“I might return to my hometown.”

At those words, she was startled.

“Return? Why?”

After staring at me for a moment, she asked carefully,

“Do you perhaps have a woman back home? A woman you like?”

“I do.”

For an instant, she flinched in surprise. Perhaps because I answered too easily, she looked half-doubtful.

“There’s a woman prettier than you.”

“Prettier than me? Then the credibility of your words suddenly drops sharply.”

“You’re the foremost beauty of Hubei, aren’t you? The one over there is the greatest beauty under heaven.”

She relaxed and laughed. It seemed she thought I was teasing her.

“Be sure to show me next time, that greatest beauty under heaven!”

“If the opportunity comes.”

Would the day ever come when Ian and Jin Haryeong met? That was something even I could not know.

By the way, what was Ian doing right now? She was probably immersed in martial arts training even at this very moment. Had she overcome her limits and advanced to the next stage? Ah! I miss you, Ian!

After strolling through the inner garden, I returned to the banquet hall with Jin Haryeong.

When we arrived at the banquet hall and looked inside through the window, Seo Daeryong was surrounded by the rising talents.

I did not know what they truly felt inside, but everyone was smiling and trying to build a connection with Seo Daeryong. From now on, Seo Daeryong would enter one of the Murim Alliance’s core elite organizations and rise swiftly through the ranks.

“Our young master looks happy.”

No one ignored him now, the short man with the gloomy impression. Clothes made the man, and he looked brighter than usual.

Yes, enjoy today to your heart’s content.

As Jin Haryeong watched Seo Daeryong, she said to me,

“I’ve talked so much with you, but I’ve never properly spoken with your young master, the one who actually defeated me.”

“Go and talk to him.”

“I don’t want to. I’ll stay here. Even if I go in, all I’ll receive is more consolation, won’t I? Your consolation is enough for today!”

Just then, someone new entered the banquet hall.

When I confirmed who it was, I was startled. Astonishingly, that person was the very woman who had cheered for Seo Daeryong.

Seo Daeryong was also startled when he saw her. Only those who had been invited were attending the banquet, yet she had appeared.

How on earth had she entered when she had not been invited?

Receiving everyone’s gaze, the woman slowly walked toward Seo Daeryong.

Chapter 170: The Body Knows First.
----------------------------------
“How did you come to be here, miss?”

Seo Daeryong’s voice trembled as he asked. He had never imagined he would see her here.

“I was invited too.”

“How can that be?”

Today’s banquet had only been for those connected to the Lesser Dragon Tournament.

Then, an astonishing fact spilled from the woman’s lips.

“I advanced to the Top Eight at the Lesser Dragon Tournament held three years ago.”

Seo Daeryong, as well as the junior martial artists nearby, were all greatly surprised.

Since the Lesser Dragon Tournament was held once every three years, she had been a Top Eight contestant at the previous tournament. It was customary to send invitations to past finalists who belonged to the Martial Alliance for the Lesser Dragon Tournament victory banquet.

“Let me formally introduce myself. I am So Ok of the White Dragon Corps, Third Generation, First Squad Leader.”

“Ah, so you were a martial artist under the Martial Alliance.”

And a squad leader of the White Dragon Corps at that. When she had shyly approached him to cheer him on and left, she had not been wearing a Martial Alliance uniform, so he had never dreamed she was a martial artist of the Martial Alliance.

“I didn’t know.”

“Of course you didn’t know. I never told you.”

“Why did you cheer for me?”

The two people’s gazes intertwined in the air. Even from beside him, one could feel Seo Daeryong’s excited emotions. A fluttering feeling, as if he had met his first love again.

In contrast, So Ok was calmer.

“Seeing Warrior Seo reminded me of my old self when I participated in the Lesser Dragon Tournament. I had also come up from the countryside knowing nothing. I didn’t know anyone, and no one knew me. I even ran out of money to stay at an inn, so later I camped in the mountains and came down to participate in the tournament.”

So Ok spoke of these things without a hint of shame. That demeanor appealed even more to Seo Daeryong. A fine fragrance emanated from her.

“I watched every single one of Young Hero Seo’s duels. Your dao technique was truly excellent. Especially the final match—it was so wonderful. My heart pounded so much I wanted to jump onto the dueling platform.”

Others might have taken it to mean she was so moved by the wonderful duel that she wanted to participate in the tournament again, but to Seo Daeryong, it was a statement that could be misunderstood as her wanting to jump up because she liked him.

“To think you defeated Miss Jin. Truly, it was the greatest duel in the history of the Lesser Dragon Tournament.”

Seo Daeryong felt as if he could fly. He had received much praise and acclaim after winning, but this moment, receiving it from her, was the best. A smile formed naturally at the corners of his mouth.

“You’ll be going to the Demon Extermination Squad now?”

The Demon Extermination Squad was the Martial Alliance’s most elite organization composed of young experts. Generation after generation, the Lesser Dragon Tournament winners had entered the Demon Extermination Squad.

“I could apply to the White Dragon Corps where you serve, Miss.”

At that, So Ok’s face turned slightly red.

“Thank you for saying so, but please don’t. Starting in the Demon Extermination Squad is the royal road to success in the Martial Alliance.”

“Thanks to your support, I was able to win. Therefore, I would like to treat you to a meal separately.”

It was a proposal made with truly great courage.

Having lived a life where he had been rejected by women more often than accepted, Seo Daeryong was inwardly anxious. Fortunately, contrary to such trembling, So Ok gladly accepted the proposal.

“It would be my honor. The Lesser Dragon Tournament winner is buying me a meal, after all.”

Seo Daeryong was truly overjoyed, as if he could fly.

Geom Mugeuk and Jin Haryeong were watching that scene from outside the window.

Geom Mugeuk could tell.

That she had been recruited by the Heavenly Mandate Society at the Lesser Dragon Tournament three years ago. In other words, it meant the Heavenly Mandate Society had been active in the martial world three years ago as well.

‘These fellows have taken deeper root in the Martial Alliance than I thought.’

It was a moment when, as the woman’s identity was revealed, he learned an unexpected fact.

He asked Jin Haryeong, who was beside him.

“Do you know that woman?”

“Squad Leader So? We’re acquainted enough to exchange greetings.”

“What kind of person is she?”

“She’s easygoing, has a good personality, and is popular with people. But it’s unexpected. I didn’t know she was cheering for your young master like that. Why? Are you worried about the young master? That he’ll be bewitched by a fox and hand over his prize money?”

“Of course I should worry. I’m his servant.”

Then Jin Haryeong’s true feelings suddenly burst out.

“Can’t you quit being a servant?”

After speaking, she thought, *Oops.* She had been told not to say things she hesitated over, and this was something she had even decided not to say. Thinking she had already come this far, she said what she had wanted to say.

“Can’t you just live as an ordinary martial artist? Since you’ve learned martial arts, you could take the alliance entrance exam, right? If necessary, I’ll help you.”

When Geom Mugeuk said nothing, Jin Haryeong spoke hurriedly.

“Don’t misunderstand! It’s not because I’m ashamed of you being a servant. I was just saying there’s a new life available to you too.”

Her face was slightly flushed. She was worried she might have hurt Geom Mugeuk’s pride.

After a while, Geom Mugeuk opened his mouth.

“It’s something I used to say often to someone. To try living a new life.”

“To whom?”

“To someone. A stubborn person who hated change.”

“Like you?”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and looked toward Seo Daeryong again. Though he was still monopolizing popularity and surrounded by people, Seo Daeryong’s gaze kept drifting to one spot.

* * *

The next day, Seo Daeryong went out to meet So Ok.

Having woken up before the crack of dawn, bathed, combed his hair, and changed clothes over and over again, he then said this:

“It’s not that I like her, but rather a meeting with someone who cheered for me. It’s a pure heart.”

“If you’re pure twice more, your mouth will tear. Please stop smiling.”

They were worried that Seo Daeryong liked her too much. When he learned the truth, the heartbreak would be great.

He had gone out with the vigor of someone marching to battle, but when he returned, he somehow seemed dejected.

“Why? Didn’t it go well?”

“No. I had a good time. She said she likes tea better than alcohol and enjoys taking walks. So we ate, drank tea, and took a walk.”

“Then why that expression?”

After a brief pause, Seo Daeryong sighed and spoke.

“We were sitting there in such a good mood, and suddenly I had this thought. This isn’t right? My life couldn’t possibly go this well. It was as if someone was whispering in my ear. When something this good happens, you should prepare for something bad?”

His instinct, which had been dormant until now, seemed to have awakened. Once fate begins to move, it shakes people awake like this.

“Did you tell her?”

“No. I parted with her with a smile, hiding my feelings as best as I could.”

“Inspector Seo. I told you, didn’t I? The path you chose is one of tying up an injured arm and stepping onto the dueling platform.”

“Blood flowing down from my whole body, limping on my leg, and even if one eye cannot see, it is a path I must walk—that is what you said.”

“Yes. It is also a path where you must suspect people even when meeting them. That is why making friends and truly loving someone are difficult on this path.”

Seo Daeryong, who had been silently lost in thought for a moment, uttered words he definitely did not want to say.

“Speaking in that sense, I must say something. This woman is suspicious no matter how I look at it. It feels like she approached me intentionally. Could she be someone sent by the Heavenly Mandate Society?”

I didn’t answer at all. Staring at me, he flinched in surprise.

“Is it true?”

“……”

“Is it really true?”

Seo Daeryong’s voice trembled. He must have felt something was suspicious, yet had hoped it wasn’t true.

“It’s true. She is a member of the Heavenly Mandate Society.”

“!”

Seo Daeryong opened his eyes wide. I had expected him to react as if the heavens were collapsing, but unexpectedly, Seo Daeryong was calm.

“As expected, my intuition was right. I knew it. How could someone like me... It was strange from the start. With handsome junior martial artists everywhere, the fact that she came to cheer for me specifically—I noticed it then. How pitifully must she have regarded me inwardly?”

“Rather than such self-torment, how about we be happy and celebrate that you correctly identified her true identity?”

“How did you know, Lord?”

“During the final duel.”

“Then why didn’t you tell me?”

“Because I was afraid you’d act awkwardly and give yourself away.”

“Then why are you telling me now? You could have just pretended not to know.”

“Because a problem more important than whether you act well or not has arisen.”

“What is it?”

“Your survival instinct seems to have awakened in earnest.”

At my words, Seo Daeryong jumped in surprise.

“Everyone possesses a survival instinct. But a martial artist’s survival instinct is different. The greater the expert, the more different it is. The body knows first. Even if the other party smiles and speaks kindly, the body refuses. The fine hairs all over the body stand on end, the heart pounds. Conversely, you may also feel your mood sink, like you did today.”

“So my survival instinct manifested.”

“Yes. Remember well how this survival instinct manifested and what kind of feeling it was.”

Seo Daeryong was moved. That I regarded his survival instinct as more important than catching the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society.

“Thank you, Lord.”

“From now on, you must act well.”

“You worried needlessly. I act better than you think. I’m a sly fellow.”

“We’ll see.”

“What do you intend to do from now on?”

“You must do it. You must guide her so that she leads you to the Heavenly Mandate Society. And as quickly as possible.”

There was no time. If she took her time to get close, it could take months or even over a year. But we were in a situation where we had to resolve this quickly and return.

Seo Daeryong, who had been pondering for a moment, recalled one method.

“Do you remember what she said at the banquet? That when she came to participate in the Lesser Dragon Tournament three years ago, she was in the same situation as me. I think those words weren’t a lie. I will dig into that aspect.”

“You’ll use a sense of solidarity? Since it’s an emotion you’ve experienced yourself?”

“Yes.”

“One more thing. You must definitely have me accompany you to the occasion where she introduces her backer.”

Seo Daeryong nodded.

“Please leave it to me.”

* * *

Two days later, Seo Daeryong and So Ok stood atop a hill overlooking the Martial Alliance at a glance.

“The scenery is good here.”

“It’s where I come when I feel stifled.”

“It’s a wonderful place.”

Seo Daeryong was inwardly astonished at the vastness of the Martial Alliance spreading out endlessly before him. He had thought there was no place larger than the Heavenly Demon Cult, but the scale of the Martial Alliance was no less.

“Actually, I was moved by your words that day, Squad Leader So. That when you participated in the tournament, you knew nothing and had no money, so you slept in the mountains. I am in a similar situation. I too will surely succeed here in the Martial Alliance.”

“It won’t be easy.”

“Pardon?”

“Though I hesitate to say this to someone who hasn’t officially joined the alliance yet, I’ll tell you for your sake, Warrior Seo. In the Martial Alliance, you cannot succeed without a backer who supports you. You might become a squad leader, but you cannot become a great lord. Even if you somehow go mad with effort and become a great lord, you can absolutely never become a faction lord. The limits are clear.”

So Ok’s acting was just as impressive as Seo Daeryong’s. If one didn’t know the truth, it would have sounded like advice given out of genuine concern.

“You’ll likely be even more disappointed, Warrior Seo, since you’re a winner. You might end your life as a squad leader after starting as one. Overcome it with skill? How? In peaceful times like these, it’s impossible. The myth of succeeding bare-handed is only possible in storybooks.”

Seo Daeryong asked with a serious expression.

“Then what must I do?”

Right now, Seo Daeryong was displaying his current emotions without the slightest sense of incongruity.

“Form connections. Throw away your pride and pledge loyalty to those who will push you forward. That’s what I did.”

“The connection that Great Lord So held—please let me hold it too. I have no such thing as the pride of a martial artist. Please help me.”

She said nothing for a moment. As she pondered, Seo Daeryong urged her.

“I beg of you.”

“Very well, it’s a connection that we met like this. I’ll speak to him.”

“Thank you.”

“Don’t expect too much. The elder may refuse.”

“Ah, and there is one more favor I wish to ask.”

“What is it?”

“Please introduce my servant as well.”

“Your servant?”

“If the Martial Alliance is such a place, I must build my own strength too, must I not? I intend to have my servant pass the alliance entrance exam and raise him as my right-hand man. In the Martial Alliance where there is no one, I must have at least one person I can trust. That fellow’s loyalty alone is trustworthy.”

Seo Daeryong pushed forward like a country bumpkin displaying stubbornness.

“I’m not sure if that’s possible.”

Seo Daeryong bowed his head with an earnest face.

“I beg of you.”

* * *

Contact came from So Ok again three days later.

She took Seo Daeryong and me to a manor located far from the Martial Alliance.

They were not numerous, but the level of the masked men guarding the surroundings was no ordinary matter. It meant the person she was trying to introduce was no ordinary person.

“Elder, it’s Ok.”

“Enter.”

We followed So Ok into the room.

In the center of the large room, a screen was lowered, making it impossible to see the opponent’s face.

I unleashed the Divine Eye Technique. Focusing power in my eyes, I peered past the screen to see who was there. And the moment I confirmed who that opponent was, I was shocked. He was someone I knew as well.

The True Dragon Branch Lord, Jo Ibaek.

He was Jo Sin’s father.

Either the one who recruited Jo Sin was this Jo Ibaek, or the Heavenly Mandate Society had recruited this father and son separately. If that was the case, there was also a possibility that neither knew the other had been recruited.

Both cases were astonishing.

Jo Ibaek spoke in a low voice from beyond the screen.

“You wish to succeed in the Martial Alliance?”

His demeanor was completely different from when he was beside the Martial Alliance Leader. The father Jo Ibaek who had tried to form a marriage alliance with the Martial Alliance Leader by any means, and the imposing pressure from behind the screen now were completely different. This was likely his true nature.

Seo Daeryong bowed his head respectfully and spoke.

“Yes! I wish to succeed no matter what.”

I could tell.

That after the Hwamugi swept through, the Divine Dragon Clan had become the pillar of the orthodox martial world. It was because they had been scheming like this since that time.

Then was Jo Ibaek the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society? Or was the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society someone else?

One thing was certain: the Heavenly Mandate Society, which was gradually revealing its body, was a much greater monster than I had thought.

“If I help you, you will surely be able to succeed. Then, you…….”

Jo Ibaek added in a low voice.

“What can you do for me?”

Chapter 171: Desire Is Like Clothing.
-------------------------------------
I left the situation entirely to Seo Daeryong.

Seo Daeryong looked toward the curtain and spoke in a voice that showed not the slightest sign of intimidation.

“I want to earn a great deal of money and gain honor as well. And I would like that path to be comfortable. I want to walk a path where I need not limp or bleed. If I can take that path, I will do anything.”
