A smile formed on my lips as I lay prostrate behind him with my head lowered. What Seo Daeryong had just said was the opposite of what he had said to me. Even in a situation like this, Seo Daeryong was quick-witted.

The reaction from behind the curtain was not bad.

“You are honest.”

Seo Daeryong had safely passed the first question. Now the gaze behind the curtain turned toward me.

“You say you wish to make that attendant your right hand?”

“He is quick on the uptake and quite outstanding in appearance, so I am certain he will be useful for something.”

Jo Ibaek asked me,

“If your master’s command and mine were to conflict, what would you do?”

“I follow only the young master’s will. That is a fact that would not change even if the Murim Alliance Leader himself came.”

“You are a man of remarkable loyalty.”

Though he said that, Jo Ibaek was sneering behind the curtain. I could tell he was someone who did not believe in people’s loyalty—no, someone who did not believe in people to begin with. In his eyes, even Seo Daeryong must have seemed laughable, let alone a mere attendant. His interest in me ended there.

Jo Ibaek asked Seo Daeryong again,

“If I asked you to kill someone, would you kill them?”

Seo Daeryong flinched and answered cautiously.

“That would depend on who it was. If they were someone who deserved to die, I could kill them.”

“What if I told you to kill someone whose identity you did not even know?”

Seo Daeryong hesitated before answering.

“I would kill them! Just leave it to me!”

A brief silence passed.

Soon after, Jo Ibaek laughed loudly from behind the curtain and said,

“It was a joke, a joke. My good man, we are not the Demonic Cult. Would we commit such evil deeds?”

Seo Daeryong tried hard to hide his confusion, his face caught between tears and laughter.

“I know you were joking as well. Would the winner of the Soryong Tournament commit such a dreadful act?”

“That is correct. I was joking too.”

Seo Daeryong answered awkwardly and wiped away cold sweat. He was acting well. To anyone watching, he was a country bumpkin who had come up from the countryside, crazed with success. Bringing even an attendant to a place like this and saying he would make him his right hand would make him look like a truly simple-minded rustic.

“Desire is like clothing. If you try to wear something that does not fit your body, the clothes will tear, or your appearance will become unsightly.”

It sounded like an admonition to harbor ambitions suited to one’s station. At that, Seo Daeryong raised his head and abruptly asked,

“Then what sort of clothes are you wearing, elder?”

It was a truly bold and insolent question asking what his ambitions were.

Well done, Daeryong. If you want to learn what kind of person the other party is, you have to keep shaking them.

“If you are not going to entrust me with such a nasty task, then what benefit do you gain by helping me, elder? I may be a country bumpkin, but there is one thing I do know. The world never offers kindness without reason.”

Jo Ibaek behind the curtain did not reveal any discomfort. Instead, a pleasant laugh flowed out, as if he rather liked this attitude of Seo Daeryong’s. He would feel that this sort of person was easier to use.

Though my head was lowered, I was emitting my qi and sensing Jo Ibaek’s emotions. The person facing him at the front was Seo Daeryong, but I was the one fighting the real battle.

“I am investing for the future. If one wishes for a tree to become a great tree, should one not begin from the sapling?”

“Ah! I failed to grasp your grand intention, elder. Forgive me.”

Daeryong, there is no need to be sorry. Most of the villains I have experienced do not make long investments in people weaker than themselves. Use and discard, use and discard. To them, subordinates are always nothing more than consumables.

If they invest in saplings, it is only so they can use those trees as windbreaks while they themselves become great trees.

The reason he is investing in Seo Daeryong is the same. He has something to make him do. Whether it is making him kill someone or having him steal secrets from the Murim Alliance. He will try to take back as much as he has given. No, he will try to take back more than he gave. That is the way villains conduct business.

What I had to confirm was precisely this.

Are you the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society? Or have you also been recruited?

Jo Ibaek said to Seo Daeryong,

“I like your frankness. In the future, grow into a great tree that will support the righteous murim.”

“Thank you.”

With that exchange, we left the room.

As we walked toward the main gate, Seo Daeryong said to So Ok in a dejected voice,

“I think I made a mistake in my answer.”

With a self-reproachful expression, Seo Daeryong asked her,

“Did you receive that question as well, Squad Leader So? The question about whether you could kill someone you did not know.”

“I did.”

“What did you answer?”

“I said I could not kill them.”

“Ah! I should have answered that way too. I suppose I was blinded by my desire to make a good impression.”

I casually joined the conversation and asked So Ok,

“Do they really not make you do such things?”

So Ok stopped walking and glared at me.

From the moment we met until now, she had not shown the slightest interest in me. If an ordinary woman saw me, she would inevitably show some interest as a member of the opposite sex, but So Ok had none whatsoever.

I knew what women like this were like. They were people who ran only toward their own goals. The fact that she had no interest in me at all, yet treated Seo Daeryong so well, was enough to show how great her ambition was. She was trying to wear clothes far too large for her.

“Of course they don’t. What are you saying right now?”

At a glance, her cold reaction seemed like anger at the presumption of a mere attendant daring to ask such a question, but I could feel it. That sharp response that had sprung out so suddenly was a different kind of guilt.

‘So you did it.’

She had undoubtedly killed someone under Jo Ibaek’s orders.

“Forgive me. I was suddenly curious.”

I quickly lowered my head and stepped back.

Seo Daeryong stepped in and scolded me.

“Do not speak out of turn!”

“Yes.”

And so we parted from her in front of the manor’s main gate.

“Thank you very much for introducing me to the elder. I will be sure to repay this favor.”

“Think nothing of it. We should band together, at least among ourselves.”

So Ok showed Seo Daeryong a bright smile. Then she glared coldly at me behind him and left.

Once she disappeared, Seo Daeryong said to me,

“I wish all the women in the world treated me and the Pavilion Lord the way she did.”

“That is far too cruel to me, isn’t it?”

“I have lived like that my entire life.”

Seo Daeryong lowered his head deeply and began walking.

“Is this acting too?”

“This is my reality.”

“Since the person you are has changed, your reality will change as well.”

Seo Daeryong stopped walking and looked back at me. I could not bring myself to say, while looking at that expectant face, “Even so, women will probably still like handsome men,” so I nodded vigorously.

With his face noticeably brighter, Seo Daeryong asked,

“Did you find out anything from this meeting?”

For now, discovering that Jo Sin’s father, Jo Ibaek, was behind this was already a major achievement.

Seo Daeryong had noticed something as well.

“Squad Leader So seems to be doing something bad under that man, doesn’t she?”

When I nodded, Seo Daeryong burst out in indignation. His anger was directed at both So Ok and Jo Ibaek.

“Damn it! Just how successful does she want to become, going so far as to do things like that? Using people who have no money and are desperate! Pavilion Lord, we cannot leave these people alone.”

Was Seo Daeryong the only one enraged? I was merely holding back in order to pull them out completely, roots and all. If not for that, the Lord of Jinryong Manor would not have left that manor alive.

“But in the process, So Ok will have to pay the price as well.”

“Of course she should.”

Seo Daeryong let out a regretful sigh. Perhaps Seo Daeryong felt some sense of kinship with her. He, who had thrown himself into our cult without money or background, and she, who had thrown herself into the Murim Alliance, must have had the same heart in the end.

“I will stop by to send a carrier pigeon to Tongcheongak, so you go back first.”

“Will you be calling the Demon Supremes?”

“I will only report how the situation is unfolding.”

I had been given permission to receive more of their help, but even so, this was a successor’s trial. It would be best to handle it with my own hands as much as possible. I missed the Demon Supremes, but I would endure it a little longer.

“I intend to resolve this matter with the Murim Alliance Leader.”

Forming a deep connection with him was something absolutely necessary for the future as well.

After sending Seo Daeryong ahead, I spoke into the air.

“You must have heard the voice of the person behind the curtain.”

The martial artist in hiding would not have been able to see beyond the curtain directly, but he certainly would have heard Jo Ibaek’s voice.

“There is no need to wonder whether it was him or not. It is the person you are thinking of. This is an emergency, so let me meet the Alliance Leader at once.”

* * *

A single carriage was parked in an alley.

When I climbed into the carriage, Murim Alliance Leader Jin Paecheon was inside.

“You have probably received a report from your subordinate. That we met with the person attempting to recruit the winner of the Soryong Tournament.”

“I heard.”

From that point on, I spoke through voice transmission.

―Since you must have heard the general story from your subordinate, I will get to the point. He was the Lord of Jinryong Manor.

―Jinryong Manor has been, for generations, a prestigious sect among those that represent the orthodox murim. There is no way they would have been recruited by the likes of the Cheonmyeong Society.

―I confirmed it myself.

―I heard there was a curtain?

―I was able to see through that curtain. It was certainly him.

The Murim Alliance Leader still looked at me with suspicion. Since it was a house with which he had even been discussing marriage to Jin Haryeong, it would be even harder to believe. It was a situation where it would be easier for him to suspect me than to suspect the Lord of Jinryong Manor.

―That is not all. His son, Jo Sin, may also have been recruited by the Cheonmyeong Society. And he does not know that his father is in league with the Cheonmyeong Society.

The Alliance Leader’s shock grew even greater.

―Are you saying the Cheonmyeong Society recruited them separately?

―That is what I believe. Or perhaps the father recruited his child without revealing his identity.

If that were true, then the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society was truly an extraordinary bastard. He would be someone who recruited father and son separately and used them separately.

It would be the same even if Jo Ibaek were the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society. It would mean he had hidden his identity and recruited even his own child.

―Because the opponent is this kind of madman, you must trust no one until this matter is completely resolved.

The Murim Alliance Leader’s expression hardened. Hearing those words from the Second Young Master of the Demonic Cult must have made him feel truly stifled.

―If it had been someone else, I would have dealt with it in secret. But he is someone with whom you were even discussing marriage, Alliance Leader, and that is why I am telling you this.

―Why are you showing me goodwill?

―I will not lie and say it is because I respect you, Alliance Leader. Nor is it for Miss Jin. It is for our cult. If trouble erupts in the Murim Alliance, it will ultimately affect our cult as well. I do not want my father to use a crisis in the Murim Alliance as an opportunity to unite murim under him.

When I mentioned my father, Jin Paecheon’s eyes grew intense. On top of that, the provocative words “unite murim” had come out as well.

―I am not someone with enough nerve to lie while daring to bring up my father. So please believe me.

I did not avoid the Alliance Leader’s gaze as it bore down on me like a tiger. Because he had experienced all manner of hardships and battles, I trusted that he had the discernment to see the truth contained in my words.

―Very well. Let us say, as you claim, that it is the Lord of Jinryong Manor. Why do you think he is doing this?

―He is proceeding with marriage talks with you, Alliance Leader, and recruiting warriors of the Murim Alliance. Then is there not only one reason?

Jin Paecheon knew the answer as well.

―My position?

―It may be groundwork to place Jo Sin in the position of Murim Alliance Leader in the future. It may also be, disrespectful though it is to say, in order to assassinate you, Alliance Leader. We will only know their true intentions accurately by hearing it from their own mouths, but whatever it is, it will not be something pleasant for you to hear.

Jin Paecheon was not a benevolent, gentlemanly type of Alliance Leader. He was more domineering than any Alliance Leader in history, and his temper was as fiery as well. He was merely being as cautious as possible because his granddaughter was involved. Once his seal was undone, he would be a man no one could handle.

―I understand. From now on, I will confirm it myself.

He looked as though he wanted to seize the Lord of Jinryong Manor immediately and uncover the truth.

―You must not do that. As of now, he has committed no visible crime. Supporting a warrior of the Murim Alliance is something that merchant groups deeply connected to murim and murim sects openly do. We must find direct evidence that he is connected to the Cheonmyeong Society.

I made him one proposal.

―Let us resolve this matter between the two of us, without telling anyone else. If you trust me, I have a method.

―Do not look down on this alliance. If you have a method, then we have methods as well.

―I am not saying this because I look down on the Murim Alliance.

―Then why?

―Do you remember? The first moment I risked my life and revealed my identity.

I reminded him that we had been able to dig into this incident because I had told him all these facts. Jin Paecheon could not deny that much. He was a man, and a true martial artist.

Even so, I understood the Alliance Leader. In such a situation, it was only natural that he would not feel inclined to leave this matter in my hands. So I made him an extraordinary proposal.

―I will resolve it within five days. If I exceed that time limit, then you may resolve it yourself, Alliance Leader.

At my words, Jin Paecheon was startled.

“Can you truly resolve it within five days?”

“In return, you must actively assist me, Alliance Leader.”

“What do you intend to do?”

“They tried to scheme by using your blood kin, Alliance Leader. Then we must return it to them in kind.”

Jin Paecheon smiled coldly.

“That much, I like.”

After pondering for a moment, Jin Paecheon made his decision.

The words that marked the beginning of a joint operation between the Murim Alliance Leader and the Second Young Master of the Demonic Cult, something that had never once occurred in the history of murim, flowed from Jin Paecheon’s mouth.

“Proceed!”

Chapter 172: No Time, Get on My Back.
-------------------------------------
“Where the hell have you been?”

Jo Sin greeted Geom Mugeuk with an angry face. He had waited a long while at the inn before finally seeing him.

“I went out for some air.”

Jo Sin could never have imagined it. That Geom Mugeuk was returning after meeting his own father, and even the Murim Alliance Leader.

Nor that Geom Mugeuk’s prey was none other than himself. He had come of his own accord and was sticking his head into the tiger’s jaws.

“You certainly have a lot of leisure for a mere attendant.”

He took a dagger from his bosom. It was the dagger he had meant to give Jin Haryeong but had failed to, the one engraved with words congratulating her on her victory.

“Should I stab your master to death with this? It seems like the perfect ending to this affair.”

“The best ending to this affair would be for you to marry Miss Jin, Young Master, and for me to receive one hundred thousand nyang.”

“Then you should earn your keep.”

“I can only earn my keep once I’ve been paid.”

“How much do you think it would be worth, in money, that I’m not stabbing your neck with this dagger right now?”

Jo Sin was growing anxious, worried that the marriage might not go through at this rate. Moreover, he had even blustered to Cheolgon, who had come in person to pressure him about the marriage, so he had to make it happen somehow. That was why a man who had never waited for anyone in his life had waited an entire si-jin for Geom Mugeuk at the inn.

Looking down at the dagger on the table, Geom Mugeuk said,

“What you need is one decisive move.”

“One decisive move? What kind of decisive move?”

“Living as the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader, how many scions of prestigious families do you think she’s seen? They all boast about their clans, their money, their martial arts, but in her eyes, how pathetic and dull must they be?”

Not long ago, Jo Sin himself had boasted about his money and family, so he had nothing to say.

“You need to show her something different from the scions of prestigious clans.”

“So what is that supposed to be?”

“There is one decisive move that works on every woman—pretty women, ugly women, kind women, wicked women, young women, older women, all of them. This is it.”

Geom Mugeuk picked up the dagger and moved as if to stab Jo Sin. Jo Sin swiftly seized his wrist.

“You’re going to kill me? Do you want to die?”

“This is it.”

“What?”

“You save her from danger. A man who saved her life. What more do you need?”

“!”

Jo Sin’s eyes widened. He had the thought that this was it. Why had he not thought of this before? He even felt a belated sense of self-reproach.

“It’s an old-fashioned method that’s been around for hundreds of years, but it’s also the best method because it always works. What in this world is stronger than a debt of life? But you’ll have to plan it well. Miss Jin is quick-witted, after all.”

Geom Mugeuk could already feel it. Jo Sin was already half won over.

“Ah, and one truly important thing! You must entrust it to someone reliable. If the slightest thing goes wrong and Miss Jin is injured or killed, Young Master Jo’s life will be blown away along with it.”

Geom Mugeuk expected it. If Jo Sin had anything to do with the Heavenly Mandate Society, he would ask them for help. He could not entrust something this important to just anyone.

“You absolutely must not use anyone from your own family. If it’s ever discovered later, it will be a disaster. Is there no expert among people completely unrelated to you? It has to be someone skilled enough to subdue Miss Jin. And someone trustworthy.”

The moment Jo Sin heard those words, one person came to mind.

Cheolgon.

If it was that frightening man, he would certainly be suited to this task. Of course, there was no telling whether he would accept, but there was hope. His mission was to make Jo Sin and Jin Haryeong marry as quickly as possible. He too was anxious enough to come in person and apply pressure.

Geom Mugeuk noticed that Jo Sin was thinking of someone right now.

‘As expected! There’s someone around him.’

Pretending not to know anything, Geom Mugeuk said,

“Don’t drag me into such a dangerous matter! I’ve done everything I needed to do.”

Of course, the words were filled with the intention of please, drag me in. Human nature was to want to do what one was told not to do, and right now Jo Sin even had a reason why he absolutely had to include Geom Mugeuk.

“You must help me until the end.”

“I absolutely hate dangerous work.”

Jo Sin imagined it. When this attendant and Jin Haryeong were in danger, he would appear and save them. He would show himself in contrast to this incompetent wretch. Yes, there was no need to think too complicatedly. This would be the end of it.

“And you must bring Miss Jin out. If I take her somewhere and then a villain appears, anyone would think I arranged it, wouldn’t they?”

“Very well. I’ll help. I need to get paid too, after all. If you’re going to do it, you should hurry. If this happens after Miss Jin formally joins the Murim Alliance, the Murim Alliance will begin an official investigation.”

Even without that reason, Jo Sin’s heart was in a hurry.

“Do not leave the inn and wait until I contact you.”

“Understood.”

Jo Sin hurried away. Geom Mugeuk quietly followed after him.

* * *

Jo Sin and Cheolgon met in a shabby warehouse near the Murim Alliance.

“I told you not to contact me unless it was an important matter related to life and death.”

“It is that important. You must help me just this once.”

“What is it?”

“If you help, I can marry Jin Haryeong.”

Just as Cheolgon made a face as if wondering what absurd nonsense this was,

“Please attempt to kidnap her. Then I will appear and save her.”

Cheolgon was so dumbfounded that he almost cursed on the spot.

“See here, Young Master Jo. Are you drunk?”

“I’ll be honest. I talked big to you, but right now Miss Jin has no intention of marrying me at all.”

Jo Sin revealed everything frankly. He had judged that, with this cold and frightening man, pleading outright would be more effective than some other scheme.

“If you pressure me to marry this woman under these circumstances, what ability do I have to make it happen? If it were another woman, I could make it happen even by threatening her, but the other party is the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader. Do you think I’d resort to this method if I weren’t desperate?”

“Do you think such a shallow trick will work?”

“That is why I need you. Someone with the skill to deceive Jin Haryeong. Someone who can subdue her decisively without injuring her.”

“So you mean, after I subdue Jin Haryeong, you will appear and save her. With your skill, if you drive me away, do you think she’ll believe it?”

Cheolgon considered Jo Sin a step below Jin Haryeong.

“Why do you think I’m weaker than Jin Haryeong?”

Jo Sin revealed his aura. It was a sharp force not befitting a rising talent of the younger generation, and it did not fall behind Jin Haryeong’s in the slightest. Jo Sin had a hidden card of his own.

“Isn’t your mission to have me marry Jin Haryeong? If we miss this chance, you will never be able to carry out the mission given to you. To be honest, it doesn’t matter to me. I’m only trying because you people are forcing me to marry her. What do I have to worry about?”

Cheolgon pondered. It was an absurd proposal, but if this method worked and the two of them married, then his mission would be complete.

“You said you wanted me to grow into a great tree, didn’t you? Then shouldn’t you occasionally give fertilizer to the sapling so it grows well?”

After thinking for a moment, Cheolgon nodded.

“Very well. Let us try it.”

When Cheolgon accepted, Jo Sin’s face brightened.

“Ah, and in the process of subduing her, make that attendant bastard a cripple. Cut off an arm, and a leg too.”

With an openly annoyed expression, Cheolgon said,

“If you hate him that much, why not just kill him?”

“I want to see how she acts when he becomes miserable.”

Jo Sin smiled coldly as he imagined the attendant bastard rendered disabled. Would Jin Haryeong pity him and keep him with her for the rest of her life? He was certain there was no chance of that.

“If she shows such a self-sacrificing side, I’ll acknowledge her.”

He simply wanted to see that Jin Haryeong was a shallow, insignificant woman.

To Jo Sin, Jin Haryeong was still a tainted woman who had shown interest in an attendant instead of him. Even if they married, he would never love her.

Cheolgon inwardly found such a Jo Sin pathetic. He did not know whether it was because Jo Sin was stupid or because he was still young, but if it were him, he would never think that way.

“Let me know once the time and place are decided.”

Cheolgon left first, and Jo Sin followed after him.

After they disappeared, two more people appeared in that place. They were none other than Geom Mugeuk and the Murim Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon. After saying he would enter closed-door seclusion for several days, Jin Paecheon had been moving secretly with Geom Mugeuk.

Jin Paecheon was dumbfounded after seeing Jo Sin’s true nature. Truly, he had barely restrained himself from rushing out at once and smashing him to pieces.

“Why are we not capturing that Heavenly Mandate Society bastard here?”

“He is not our target. Even if he is an expert, in the end he is nothing more than a consumable pawn. No matter how much we interrogate him, he will not lead us to the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society.”

Judging by what they had seen so far, the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society was not someone who could be caught by torturing one subordinate.

“Then what do you intend to do?”

“Our target is Jo Sin.”

“What are you going to do with Jo Sin?”

“I will take him to the gates of hell. When we push Jo Sin to the edge of the cliff, let us see how Jo Ibaek reacts. The person who will guide us to the leader of the Heavenly Mandate Society is Jo Ibaek.”

Jin Paecheon was inwardly admiring Geom Mugeuk’s intelligence and his bold drive. Truly, if he had not been the second young master of the Demonic Cult, he would have used any means necessary to make him his son-in-law.

‘What a waste.’

But let alone being his son-in-law, Geom Mugeuk was a potential enemy of the orthodox murim. And one who looked far too dangerous.

Hiding those inner thoughts, Jin Paecheon said bluntly,

“Four days remain now.”

* * *

The next day, Jin Haryeong put on her treasured martial attire and left her residence.

Geom Mugeuk had asked her to go enjoy Dongho with him.

Throughout the ride there, she was in a good mood. At times like this, she could confirm just how hypocritical a person she was.

The better her mood, the greater her regret.

‘If only he weren’t an attendant.’

Behind this feeling lay the thought that she could not marry an attendant, or would not marry one.

And she knew it. She would never be able to cross that line of status.

‘And then you’ll regret it later, won’t you? You pathetic wench, you snob! Just live alone. Live alone.’

Thinking this and that, the two arrived at Dongho.

After gazing silently at the lake for a long while, Geom Mugeuk spoke first.

“Someone once said that you have to be better at parting than at meeting.”

“Why are you suddenly saying that?”

“I’m leaving in a few days.”

“What?”

“Thank you for everything.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, she was startled.

“Where are you going?”

“To my hometown.”

She felt regretful. She liked being alone, so she had few friends, and even among them, none had given her this sense of closeness. The words don’t go rose all the way to her throat.

But it was not as if she would take responsibility for his life. Once she joined the Murim Alliance, she would be busy and would not be able to see him often anyway.

“Become the Murim Alliance Leader one day.”

“What?”

Just as she opened her eyes wide in surprise,

Someone ambushed her from behind. Distracted by Geom Mugeuk, she had failed to notice her opponent’s approach in advance.

The moment she turned around in belated alarm,

Tap, tap!

Cheolgon, wearing a mask, subdued her paralysis acupoints in an instant. There was already a gap in their skill, and since it was a surprise attack as well, she was utterly helpless.

Of course, Geom Mugeuk had sensed him approaching from far away. He had also prepared for the unlikely event of an emergency. After all, some other variable might occur.

Cheolgon’s gaze, after subduing her, turned toward Geom Mugeuk.

At that moment, Jin Haryeong shouted,

“Not him! Don’t kill him!”

Jin Haryeong thought the masked man would kill Geom Mugeuk. If she was his target, there was no way he would leave alive the attendant who had been with her.

“No! Please! Let him go!”

Despite her desperate cries, Cheolgon strode over and tried to cut off Geom Mugeuk’s arm with a single slash.

At that very moment!

Cheolgon’s sword stopped in midair. An enormous presence could be felt behind his back. Before he knew it, every hair on his body had risen at once, and his survival instinct was screaming at him to flee. At any other time, he would have launched himself away and escaped without looking back.

But his body would not move. He was floundering in the sea. Around him was the middle of a boundless ocean, with not a single ship or island to pull him out.

Cheolgon desperately drew up his internal energy and turned his body.

A colossal tidal wave of unknowable height was surging up before him. The Martial Alliance Leader, Jin Paecheon, was standing right behind him.

“……Damn it!”

He desperately tried to turn and flee again, but before he could take even a single step, his paralysis acupoints were subdued in an instant. No matter how much of an expert Cheolgon was, before Jin Paecheon he was nothing more than a man who could not withstand a single move.

Cheolgon could not understand this situation.

‘Why has the Martial Alliance Leader appeared here?’

Originally, after he cut off this attendant bastard’s hands and feet, Jo Sin was supposed to appear and save her. But why?

If things had gone awry, then Jo Sin should appear even now. Then, with a laugh, he should smooth over the situation by saying he had played this prank in an attempt to win Jin Haryeong’s favor. He should say, This man is my subordinate.

But Jo Sin did not appear.

Then a thought sent a chill down his spine.

‘A trap?’

Had he sold him out to the Martial Alliance? Otherwise, there was no way he would fail to appear, was there?

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk noticed Jo Sin hiding in the forest far off in the distance.

‘Don’t come out!’

The rift between Cheolgon and Jo Sin was exactly what Geom Mugeuk wanted.

Jo Sin was hiding in the forest, watching the situation unfold. The Alliance Leader’s appearance had thrown him into a panic.

‘It’s over.’

If he went out, what was he supposed to say to the Alliance Leader? That he had faked a kidnapping so he could rescue her? The moment he said that, the marriage would be gone forever. And on top of that, how was he supposed to explain who Cheolgon was?

But he could not simply stay like this, either. He could not just let Cheolgon be captured like that.

He tried to step out, but his feet would not move. He thought it was because he feared the Alliance Leader, but in truth, the energy Geom Mugeuk had released was secretly pressing him back. Panicked and frightened, he did not notice it at all.

‘Damn it!’

In the end, Jo Sin decided to withdraw for now and then find a way to settle the situation. When he stepped back and turned around, his body moved as he wished. Without looking back, he left the place.

Jin Paecheon released his granddaughter’s paralysis acupoints.

“Grandfather! How did you come here?”

“I’ll explain everything later.”

For now, it was important to proceed according to plan.

Jin Paecheon and Geom Mugeuk faced each other and nodded once.

Jin Paecheon tucked the immobilized Cheolgon under his arm, shot up into the air, and vanished from the place in an instant. It was truly a supreme lightness skill that drew admiration all on its own.

Jin Haryeong asked Geom Mugeuk,

“What is all this?”

She sensed that something she did not know about was happening.

“Let’s go back too. We don’t have time for this here. Can you handle dizziness well when using lightness skill?”

“What is that supposed to mean?”

“I’m asking if you can handle it.”

“I’ve never felt dizzy during lightness skill before.”

“If you get dizzy, keep your eyes shut tight. Don’t throw up on my back.”

Geom Mugeuk turned around and lowered himself.

“Get on. We don’t have time, hurry.”

Since the situation was clearly unusual, she first climbed onto Geom Mugeuk’s back. He’s going to run while carrying me? Why? I’m not even hurt. She was in a mood to see just what he intended to do.

“Don’t tell me you were worried I’d get dizzy while you ran with me on your baaaaaaaack!”

Chapter 173: It Doesn't Matter Who I Am.
----------------------------------------
Jin Haryeong couldn't come to her senses.

Such speed in lightness skill was unlike anything she had experienced since birth. The surroundings flashed by so quickly that she couldn't make out any of the scenery.

She couldn't open her eyes properly, and it was difficult just to breathe.

'Just how is he running without crashing into anything?'

It was astonishing and wondrous. At the same time, she felt a strange joy.

'This person was such an expert?'

If this was the level of martial arts capable of displaying such lightness skill, then it was a realm beyond imagination.

'He's no ordinary servant.'

She didn't know if this was something to be so happy about, but she was glad. It felt as though an uncrossable line drawn between him and her had disappeared.

When Geom Mugeuk, who had been running at a maddening speed, came to a brief stop,

"……Let me down for a moment."

Jin Haryeong got down from his back, staggered, and collapsed onto the ground. Only after taking several deep breaths did her mind finally settle.

"What the hell, you? Did you learn to walk using lightness skill?"

Geom Mugeuk was smiling as he looked somewhere. Her gaze naturally turned in that direction. In that moment, Jin Haryeong was startled.

She had thought he stopped to let her rest, but that wasn't it.

Far in the distance, she could see someone's back as they ran. To her astonishment, it was her grandfather, who had been running ahead.

'He caught up to Grandfather!'

She realized the reason he had stopped briefly was because he didn't want to overtake Grandfather.

'Faster than Grandfather?'

She was so surprised she nearly screamed. She had believed her grandfather was the strongest expert under heaven. There were those who said the Cheonma was stronger, but she believed Grandfather could defeat even the Cheonma.

Yet this man had caught up to Grandfather. Grandfather had run while carrying the one who ambushed her under his arm, while this man had run carrying her on his back. To catch up to Grandfather even under similar conditions?

She truly regretted having to ask such a clichéd question at a time like this, but nothing else came to mind.

"What is your true identity?"

Then Geom Mugeuk answered.

"Geomyeon. A person who disappears like smoke when the work is done."

"Fine, if you're going to disappear like smoke, at least tell me the truth before you go. Who are you, really? Since you've revealed your skills like this, weren't you planning to reveal your identity too?"

Geom Mugeuk finally revealed his aura. It was a presence of an entirely different dimension from anything he had shown until now. In that moment, a certain mental image rose in her mind.

It was a vast, clear sky. The endlessly unfolding heavens were so wide and blue.

Lost in that beauty, she reached out her hand.

Plop!

She soon realized it was the sky reflected in water.

She dipped her hand into the water, and her body slowly sank.

She had thought it was shallow, but it was deep. Looking down, she couldn't see the bottom. What lay in those depths? The moment she felt fear, the mental image vanished from her mind, and she returned to reality.

Then, a single utterance reached her ears.

"Geom Mugeuk. The second son of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult's leader."

Jin Haryeong blanked out for a moment.

'What? Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? Leader? Son?'

At first, she thought she had misheard something. But which part could she have misheard? The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult? The leader? The son?

"I misheard. Which sect did you say?"

"The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult."

"I misheard again. Where?"

"The place you people call the Demonic Cult. The Heavenly Demon Divine Cult."

Like someone waking from a dream, Jin Haryeong's eyes grew wider and wider. Just when one might wonder if this girl's eyes had always been so large, she drew her sword and stepped back.

Her sword pointed at Geom Mugeuk. Beyond the sharp blade, she spoke.

"I can accept any other joke, but you can't joke about the Demonic Cult!"

He could see how the main cult was viewed by the younger generation of the Murim Alliance. To them, the Demonic Cult was a rooted fear planted since childhood, a gathering of evil, and the very reason they trained in martial arts.

So when he said he was the son of the Cheonma, how great must her shock have been?

Her aimed sword trembled. Never in her life had she been so surprised and afraid. In contrast, Geom Mugeuk standing before her blade was utterly calm. He possessed an aura as clear and quiet as that sky, yet as deep as that sea.

Then a sudden doubt arose.

"Does Grandfather know your identity?"

"He knows."

She couldn't understand. If this man was the second son of the Demonic Cult, there was no way Grandfather would leave the two of them alone.

"At the very least, he believes I am not someone who would harm you. So put that sword down now."

She slowly sheathed her sword. She had been too surprised earlier, but knowing that Grandfather was aware calmed her down.

Geom Mugeuk explained the situation to her.

"A mysterious power called the Cheonmyeong Society has appeared in the Murim world. While chasing them, I uncovered signs that they were trying to recruit you at this Little Dragon Tournament. Afterward, I revealed my identity to the Alliance Lord, and we are currently pursuing the mastermind together. If it weren't for his granddaughter's matter, the Alliance Lord probably wouldn't have moved personally. And certainly not with the second son of the Demonic Cult."

She immediately understood the situation.

"The Alliance Lord wanted to keep my identity hidden until the end. But I told him not to."

"Why?"

"Because that would be disrespecting you. I believe knowing my identity won't change a thing. Whether I'm a servant or the second son of the Cheonma. Nothing will change."

"How could nothing change? Aside from both being men, those two are as different as heaven and earth!"

"Is that so?"

Geom Mugeuk laughed, and Jin Haryeong made an incredulous expression before eventually laughing along.

The line of status as a servant had disappeared, and a new line as successor to the Demonic Cult had appeared. The former was a line that could be forcibly crossed, but the latter was so high and perilous that mishandling it could spark a war between the orthodox and demonic.

"If you're upset, let it out."

"I can't let it out. How could I, after you shocked me like this? I'll never let it out for as long as I live."

But contrary to her words, she wasn't actually in such a bad mood. Rather, she felt a strange excitement at the fact that her counterpart was the second son of the Demonic Cult.

"Then what did you mean earlier at the teahouse when you told me to become the Alliance Lord?"

"Exactly what I said. Become stronger, accumulate more experience, and become the Alliance Lord. I thought you could do it well."

"So the Murim Alliance Lord's friend is the Heavenly Demon?"

"I wish that were the case, but my side is too treacherous. I don't know if I'll survive to see you again."

For some reason, it didn't sound like a joke, so Jin Haryeong wished he would survive. With that feeling in her heart, she extended her hand.

"Now that I know your real identity. Let's shake hands formally."

Geom Mugeuk took her hand.

Jin Haryeong gripped his hand tightly, then perhaps feeling awkward, shook it playfully.

"To think the day would come when I'd shake hands with the second son of the Demonic Cult."

"Same here. Now, let's go. We need to hurry too."

Geom Mugeuk turned his back, signaling for her to get on.

She got on again. The feeling was completely different from the first time.

"I'll have to brag to people later. I'll tell them all about how I rode on the Heavenly Demon's back when I was young!"

* * *

Cheolgon was locked in a secret room.

Since the Alliance Lord had personally subdued him, no one else could unlock his acupoints even if they tried.

Sitting alone in the room, he struggled to grasp the situation.

It hadn't happened by chance. The Alliance Lord wouldn't have appeared there by coincidence.

Nor had he been guarding his granddaughter. If that were the case, the Heavenly Dragon Guard would have handled it.

In other words, it meant he had known about this ambush. And the only one who could have known about it was Josin.

Then a person entered.

"You?"

Cheolgon was startled when he recognized who it was. The one who entered was Geom Mugeuk. He could tell just by the gaze. This was not the cowardly servant whose arm he had tried to cut off.

"Ah!"

Cheolgon sighed. He assumed Geom Mugeuk was a subordinate of the Alliance Lord. The Alliance Lord had moved personally to uproot the Cheonmyeong Society? Then this incomprehensible situation all fell into place.

Geom Mugeuk sat down facing him.

"I know you are affiliated with the Cheonmyeong Society."

Cheolgon said nothing.

"Tell me who the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society is, and I'll spare your life."

Still, Cheolgon said nothing. Geom Mugeuk sensed the Cheonmyeong Society in the way he sat in silence with faint murderous intent. They were exactly that kind of organization—silent with insidious killing intent.

"Yes, even if you wanted to talk, you probably don't know who the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society is."

Cheolgon's value had already ended with showing Josin that he had been captured by the Alliance Lord anyway.

Then Cheolgon opened his mouth for the first time.

"Release my suppressed acupoints and let's fight like men."

Cheolgon's eyes flickered with killing intent.

Swish! Thud!

Geom Mugeuk's sword, drawn in an instant, pierced his heart. Even if he had released the man's acupoints to fight, it would have been settled in a single second regardless, but Geom Mugeuk didn't even give him that chance.

Because Jin Pacheon, the Murim Alliance Lord, was waiting outside.

As he opened the door and stepped out, Geom Mugeuk spoke.

"You must not give these people any opportunity."

Jin Pacheon gazed at Geom Mugeuk in silence. He had no interest whatsoever in Cheolgon's death. What was shaking Jin Pacheon's heart now was this young successor of the Demonic Cult, unstoppable in his momentum.

"Now, arrest Josin."

"He may have gone into hiding out of fear."

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke with a smile.

"He will come to me of his own accord."

* * *

Just as Geom Mugeuk had predicted.

As Geom Mugeuk was about to enter an inn, Josin approached wearing a low-pulled bamboo hat and grabbed his arm, dragging him into an alley.

"What is going on?"

"That is what I should ask. That man tried to cut my arm off. What on earth is going on?"

"I was supposed to appear before he cut your arm off. But before that, the Alliance Lord appeared. Why did the Alliance Lord show up?"

"How would I know that?"

"You don't know?"

"Of course. How would a mere servant like me know what the Murim Alliance Lord is doing? Didn't the young master Josin leak secrets while handling matters?"

It was a hard argument to refute. Yes, exactly. It was strange to ask a servant about the Alliance Lord.

"So? What happened?"

"The man who tried to kill me was captured by the Alliance Lord, and I returned with the young lady Jin."

Josin, who had been silently staring at Geom Mugeuk, spoke coldly.

"You bastard, this is all because of you!"

Swish!

He stabbed at Geom Mugeuk's neck with the dagger he had planned to give Jin Haryeong as a gift.

But his wrist was already caught by Geom Mugeuk.

"That's right, it's because of me."

Tap, tap!

In an instant, Geom Mugeuk suppressed his two critical acupoints.

Josin's eyes grew so wide from shock that they couldn't get any bigger.

Soon after, Josin was dragged into the secret room where Cheolgon had died earlier. The corpse had been removed, but the smell of blood he had shed still lingered in the room.

Josin sat there with a fearful face.

He was confused. The fact that the servant was a stronger expert than himself was shocking. He was bewildered by what kind of situation this even was.

Then the door opened, and Geom Mugeuk entered.

Josin asked with a frightened face.

"Who are you?"

"It's not important who I am. What does it matter if I'm the servant you tried to cripple because of your petty pride, or the Cheonmyeong Society you secretly joined? What's important is the situation you are in right now."

Josin intuited it. That this man was at least not someone from the Cheonmyeong Society.

'He's on the Alliance Lord's side!'

Josin judged it to be so.

"The one who ambushed Jin Haryeong has confessed everything."

At those words, Josin's heart sank. That Cheolgon, who had been so full of terrifying killing intent, had confessed so easily? And not even a few hours since being captured?

"What exactly did he confess?"

"That you ordered him to kidnap Jin Haryeong."

Josin shouted in anger.

"This was your plan!"

"When did I?"

"What?"

Josin's face contorted.

"You know that attempting to harm the Alliance Lord or his family is treated as the gravest of crimes? If the crime is uncovered, it's execution without question."

At the word execution, Josin was consumed by terror. He could tell that the man before him had trapped him.

"What do you want from me?"

Geom Mugeuk, who had been watching him, rose from his seat.

"You look tired. Get some sleep."

Flick.

A gust of wind suppressed his sleep acupoint.

Josin collapsed and fell asleep just like that.

Outside the secret room, Jin Pacheon was waiting, having listened to the conversation inside.

"What's the next plan?"

"We must bait his father, Jo Ibaek. Please meet with him and tell him that Josin was recruited by an organization called the Cheonmyeong Society and was arrested on charges of attempting to kill the young lady Jin under their orders."

Geom Mugeuk was truly unstoppable.

"Then Jo Ibaek will make some kind of move. We will learn whether he is the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society, or merely someone recruited by the leader. If he was recruited by the Cheonmyeong Society and didn't know his son was also recruited, he may feel betrayed and hand the leader over to you. Of course, you would need to grant him some breathing room."

"What if he is the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society?"

"Then I believe he would abandon even his own child."

Hearing Geom Mugeuk's words, the Murim Alliance Lord thought once again.

'If this man becomes the Cheonma, the orthodox Murim will be in for a hard time.'

To possess such drive and insight at this age meant he would be absolutely uncontrollable when older. At least in this moment, Geom Mugeuk felt like a more troublesome adversary than the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society.

Whether he knew that inner thought or not, Geom Mugeuk expressed concern for the Murim Alliance Lord.

"I have a request to make in advance."

"What is it?"

"If we uncover the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society, please leave the finishing blow to me. Not because it's my successor test, but judging by his meticulousness so far, if we keep him alive, he'll scheme even from prison. He will definitely become a lingering problem later."

Handled fairly and openly, or by the book, the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society and his men might have to be imprisoned in the dungeon. This was advice not to let that happen.

As this was not something the Murim Alliance Lord could easily answer in his position, Jin Pacheon said nothing.

With a gaze saying he understood everything, Geom Mugeuk added in a low voice.

"I will sweep them all away and disappear quietly."

Chapter 174: It Won't Work on Us.
---------------------------------
When Jo Ibaek reached the summit of the highest mountain near the Murim Alliance, the Alliance Leader was standing at the edge of a cliff with his hands clasped behind his back.

“Alliance Leader.”

“Manor Lord Jo, you’ve come? Come here.”

Jo Ibaek stood beside the Murim Alliance Leader. As high as it was, the view was magnificent.

“The scenery is truly splendid.”

“That is why I come here often.”

“It is all thanks to you, Alliance Leader, that we can enjoy such beauty. Is it not because you so steadfastly support the gangho that the Demonic Cult rabble cannot stir?”

“I am grateful that you say so.”

When the Murim Alliance Leader had asked to meet him here, Jo Ibaek had privately expected that they would discuss the marriage. Moreover, since he had asked to meet at a mountaintop rather than within the Murim Alliance, Jo had thought they would speak frankly as parents, away from prying eyes.

“Manor Lord Jo.”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

“How long has it been since we came to know each other?”

“I believe it has been nearly thirty years.”

“They say time flows like water, and already so much has passed.”

For a moment, the two stood there, looking back on the years gone by.

“Is there some particular reason you called me here today?”

“I asked to see you because of your son.”

As expected. Thinking he had indeed been summoned over the marriage, Jo Ibaek’s expression brightened. He had promised to provide the Murim Alliance with an enormous sum of money. Even so, the Alliance Leader had shown only a lukewarm attitude, so Jo had wondered if matters were not going well.

‘Is it not too much money to refuse, Alliance Leader?’

Jin Paecheon slowly turned his head and looked at Jo Ibaek.

“Have you perhaps heard of the Cheonmyeong Society?”

Though it was a question that struck at his blind spot and made his heart drop, Jo Ibaek answered calmly.

“That is the first I’ve heard of it.”

He even asked, with composure, what sort of place it was.

“It claims to be a mysterious sect, and I understand it has been involved in many wicked affairs.”

“The murim is full of all manner of trash.”

“Manor Lord Jo.”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

“It seems your son is involved with that trash.”

Though Jin Paecheon spoke cautiously, his gaze was sharp enough to pierce through Jo Ibaek.

“What do you mean by that?”

“I mean that Young Master Jo was recruited by the Cheonmyeong Society.”

Jo Ibaek was startled. Whether he was truly shocked or merely pretending, it was so natural that there was no way to tell.

“Your son, Jo Sin, was recruited by an organization called the Cheonmyeong Society and attempted to abduct my granddaughter.”

As his shock grew, Jo Ibaek’s voice rose as well.

“That is absurd. The marriage talks are underway, so why would the fool do such a thing unless he had gone mad? There must be some misunderstanding.”

“I hope so as well, but at present, there is clear evidence.”

“What evidence?”

“There was a confession from someone who received orders from Young Master Jo.”

“It may be a confession meant to frame my son.”

Without a word, Jin Paecheon looked out at the sheer stone mountain rising in the distance, his hands still clasped behind his back. At that moment, the hearts of the two men were aimed at each other more sharply than that jagged stone peak.

“Alliance Leader, please let me meet my son. I will see him and hear what happened.”

“Under the Alliance rules, I cannot do that.”

“Considering the ties between us over all these years, you cannot do this.”

“You are right. Considering our ties over all these years, I cannot do this.”

Jin Paecheon’s cold and solemn gaze flew toward Jo Ibaek.

“I know that you are a member of the Cheonmyeong Society.”

“!”

“Do not think to deny it.”

Jin Paecheon released his aura. A frost-edged force assailed Jo Ibaek. It was a violent storm crashing down on him across a boundless sea. Emotion rode within that pressure. I could kill you. I want to kill you.

Jo Ibaek gritted his teeth and resisted the force, but his precarious body looked as though it might be swept over the cliff at any moment.

The storm that had seemed ready to tear him to shreds soon began to subside, slowly. Jo Ibaek was able to climb onto a small island in that boundless sea.

“I do not wish to end my long relationship with you like this. Only I and one of my subordinates know of this matter.”

As Geom Mugeuk had said, Jin Paecheon was giving Jo Ibaek room to breathe.

The fact that he had not pressured him by asking whether he was one of the Cheonmyeong Society’s leaders, but had simply called him a member, and that he had said only he and one subordinate knew, were both hints that if Jo cooperated properly, the matter could be buried.

Jo Ibaek knelt before the Alliance Leader.

“There were circumstances I could not avoid.”

“I understand. When one has many people under one’s care, one sometimes ends up walking a path one never wished to take.”

“I do not know what to say in the face of your oceanlike generosity, Alliance Leader. I am sorry, Alliance Leader.”

Tears flowed from his eyes. They streamed down so freely that one might think they could not possibly be anything but sincere.

Jin Paecheon’s expression as he looked down at him was only bitter. They had known each other for thirty years, yet Jo had chosen the path of betrayal. Was it the other man’s fault? Or was it his own fault for making him feel he had no choice but to leave?

“Hand over the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society. Then all of this will become a thing of the past.”

Though he did not say it directly, he was saying that he would forgive him. Jo Ibaek rose through the breathing space he had been given and drew breath.

“That man even recruited my son without my knowledge. That much, at least, I can never forgive. I will have a meeting with the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society tomorrow, so please bring my son to that place.”

“Did you say tomorrow?”

“The leader of the Cheonmyeong Society is not far from here.”

That meant the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society was close enough to come in a single day. Truly, it was astonishing—darkest under the lamp, as they said.

“You have made the right choice.”

“Then I will contact you after arranging the place. Once again, I thank you for your grace, Alliance Leader.”

He bowed at the waist and turned around.

With every step he took, his expression slowly began to harden. There was neither heat nor cold in it. Because it held no emotion at all, it was impossible to guess what thoughts he harbored, but at the very least, it was certain that he was an entirely different man from the one who had knelt and shed tears.

A short while after he disappeared, Geom Mugeuk appeared there.

“Is it all right to let him go like that without surveillance?”

“It should be fine.”

Geom Mugeuk was hoping that by letting him loose, he would instead do everything he possibly could. Only then could everything be dealt with at once.

“I think we should keep in mind the possibility that he may betray us. Asking you to bring his son tomorrow means he wants to exchange the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society for his son. Does that not mean he cannot fully trust you, Alliance Leader?”

“You mean he suspects my mind may change after I catch the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society?”

“If his suspicions only go that far, that would be fortunate. But he may even join hands with the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society and set an all-or-nothing trap. As things stand, I think the chances are half and half. Please leave what comes next to me.”

Whether Jo Ibaek betrayed them or not, the situation was dangerous. They were going to capture the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society, whose identity they did not even know.

“Do you truly mean to handle it alone?”

“If by any chance they harbor other intentions, it could be dangerous. It is not something someone as important as you should go to handle alone.”

“And you are not in danger?”

“It was my affair from the start.”

Jin Paecheon gazed at Geom Mugeuk for a moment. Incredibly, he found himself thinking this: how good it would be if Geom Mugeuk were a warrior of the orthodox faction.

“Do not be too disheartened by his betrayal. He was an evil man from the beginning. He merely remained at your side while hiding himself. This side acted with goodwill; that side acted with malice. That is all.”

He was even consoling him. Jin Paecheon wanted to share a drink with this Geom Mugeuk.

“Will you drink with me?”

Geom Mugeuk had intended to leave after forming a good connection with the Alliance Leader. There was no reason he would refuse a drink alone with him.

“Let us drink here. I will go buy some.”

“I will wait.”

“Is there any liquor you prefer?”

“Buy something strong.”

Geom Mugeuk vanished from the spot in an instant.

A short while later, Jin Paecheon was astonished. Geom Mugeuk had returned with a bottle of liquor in hand, but no matter how outstanding his qinggong was, it was not enough time for him to have gone down and come back already.

“Did you bury liquor in the mountain?”

“It has been a while since I had a drink, so the wine bug in me stirred, and my feet moved a little quickly.”

When they had returned from Dongho before, he had deliberately refrained from catching up with the Alliance Leader using qinggong and wounding his pride, but that did not mean he would deliberately come slowly today.

“I also bought good cups.”

He poured him a drink.

Srrrk.

It was right then. Someone revealed himself. It was the warrior who had always been hidden near Geom Mugeuk. This was the first time he had shown himself.

“I will check the liquor.”

He used a silver needle to see whether there was poison in the liquor.

I had thought he would be a sharp, angular sort of person, but he was a man with a round, gentle impression.

I could tell. The person the Murim Alliance Leader trusted most was this man. If my father had Hwi, then the Alliance Leader had this man.

Though this loyal subordinate had stayed near me all this time and knew that I was not someone who would poison the wine, when it came to the Alliance Leader’s safety, he permitted not the slightest carelessness.

Only after confirming that there was no poison in the liquor did he conceal himself again.

“You could have at least greeted me before leaving.”

When I expressed my regret, half joking and half sincere, Jin Paecheon smiled faintly and said,

“He has always been inflexible, so please understand.”

“I know that well. The person I like most was once my bodyguard too.”

“Why is he no longer your bodyguard?”

“I forced him away, telling him to stop following someone else’s life and try living his own.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, Jin Paecheon felt a strange emotion. Because his nature was not at all like that of a demonic practitioner, he felt drawn to him, and at the same time, he felt an odd fear. It was a fear that began with the thought: What if this man becomes the Heavenly Demon?

After both their cups were filled, the two raised a toast and drank.

“Do you enjoy liquor?”

“I do not drink often. And you?”

“I drink occasionally as well.”

Drinking for the first time in a while made me think of the Drunken Demon. Was he still drunk, swimming around that lake?

In one sense, this was the precarious eve of a decisive battle, and yet the drinking with the Alliance Leader was calm and peaceful.

Perhaps it was because both of them knew well that great events that change one’s life cannot be suddenly prepared for and endured; they are endured by all the days one has lived up to that moment.

“Is your father well?”

The Murim Alliance Leader asked about the Heavenly Demon.

“He is well.”

“Seeing you makes me want to see Cult Leader Geom. It has been quite a long time since I last saw him.”

“When I return, I will convey your words to him. My father will be pleased as well.”

“Hardly. Your father must want to kill me.”

“What about you, Alliance Leader?”

“Until now, I did as well…”

After a brief pause, Jin Paecheon revealed his honest feelings.

“Seeing you has changed my mind.”

He felt that if the current Heavenly Demon died, this Geom Mugeuk would become the Heavenly Demon. Would it not be better for the orthodox murim if things remained as they were? Astonishingly, he was thinking such thoughts about this young man.

“How are you and your father?”

“We were awkward before, but it has improved a great deal recently.”

“That is fortunate.”

“It got even better while playing baduk with him. My father learned baduk from our cult’s chief strategist, so he beats me every time. On the other hand, the strategist I gained does not know how to play baduk.”

“A strategist who cannot play baduk?”

“As expected! The same reaction. I will make sure to tell my strategist. Even the Murim Alliance Leader reacted the same way!”

At Geom Mugeuk’s teasing, the Alliance Leader laughed.

“I also went hunting in the mountains with my father. We even drank at an inn. Do you know how the innkeeper reacted when my father came in?”

Geom Mugeuk deliberately told him about the things he had done with his father. He made the stories as cheerful as possible, and Jin Paecheon listened to them with a smile.

Geom Mugeuk knew the sadness dwelling within that smile of the Murim Alliance Leader’s. He was a father who had lost his son. Geom Mugeuk could not dare presume to understand what that must feel like, but he could tell that, at this moment, Jin Paecheon was thinking of his own son.

And as always, Geom Mugeuk was someone who believed that wounds of the heart should not be buried.

“Do you think of your son often?”

For an instant, the Murim Alliance Leader flinched. His expression changed from moment to moment. Anger, bewilderment, sorrow, and longing. All manner of emotions passed across him in that brief span.

“Sometimes.”

Jin Paecheon said no more and drank. Geom Mugeuk asked no more and drank as well.

Had anyone asked him that question since his son died? Probably not. Who would dare ask the Murim Alliance Leader such a thing?

“Let us go together tomorrow.”

“No, I will go alone.”

“I know you are a master without peer. But if you go alone, it will be dangerous.”

“If you mobilize the Murim Alliance’s experts, some among them may be connected to the Cheonmyeong Society. If the secret leaks and the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society escapes, we may never be able to find him again.”

“Then let the two of us go.”

Geom Mugeuk knew that Jin Paecheon meant it sincerely. He was genuinely impressed. What Murim Alliance Leader in the world could say he would go alone with a blood descendant of the Demonic Cult Leader to a place where there might be mortal danger?

As long as Jin Paecheon remained alive and well, he thought, the orthodox murim would remain alive and well too.

“There is something else you must do, Alliance Leader.”

“What is that?”

“You must prepare for the possibility that the Lord of Jinryong Manor betrays you. He is facing a crisis in which everything he has built over his lifetime may be swept away. You said you would forgive him, but will he truly believe those words? If not, he will use every means at his disposal.”

“For example?”

“They tried to use Miss Jin under the pretext of marriage, so we used Jo Sin. We used him a little more violently than they did. Then how will they respond?”

Jin Paecheon’s expression hardened as if to say, Surely not, as one possible situation came to mind.

“During the time you and I move together, they may target Miss Jin. They would feel, You used my child, so suffer the same yourself. Since it would be a choice made at the risk of their lives, an expert far from ordinary would go. You must remain here. Protect your family.”

At the worry in Jin Paecheon’s gaze, Geom Mugeuk lifted his cup.

“Just this once, please trust the Demonic Path and leave it to us. No matter how vicious that evil is, it will not work on us.”

Jin Paecheon drank with him.

They shared exactly one bottle over a short time, yet it was better and more memorable than any drinking session he had ever had in his life.

Setting down the final cup, Geom Mugeuk bowed respectfully.

“I will give my farewell in advance today. Until we meet again, please take good care of yourself.”

Chapter 175: I Have Good News and Bad News.
-------------------------------------------
Gazing at my reflection in the bronze mirror, I slowly undressed.

Stripped down to nothing but my undergarments. A smooth physique without a single superfluous ounce of flesh, honed through martial training.

I unwound all the Supreme Heavenly Silk wrapped around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword and carefully bound it around my vital points—my heart, neck, dantian, and other key areas. Over that, I donned the precious armor given to me by Hyeolcheon Domah.

Seo Daeryong sat perched on the bed, silently watching me prepare for the final battle.

It wouldn't do to skip the banter at a moment like this, so I tossed a joke his way.

“Shall we go fight together, or not?”

“I shall not. The greatest adventure of my life is already over. Victory in the Lesser Dragon Tournament! I'll boast about it for the rest of my days, rehashing it like bone broth. There will be no more danger in my life.”

I donned light, comfortable martial attire as I spoke to him.

“When your realm rises higher, your heart will change! You will throw yourself into life-and-death peril of your own accord. You will find ecstasy there, and seek out even greater danger.”

“Don't speak such strange prophecies!”

Seo Daeryong stuck his head out the window and looked up at the sky.

“Let's pretend I didn't hear that! No, absolutely not. My adventuring days are over. I already have plenty of stories to tell my future grandchildren on my lap.”

When he reacted so earnestly, how could I resist teasing him?

“Please return safely.”

“Mysterious organizations are only dangerous while they remain mysterious; once exposed like this, they become rather pathetic. Don't worry.”

“No matter what you say, I am not following you.”

“Not working, huh.”

I know. I know how much that Seo Daeryong worries about me right now. How much he wants to fight alongside me. The burning heat in his eyes tells me. Asking me to wait. Until he grows stronger.

* * *

Cheonmyeong Hoeju and Jo Ibaek sat facing each other in the manor's pavilion.

“Have you been well?”

“Thanks to you, I have been.”

Jo Ibaek had met Cheonmyeong Hoeju several times. Cheonmyeong Hoeju did not act like a man shrouded in mystery. If something needed doing, he would come by, and if he wished to meet, he would summon him like this.

Cheonmyeong Hoeju's martial arts were far superior to his own. But setting aside that prowess, the impression he gave was surprisingly ‘ordinary.’

It wasn't a word that suited the leader of a dark organization, but he was undeniably ordinary.

He had the kind of face and attire that, if you looked over while eating at an inn, you would expect to see him sitting at the next table eating as well. His speech and conduct were ordinary.

Because someone who should not be ordinary was ordinary, it instead felt extraordinary. He was precisely the kind of man who could hide as a mastermind because he was so ordinary.

“Why did you ask to see me today?”

“A problem has arisen.”

“Please tell me.”

“The Martial Alliance has discovered that my son joined this society.”

Jo Ibaek gazed at Cheonmyeong Hoeju and added,

“When did you have my son join?”

It was a situation that called for anger, yet Jo Ibaek remained calm.

“Your son joined as well?”

At this, Cheonmyeong Hoeju showed a reaction as if he had not known.

“I was unaware.”

It seemed so natural that he appeared genuinely ignorant, but Jo Ibaek did not believe him.

He didn't know about recruiting his son, the successor, when he had already recruited Jo Ibaek, the Lord of the True Dragon Stronghold? If it were such a sloppy organization, leaving as soon as possible would be the wise choice.

Jo Ibaek didn't bother challenging the obvious pretense. He hadn't come here today to quarrel with Cheonmyeong Hoeju. Today was the day he had to walk the most dangerous and important tightrope of his life.

“My son is presently imprisoned in the dungeon. I came hoping you might find a way, Hoeju.”

“They couldn't have thrown him in the dungeon merely for being associated with the main society.”

“He fell into their scheme and stands accused of attempting to kidnap the Alliance Lord's granddaughter.”

“Dear me! That's exactly the sort of thing those shameless Martial Alliance dogs would do.”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju clicked his tongue and shook his head.

“How could there be a solution?”

“Are you not on close terms with the Martial Alliance Lord, Lord Jo?”

“Does the Alliance Lord not cherish his granddaughter dearly? I don't believe this can be resolved through connections alone.”

“I understand. I shall think on what can be done.”

“Thank you.”

Just then, a door opened in the distance.

If one were to ask Jo Ibaek for the most nerve-wracking moment of his life, it would surely be this instant.

He had naturally assumed it was the Martial Alliance Lord, but the person who entered was a young man.

‘The Alliance Lord didn't come?’

Since the Alliance Lord hadn't come, he naturally hadn't brought his son either.

‘Why?’

When he wanted Cheonmyeong Hoeju so badly?

‘What scheme is he trying to pull?’

Of course, he was trying to pull a scheme as well.

It was a contingency for when the Alliance Lord refused to release his son even after dealing with Cheonmyeong Hoeju, or tried to imprison him too. He had prepared experts to truly kidnap Jin Haryeong.

But he hadn't expected this. Neither the Alliance Lord nor his son came.

How on earth should he react?

Jo Ibaek's gaze turned to the young man walking toward them.

Given his precious status, the Alliance Lord might not come personally and could send other experts instead. But to send just one young expert?

‘Could it be? Is this young man an expert strong enough to face Cheonmyeong Hoeju?’

That couldn't be. He had undoubtedly come to deliver the Alliance Lord's message.

Geom Mugeuk walked over without anyone stopping him. Neither Cheonmyeong Hoeju nor Jo Ibaek had given any orders.

When Geom Mugeuk joined them at their table, Cheonmyeong Hoeju asked Jo Ibaek,

“Who is this young hero?”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju had naturally assumed that Geom Mugeuk, who had walked up to them so calmly and boldly, was Jo Ibaek's subordinate.

Then Jo Ibaek feigned ignorance and asked,

“Was he not someone you brought, Hoeju?”

Geom Mugeuk spoke calmly,

“I am someone sent by the Martial Alliance Lord.”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju flinched in surprise.

The surrounding atmosphere changed. Geom Mugeuk sensed those hiding around them busily shifting positions.

“A precious guest has arrived, so I shall take my leave. Please speak among yourselves.”

The moment Cheonmyeong Hoeju naturally offered his greetings and tried to rise from his seat.

“Sit, Cheonmyeong Hoeju.”

“!”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju stared quietly at Geom Mugeuk and sat back down.

“You knew I was here.”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju's gaze turned to Jo Ibaek. Jo Ibaek shook his head slightly, disavowing any knowledge.

Cheonmyeong Hoeju asked,

“For what matter did the Alliance Lord send you, young hero?”

“Lord Jo here negotiated. To trade his son for the Hoeju.”

Jo Ibaek was flustered because he hadn't expected it to be spoken so openly. On the other hand, Cheonmyeong Hoeju seemed to have expected as much and did not get angry at Jo Ibaek.

Jo Ibaek vehemently denied it.

“Don't believe him. It's a scheme by the Alliance Lord to drive a wedge between us.”

Jo Ibaek had to desperately deny it until he learned why the Alliance Lord had sent this young man in his stead.

Of course, with Geom Mugeuk present, it was no easy task.

“Don't blame Lord Jo too much. It must have been a difficult decision, being a matter of his child.”

“I understand.”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju nodded.

Jo Ibaek denied it to the end.

“Don't be deceived. Don't fall for their tricks.”

Jo Ibaek couldn't help but misunderstand. That the Martial Alliance Lord was trying to eliminate him through Cheonmyeong Hoeju. That was why Jo Ibaek was furious.

‘So this is how you're playing it?’

Jo Ibaek sent a telepathic message to his hidden subordinate.

-Tell them to proceed with Jin Haryeong's side. Right now!

-Yes.

He judged that he could only survive by getting Jin Haryeong into his hands as quickly as possible.

But he didn't know. He didn't know that the reason the Martial Alliance Lord hadn't come here was because he had heeded Geom Mugeuk's advice to protect Jin Haryeong.

Geom Mugeuk asked Cheonmyeong Hoeju,

“I have good news and bad news for you. Which would you like to hear first?”

“I like to start everything in good spirits, so let us hear the good news first.”

“I came alone.”

“You really came alone?”

Cheonmyeong Hoeju was surprised. He had thought elite experts of the Martial Alliance would be encamped outside.

“Then what is the bad news?”

“I came alone.”

When Geom Mugeuk repeated his previous words, Cheonmyeong Hoeju made a puzzled expression.

“Why is that bad news? Did you not say it was good news just now?”

“It is also bad news. Because there is no one to stop me.”

Though he was speaking words that surpassed confidence into arrogance, Cheonmyeong Hoeju rather looked pleased.

“It's not easy to find spirited young people like this these days. Isn't that right, Lord Jo?”

“Indeed. Not only the newcomers but even the experienced veterans are all too busy protecting themselves.”

He voiced his agreement, yet doubt crept in.

‘Is he truly an expert strong enough to kill Cheonmyeong Hoeju? Is that why he's speaking such words?’

Should he throw his lot in with the Martial Alliance Lord even now? If he had the merit of handing over Cheonmyeong Hoeju, he might somehow manage the aftermath.

As though reading that conflict, Geom Mugeuk said to Jo Ibaek,

“Lord Jo, I believe you must now make clear which side you stand on.”

Jo Ibaek was truly torn. But no matter how much he thought it over, he couldn't believe this young man could kill Cheonmyeong Hoeju. Had the Martial Alliance Lord been here, perhaps, but the one at his side now was Cheonmyeong Hoeju.

“What are you saying? I have never betrayed the Hoeju, not for a single moment.”

“May I take that as your official stance?”

It was a statement carrying the implication that he would have no choice but to face execution.

Jo Ibaek was swept up in conflict. Unable to predict why the Martial Alliance Lord was doing this, the decision was anything but easy.

Then Cheonmyeong Hoeju spoke with a smile.

“I shall resolve our Lord Jo's dilemma.”

The moment his words fell, figures revealed themselves from all directions.

Just the first among them was enough to tip Jo Ibaek's heart.

The man wearing a short straight blade at his waist was Demon-Slaying Blade Guryeong. He was once renowned as one of the orthodox faction's three greatest blade experts.

Cheonmyeong Hoeju plus Demon-Slaying Blade Guryeong.

These two alone would have been enough to sway him completely, but there were others as well.

A monk appeared wearing monastic robes and holding a massive iron staff. He was Tyrant Monk Yorai, an absolute expert once active in the Western Regions.

These two alone made it difficult to think of anyone who could stand against them, but lastly, a hunchbacked elder in unique attire appeared as well.

The worn green pouch at his waist revealed his identity: Great Poison Elder Gokchu. He was so renowned for his skill with poison that not a single blade of grass could survive where he walked, turning the land into a permanent field of death. Everyone who knew him feared him.

But it wasn't these three ultimate experts who completely changed the atmosphere.

Masked figures revealed themselves from all sides. Like ants pouring out of an anthill, countless masked individuals appeared from who knew where.

They filled the surroundings. From below the walls to atop them, the courtyard and the roofs. Their numbers were surely at least a hundred. They were not mere headcount. The eyes above their masks and their momentum were fierce and sharp.

Jo Ibaek realized for the first time that Cheonmyeong Hoeju traveled with such tremendous experts.

‘Truly astounding. They could never catch Cheonmyeong Hoeju even if they wanted to.’

Further deliberation was meaningless.

When Jo Ibaek sent a signal, four people appeared.

They wore martial robes respectively adorned with the motifs of plum blossom, orchid, chrysanthemum, and bamboo. They were the Four Gentlemen, Jo Ibaek's hands and feet.

“We will handle that brat.”

To be forgiven for this matter, he had to be thorough. His own people had to step forward and eliminate that man.

Jo Ibaek spoke candidly to Cheonmyeong Hoeju.

“Man to man, how about we forget our past transgressions? After all, this happened because you drew my son in behind my back. Let us simply forget it.”

“Very well. They say the earth hardens after the rain.”

The two looked at each other and smiled.

Then Geom Mugeuk spoke.

“Once they kill me, they will kill you. They won't keep a traitor alive.”

“Brat! Stop sowing discord.”

Once Jo Ibaek made his decision, he didn't look back.

“You will never leave this place alive. Not even your Alliance Lord could survive.”

“I was honestly worried. That you might take my side.”

“What do you mean?”

“I had already made up my mind to kill you too, but if you took my side, wouldn't I feel sorry when the time came?”

“Even in this situation, such words come from your mouth? Truly an arrogant whelp.”

Jo Ibaek gave the order to the Four Gentlemen.

“Kill him.”

The Four Gentlemen stepped out into the open ground.

Geom Mugeuk also slowly walked out and stood facing them.

Over a hundred masked experts surrounded them without gap, and the absolute experts Demon-Slaying Blade Guryeong, Tyrant Monk Yorai, and Great Poison Elder Gokchu were there as well, along with the Lord of the True Dragon Stronghold and Cheonmyeong Hoeju watching on.

Geom Mugeuk gazed at Cheonmyeong Hoeju amidst that massive throng and spoke calmly.

“Falsely claiming to be the Extremely Evil Lesser Demon, plundering the lives and wealth of the martial world, and scheming to overthrow the Martial Alliance through marriage to the Alliance Lord's granddaughter—I shall pass judgment and summarily execute all present here, save Cheonmyeong Hoeju and the Lord of the True Dragon Stronghold.”

Silence fell for an instant.

But soon someone couldn't hold back and burst into laughter, and from there, ridicule spread to everyone. No one present was nervous or afraid. Yes, he must be an expert. He must have confidence in his skills to speak such words.

But they possessed martial strength that wouldn't be strange to storm into the Martial Alliance immediately. Thus, they merely enjoyed the arrogance of a reckless youngster who couldn't tell heaven from earth.

The moment the murmuring and laughter swelled louder, as if commanding everyone to fall silent—

Oooooooh!

With a deep, resonant hum from the abyss, the Black Demon Sword began to hum on its own.

Chapter 176: The Person I Want to See Most.
-------------------------------------------
Have you ever seen a sword cry on its own?

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader’s eyes flew open at the sight. Jo Ibaek, too, had never before seen a sword weep by itself.

In truth, the most astonished of them all was Geom Mugeuk.

He had shed tears for the first time since receiving the Black Demon Sword. It was said that famous swords such as the Heavenly Demon Sword and the Black Demon Sword would only cry on their own when they had achieved perfect communion with their masters.
