‘Yes. I’ll make sure they can’t laugh at me.’

When Geom Mugeuk grasped the sword’s hilt, the Black Demon Sword finally ceased its weeping.

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader looked at Jo Ibaek. At that moment, the two men were thinking the same thing.

Jo Ibaek said to the Four Gentlemen,

“Do not underestimate your opponent. Fight with caution.”

The Four Gentlemen, too, had never seen a sword cry on its own, and they were inwardly tense.

Geom Mugeuk pressed them.

“Those men are one thing, but aren’t you martial artists of the righteous sects? Is it acceptable for you to try to kill someone who came here under the command of the Martial Alliance Leader?”

Yet not one of the Four Gentlemen felt the slightest pang of conscience. There was not even a moment’s hesitation or reluctance. It meant that, until now, they had carried out every order of the True Dragon Manor Lord without distinguishing right from wrong.

Geom Mugeuk said to them,

“That is a sobriquet unbefitting of you.”

The instant he finished speaking, the Black Demon Sword was drawn with the speed of a bolt of lightning.

Flash!

A streak of sword light burst forth, and the bamboo was severed cleanly in two.

It was the fifth technique of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Azure Heaven Technique, one of its swift-sword forms. The attack was even faster than before, and Bamboo of the Plum, Orchid, Chrysanthemum, and Bamboo could never have blocked it. In that flash, his body had already been severed—how could he possibly defend against it?

Seeing Bamboo collapse beside him, blood spraying from his body, Plum launched himself forward.

Screeech! Thud!

Plum saw it. His lower body remained where it was, while only his severed upper body flew forward. The technique that had cut him in two was the first form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Equal Heaven Technique.

Orchid and Chrysanthemum charged in simultaneously from both sides. Having witnessed Bamboo and Plum die in an instant, their movements were no longer as nimble as usual.

The Black Demon Sword changed twelve times in midair.

It was the second form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Transforming Heaven Technique.

The lofty orchid was uprooted at the sixth change, while the snow-white chrysanthemum’s petals were torn away and vanished at the ninth.

This was the might of the Flying Heaven Sword Art after reaching the twelfth level of mastery.

Geom Mugeuk could clearly feel it. The Flying Heaven Sword Art, having reached the twelfth level, was becoming increasingly mature. His Ten Thousand Poisons Immunity Body, the Wind God’s Four Steps having reached the ninth stage—all the improvements in his martial arts were affecting the Flying Heaven Sword Art as well.

Hoooooong!

The Black Demon Sword, having cut down the Four Gentlemen, cried once more.

When facing a large number of opponents, the most important thing was to seize the initiative. One had to instill fear in the hearts of those who charged in, relying only on their numbers—the fear that even with so many of them, they might still die.

Now, only silence hung over the place. No one was laughing anymore.

Geom Mugeuk gave his opponents no chance to compose themselves.

Before the order to kill him could be given, Geom Mugeuk moved first.

In a flash, he was cutting down the masked man on his left.

After stabbing through his heart, he spun and swung his sword. The two masked men behind him had their throats split open simultaneously, spraying fountains of blood.

He leaped over the masked man charging in from the side, smashed his face with a knee, and then stabbed the masked men behind him in rapid succession.

When the sound of flesh being torn rang out with a wet thunk, Geom Mugeuk had already cut down the masked man who had been staggering on the opposite side. Given his original skill, he should never have been defeated so easily, but in that flash, his throat was cut and darkness descended before his eyes.

The masked men who leaped from the roof to launch a surprise attack were instead stabbed by the sword in midair. They fell in a shower like autumn leaves.

Geom Mugeuk was simply too fast.

More than ten men became corpses in an instant, and the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader shouted belatedly,

“Attack him together! Attack him together!”

By the time he finished speaking, four more men had died.

Geom Mugeuk was a tiger that had leaped into a flock of sheep. Of course, these were no ordinary sheep. They charged like madmen and swung their swords. Even as they were cut down, some swords sank into Geom Mugeuk’s body.

But because he never allowed an attack to strike him squarely, the blows that merely grazed him could not pierce the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk or the Ghost Fox Robe.

“He is faster than the Alliance Leader was in his prime.”

At Jo Ibaek’s words, the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader said nothing. Before the words had even ended, three more men collapsed.

“He is human, too. He will grow tired.”

That was all they could rely on. So long as he was human, his internal energy would eventually reach its limit.

More than ten men charged in, yet Geom Mugeuk rushed toward them instead. He ran, but it did not look like running. He slid forward with a scraping sound, like someone gliding across ice.

The masked men fell to either side in his wake.

Hidden weapons rained down from every direction.

Clang-clang-clang-clang-clang-clang-clang-clang!

Geom Mugeuk’s sword scattered sword light, embroidering the air. Every incoming hidden weapon was deflected. Several of them lodged in Geom Mugeuk’s body, but his movements did not slow in the slightest.

Geom Mugeuk shot upward. The masked men who had been throwing hidden weapons from the roof fell one after another as corpses.

They died whether they charged him or turned their backs to flee. They died whether they attacked alone or joined forces. Even when they succeeded in stabbing him, Geom Mugeuk did not fall. When he had no choice but to allow an attack, Geom Mugeuk offered only the areas protected by the supreme-grade Heavenly Silkworm Silk and the Ghost Fox Robe.

Crack.

A masked man, reduced to a corpse, flew into the table where the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader and Jo Ibaek had been sitting and smashed it apart.

“One cannot fight like that through martial prowess alone. That man… has killed countless people.”

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader could tell from watching Geom Mugeuk fight. His opponent truly had extensive experience in combat. One could not fight that way without crossing a river of blood. He was someone who knew the fastest and most efficient way to kill an opponent.

“How can someone so young have such experience?”

“That is why we threw ourselves into this martial world, is it not? Because it is so much fun.”

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader was not afraid. Whether it was because he had something to believe in or because he had resolved himself to die, no one could say.

When more than half of the masked men had been killed, the survivors lost their will to fight. From then on, their role was no longer to kill Geom Mugeuk, but merely to wear him down.

And so the last masked man fell.

“Huff, huff, huff!”

Only Geom Mugeuk’s ragged breathing echoed through the place.

Of the three experts—the Demon-Slaying Saber, Paeseung, and the Great Poison Elder—the first to launch himself forward was Paeseung Yorai.

He rushed in, swinging his iron staff. He gave Geom Mugeuk no time to rest.

Kaaang! Kaang! Kkang!

The iron staff and sword collided in rapid succession. Yorai drove him back with tremendous force.

Geom Mugeuk retreated, losing out in strength.

Believing he had seized the advantage, Yorai poured his internal energy into the iron staff. Confident in his own internal energy, he was deliberately turning the fight into a contest of internal power. He was sure of his own strength, while his opponent was exhausted.

Yorai pressed forward with his internal energy. He thought victory was certain, but he was overlooking one fact: the reason Geom Mugeuk was this tired was because he had fought while conserving his internal energy as much as possible.

No matter how abundant Yorai’s internal energy was, it could not compare with Geom Mugeuk’s.

As Geom Mugeuk’s power surged toward him like a flood, Yorai’s arms trembled violently around the iron staff.

It was then that the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader, watching the situation, gave an order.

“Great Poison Elder!”

The instant his words fell, something flew from the Great Poison Elder’s hand. Since Geom Mugeuk and Yorai were locked in a contest of internal energy, neither could evade it.

Boom!

Black poisonous smoke rose and swallowed the two men.

“That’s it!”

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader sprang to his feet.

Jo Ibaek rose with him and asked,

“What kind of poison is it?”

“The most powerful poison among the Great Poison Elder’s poisons—the Extreme Yin Death-Ending Poison! That fellow is finished!”

“Ah!”

Only then did Jo Ibaek relax at the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader’s certainty. He had been tormented by the thought that he might have made the wrong choice.

Someone staggered out of the black smoke. In that brief span, the poisoned man was bleeding from every opening in his body. He muttered something unintelligible, then collapsed face-first.

And when the poisonous smoke dissipated, everyone was aghast.

Geom Mugeuk was still standing there, completely unharmed.

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader and Jo Ibaek shouted simultaneously,

“Immune to all poisons?”

“The Ten Thousand Poisons Immunity Body!”

They were enemies, but they could not help feeling awe. This was the moment they witnessed with their own eyes the legendary body that was immune to all poisons.

The fact that he had endured the poison was not the issue.

Shrrrraaaaack!

The things now floating before Geom Mugeuk’s chest.

Within the poisonous smoke, sword qi in the shape of swords had already appeared—sword qi he himself had created. Their number had increased again, and now no fewer than twenty swords floated in the air.

The seventh form of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, the Wandering Heaven Technique.

Swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish, swish!

Twenty sword qi flew like rays of light and pierced the Great Poison Elder. He swung both palms, trying desperately to block them, but he could not stop the twenty sword qi flying toward his entire body.

Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud! Thud!

A torrential rain of sword qi pierced through him.

As the Great Poison Elder collapsed, the Demon-Slaying Saber was already bringing his weapon down upon Geom Mugeuk’s face.

While launching the Wandering Heaven Technique, Geom Mugeuk’s sword had become embedded in the wall behind the Great Poison Elder. The Demon-Slaying Saber thought that if he missed this chance, he would die.

Geom Mugeuk narrowly evaded the attack, and at the exact moment the two men’s faces passed within inches of each other—

Whoosh.

Geom Mugeuk exhaled the smoke he had been holding in his mouth.

“Aaaaaaaaaagh!”

He had held the earlier poisonous smoke in his mouth and spat it at the Demon-Slaying Saber. One mouthful had been enough.

The Demon-Slaying Saber clutched his face and rolled across the floor, screaming horribly. He, too, died while blood flowed from the seven apertures of his face.

Now, only Geom Mugeuk, the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader, and Jo Ibaek remained.

Looking at the corpses scattered in every direction, Jo Ibaek felt as though everything were a dream. But he realized that unless he awoke from this nightmare quickly, he too would die within it.

Jo Ibaek shouted,

“Do not forget! I am on the Alliance Leader’s side! I was the one who told you that the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader was coming here—”

Flare!

Boom!

Half of Jo Ibaek’s face was blown away, and the remaining portion had turned to ash as though burned. A powerful scorching-yang force had been unleashed.

Retracting the hand with which he had released the palm strike, the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader lifted the wine cup before him and slowly drank.

“I’m the sort of person who can’t rest easy unless I finish what I’ve started.”

It meant that he had intended to kill him all along.

When Geom Mugeuk extended his hand, the Black Demon Sword embedded in the wall flew back into his grasp.

“You may think yourself impressive, but in my eyes, you are insignificant. If you want to live quietly, then live quietly. If you want to become a hero, then step forward proudly. But what are you, exactly? You scheme in hiding, sow discord from behind the scenes, and manipulate people without their knowledge. You recruit father and son separately, keeping each unaware of the other. Do you think that is impressive? No. It is nothing but cowardly and despicable.”

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader smiled bitterly.

“What can I do? That is simply who I am. Scheming behind the scenes is so much fun, and it makes me happy.”

He should have continued to do just that. But as his organization and power grew, he had naturally become careless. Something like today? It would have been unimaginable when he had only two or ten people protecting him. He had hidden, and hidden again. But now that a hundred people guarded him, he had grown arrogant, acting as though he were invincible.

“I suppose I’ll have to return to the beginning and start over.”

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader’s body began to heat up fiercely. At the very least, his martial arts were not insignificant.

The air around them grew hot in an instant.

Geom Mugeuk immediately understood. The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader wielded a martial art of the Ultimate Yang—even among the most extreme yang arts.

‘Dangerous.’

The Black Demon Sword, having read his thoughts, let out another long cry.

His head grew dizzy and his breath caught in his throat. Because this was pure martial power rather than an illusion, there was no different way to respond. He raised his protective aura and endured. Never in his life had he experienced such heat. It felt as though he had been thrown into molten lava.

Just as the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader was about to unleash a palm strike of Ultimate Yang imbued with flames—

The surroundings changed, and the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader was startled.

Wheeeeeeeeng.

In the blink of an eye, the surroundings had transformed, and he was standing on a snowy mountain as a blizzard raged around him.

The cold snowstorm striking his face was real. Even the Heavenly Mandate Society Leader, who had endured every kind of hardship, was shocked by the situation.

“This is…”

Far ahead, Geom Mugeuk was scooping up the snow piled on the ground and rubbing it across his face.

The Heavenly Mandate Society Leader reached down and touched the snow as well. It was real snow.

Wheeeeeeing.

Under the fierce blizzard and bitter cold, the supreme-yang qi of the Lord of the Tianming Society, which had burned like a flame, died down. As the heat subsided, so too did the killing intent and fighting spirit.

The two men returned to their original positions.

In the middle of the estate, strewn with corpses, the Lord of the Tianming Society asked,

“What martial art is that?”

“Space-Time Transposition Art.”

The very moment he heard the answer, the Lord of the Tianming Society was no longer there.

The final technique he had employed was an art of concealment. It was his last and most secret move, allowing him to vanish before his opponent’s eyes and escape the place.

His belief that he could survive in any situation had left him no better off than prey caught in a spider’s web.

Squelch!

The Lord of the Tianming Society opened his eyes and looked down at his chest.

Geom Mugeuk’s sword, which had uncovered his concealment, had pierced straight through his heart.

The Lord of the Tianming Society summoned the last of his qi and examined Geom Mugeuk.

Qi had been released all around Geom Mugeuk’s body, spreading out like a spider’s web. It was the same qi projection he had learned from the Heavenly Demon during his hunt, but now it had spread out as densely as a spider’s web, just as his father had once done. There had been no opening through which the Lord of the Tianming Society’s concealment could escape.

As though he had no regrets, the Lord of the Tianming Society said,

“…If even that martial art, the Space-Time Transposition Art, cannot be countered by poison, and even concealment cannot get through. Then I suppose I have to die.”

In truth, in today’s battle, Geom Mugeuk had used everything he had learned thus far.

Before drawing his last breath, the Lord of the Tianming Society left him one final remark.

“I… have met someone like you before.”

Geom Mugeuk was startled.

“Where? Where?”

But those were the Lord of the Tianming Society’s final words. He had already breathed his last.

‘It was Hwa Mugi! This Lord of the Tianming Society had met Hwa Mugi before.’

Of course, it could have been someone else, but Geom Mugeuk believed that the person the Lord of the Tianming Society had spoken of was Hwa Mugi.

Hwa Mugi was alive somewhere. He was growing as powerful as Geom Mugeuk himself, walking toward their fate.

Geom Mugeuk was no longer afraid of him. He, too, was doing his best to live.

Yes, Hwa Mugi was Hwa Mugi. For now, it was time to savor the joy of victory.

At last, he had safely completed the successor’s trial his father had given him. More importantly, he had brought it to an end on his own, without the help of the Demon Venerables.

Geom Mugeuk left the estate. Once he was gone, the Martial Alliance Leader would take care of what remained. He had already said his farewells to Jin Paecheon and Jin Haryeong, so he could leave just like this.

Outside the estate, Seo Daeryong was waiting in the driver’s seat atop a carriage.

“This is a carriage driven by the winner of the Lesser Dragon Tournament. You’d better consider yourself honored.”

Contrary to his words, Seo Daeryong’s eyes were trembling. His face was filled with the ardent emotion of someone grateful that Geom Mugeuk had returned safely.

“I bought you a new set of martial robes inside. Change into them.”

“Thank you.”

Geom Mugeuk climbed into the carriage.

“Let’s go. Home.”

“Who do you want to see the most?”

Leaning against the carriage, Geom Mugeuk closed his eyes.

“The person I want to see most is…”

The only thing that heard his answer, so faint it was almost inaudible, was the wind brushing past his hair.

“Who was that?”

When Seo Daeryong turned around, Geom Mugeuk, having poured every ounce of his mental strength into the battle, had fallen asleep. The new martial robes Seo Daeryong had prepared lay draped across his knees, still untouched.

‘You’ve truly worked hard, Hall Lord.’

And so the carriage began to race toward the Heavenly Demon Divine Church.

Chapter 177: Returned After Completing the Mission.
---------------------------------------------------
When he awoke, Seo Daeryong was lighting a campfire beside the parked carriage.

“You’re awake?”

“How long did I sleep?”

“Long enough. No, seriously, how can a martial artist sleep so soundly he wouldn’t know if someone carried him off?”

“I was keeping watch even in my sleep.”

“While snoring like that?”

In truth, I had slept deeply without waking even once.

Of course, Seo Daeryong wouldn’t know. That if I sensed real danger, the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art would wake me.

“You’re hungry, right? Have some of this.”

Seo Daeryong set out the food he had bought in advance at the inn before we left. Meticulously enough, they were all dishes I enjoyed.

“After this meal, we’ll have to hunt for our food.”

Eating the foods I liked after a great battle truly put me in a good mood. It might seem like nothing, but people are moved by such small things. Thanks, Daeryong.

After eating my fill and drinking cool water, the words came out on their own.

“Ah, now I feel alive.”

As I sat basking in the campfire, Seo Daeryong handed me Golden Wound Medicine.

“Then you should treat your injuries now.”

I took off my blood-soaked clothes and the Ghost Tiger Robe. Then I unwound the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Thread and wrapped it around the hilt of the Black Demon Sword again. Gradually, the Supreme Heavenly Silkworm Thread was soaking in blood and turning red. I thought about washing it, then left it as it was. The hilt of the Black Demon Sword wrapped in bloodstained cloth had its own sort of charm.

I applied the Golden Wound Medicine to the injured places. I had avoided any fatal wounds, but I had been cut and scraped here and there, and there were many places bruised black all over, so my body was a mess.

“You’re still in the prime of youth. What are you going to do with so many scars on your body? If women see them, they’ll all run away.”

“Later, I’ll undergo a complete rebirth and overhaul everything.”

“An opportunity like that… I was about to say it wouldn’t come so easily. But if it’s you, Pavilion Lord, I’m starting to think it might.”

After I finished applying the medicine, I changed into the new martial attire Seo Daeryong had bought.

“It fits perfectly. I like it.”

“You worked hard.”

“Our first mission was a success.”

“Our last mission was a success.”

There are moments when just seeing a certain person puts you in a good mood. For example, when Father sits in the grand chair and looks down at me with a displeased expression, or when the Blood Heaven Saber Demon drives that huge Heaven-Extinguishing Great Saber deep into the ground and leans against it.

With Seo Daeryong, it was when we exchanged pointless jokes like this.

“Truly, while I was waiting in the carriage, I wondered at least ten times whether I should go in.”

“If you had come in, I would have had a hard time rescuing a hostage on top of everything else.”

“Then you would have grown even more. Should I have gone in after all?”

Saying that, Seo Daeryong laughed. I laughed along with him.

After we bantered for a while, Seo Daeryong spoke about what he had felt during this mission.

“I keep dreaming about that martial contest. I hear the cheers I heard on the dueling platform, too. I miss that moment. Seeing that, I suppose rather than being some hidden master, I want to become a martial artist who receives people’s attention.”

“You have to go down that path anyway.”

“Pardon?”

“Because you’ll become the Demon Venerable.”

Since the Blood Heaven Saber Demon had passed on his martial arts to him, for now, the path of successor had been decided.

At that, Seo Daeryong’s expression turned serious.

“Pavilion Lord. Can I become the Demon Venerable? Can I truly bear such a position?”

“Does your master look like the sort of person who would hand over his place so easily?”

“No.”

“Then what are you worried about? If you can’t believe in yourself, believe in your master’s judgment.”

“Still… I can’t believe it. Me, the Demon Venerable.”

Well, that was understandable. A mere investigator of the Hwangcheon Pavilion becoming the Demon Venerable. It would be stranger if it felt real to him so easily.

“I will truly work as if my life depends on it.”

To the burning Seo Daeryong, I spoke calmly.

“Of course you have to work hard, but keep this in mind. They say that when you climb uphill, you can see only the summit, but when you walk downhill, you can see the world. Look back every now and then as you climb.”

Seo Daeryong looked at me with a strange smile.

“You’re ten years younger than me, yet you say things like that so well.”

When I lay down beside the campfire, Seo Daeryong lay down on the opposite side.

Lying there by the fire and looking up at the stars in the night sky felt wonderful.

“Later, I should just travel around like this.”

“Please be sure to take me with you.”

“You should go around receiving applause and attention.”

“I can receive all the attention I need, then go with you.”

“No. I’m going to take Ian with me.”

Seo Daeryong pouted. You fool, if that were so easy, would our father be trapped in the Heavenly Demon Hall?

I slept deeply that night as well. The tension and fatigue that had built up over a long time were loosening.

* * *

We traveled by carriage until my body recovered.

As soon as I had recovered, I sold the carriage, carried Seo Daeryong on my back, and returned using Swift Steps.

At first, Seo Daeryong acted as if he were dying, but later he adapted completely and even cracked jokes while being carried. It was a moment that made me feel anew the adaptability of human beings.

After running and running like that, we arrived at the main cult.

The first place I headed was the Heavenly Demon Hall.

Beyond the red carpet, far in the distance, Father was seated in the grand chair. Today, I felt especially grateful to Father for remaining steadfastly in his place as always.

The moment my eyes met Father’s as he looked down at me expressionlessly, my heart surged. Afraid I might show too much emotion, I quickly bowed.

“I have executed the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society and returned after completing the mission.”

“You worked hard. Rise.”

When I stood up, Chief Strategist Sama Myeong, who had been standing beside him, greeted me.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

“I heard that dealing with the Cheonmyeong Society this time was no easy matter.”

Because I had sent letters from time to time to report the situation, Father and Sama Myeong knew about the process and results.

Of course, I could not include this sort of complaining in the letters.

“Don’t even mention it. I nearly died more times than I can count. This was far too difficult a test. No, it was an impossible mission!”

“I admit it. It was my mistake. I did not realize that an organization called the Cheonmyeong Society had grown to such a scale.”

Sama Myeong readily acknowledged his error. After all, the one that had assigned the mission was the Tongcheon Pavilion, which Sama Myeong led.

At that moment, Father, who had been watching, suddenly spoke.

“So you tried to kill our son.”

Sama Myeong and I both looked at Father in surprise. Of course, both of us were surprised that Father had made such a joke in this situation, but I was surprised for a different reason.

Our son.

It was the first time I had heard those words from Father. I might have heard them when I was very young and did not remember, but as far as my memory was concerned, this was the first time.

I needed to respond with some sort of joke, but nothing came to mind.

Sama Myeong smiled and said to me,

“How is it? Would this be enough for you to forgive me?”

He knew as well. That this was the first time I had heard the words “our son.” That I was moved because of it.

“Please send me again. Where is it this time? The Tianxia Society? The Tiandi Society?”

At my words, Sama Myeong laughed.

I was in a good mood. It had been just one phrase, but for me, it would become a moment I would never forget for the rest of my life.

Calming my trembling heart, I asked Sama Myeong,

“What happened to my brother?”

“The Eldest Young Master has already handled the task he was assigned safely and returned.”

As expected, just as before the regression, my brother had completed it safely and returned. The fate that had changed was on my side. The hidden organization called the Cheonmyeong Society had disappeared from the martial world, and I had passed the test.

“Then both of us passed the test.”

“That is correct.”

“My test was far more difficult, so shouldn’t you declare that I won? Please make me the successor!”

I demanded it confidently.

“It is a request well worth making, but from the Eldest Young Master’s perspective, would it not feel unfair? We must also consider the possibility that the Eldest Young Master might have chosen the more difficult side.”

As the two of you probably expect, my brother never would have solved it. If he had gotten deeply involved, he likely would have lost his life. In that sense, it was fortunate that I chose this side.

But I could not bring myself to say that.

“Then isn’t this far too unfair to me?”

“Therefore, separate from the result of this test, I believe we should give you a reward.”

It seemed he had already discussed this with Father.

“Is there anything you want?”

Without hesitation, I looked at Father in the grand chair and said,

“As my reward, come hunting with me once more.”

Father looked as if this was unexpected.

“Every time you receive a reward, you ask to go hunting with me.”

After my first regression, when I won the martial contest, the wish I asked for had also been a hunt.

“We have to catch the tiger we failed to catch back then.”

“Very well. Let us go.”

Father readily accepted my proposal.

“We depart tomorrow morning.”

* * *

After leaving the Heavenly Demon Hall, the first place I went was Ian’s training ground.

I had thought she would be sweating as she trained in martial arts, but unexpectedly, Ian was reading a book.

So beautiful that she startled me every time I saw her, she gave off yet another charm as she read. It was a sight that would make every scholar in the world fall for her.

The thought came to me again. I really did have to show Ian to Jin Haryeong at least once.

Wondering what sort of martial arts manual it was, I used the Divine Eye Art and looked closely, but surprisingly, it was a collection of poems and paintings.

As I entered the training ground, I said,

“Are you planning to quit being a martial artist and become a poet?”

At my words, Ian glanced up, then looked back down at her book and said,

“You’re here?”

She acted as if she had seen me just yesterday. No, even if she had seen me yesterday, she would have greeted me more warmly than this. I immediately sensed that she was teasing me.

Pretending not to notice, I sat down beside her.

“Congratulations.”

“Congratulations for what?”

“I heard there will be noodles to eat soon?”

Only then did I understand. Our loose-lipped Seo Daeryong must have come and told her everything about Jin Haryeong.

“I hear the person you’re fated to love is the granddaughter of the Murim Alliance Leader?”

“Not love, meeting. Not daughter, granddaughter.”

“If she’s the foremost beauty of Hubei, she must be incredibly pretty.”

“Not prettier than you.”

“What about her personality? Is it good?”

“She’s decent enough. She wasn’t raised without manners.”

“A perfect spouse, then. Truly, congratulations.”

“Are you done being jealous?”

“I told you, I’m not jealous. I’m congratulating you.”

“Then you should do it while looking at me.”

“I don’t want to.”

Ian looked so adorable being jealous.

“I told her. That when I returned, someone far prettier than her would be waiting for me.”

I saw a smile form at the corners of Ian’s mouth as she lowered her head.

“So what did she say?”

“She didn’t believe me. So I’m going to be sure to show you to her next time. I’m leaving.”

When I rose and began walking away, Ian sprang to her feet.

“Where do you think you’re going! After making someone wait for you like this.”

Ian jumped up and ran to me. She threw herself into my arms.

I could feel her tears falling behind my shoulder. I could tell how much she had worried about me while waiting.

“Just a moment.”

I stood there holding her for a while. Feeling her warm body temperature, my heart grew calm. It felt as if all kinds of complicated thoughts were disappearing. How fortunate was it to have even one person like this in one’s life?

After a while, she pulled away from me. As if she herself thought she had been too bold in running over and embracing me, Ian’s cheeks had turned red. She quickly changed the subject.

“The Blood Heaven Saber Demon lent me this poetry collection.”

“The elder did? Why?”

“I went to see him. There was a rumor that the Demon Venerables had gotten involved in your mission, young master. I asked the Saber Demon whether you were all right.”

I could imagine how much she must have hesitated before going to see the Blood Heaven Saber Demon in person.

“What were you going to do if he said I wasn’t all right?”

“Then I would have gone to find you. No matter what, I would have gone. If I thought you might die because I didn’t go, I wouldn’t be able to eat or sleep. I would have to go, even for my own sake.”

“You worrywart. If you’d come, I would have left you at the Murim Alliance.”

“Then I would have lived without a single worry, running a gukbap shop near the Murim Alliance.”

“If you ran a gukbap shop, not a single day would pass quietly.”

She burst into laughter. Her bright personality had not changed, whether when she had been plump or now. The fact that she was not shaken by any external factor or change was her greatest charm and strength.

“At that time, the Blood Heaven Saber Demon told me not to worry about useless things and lent me a book to read. The book he lent me then was about human life and death. I mean, lending me a book about life and death while telling me not to worry? Anyway, since then, I’ve been borrowing one book at a time and reading them. But isn’t it strange? When I read books, it feels like my martial arts training goes better too.”

“That’s because your martial arts have finally become connected to your life.”

“My life and my martial arts are connected?”

“At first, martial arts improve through talent and effort. Then, once you reach a certain level, martial arts begin to reflect that person’s life. You know how martial artists often hit a wall and can’t overcome it?”

“Most do.”

“The reason is that they can’t change their lives. A person has to change for their perspective on martial arts to change, but if the person remains the same, they can’t get past the wall blocking them.”

“Ah!”

Ian seemed to have found an answer. It was the moment her question was resolved—why reading poems and books about life and death seemed to help her martial arts.

“Let’s talk about it again later. Right now, I have to go meet the master of life and death.”

As I was about to turn away, Ian said spiritedly,

“Young master, I’m so happy you returned safely.”

“It’s good to see you.”

Leaving Ian’s residence like that, this time I headed for the Southern Saber Sect, where the Blood Heaven Saber Demon’s residence was located.

Chapter 178: Who Is the Third?
------------------------------
The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was in the midst of training. He had entered seclusion long before I took this trial, so he had been absorbed in cultivation for quite a long time without rest.

“Are you truly preparing for war or something?”

Without even turning his head, he answered.

“When you’re old, living each day is war.”

“I’m back.”

This time, I ran over and threw my arms around the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“What disgusting thing are you doing?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon recoiled and forcibly pushed me away. To be honest, I wanted to hug Father like this. But I was too afraid I’d get beaten with the Nine Flames Demonic Art to try.

“I missed you, sir.”

“You’re acting as if you haven’t seen me in ten years.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon drove the Heaven-Destroying Great Saber deep into the ground.

Then he leaned against it. At times like this, that blade looked like his friend, and it was quite a pleasant sight.

“And the leader of the Cheonmyeong Society?”

“He’s dead.”

“There must have been many people around him.”

“I got a lot of blood on my hands this time.”

Perhaps sensing the bitterness in my answer, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon went inside, brought out some liquor, and poured me a cup.

“What can you do? That is your fate. Drink and shake it off.”

I drained the liquor he gave me in one refreshing gulp. It felt as though the Blood Heaven Blade Demon were washing the blood from my hands. At the very least, it was liquor poured by someone who knew better than anyone what it meant to kill a person, and it was comfort.

I did not say thank you. As always, it was not something I could repay with words.

“The First Young Master will never give up, so things won’t be easy from here on. His ability and talent may not match yours, but when it comes to his obsession with becoming the Heavenly Demon, he is stronger than you.”

That was why it would be difficult, trying to go forward while keeping such a brother alive.

“By the way, have you met your disciple?”

“He came by earlier.”

“You truly made the right decision choosing him as your successor.”

“We’ll have to wait and see.”

“He’ll do better than anyone. Ah, you must have heard he won the Little Dragon Tournament.”

“He won?”

“You wrote that he’d die if he lost, so how could he not win? Even if it wasn’t the Little Dragon Tournament but the Greatest Under Heaven Martial Competition, he’d have to win. And whose martial arts is he using? How could he lose?”

As if he had not truly expected him to win, joy flickered across the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s face. Seeing him like this, it was clear he did have affection for Seo Daeryong.

“Good grief. The fact that that chatterbox didn’t boast about it shows just how difficult he finds you, sir. Please treat him a bit more comfortably.”

“If master and disciple are too comfortable with each other, trouble is bound to follow. In any case, he must be unbearably arrogant now. He’ll need hellish training.”

Ah, Inspector Seo. This was something I failed to foresee. Sorry.

“Go on now. Don’t you have to see the other Demon Venerables too?”

“No. I’ll see the others slowly. Today, I’m only here to see you, sir.”

If you like someone, you should like them this clearly. Just as they say that a person who likes everyone likes no one, regardless of that person’s true feelings, everyone ends up thinking that way. Isn’t that person fond of everyone anyway? At least when it came to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, that was not a misunderstanding I wanted.

“You’re still good at flattery.”

But an unmistakable smile had bloomed at the corners of the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s mouth. He secretly liked this sort of thing.

“Go greet the Demon Venerables. They all stepped forward for your sake, so it’s proper to pay your respects.”

“Yes, then I will.”

“You wanted to go in your heart anyway.”

“All you need to know is that you are always my first priority, sir.”

Leaving the Blood Heaven Blade Demon behind as he swung his saber once more, I departed from his residence.

Before going to the Valley of Villains, I stopped by home and brought my white mask. When going to the Valley of Villains, wouldn’t wearing a mask be proper etiquette?

The Faceless Ones wearing white masks no longer watched me especially closely as they had before. The hostility in their gazes toward me had also disappeared.

From their reactions, I could tell that my friendship with the Extreme Evil Young Demon was deepening. If they were to look at me with friendly eyes and even greet me, then that would mean we had become even closer than we were now.

Guided by a Faceless One, I entered the residence of the lord of the Valley of Villains.

At first, I had thought these Demon Venerables were as important as martial arts training, so meeting them had been work. But now it had become a relationship, not work. Surprisingly enough, I was visiting them because I truly wanted to see them.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon was looking at the white wall again today. Truly, what was he thinking about while staring at that wall like that?

“I’m here.”

Lifting the mask onto the top of my head, I greeted him cheerfully. Then the Extreme Evil Young Demon turned his body toward me.

My gaze met the two eyes behind his mask. To think a day would come when I would feel this glad upon meeting eyes with the Extreme Evil Young Demon.

Together, the Extreme Evil Young Demon and I had joined forces and fought battles risking our lives twice. The first was the fight against the Sword Emperor Baek Manggi, and the second was the fight against his martial uncle Yang Cheogi.

Both of those battles had been fights where we staked our lives, and perhaps because of that, there was a special feeling between us as comrades of the battlefield who had protected each other’s lives.

“I heard you resolved the matter with the Cheonmyeong Society well.”

“Whose revenge was it? How could I have been careless?”

In truth, this trial had been connected to the Extreme Evil Young Demon as well. After all, they had recruited Yang Cheogi, his martial uncle.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon said with a smile,

“As you know, I am very clear about debts of grace and grudges.”

He was referring to the time he had given Chusaeng of the Wolpyeong Sect a token of debt and grudge. The Extreme Evil Young Demon and such a token—truly an unfitting combination.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon held out a small box.

“What is this?”

“Open it.”

When I opened the box, there was a single white pill inside.

“It is the White Spirit Pill, a secret elixir handed down in our sect.”

A sharp yet intense fragrance quickly filled the room. I could tell its effects were anything but ordinary.

“My sect sent it to me. It seems peace has come after the death of Martial Uncle Yang Cheogi.”

It seemed they had sent it in gratitude because I had sent back the warriors of his sect who had come under his martial uncle’s orders without injuring a single one of them.

“I will give it to you, Second Young Master.”

“Why would you give something this precious to me?”

“Because I received it thanks to you.”

It was a gift he was giving sincerely for my sake. Caught off guard by the unexpected gift, I was flustered.

“No. You should take it, Young Demon.”

Then the Extreme Evil Young Demon said something unexpected.

“I am giving it to you because I want to earn some points with you as well, Second Young Master, so stop refusing and accept it.”

“I want to earn points too. Very well. Then how about this? Let’s split it in half and eat it together.”

At my words, the Extreme Evil Young Demon was startled.

“Are you serious?”

“Yes. I’m not the sort of person with enough greed to share an elixir, but I want to make an exception this time. To commemorate fighting together and surviving, let’s each take half.”

The Extreme Evil Young Demon’s eyes shone brightly. It was the clearest look I had seen in his eyes since meeting him. I could feel it. In our relationship, we were passing through yet another turning point.

“Very well. Let us do that.”

I drew the Black Demon Sword and split the White Spirit Pill exactly in half.

As if drinking liquor, we consumed it at the same time.

I swallowed the White Spirit Pill down my throat. A cooling energy filled my mouth, then soon melted and slid down my throat.

It was an elixir of extremely potent nature, but because my existing internal energy was so profound, I absorbed all of its medicinal effects without any problem. Though it was only half, the amount of internal energy added to my dantian was not small.

My two eyes shone with a clear and deep light before returning to normal.

The Extreme Evil Young Demon also finished circulating his energy and rose from his seat.

“It tastes good.”

“It is more delicious than any elixir I have ever eaten.”

No other words were needed. We simply laughed together.

“By the way, what would you have done if I had given you poison? What if that had been a fake White Spirit Pill?”

“That’s why we split it, didn’t we? So we could die together.”

The Extreme Evil Young Demon laughed loudly. To everyone else, his laughter might be nothing more than habitual laughter, but at least to me, it carried emotion. I could feel his feelings in that laughter.

“But is it really all right for you to keep looking at that wall like that?”

“Isn’t it good? It makes me look like a madman. Originally, madmen are not to be touched carelessly. Why? Are you worried? That I might go mad?”

“A little.”

At my honest answer, the Extreme Evil Young Demon told me an unexpected fact.

“Shall I tell you a secret? Our sect’s mental cultivation method becomes more effective when trained while looking at a white wall.”

“Ah, so that was why.”

I had not known the Extreme Evil Young Demon had been training his mental cultivation while looking at the wall. I realized it once again. To my eyes they might look unstable, but everyone was living their own life just fine in their own way.

“After returning, you saw the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and then came here?”

“Yes.”

“It must be tiring to look after each of us one by one.”

“There are still only four of you, aren’t there?”

“You intend to look after all eight.”

Surprisingly, his guess was correct.

“Yes. Whether they are allies or enemies, I intend to manage and look after all eight.”

“Can you endure it?”

Of course, it would not be easy. Even if things were pleasant and we laughed like this now, I did not know when or how the relationship might go wrong. If I took the Demon Venerables lightly, someday I would suffer for it.

“I said something like this to Pretty Heart. That martial arts and life are connected. How could that apply only to her? If I become a vessel capable of embracing all Eight Demon Venerables, then I should also be able to overcome the wall in martial arts that blocks me.”

After gazing at me for a moment, the Extreme Evil Young Demon conveyed what he had felt.

“What are you looking at, Second Young Master? Sometimes, when I see you, I get the feeling that what you are gazing at is not merely the succession, not merely becoming the Heavenly Demon, but something else beyond that.”

Was it because we were a pair who looked only into each other’s eyes? He, too, was seeing that far place I was looking toward. Something even those closer to me could not sense, only the Extreme Evil Young Demon sensed.

Someday, perhaps the day would come when I would tell him. About my enormous enemy. And perhaps the moment would come when we once again stood back to back and crossed through a moment of life and death.

“If it is you, Young Demon, and not someone else, I do not think that moment would be so bad.”

I spoke without any context before or after, but the Extreme Evil Young Demon did not ask me what I meant.

“I will wait for that moment.”

“Then I’ll see you again.”

After bidding him farewell, I left the Valley of Villains. My reunion with him was as clean as that white mask.

* * *

The One Blossom Sword Venerable and the Drunken Demon were drinking together, so I was able to see both of them in one place. As friends, the two of them often drank together, and today happened to be one of those days.

“Welcome, Second Young Master!”

Both the One Blossom Sword Venerable and the Drunken Demon were drunk. It was the first time I had seen the One Blossom Sword Venerable this intoxicated.

“You have fallen to the temptation of evil.”

“Since you’re here, hurry and rescue me.”

Even as he said that, the One Blossom Sword Venerable poured me a drink.

“Thank you for what happened at the Valley of Villains last time.”

“What did we do? We merely rushed over in a crowd.”

At that moment, the Drunken Demon asked me with a dissatisfied look.

“You already met the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and the Extreme Evil Young Demon, didn’t you?”

“Yes.”

“Why are we last? Why third and fourth? If you came back, you could have come to see us first, couldn’t you?”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable joined in that complaint as well. When he glared at me with a face flushed red from drink, I smiled and said,

“Which one of you is third?”

The two of them gladly accepted this powerful attempt at sowing discord.

“I am.”

At the One Blossom Sword Venerable’s confidence, the Drunken Demon shook his head as if finding it unfortunate.

“My friend. I’m sorry, but this one is me. The Second Young Master and I are sworn brothers.”

“Listen, my friend. If you were truly sworn brothers, would you be fighting over whether you’re third or fourth?”

“We are close enough to drink together and swim together.”

“I wasn’t going to go this far, but we even sparred beneath the moonlight.”

The two of them, who had been bickering like that, looked at me. They wanted me to choose. Who was third, and who was fourth. The Drunken Demon asked with an earnest gaze,

“Little brother. I’m not last, am I?”

“You are not last.”

“Right?”

Just as he smiled brightly,

“You’re in the middle. Fourth among the Eight Demon Venerables.”

The One Blossom Sword Venerable drank the victor’s wine.

“My friend, not all brothers are close, are they?”

The Drunken Demon pouted and drank.

I was so grateful to the two of them. Were these people who should be treated like this? Were they people who should have been in positions like third or fourth in the first place? They were putting up with it for my sake. They were willingly fighting over third and fourth place.

“I’ll tell you both clearly today. You know the two who came before you, don’t you? Picky, stiff-necked, eccentric. In contrast, what about the two of you? Aren’t you the most broad-minded among the Demon Venerables?”

“Because we’re broad-minded, right? Not because we’re pushovers?”

When the Drunken Demon asked that, I deliberately flinched, and the two of them burst into laughter as if amused.

The three of us toasted and emptied our cups.

As he set down his cup, the Drunken Demon also shared somewhat surprising news.

“Not long ago, the Blade Demon, I, and the Sword Venerable here had a drink together.”

It was not easy to imagine the three of them drinking together.

“How was it? Was it good?”

“Would it have been?”

The Drunken Demon let out a sigh.

“No blades were drawn, right?”

“They nearly were.”

“Then that’s good enough.”

At that, the One Blossom Sword Venerable said,

“What do you mean, good enough? Don’t ever invite me to such a gathering again!”

Yes. If people whose relationship had gone bad could reconcile so easily, why would there be fights in this world?

In any case, it had meaning. The Drunken Demon was trying to reconcile the two of them.

Holding up my cup, I spoke with my eyes.

You worked hard, brother.

The Drunken Demon smiled as he raised his cup. Sometimes, having that hardship recognized just once is better than a hundred kind words.

“I’ve given my greetings upon returning, so I’ll be going now.”

I said my farewells and stood up. The Drunken Demon even clung to the hem of my trousers, insisting that I drink more, but I forced myself to rise.

I could spend time with the Demon Venerables another day, but hunting with Father was not an opportunity that came often.

At dawn the next day, carrying a leather pouch even larger than before, I headed toward the Heavenly Demon Hall for my second hunt with Father.

Chapter 179: I Have Never Once Felt Lonely.
-------------------------------------------
I climbed the mountain with Father.

It felt like only yesterday that I had climbed a mountain with him right after my return to the past, yet so much had happened since then. If I had to choose just one thing among them, it would be that my relationship with Father had become far better than it had been back then.

So although the same two people were climbing the same mountain, it was clearly a different situation.

“Why did you ask me to come hunting?”

“I wanted to spend time with you, Father.”

Father stopped walking and looked back at me. Because I meant it sincerely, I was able to meet his gaze calmly.

Father, when I returned and saw you sitting in the taishi chair, I really did feel tears welling up.

Father started walking again and asked me,

“Why did you bring so much baggage?”

“As you saw last time, they’re all necessary things. They may be inconvenient now, but later they’ll make our bodies comfortable and our mouths happy.”

Father walked ahead again. I quietly followed behind him.

At first, when I said, Let’s go hunting together, I thought I would chatter away like a talkative fool, but once I was actually with Father, I found I had little to say.

Fortunately, even walking like this in silence wasn’t awkward at all. No, if anything, it felt as if we were sharing a wordless conversation, and I liked it even more.

Suddenly, a thought came to me.

I had no intention of marrying in this life either, but if I did marry and have a child, would there come a day when I went hunting like this with my son? Could I become the kind of father to whom my son could comfortably say, “Father, let’s go hunting”?

Would my son also walk behind me like this and think these thoughts? And what was Father thinking right now?

After walking for quite some time like that, I broke the silence.

“The Murim Alliance Leader said he wanted to meet you, Father.”

I had already reported my meeting with the Murim Alliance Leader by letter, so Father knew about it.

“Was he well?”

“He was hale for his age. His personality was fierce, and his martial arts were domineering. He was quite different from the Alliance Leader I’d imagined.”

“He is not the man the rumors make him out to be.”

“The Alliance Leader said he wanted to see you, Father. So I told him you would likely feel the same, but he said you wouldn’t. He said you would want to kill him.”

“He knows me well.”

“Are you serious?”

“Did you think the Demonic Cult Leader would yearn for the Murim Alliance Leader?”

“There could be courtesy toward the head of the opposing camp, respect as a martial artist, things like that.”

“There is no such thing. Respect as a martial artist only comes after crossing hands.”

“Do you want to fight the Alliance Leader?”

After a brief pause, Father answered.

“He is someone I would like to face at least once.”

Victory in that fight would mean the death of the other. If the Heavenly Demon and the Murim Alliance Leader clashed, it would not end with them brushing dust off their clothes.

And the result would change the fate of the entire murim. That was why this fight could not easily come to pass.

I knew that Father dreamed of unifying all under heaven. Since the topic of the Alliance Leader had come up, I took the chance to probe Father’s intentions.

“If our cult and the Righteous Faction clash, will the Evil Dao Alliance side with us?”

“If you think the Evil Dao Alliance is closer to us than to the Righteous Faction, you are mistaken.”

“They aren’t?”

“If the Evil Dao Alliance had to join hands with either us or the Murim Alliance, they would join hands with the Righteous Faction.”

“Is there a reason you’re so certain?”

“Because the Evil Dao Alliance hates us more than they hate the Righteous Faction. They probably want to erase us and then divide the murim with the Righteous Faction.”

I thought Father was right. Before my return, I had once spoken with a master from the Evil Faction. He truly hated our cult as well, and the reason was simple. He said that as the Demonic Cult came to symbolize true evil, the Evil Dao Alliance had been reduced to an organization pursuing cheap villainy.

Why should the Evil Dao Alliance not become the master of the murim?

It had been nothing more than the lament and personal opinion of a single Evil Faction master, but at the time, I had thought many Evil Faction people might harbor similar thoughts. Perhaps the Evil Dao Alliance’s hatred toward our cult that Father spoke of had begun from that very place.

I did not bother to dissuade Father from his ambition. In any case, we would have to wage war at least once. The target simply would not be the Murim Alliance or the Evil Dao Alliance, but Hwa Mugi.

“Father, you must grow stronger too!”

That evening.

We settled down in a quiet place where a stream flowed.

I laid out thick leather in a spot where Father could comfortably lean back and lie down. It was the most important item among all the baggage I had carried.

After preparing a comfortable place for Father to sleep, I lit a campfire and cooked the game we had caught.

I did not simply sprinkle on some salt and eat enough to fill our bellies; I properly grilled it with the seasonings I had prepared in advance. This time, I had brought not only the secret seasoning Cook Im had made, but also various vegetables and foods.

“How does it taste?”

“It is all right.”

Of course it was. Through Cook Im, I had learned in advance what flavors Father liked and prepared the seasoning accordingly.

After we finished eating, I served him tea. The tea, too, was one Father liked. Seeing Father’s satisfied expression as he drank it made me think I had done well to prepare it.

“Has the Wind God Four Steps reached the ninth stage?”

As expected, Father had seen through my accomplishment at once.

“Yes. While fighting the Heavenly Mandate Society, I felt something, but unfortunately, it did not lead to great completion.”

It felt as though I had almost arrived, yet it was still distant—a truly far yet near sensation.

“If you grow impatient, it will only become slower.”

“I will keep that in mind. Would you like another cup of tea?”

Father nodded, and I poured him another cup of warm tea.

“Do you find it fun to spend time with me?”

We had truly made a great deal of progress. Father was asking me questions like this now. At times like this, there was one principle I recalled: do not think of deceiving Father.

“To be honest, how could it be fun? I’m inevitably cautious and burdened.”

“Then why did you ask me to come?”

“Because I feel reassured.”

Father looked at me with an unexpected expression. I calmly conveyed what was in my heart.

“When I’m with you, Father, I feel so reassured. No matter what bastard blocks our path, you will stop them all, won’t you? Even if something that isn’t human but a monster appears, if it’s you, Father, you’ll stop it, won’t you? When I’m simply with you, my heart feels at ease. I don’t have to protect you, I don’t feel the burden of having to make that person mine, and I don’t feel the responsibility of making someone who sacrifices for me happy. This moment, when no matter what happens, I can simply stay still, feels so comfortable. Because you are here, Father, I feel reassured.”

It was truly how I felt. Perhaps because he had not expected it, Father looked at me with slightly deepened eyes.

“Father, are you not lonely?”

At my question, Father snorted. It was a question unlikely to receive an answer, but when would I ever get to ask such a thing again?

Father lay down on the ground as if to say we should sleep. After lying there for a while and looking up at the night sky, Father suddenly spoke.

“I have never once been lonely.”

To me, it sounded like this.

I am always lonely.

And so the first day of hunting with Father passed.

* * *

On the second day, we wandered more comfortably than on the first. We spoke almost not at all, but neither Father nor I found it awkward.

We simply climbed the mountain together, walked along narrow paths, and then rested by a valley. Hunting was not the main purpose; we simply strolled and rested together.

That evening, as I was about to roast the meat we had hunted, Father came to my side.

“I will try.”

“Try what? Surely you don’t mean to cook?”

“Why would I not?”

“You, Father, cooking?”

As I stared in shock, Father asked calmly,

“Why are you so surprised?”

“Have you ever cooked before?”

“I do sometimes.”

At those unbelievable words, I was startled.

“By any chance, is there a martial art among the demonic arts called Cooking? That’s what you mean, right?”

“Enough nonsense!”

How could I not spout nonsense when Father said he was going to cook?

“When a dish doesn’t suit your taste, you kill the cook, don’t you? Do you perhaps cook on those days out of a sense of atonement?”

“But my cook’s skill is far too exceptional to kill him for that, is it not?”

“You really cook?”

As if to show me himself, Father began cooking. And he truly cooked with practiced ease. He did not roast the meat whole, but cut only the tasty parts and stir-fried them with vegetables and mushrooms. And as if to prove that he really did cook in ordinary times, he even made soup alongside it.

What was even more surprising was that it tasted excellent.

“What is this? Why is it delicious? Did you perhaps secretly pour Kongqing Stone Milk on it instead of seasoning?”

A smile passed over Father’s lips. It was the first smile he had shown since we climbed the mountain.

It was the moment I learned of a side of Father I had never known at all.

The Heavenly Demon of this generation was a cooking Heavenly Demon.

That night, lying on opposite sides of the campfire, Father and I talked about this and that. I was mostly the one bringing up topics while Father responded, and naturally, the Demon Venerables had to come up.

“As you know, Father, on my side are the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon, the One-Blossom Sword Venerable, and the Drunken Demon Lady. On my brother’s side are the Demon Buddha, the Poison King, and the Fist Demon. I’ve set aside the young Soul-Capturing Demon Venerable for now. What do you think? My side looks advantageous, doesn’t it?”

“It is evenly matched.”

“Even though we have one more person, it’s evenly matched?”

“Yes.”

If we were evenly matched despite their side lacking one person, that meant among the Demon Buddha, the Fist Demon, and the Poison King, someone was worth two people. Just whom did Father think so highly of?

“Is it time to drive out the loafer on our side?”

Even at my joke, Father said no more about it. He was telling me to experience it myself and suffer a little. Just whom had he had in mind?

“Why are you trying to become the Cult Leader?”

Because it is my mission. Because it is the mission I suffered my whole life for and even returned to the past to fulfill. I will protect it even if I have to die.

“Because I want to learn the Nine Flames Demonic Art.”

“Why are you so obsessed with the Nine Flames Demonic Art?”

“I’m not obsessed with the Nine Flames Demonic Art. I’m obsessed with becoming stronger.”

“You are already strong enough. What will you do by becoming even stronger?”

“I have to become stronger so I can protect everyone.”

Father, and my brother. Everyone in the Heavenly Demon Hall.

And perhaps a few more people would be added to that. In the past, they had not stepped forward, but now, among the Demon Venerables, some would truly step forward for revenge.

But Father’s thoughts were firm.

“I will pass down the Nine Flames Demonic Art only to the person who will become the Heavenly Demon.”

“Then I will become the Heavenly Demon.”

Just as Father’s resolve was firm, so was mine.

Father looked at me for a moment, then soon turned his back and went to sleep.

“And even if I become the Heavenly Demon, I will become a different Heavenly Demon from you, Father. A Heavenly Demon who freely roams the world. Though to do that, I will have to work far harder.”

* * *

On the third day, Father headed for the summit of Daecheon Mountain.

On the way up the mountain, I sensed the presence of tigers twice. But I pretended not to know. It was to leave myself an excuse to come hunting again next time.

If I had sensed them, Father surely had as well, but Father did not suggest we catch the tigers either. Perhaps Father, too, was looking forward to the next hunt.

“Ah, was the summit always this magnificent?”

The scenery seen from the summit of Daecheon Mountain was truly like something out of a painting.

“When I was your age, I would come up here whenever martial training became difficult.”

Imagining Father standing here in his youth gave me a strange feeling.

“I should come up here from time to time as well when training frustrates me.”

“Come here for a moment.”

“Yes.”

Father sat on a broad rock at the summit and gave me a discourse on the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

It was a martial art in which I had already attained great completion at the twelfth stage, and what I needed to learn now was the Wind God Four Steps. Yet despite knowing that, Father spoke to me about the Flying Heaven Sword Art.

As if all martial arts were ultimately connected as one, and if one fully understood what one knew best, one could find a clue to understanding the rest.

It was a very special moment: one who had attained great completion at the twelfth stage giving a discourse on the Flying Heaven Sword Art to another who had also attained great completion at the twelfth stage.

And I had a new experience. I had naturally thought I would understand everything he said, but there were many parts that differed from my own thoughts. I realized that even at the same level of great completion, the paths taken to reach the destination could be very different.

Whenever I came across something I couldn’t understand, I asked Father without hesitation. I did not pretend to know what I didn’t. Nor did I deliberately feign ignorance. I simply let the conversation flow as naturally as water, following the current of my own thoughts.

What I had thought would be a brief discussion on martial learning continued even after three sichen had passed, then four. It continued for a day, then two, and then three.

We kept talking while eating the food we had prepared, while drinking tea, and even while drinking the liquor we had brought for the final day. When we ran out of food and went hunting, we still talked; even as we roasted meat over the campfire, we spoke of martial learning. Both Father and I became completely immersed in the conversation.

At first, it had begun as a discussion of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, but we ended up debating every aspect of martial learning as a whole. It was an in-depth discussion between Father, the Heavenly Demon, and me, a regressed Heavenly Martial Body.

When that long discussion spanning three days finally ended, I sprang to my feet and unfolded the Wind God’s Four Steps. And then I knew. At last, I could properly take the four steps walked by the god of wind.

I spoke to Father with a startled expression.

“The Wind God’s Four Steps has reached Great Completion.”

As if he had anticipated this very situation, Father was not surprised. He had grabbed me by the collar and hurled me straight into Great Completion.

I bowed deeply to Father.

“It is all thanks to your immense guidance. Thank you, Father. Truly.”

“Get up. Let us head down now.”

“Yes.”

I was seized by an intense desire to run. How fast would my swift footwork be now that it had reached Great Completion? Was it a speed I could withstand? How much smaller had the Central Plains become? I was curious about all of it.

“Father. Let’s have a race. From here to the main sect. What do you say?”

At long last, I had earned the right to pit myself against Father’s Heavenly Demon Flight Art.

Father looked at me with a scornfully amused expression. Why did I like that expression so much?

“What will you wager?”

“Our pride, of course.”

“Very well.”

Since losing would mean nothing to me, the terms were overwhelmingly in my favor, yet Father accepted readily all the same. He must have intended to win and tease me for it.

Father and I stood side by side. Perhaps I had not often stood beside him like this and looked at his profile. His face felt new to me all over again. This moment, racing side by side with Father, made me indescribably happy.

“Now, we start on three. One, two…”

I counted three after I had already set off. That way, if I won, Father would have something to say about it.

As the two pinnacle movement arts began to cleave through space, a sound of wind unlike anything I had ever heard in my life began to reach my ears.

Chapter 180: If You Lack Sufficient Resolve.
--------------------------------------------
I cannot describe the sound of this wind.

If I had to, I would say it was like the sound of wind blowing through a mysterious valley at the end of the world. It was that cool, that light and buoyant—a sound that seemed as though it could not exist in this world.

The moment I heard the sound made by the Swift Step at Great Accomplishment, I knew it by instinct. I would become addicted to this sound. It was as alluring as that maddening speed itself.

At first, I was faster.

The Swift Step that had truly reached Great Accomplishment was so fast it could not be put into words.

Naturally, it was far faster than the Nine-Star realm. When I first suggested we have a race, I had actually prepared myself to crash into trees, roll across the ground, and show Father a complete spectacle of myself.

I had even prepared myself for Father to laugh at me and for us to cause a grand commotion that way.

But I ran better than expected. My eyesight and body were supporting that incredible speed. It was probably because, while traveling back and forth between our sect and the Murim Alliance, I had completely adapted to the Nine-Star Swift Step.

Thanks to that, though I had only just reached Great Accomplishment, I was able to run at a speed similar to Father’s.

But it required extreme concentration and a great expenditure of mental strength. As I ran, I became so dizzy and exhausted that I slowed down. The moment my speed dropped, Father surged up beside me.

“Not bad!”

“Whose son do you think I am?”

Father looked surprised and impressed. Of course, that did not mean there was any trace of yielding on his face. Whether it was his son or anyone else, once his competitive fire was lit, Father was the sort of man who absolutely refused to lose.

“My son, I have something to tell you.”

“What is it?”

His tone was grave, so I was inwardly tense right then.

Right then.

Bang!

I took my eyes off the path because of Father, slammed into a tree, and went flying to the ground.

“I was going to tell you to be careful!”

I could feel Father’s hearty laughter receding into the distance.

I sprang to my feet and ran after him. Fortunately, my protective astral qi had shielded my body, so I was able to get up without any injury.

“That’s cheating, Father! Cheating!”

I insisted that crashing because I had looked away counted as cheating. Naturally, Father did not even look back and ran even faster.

I ran after Father again.

Once I tried running, I could tell. What would decide this contest was not what kind of martial art we were using. The Swift Step of the Wind God Four Steps and the Heavenly Demon Flying Art were lightness arts of almost the same level.

The problem was eyesight. The eyes could not keep up with the maximum speed the lightness art could produce.

So in the end, once I grew accustomed to it, I would definitely win. Thanks to the Divine Eye Art, I possessed vision that surpassed ordinary limits.

I ran while watching Father’s back far ahead. Just as I had watched Father’s back the first time we went hunting, now I watched Father’s back as I ran.

He had looked lonely then, and he looked lonely now, but one thing had changed. The person looking at that back had changed.

This moment was better than any other time I had ever run in my life. I did not know how Father felt, but at the very least, I could feel the affection I held for him growing greater and greater.

The moment I thought of Father, I fell once more. Even if I lost focus for only the briefest instant, I would inevitably collide with something and fall.

I got up again and chased after him, but in the end, I could not catch Father.

When I arrived at the sect, Father had already gone ahead to the Heavenly Demon Hall.

If it had been me, I would have waited, teased him thoroughly, then left, but Father was someone who could restrain himself even from teasing. He would surely tease me the next time we met.

I bowed my head toward the direction of the Heavenly Demon Hall and gave my thanks once more.

The fact that I had achieved Great Accomplishment in the Wind God Four Steps was entirely thanks to Father. It was not only that the Swift Step had become faster. The Shadow Step had become stealthier, the Flicker Step could now escape from even greater danger, and the Underworld King Step had become the true stride of King Yama.

* * *

That evening, the drinking trio gathered at the Pungryu Tavern.

“It has been a long time since you came, so the drinks are on me today.”

The proprietor, Jo Chunbae, said he would specially treat them to drinks today. It was to celebrate Seo Daeryong’s safe return. Seo Daeryong replied that he would accept the goodwill in spirit only.

“This time, I won the martial arts tournament and brought back the prize money. Today, I’m buying a proper round. So please bring out every delicious dish you have.”

“You won! Congratulations, Inspector.”

“Thank you.”

“Young Master, you’ve been well too, haven’t you?”

“Of course.”

When Jo Chunbae went down to the first floor, Seo Daeryong’s boasting finally began.

“Ah, where should I begin this magnificent adventure tale of a solitary inspector? Kyaa! When I first saw the Murim Alliance’s building, I felt destiny itself…”

Watching Seo Daeryong begin his grand epic, Ian and Jang Ho looked at each other and smiled.

Because he told the story so entertainingly, Seo Daeryong’s adventure tale was not boring at all.

“You’re truly amazing. Even if they were formal matches, they were still real combat, weren’t they? You don’t have much actual combat experience, yet you even won.”

Jang Ho had plenty of actual combat experience, but Ian had only trained every day. That was why Seo Daeryong’s achievement seemed so astonishing and enviable.

“Of course, along the way, there was also a fateful and tragic encounter.”

Seo Daeryong spoke of the woman who had cheered him on. He even told them of its sorrowful conclusion.

“…She was a woman sent by the Heavenly Mandate Society.”

Ian jumped up and cursed furiously on his behalf, while Jang Ho consoled him, saying that even that was all experience. The two of them became so heated that Seo Daeryong felt as if all the wounds that had built up inside him were being soothed.

“This is why I looked forward to this drinking gathering the most.”

“We looked forward to it even more.”

“Now, in that spirit, let us toast.”

Seo Daeryong was deeply grateful to the two of them. A woman who could easily be called the most beautiful under heaven, and the commander of the Demon Army, an elite organization of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult, had waited for him and were listening to him for his sake. Who was he to deserve that?

“There is something I want to tell you both. I have decided to walk the path of a true martial artist as well.”

The ones who had influenced him directly were Geom Mugeuk and the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, but these two had also had an immense effect on him. He had always harbored a desire to become someone he would not be ashamed to stand before them.

Ian and Jang Ho sincerely congratulated him. An inspector of the Yellow Springs Pavilion could also be called a martial artist, but Ian and Jang Ho knew that the martial artist Seo Daeryong spoke of now meant something different.

“This is something worth congratulating me for, right? To be honest, I’m afraid I might die early. Aren’t you two afraid?”

At that, Ian smiled and said,

“I think life and death have already been decided. When I think that when and how I die have all already been determined, my heart feels at ease.”

“So this martial artist is a fatalist.”

“I suppose I am.”

Jang Ho, on the other hand, held a different view.

“I always live in fear of death.”

“Really? I would have thought you, Commander Jang, would be the one who feared death least.”

“No. I wake up several times even while sleeping. I often dream of dying as well.”

It was something neither Ian nor Seo Daeryong had known.

Their conversation about life and death continued. Then Seo Daeryong, stirred by the mood, became excited.

“I nearly died this time too.”

Seo Daeryong sprang to his feet and tore open his upper garment.

“There are four scars on my body left by those orthodox bastards. This scar on my shoulder—I received it in the quarterfinals. You see the two holes? This one was from the finals, and this scar here…”

* * *

I was listening to the three of them talk from beside the counter on the first floor.

At the sight of Seo Daeryong finally tearing open his clothes, Jo Chunbae and I laughed together.

Truly, if the Drunken Demon saw this gathering, he would come running and say he wanted to join at once.

Jo Chunbae spoke with his good-natured face.

“They really are good people. When one of them is missing, the other two come and miss that person.”

There had been times when Ian was absent, and times when Seo Daeryong was absent.

“I should be going now.”

“Did you not come to join them?”

“They’re enjoying themselves so much that I think I should let the three of them be together today.”

“Then please wait just a moment.”

Jo Chunbae went to the kitchen and brought back a bottle of liquor.

“This is liquor I have been saving. Take it with you, have a cup, and sleep. It is a gift to congratulate you on returning safely. Welcome back.”

Jo Chunbae was truly delighted at my return, as though he were family.

“I met an esteemed person in the murim this time. If there is ever a chance to drink together next time, I will introduce you.”

“Then I would be grateful.”

He would never dream that the person was the Murim Alliance Leader. But who knew what could happen in life? Perhaps a day would come when the Murim Alliance Leader tipped back a cup at the Pungryu Tavern.

“Thank you for the liquor.”

After bidding him farewell, I left the tavern.

I returned home while looking at the moonlight. There were others I wanted to see as well. Gowol and Cult Leader Pungcheon. I wanted to run to them immediately with the Swift Step, but the two of them were now far away from our sect.

Because I had returned after being away from the cult for a long time, there were many things I needed to do. So I would have to put off seeing the two of them for a while. I was not worried about them. They were together.

On my way to my residence, I turned my steps toward the Great Drunken Grove. When I thought of someone who would be awake now and drink with me, the Drunken Demon came to mind first.

Sure enough, the Drunken Demon was drinking alone at the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

Yeobin, the drunken guest, took me aboard a small ferry and headed toward the island in the middle of the lake.

“Does the Drunken Demon still drink a lot these days?”

“He has reduced it greatly compared to before. He is only drinking one bottle today as well.”

“That’s a relief.”

“It is thanks to you, Second Young Master.”

“How could that be thanks to me? It is thanks to Martial Artist Yeo looking after him and worrying about him.”

Because I knew Yeobin liked the Drunken Demon, I spoke kindly. I thought it would be good if a woman like this stayed by the Drunken Demon’s side and took care of him. Though if her words were to have proper effect, she would have to become his wife rather than his subordinate.

If it were up to me, I would have liked to step in and bring them together, but I had no intention of interfering carelessly. If I inserted myself wrongly for no reason, I might ruin a relationship that could have gone well.

From the railing of the Drunken Dream Pavilion, the Drunken Demon looked this way and waved.

I called out to him loudly.

“Brother.”

Since it was a place for just the two of us, I comfortably called him brother. The Drunken Demon showed with his expression just how happy a single form of address could make someone.

“My little brother is here?”

“I came because I thought my poor fourth-ranked brother would be drinking alone.”

“Being fourth among the Eight Demon Venerables is no small thing. Even staying in the middle is not easy.”

The boat arrived at the island like that, and I went up to the Drunken Dream Pavilion.

“What brings you here all of a sudden?”

“I wanted a drink.”

“You came well.”

I drank with the Drunken Demon, just the two of us. We said nothing and quietly drank.
