Because the Drunken Demon knew liquor well, he also knew those who drank it. He knew whether the other person wanted to drink while conversing, or whether, like now, he wanted to drink quietly with the moon floating over the lake as his side dish.

“I went hunting with Father.”

“It seemed you were gone for quite a while this time. Impressive. How did you spend such a long time alone with the Cult Leader? I would go mad.”

“It might unexpectedly be so fun it drives you mad.”

The Drunken Demon shook his head vehemently as if to say there was no way.

He should see Father cooking. It was only a pity that I could not bring myself to tell him.

“I learned this while talking with Father, but it seems Father holds the Poison King and the Fist Demon in high regard.”

“If the Cult Leader holds someone in high regard, it would be the Fist Demon.”

The Drunken Demon spoke as though it were certain.

“Not the Poison King?”

“The Cult Leader has never liked the use of poison. He has never said it openly, but everyone knows. The Poison King knows too, but pretends not to.”

“Then why does he hold the Fist Demon in high regard?”

I had said I would investigate the Demon Venerables, but after returning to the past and meeting them, I felt it anew. To say you knew a person, you had to meet them directly, talk with them, and experience them. Even then, people were hard to know. What I had known was their deeds, not them. The Fist Demon was the same.

“I once drank with the Fist Demon, just the two of us.”

“How was it?”

The Drunken Demon lowered his gaze to his cup for a moment, recalling that time, then took a sip.

“Unless one had extraordinary resolve, it would not be easy to defeat this man. He made me think that.”

It was the first time I had heard the Drunken Demon speak highly of another Demon Venerable.

“But why the Fist Demon?”

“I was thinking of making you a fifth-ranked brother behind you.”

Because that meant I intended to draw the Fist Demon to our side, the Drunken Demon asked worriedly,

“If you touch the Fist Demon in the current situation, the Eldest Young Master won’t stay still, will he?”

“That is why. So he won’t stay still.”

“What do you mean?”

“It is about time I settled things with my brother too, isn’t it?”

It meant the full-fledged battle over the succession was about to begin, so the Drunken Demon’s gaze also grew intense.

“Are you confident?”

“I’m going forward trusting you, brother.”

At the words that I would trust my brother in order to face my brother, the Drunken Demon smiled and shook the empty bottle.

“Hearing that makes me want to drink one more bottle.”

I poured the last of my liquor into his cup.

“Endure it. From now on, you will need to spend more time sober.”

* * *

The next afternoon, I headed to the Eastern Fist Gate, where the Unbeatable Fist Demon resided.

“I have come to see the Fist Demon.”

The Iron Fists guarding the entrance looked at me in surprise. That was because there was an open rumor that their leader, the Fist Demon, had sided with the Eldest Young Master.

“Please follow me.”

One of the Iron Fists guided me.

The gazes of the Iron Fists moving in and out of the Eastern Fist Gate focused on me. White martial uniforms, blue martial uniforms, red martial uniforms, black martial uniforms. Their uniform colors differed according to their respective ranks. Their gender, age, and physical abilities differed as well. But they had one thing in common.

They were barehanded, without a single piece of metal on their bodies.

Through the Iron Fists who had staked their lives on nothing but their bare fists, I slowly walked forward.

Chapter 181: It Is Not a Cliff to Climb.
----------------------------------------
The Fist Demon was in the grand training ground.

Dozens of Iron Fists were practicing their fist arts, and the Fist Demon stood before them with his arms crossed.

The Invincible Fist Demon, Duan Youqiang.

The strongest master of fist arts, said to have never known a single defeat.

He was a head taller than me, and his entire body was all muscle. His bronzed skin looked so hard it might as well have been steel, as if no blade could pierce it. His fists, in particular, were exceptionally large—without the slightest exaggeration, they were the size of a woman’s head.

If ever the phrase “his body itself is a weapon” applied to anyone, it applied to the Invincible Fist Demon.

And there was one thing that seized the eye above all else. The Fist Demon looked truly terrifying. It would not have been an exaggeration to say the devils depicted in our cult’s paintings and stone statues had been modeled after his face. It was the kind of face that made me think that, should the Great Demon-Righteous War break out, he absolutely had to be put at the vanguard. How many people in the murim would dare look that face straight on?

The Fist Demon slowly turned his head and looked at me.

It didn’t matter what he was feeling right now. His appearance itself was so chilling that even now, at the very least, he looked as if he were asking me to fight.

Of course, I held no prejudice toward appearances. In all my life, how many times had I experienced that looks and nature did not coincide? How many perfectly normal-looking bastards had there been who killed people with a smile?

I bowed my head politely in greeting.

“I pay my respects to the Fist Demon.”

But the Fist Demon did not return my greeting and simply looked back at the Iron Fists. It was an act of thoroughly ignoring me, but I did not mind.

In the past, I would have read meaning into the act itself and had my pride wounded, but not now. Don’t flare up. Please, don’t flare up. Once it passes, it is nothing at all.

If he was acting like this on my first visit, he must have his reasons.

I stood beside him and watched as the Iron Fists trained in their fist arts.

Beneath the blazing sun, the Iron Fists stood like stone statues, each holding a single posture. As if the world would end if they moved, not one of them budged. Then again, with that fearsome Fist Demon watching them, who would dare move simply because it was hard?

A suffocating tension hung in the air, but fortunately, no one made a mistake that caught the Fist Demon’s eye.

How much time had passed?

At the shout of the martial artist standing at the very front, the Iron Fists shifted into the next stance. Of the two fists they had thrust out, one drew back to the waist as their bodies twisted sideways.

Once more, they took another posture and became stone statues that seemed as though they might never move again. They poured their heart and soul into each and every movement.

I thought it was proper training. Of course, how many of them would truly understand the deep meaning contained in this training and make it their own was a separate matter.

Thus, the training that lasted an entire shichen came to an end.

The Iron Fists all dispersed, and the Fist Demon also walked off to his own residence. He ignored me thoroughly to the very end.

Since he must have heard all sorts of rumors and news about me lately, this thorough disregard could also be considered a kind of conversation.

I imagined that terrifying face looking at me and smiling brightly. I imagined exchanging jokes with him.

Fist Demon, if you treat me like this, I am the sort of person who imagines things like that.

The next day, I went to see the Fist Demon again.

But today, just like on the first day, he merely glanced at me once, then silently watched the Iron Fists train in martial arts.

I was ignored just as I had been yesterday, but no one laughed at me for it. It was discipline born from fear of the Fist Demon, but among the subordinates of the Demon Venerables I had seen so far, I thought the Iron Fists were the ones whose discipline was most properly established.

Only when the martial training ended did the Fist Demon finally speak to me.

“Second Young Master.”

Compared to that frightening face, his voice was unexpectedly gentle. I had thought it would be a heavy, cold voice, but it was softer than expected. It was as if he were merely moving his lips while someone behind him provided the voice in his stead.

“Why do you think I acted that way yesterday?”

“Was it perhaps because I visited during martial training?”

At that, as if my answer were wrong, the Fist Demon turned around and strode away. So he wasn’t going to make this easy? I looked at his back and smiled faintly.

The next day, I went to see the Fist Demon again.

I was the one visiting him, but it was the Fist Demon who had induced my visits.

Today, I changed the timing and went before the martial training began. As I sat on the railing in front of the building where his office was located, passing Iron Fists looked at me as they went by.

Though the Fist Demon was ignoring me, none of the Iron Fists looked at me with contempt. If anything, there was a subtle goodwill and respect in their eyes. The Second Young Master from when I had just regressed and the Second Young Master now could be said to be entirely different people.

Just then, the Fist Demon came out of the building.

“Did you sleep well?”

He glanced at me, then moved on without a word. I followed after him as he headed to the grand training ground.

“I thought carefully about the reason yesterday. Is it perhaps because you were not the first among the Demon Venerables whom I came to visit?”

One way or another, I had met and formed ties with all the other Demon Venerables, so excluding the Poison King, he was the seventh.

The Fist Demon stopped walking and looked at me with his frightening face as he asked,

“Do I look like such a petty man?”

“Then perhaps I am the petty one. If it were me, I think I would be very angry.”

The Fist Demon said nothing more and simply left. Watching him grow distant, I called out.

“I will see you again tomorrow.”

But the next day, I could not see the Fist Demon.

Only the Iron Fists trained in the training ground. Instead of the Fist Demon, I watched their training to the end, then returned.

In that way, I came up empty-handed three days in a row. I could not tell whether he was deliberately avoiding me or whether he simply had business to attend to.

“So that’s how you want to play it?”

In any case, these damned Demon Venerables were all uncomfortable and difficult, every last one of them. Truly, there was not a single easy opponent.

Let’s see who wins, I thought as I turned to leave, when one of the Iron Fists passed by and quietly said to me,

“The Demon Venerable is currently at the small training ground.”

When I looked at him with an expression asking why he was telling me that, he said in a low voice,

“My family received great help from the matter recently handled by the Yellow Springs Pavilion.”

After saying that, he bowed politely and left. Telling me this must have been a burden to him, but it seemed he had been very grateful for what the Yellow Springs Pavilion had done.

—Thank you.

I sent my thanks by voice transmission to him as he walked far away.

I went straight to the small training ground.

The Fist Demon was sitting there alone, lost in contemplation. One might think contemplation would not suit such a large, rugged man, but unexpectedly, the sight of him sitting cross-legged with his eyes closed suited him surprisingly well.

Standing behind him, I spoke in a low voice. I omitted entirely the matter of having come up empty-handed for three days, as well as how I had found this place, and spoke to him as if I had seen him only yesterday.

“Is the reason you are trying to keep your distance from me perhaps because of my elder brother? Is it because you have already resolved to support him, and you do not wish to shake that conviction?”

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon opened his eyes and said,

“What grand conviction would that be, that I would treat a man coldly for such a reason? The humans who ruin the world are all those with misguided convictions.”

It was an unexpected remark. Judging by his appearance alone, he looked like the sort of man who, once he made up his mind, would uphold it until death.

“Then you could support me instead of my brother.”

At that, as if he had no intention of continuing the conversation, the Fist Demon closed his eyes again.

I bowed my head politely in farewell.

“Tomorrow, I will be sure to come back with the answer.”

I could tell. He was testing me in some way. If not, he would have told me outright not to come. In any case, it was not bad. If I kept seeing him often, I would eventually be able to find the answer to our relationship.

After leaving the East Fist Gate, I headed to the Southern Blade Sect to meet the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

Strangely enough, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was also sitting in the courtyard, lost in contemplation.

“Is today the day all the Demon Venerables contemplate as a group? Then I should do some contemplating too.”

I went and sat beside him.

With his eyes closed, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon spoke quietly.

“Since all you’ve ever done is slash with a blade and kill, you don’t know how to deal with fists, do you?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon knew exactly what situation I was in.

“How did you know?”

“How else? Rumors are everywhere that you’ve been going in and out of the East Fist Gate these days.”

“How quickly rumors spread. Loose-lipped demons, the lot of them!”

“You’ll lose all the fish you’ve caught at this rate.”

“It’s all right. I’ll just go catch them again. There are only seven, so I should at least have the fun of catching them again.”

“Why seven?”

I quietly looked at the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“Have you already caught and eaten me?”

At the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s words, I burst out laughing. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon must have found what he said amusing as well, because he laughed along with me.

“That is actually why I came to see you. What kind of person is the Fist Demon?”

“Usually, bastards who use their fists are bound to be ignorant, but the Fist Demon is smart. He is a very smart man.”

A smart Fist Demon. That was likely why the Drunken Demon had said he could not be easily defeated unless one made a considerable resolve, and why Father had said the ratio was four to three, but close.

“The fists of a smart man hurt more.”

“Do you regret it? Thinking you should have caught him before this old man?”

“I suppose I should say this first. I do not know exactly what kind of person the Fist Demon is, but this much is certain. A Blade Demon already won is worth more than ten Fist Demons!”

I did not begrudge the art of flattery when it came to the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Flattery, after all, usually brought a moment where one wondered, What am I even doing? But with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, that was not the case.

I did not worry about the Blood Heaven Blade Demon. Not because I treated him like a fish I had already caught, but because I sincerely liked him. When I first asked him to become my left wing, I had said it because I needed a Demon Venerable.

But now, it did not matter if he were not a Demon Venerable, or even if he were the keeper of our cult’s stables. It did not matter if he were someone who had no influence whatsoever on my becoming the successor. I liked the person that was the Blood Heaven Blade Demon himself. As long as that feeling did not change, I would not need to worry about my relationship with him.

“Do you know why the Cult Leader likes the Fist Demon?”

“Father likes the Fist Demon?”

“He does not say it outwardly, but among the Demon Venerables, he probably likes him the most.”

“Why does he like him?”

“Because the Fist Demon is a man mad for martial arts.”

Then nothing more needed to be said. He fit the foremost condition for Father to like him.

“And not merely mad, but purely mad.”

“And yet you say he is smart? That’s impressive.”

“Impressive, my foot. You’ve met a proper match, that’s all.”

“It is all right because I have you, Elder. Truly, if you were not here, from whom would I hear such advice, and how would I live?”

“You would live just fine. Drinking with the Drunken Demon and going around having fun with the Extreme Evil Smiling Demon.”

As expected, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was jealous of the other Demon Venerables. Even so, he was giving me information about the Fist Demon without stint.

“You cannot exactly say I am wrong, can you?”

“If I need contemplation, I will come see you again. Next time, I will bring the Fist Demon with me.”

“Don’t you dare!”

With that, I left the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s residence.

* * *

The next day, I went to see the Invincible Fist Demon again.

He was neither at the grand training ground nor at the small training ground. After searching here and there, the place where I found him was before a cliff within the East Fist Gate. I knew our cult was so vast that it had a lake and all manner of things, but I had not known there was even a cliff like this.

“Did you sleep well?”

He did not return my greeting today either. Still, it did not matter. The fact that he allowed me to freely wander around the East Fist Gate meant that he did not dislike my coming all that much.

I stood beside him and looked up at the cliff.

“I think I have finally found the answer. In exchange, please promise me one thing. That you will not get angry after hearing my answer. No, I suppose you may get angry, but promise you will not hit me with those fists.”

The Fist Demon slowly looked at me. He did not say in words that he promised, but the fact that he looked at me was enough.

“You wanted to show it to your subordinates. Not ‘I am a man who can lightly ignore even the Second Young Master,’ nor ‘I support the Eldest Young Master, so all of you should know that as well.’ What you truly wanted to say was: I have no interest whatsoever in your fight for succession. Wasn’t that it?”

Was it the correct answer? Unlike other times, he did not drive me away, and his gaze turned back toward the cliff.

I, too, no longer clung to whether it was the right answer and looked up at the cliff with him.

“I did not know there was a cliff like this inside the cult. If one climbed up there, the entire cult would be visible.”

“The purpose of this cliff is not to be climbed.”

“Then what is its purpose?”

Then an astonishing answer came out.

“This cliff is a cliff to be brought down by these fists. In a single blow!”

Because I felt that he meant it sincerely, I was startled.

“You think it is an absurd goal, do you not?”

No matter that he was the Fist Demon, bringing down this cliff in one strike was impossible. Even Father would not be able to do it.

“No. I was thinking, why have I never had such a concrete goal?”

I had never had a concrete goal when it came to martial arts. Of course, that was because Hwa Mugi had occupied that place.

It had truly been a long time since I had heard it. A martial artist speaking of a concrete goal. It stirred my heart.

“May I join you in this goal?”

“You intend to cut this cliff with a single sword strike?”

“No. I, too, want to try bringing it down with my fists.”

This time, the Fist Demon was startled. Surely he had never imagined I would give such an answer.

But right now, I meant it sincerely. If I could bring down this cliff with my fists, I felt that I would be able to defeat Hwa Mugi as well. No, setting Hwa Mugi aside, as a martial artist, I wanted to challenge it once. To the Fist Demon, who was staring at me with a face as terrifying as an evil spirit, I said something even more astonishing.

“Please teach me fist arts.”

Chapter 182: I Have a Question!
-------------------------------
The Invincible Fist Demon likely hadn’t known either, but neither had I.

That I would end up saying such a thing to the Fist Demon. But the instant I heard him say he would bring down a cliff with a single blow, something blazed up inside my heart.

It was an intense desire to throw my own fist into his impossible challenge.

The Fist Demon’s gaze, which had been fixed on my eyes, dropped to my hand.

“With that baby hand?”

Ah, so the day has come when my hand is called a baby hand.

“Compared to yours, Fist Demon, it is very small and cute, but out in the world of ordinary people, it’s considered fairly well born.”

“Stop spouting nonsense and go back.”

Exuding a chill, the Fist Demon left just like that.

I looked up at the cliff again.

If a fist capable of bringing this down in one blow came flying at me, would my protective astral qi be able to withstand it?

Strength entered my clenched fist.

* * *

That afternoon, I went to see Father and played Go with him for the first time in a while.

With each move, the clear sound of stones being placed calmed my mind. Perhaps Go with Father felt this comfortable because it resembled a martial duel. Once you stepped onto the dueling stage, or once you began a game of Go, there was no need to say anything to your opponent.

“I lost.”

In our last game, I had won by two points, but today I finally lost by six. Seeing the smile Father could not hide at the corners of his mouth, I thought there was truly no helping him. Whether in Go or martial arts, Father could not stand losing.

“When you have time, learn some Go from Strategist Sima.”

“I suppose I should.”

“Why did you come today?”

Father had already noticed. He knew I had come because I had business with him.

“Can you tell just by looking at someone?”

“Your moves were especially impatient today. Amateurs always give themselves away.”

“Amateur? You only beat me for the first time today.”

Deep down, I felt pleased. It meant Father had been paying attention to my state of mind even while playing Go.

“There is something I wish to receive your permission for, Father.”

“What is it?”

“I am thinking of learning fist arts from the Fist Demon. Please permit it.”

As if he had expected it, Father showed no particular reaction.

“If you wish to learn, then learn. Why would you need my permission?”

“Of course I should tell you before learning. And I need your permission as well.”

“What if I do not permit it?”

“Then I’ll have to work harder until you do.”

I told Father my honest feelings.

“At first, my intention was largely to make a feint to the east and strike in the west. I was testing Brother’s response by poking at the Fist Demon. But today, the Fist Demon told me that his goal was to bring down a cliff with a single blow.”

Father seemed to know of that goal. He was not surprised at all by my words.

“At those words, what collapsed was not the cliff but my wariness. I thought it was magnificent, and I came to want to bring down a cliff too.”

“That is your chronic weakness. A naive sentiment perfect for getting yourself killed.”

The very first time he had said that to me, he had called it “cheap sentiment perfect for getting yourself killed.” Now the word cheap was gone, and naive had taken its place. Father and I had grown closer by exactly that much.

“That naivety sometimes works as an advantage. There are Demon Supremes I gained because of it.”

“Gained? Do you truly think the Demon Supremes are on your side?”

Father truly trusted no one. Perhaps that very set of values was necessary for the Cult Leader who led the cult.

“I believe there is no absolute feeling in this world except a parent’s heart for their child. However, I am trying not to create circumstances that would cause the Demon Supremes’ hearts to change.”

People rarely changed their minds all of a sudden while living their lives. Most of the time, people changed because their circumstances changed.

“Do you know this? If you become entangled with the Fist Demon through martial arts, there will surely come a moment when you must face him. He is the type who absolutely cannot live with losing. Then you may end up with a result different from what you intended.”

It was advice only someone who knew the Fist Demon’s personality well could give. It meant that in trying to grow closer, I might instead make our relationship worse.

“What happens if I win?”

“His goal will become you, not the cliff.”

After a brief pause, I said to Father,

“Wouldn’t a life in which one becomes someone’s goal be worth living at least once?”

Father gazed at me for a moment, then nodded.

“With the Fist Demon, you will need to set your path with orthodox moves.”

He was telling me not to seek lucky breaks through petty tricks, but to do my utmost through proper means. Father had given his permission.

“Thank you.”

After bowing respectfully to Father and turning around, I heard him speak from behind me.

“From now on, I take white.”

“You have to win three games in a row first, don’t you?”

“That rule changed as of today.”

Well, what could I do if the Heavenly Demon himself said he would change it?

“Then I’ll have to teach my strategist Go as soon as possible.”

* * *

The next day, I went to see the Fist Demon again.

After missing training for several days, today he was watching the Iron Fists train.

His reaction to me was exactly the same as on the first day. He glanced at me, then turned his gaze back to the Iron Fists. But we were clearly standing in a different relationship from the first day.

“May I learn together beside the Iron Fists?”

The Fist Demon looked at me. His face was still fierce. To anyone else, it would have looked as though he were glaring at me as if to kill me, but I could read the emotion contained in his eyes.

Are you truly saying you wish to learn fist arts from me?

I answered in place of words with a gaze filled with resolve.

Yes.

The Fist Demon looked at me as if I were ridiculous, then soon nodded. His attitude said he would see how I played this.

“Thank you.”

I set my sword down beside me and imitated the Iron Fists’ stances as they were.

It was the first moment in my life that I learned fist arts. Even before my regression, I had never learned fist arts. Fighting after letting go of my sword? It was not easy to imagine.

The gazes of the Iron Fists brushing past me were filled with astonishment.

Since the Heavenly Demon’s Second Young Master had stepped forward to learn fist arts alongside them, they would need no side dishes at their drinking table tonight.

The Fist Demon taught me nothing.

I followed the movements of the Iron Fists. This martial art, which dozens were learning, could not possibly be the Invincible Fist Demon’s exclusive fist art. It was likely the basic fist art learned by the martial artists of the Eastern Fist Gate.

All the better. Because I knew better than anyone that even a fist art capable of toppling Mount Tai began from these basic movements.

I took the movements precisely.

I had watched their training for several days, and above all, dozens of people before my eyes were taking the same posture.

Even if someone took an incorrect posture, there were more who took the correct one. Thanks to that, I was able to assume the proper stance.

Thus the first day’s training ended, and everyone quietly dispersed. I too left the place as though I were one of them.

The next day, I also participated in the training.

The Iron Fists of the Eastern Fist Gate were divided into four groups according to skill.

The White Fists, who wore white martial uniforms; the Blue Fists, who wore blue martial uniforms; the Red Fists, who wore red martial uniforms; and the Black Fists, who wore black martial uniforms.

Those I was with now were the White Fists, who wore white uniforms. Of course, one must not look down on them just because they were White Fists. The very fact that they had been able to enter the Eastern Fist Gate meant they were not weaklings as martial artists.

Was training with the White Fists a waste of time? How could that be? On the contrary, it was better because I could build up from the basics.

My realm in martial arts was one in which I could predict dozens of variations from a single small movement.

From one basic movement of thrusting out a fist, I imagined dozens of situations and predicted the changes. The Wind God’s Four Steps and the form I was now performing combined in my mind. Since the Wind God’s Four Steps had combined splendidly with the Flying Heaven Sword Art, naturally it also achieved a fantastic harmony with fist arts.

I was absorbed in training fist arts, but the Fist Demon still seemed not to believe me. I could read it from his expression.

So what? Do you think you will come for a month? For a year?

Because he harbored such thoughts, he merely sent me a look of contempt.

But his opponent was me. The next day, I even asked a question.

“I have a question!”

Everyone was startled by my shout. Come to think of it, I had never seen anyone ask a question during training. They merely took the postures they were told to take.

So perhaps I was the first to ask one.

The Fist Demon was equally surprised. He could not have expected that I would ask a question.

“In this form, Crouching Tiger Emerges, when rotating the right foot, how much freedom is permitted for the left foot?”

Everyone had been keeping the left foot completely fixed, but in my opinion, it seemed more effective to move it slightly.

I felt agitation around me. Is this all right? No, he dares ask a question? How will the Fist Demon react? Will we be struck by lightning for no reason too? I sensed all sorts of emotions like that.

Unexpectedly, however, the Fist Demon answered without any particular resistance.

“When you have completed the final rotation, if your body does not waver, there is no need to place restrictions on the left foot.”

At the Fist Demon’s clear answer, I performed the form as he had instructed.

My left foot moved enough to lift slightly, but that was in order to link into the next form as quickly as possible. Right now, we were training each movement separately, one by one, but in actual combat, continuous movement was important.

For the first time, the Iron Fists’ gazes turned toward me. They did not yet know. What they were seeing now was not a change in a form. It was a change in the Eastern Fist Gate’s training, which had long grown rigid.

I came to the training ground the next day as well, and the day after that too.

I did not deliberately ask questions for the sake of asking. I simply asked about what I was truly curious about, and when I asked, the Iron Fists listened closely. In a way, I was asking the questions they too had wondered about but had not dared to voice. After training ended, some Iron Fists began greeting me.

When five days had passed like that, I performed the forms with the most proper posture among the Iron Fists learning martial arts there. There were even those who asked me about movements after training was over.

It was the seventh day.

Formal training had ended, but I remained alone and continued training. I was being drawn into a new world led by fist arts. Apart from the situation I was in, learning fist arts itself was enjoyable.

I felt the Fist Demon watching me, but I paid it no mind and immersed myself in the forms. I focused only on the fist I extended.

What must be placed into this fist to bring down a cliff?

At last, the Fist Demon, who had been watching, opened his mouth.

“What are you trying to do?”

“Did I not tell you that I wished to learn fist arts from you, Fist Demon?”

“Are you truly saying you will become my disciple?”

“If you will accept me.”

The Fist Demon flinched in surprise. There had never been a case of the Heavenly Demon’s son becoming the disciple of a Demon Supreme.

“Are you sincere?”

“Yes, I am sincere. I have already received Father’s permission.”

“The Cult Leader permitted it?”

“He did.”

It was the moment the Fist Demon learned that my resolve was no joke.

“Do you know what kind of person I am, and still do this?”

“I do not know well. But the moment you said your goal was to bring down a cliff, I think I fell for you, Fist Demon.”

Half sincerity, half flattery was being brought to bear.

“You think such a pathetic little ploy will work? Do you think I will support you as successor if you do this?”

“It is fine if you do not. No, please do not. I do not know if you have heard the rumors about me, but my goal is to end this successor selection without bloodshed between brothers. That is why I feel reassured that you are with my brother. Because you will stop him from crossing the line.”

“Why do you think I would stop him?”

“Because you are someone who tries to bring down a cliff with your bare fist. Because you know well that the moment filthy schemes or plots are held in that fist, you will never be able to bring down the cliff.”

“!”

“I believe you are the only one who can beat sense into either my brother or me if we go mad and run wild over the successor struggle. If I, or my brother, try to cross the line, please stop us. Strike us mercilessly with that fist.”

I did not avoid that displeased and fearsome gaze directed at me. You are a man mad for martial arts? When it comes to going mad over something and digging down one path, I will not lose to you.

The next instant!

The Fist Demon’s great fist was before my face.

He had thrust his fist this quickly, yet there had not been a single sound of wind. As though the fist had been here from the very beginning, it was simply in front of my face.

The sound erupted afterward.

Whoooooooosh!

Just as an enormous rush of wind seemed to sound from behind me—

Kwaaaaang!

A huge hole had been punched through the wall far behind me.

Astonishingly, while leaving my face intact where his fist had stopped, he had completely blown away what was behind me. It was truly an astounding and lofty technique.

The Fist Demon coldly stared at me, who had not so much as blinked at the fist that flew in, then turned away without a word and left.

Standing there, I watched his back as he walked far away.

The more you act like this, the fiercer the flame in my heart will burn.

At the end of the road, the Fist Demon stopped.

“From tomorrow on, come to the Blue Fist training ground.”

Chapter 183: When a Rift Forms Between People.
----------------------------------------------
The next day, I went to the place where the Blue Fists trained.

Dressed in blue martial robes, they were clearly different from the White Fists. They possessed the composure that came from a gap in skill.

Since the Fist Demon had yet to arrive, I exchanged greetings with them comfortably. Naturally, they already knew that I had trained with the White Fists.

One of them asked me,

"Have you learned everything you need to learn from the White Fists? I heard you've only been here about seven or eight days?"

"How long did it take you?"

"About two years. If you didn't learn properly from the White Fists, Blue Fist training won't be easy."

He seemed to be warning me out of concern, but in truth, he was revealing his displeasure. We suffered for two years, yet you passed in just a few days. He was subtly expressing his dissatisfaction, questioning whether I was benefiting from my status as the Second Young Lord—a matter of status rather than skill.

I didn't bother making excuses to him. There would be others with similar grievances anyway, and this wasn't something that could be resolved with words.

Shortly after, the Fist Demon appeared. I learned after coming here that he personally guided martial arts training from the White Fists all the way to the Black Fists. While other Demon Lords only guided their direct disciples, leaving their subordinates to be taught by those disciples, the Fist Demon personally looked after all his subordinates.

"I believe that personally guiding all the Iron Fists is truly something worthy of respect."

I spoke loudly. Everyone probably thought I was sucking up, but I meant every word. No one asked questions in this atmosphere, and the Fist Demon had probably never heard such words before. That's why I said it.

The Fist Demon's gaze turned toward me. Looking at his fierce, stony face, I spoke even louder.

"Magnificent!"

Better to say it once in front of everyone than a hundred times when we're alone. At least in this regard, it wasn't the Divine Art of Bootlicking. He deserved the praise. It truly wasn't easy for a Demon Lord to look after subordinates so far beneath him.

Nevertheless, the Fist Demon's eyes said this:

—Your tricks won't work.

My eyes answered thus:

—Then they will work, since it isn't a trick.

Once formal training began, the Blue Fists all removed their tops.

From the Blue Fists onward, they trained with their upper bodies bare. This was to scrutinize every movement of their muscles.

Everyone had stripped their tops, and their gazes turned to me. Their eyes said, "Let's see the body of our pale Second Young Lord."

I too slowly removed my clothes. When they saw my body, everyone was startled.

Battle-hardened muscles forged through training, and countless scars. If they knew who I had received each one from, even the Fist Demon would have been appalled.

I smiled brightly and said to everyone,

"I came here so I wouldn't get hit anymore."

A clear sneer brushed the Fist Demon's lips. He still regarded me with doubt, waiting to see how long I would last.

Formal training began.

This time as well, the Fist Demon didn't teach a single movement, so I watched them perform and followed suit. From the forms these practitioners learned, breathing techniques were also important, and I mastered those breathing techniques as well. Using the Divine Eye Technique, I carefully observed their noses, throats, bellies, and mouths to figure out the breathing method.

And I asked the Fist Demon to verify it.

"Please check if this breathing technique is correct."

"From whom did you learn the breathing technique?"

"I learned it by watching the martial arts being performed just now."

The Blue Fists looked as if none of them believed it.

But the Fist Demon would believe it. He was definitely reading my energy. How was he evaluating my skill?

Father's words came to mind. He had said that if I became entangled with the Fist Demon through martial arts, we would inevitably clash.

Looking at him, I raised my hand high and spoke,

"I have another question!"

* * *

The next day, and the day after that, I trained diligently.

The somewhat hostile gazes of the Blue Fists toward me had vanished before I knew it. I worked hard, I performed better than anyone, and above all, I drew out a new side of the Fist Demon.

"How should this form be utilized in actual combat?"

Strictly speaking, this question wasn't for the Blue Fists, but for the Fist Demon.

Surely the Fist Demon had wanted to tell his subordinates about the practical combat applications of the forms. If it were me, I would have wanted to. Wouldn't the Fist Demon feel the same?

Indeed, the Fist Demon explained in such detail that one wondered what would have happened if I hadn't asked. The Blue Fists also looked surprised—they probably hadn't expected the Fist Demon to explain so well.

"Is there a special mindset for not fearing swords?"

I asked questions about such psychological aspects.

"Why fist arts?"

I even asked fundamental questions.

In truth, this was a question that should be continually asked from the White Fists to the Black Fists, and even by the Fist Demon himself.

At the same time as asking that question, I naturally asked myself,

'Why the sword?'

How one sought the answer to this question would determine how far that person could grow.

Five days after beginning Blue Fist training.

In front of the Blue Fist martial artists watching, the Fist Demon spoke to me.

"Come out for Red Fist training starting tomorrow."

"Yes."

None of the Blue Fists harbored dissatisfaction with this decision. In just a few days, I had perfectly executed the forms they had learned.

So much so that the man who had spoken to me on the first day came to apologize.

"Please forgive my rudeness on the first day."

"Anyone would have thought the same. I'm sure there will be someone in the Red Fists who says the same thing."

"That person too will come to apologize like me in a few days. Thank you for opening the eyes of us foolish and lacking ones."

All the Blue Fist martial artists standing behind him lightly bowed their heads to me. I also bowed my head to them in return. Just being able to see this new side of the Fist Demon must have been a deeply meaningful time for them.

* * *

"I heard Geom Mugeuk is training under the Fist Demon."

Geom Muyang was keeping a sharp eye on his younger brother's movements.

"They say he broke through Blue Fist training in just over ten days and started training with the Red Fists a few days ago."

Mabul, sitting across from him, answered.

"Given the Second Young Lord's martial arts skill, that's entirely possible."

Mabul knew what the First Young Lord was worried about. He must be concerned that the Second Young Lord might actually become the Fist Demon's disciple.

"There's no need to worry about the Fist Demon. If the Second Young Lord tries to pull him in, it'll only backfire."

At that, Geom Muyang recalled past events.

"Do you remember what you once told me?"

"What did I say?"

"Chwima is an unpredictable person. Once he starts stirring things up, even the Second Young Lord won't be able to handle him easily. The Second Young Lord will lose the Demon Lords he has carefully gathered."

These were words Mabul had spoken to Geom Muyang when he received the broken liquor bottle from Chwima.

But the news that eventually came was that Geom Mugeuk had been drinking and merrymaking with Chwima.

"Please don't take it the wrong way. I only mention it because I can't ignore what happened with Chwima."

He had told him not to take it badly, but Mabul felt quite displeased. Was it really necessary to bring up his own words so accurately? Didn't that mean he had been holding onto that incident all this time?

Was it true that once a crack formed between people, it could never be repaired?

At first, he had deliberately ignored the crack, been careful not to let it widen further, and eventually convinced himself that as long as it didn't break completely, it would be fine.

But Mabul was beginning to realize.

That in human relationships, the important thing wasn't whether the crack shattered or not. The very existence of the crack was the problem.

If the other party hadn't been Geom Muyang, this relationship would have already been ended one way or another. But he couldn't do that. Because Geom Muyang was the path he had chosen. Because he had staked his life on him.

Geom Muyang spoke to Mabul.

"Would you meet the Fist Demon and find out what he thinks?"

"Yes, I shall meet with him."

Mabul readily accepted the request.

As Mabul rose from his seat, he looked back at Geom Muyang. He wanted to tell him something. That it was okay to show his anxiety. That he could tell him everything. That his younger brother was too strong to handle. That he would feel a little better if he got it all out.

But those words were stuck in his throat and wouldn't come out.

"Is something wrong? Do you have something to say?"

"No. I'll be back."

Leaving the office, Mabul reproached himself.

If the First Young Lord couldn't do it, then he should have spoken frankly... It wasn't Geom Muyang's fault.

* * *

The next day, Mabul's steps halted as he entered the East Fist Gate.

He saw Geom Mugeuk together with the Red Fist martial artists.

"Spread your legs wider. Lower your arms."

Geom Mugeuk was checking their movements. He knew the boy wasn't ordinary, but had it only been a few days since he started acting so friendly with the Red Fist martial artists?

Then Mabul saw it. Geom Mugeuk's eyes and expression. He was talking with the Red Fists with the passion of someone truly immersed in fist arts.

Yes, that's how it is. That's how one gets drawn in. That strange heat that could have made even him fall for it.

Then Geom Mugeuk saw Mabul and ran over to greet him.

"It's been a while. How have you been?"

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly. How wonderful it would be if Geom Muyang smiled at him like that?

'No. Stop thinking such needless thoughts. He smiles like that because I'm the enemy.'

Don't people smile brightly at complete strangers while frowning at their own people when things are hard? I mustn't be deceived by that smile. The First Young Lord gets annoyed because he's on our side, and the Second Young Lord smiles because he's the enemy.

"Rumors say you've been quite immersed in fist arts lately."

"It's because serving as the Lord of Yellow Heaven Pavilion makes me want to punch too many people."

"Now, now, let's not put on airs between us. You've come to win the Fist Demon over to your side, haven't you?"

"The phrase 'between us' sounds truly wonderful."

My ears kept melting at that silver tongue so obviously weaving words to please me.

"Stop putting on a show and go swing those cotton fists of yours."

After deliberately responding coldly, Mabul headed toward the Fist Demon's office. From behind, Geom Mugeuk's shout reached him.

"The Fist Demon said his hands are like a baby's, but are yours cotton fists, Lord Mabul?"

* * *

"You're a busy man, so I'll get straight to the point. I came today because of the Second Young Lord."

As if he had expected as much, the Fist Demon waited silently for Mabul's next words. The contrast between the tall, bulky Fist Demon and Mabul—who was much smaller than an average person—was truly stark.

"The Second Young Lord's desire to learn fist arts is a ploy to pull you to his side."

In truth, Mabul hadn't been particularly close to the Fist Demon until now, but he harbored a sense of internal kinship. He considered his short stature and the Fist Demon's frightening face to be equals. He had thought of them as similar physical weaknesses in that people frowned when they saw them.

Of course, he had never revealed such private thoughts to anyone.

"So? You came here because you're afraid I'll fall for the Second Young Lord?"

"Exactly. I was worried about that. Blood Sky Saber Demon fell for him. One Flower Sword Ancestor fell for him. Even Extreme Evil Lesser Demon and Chwima."

Mabul spoke honestly. Beating around the bush might cause trouble later. He had to tell him like this.

"The Second Young Lord is a fox. A fox with a hundred tails. His silver tongue is sweeter than a sycophant's. If you let your guard down, you'll find yourself having taken him as your disciple before you know it."

The Fist Demon stared intently at Mabul and suddenly asked,

"Did you almost fall for him too?"

Mabul inwardly flinched for a moment but spoke confidently.

"From the beginning until now, and in the future as well, I am on the First Young Lord's side."

"The Second Young Lord does so too. He told me to protect the First Young Lord."

Mabul was startled. He had been told something similar before.

"He said he would end the succession struggle without bloodshed."

"Do you believe those words?"

"Do you?"

Mabul gave no answer. It was a moment where he should have said he didn't believe it, but those words wouldn't come out. He had come to give the Fist Demon confidence, yet he was only confirming that he himself had been shaken by Geom Mugeuk.

"Go and tell the First Young Lord. Tell him my resolve to support him has not changed."

He couldn't tell what the Fist Demon truly felt, but Mabul thought giving such a warning was enough.

"The First Young Lord will be greatly pleased."

With that, Mabul left the office.

On the way back, he saw Geom Mugeuk again. Far away in the training ground, he was still honing fist arts with the Red Fists. Even from a distance, one could feel that he was training with his whole heart immersed in fist arts. Just like when Mabul himself had been consumed by martial arts in his youth.

He had thought Geom Mugeuk impressive many times before, but today's feeling was slightly different.

It was probably because of the Red Fists training with him. Have you already been deceived too? How long have you even known him, and what do you like so much that you're swinging fists together?

Suddenly, Geom Mugeuk became terrifying to Mabul.

Doubts surged forth like a demon. What if that scheme to sway people's hearts was sincere? What if, in truth, I was the fool and he was the one employing schemes? What if I had chosen the wrong side?

But soon Mabul shook his head. He had to push himself harder precisely because of this. Emitting a golden radiance, he strode forward vigorously. Though he was confused, pushed back, and cracked.

"First Young Lord, let's fight on."

Chapter 184: What Kind of Person the Fist Demon Is.
---------------------------------------------------
“You seem to be in good spirits.”

At Geom Muyang’s words, Mabul smiled and replied.

“Perhaps it’s because I’ve brought good news.”

Mabul was acting brighter and more energetic than usual.

“I don’t think you need to worry about the Fist Demon. He made it clear that he supports you, Young Master.”

Though it was good news, Geom Muyang’s expression did not brighten all at once.

“Eldest Young Master, allow me to speak frankly. I know there have been things recently that disappointed you in me. In my own way, I tried to do well, but there were matters that did not go as intended. For that, I apologize to you.”

Without the slightest pretense, Mabul apologized sincerely. It was an extension of the emotions he had felt earlier while watching Geom Mugeuk train in martial arts.

He had felt that if things continued like this, they would not be able to defeat Geom Mugeuk. Change was necessary, and he had resolved that the change had to begin with himself.

“No. The one who should apologize is me.”

From Geom Muyang’s reaction, Mabul could tell. Geom Muyang was flustered, and he was doubting his words.

Geom Muyang was the sort of person who looked first at what lay behind another person. He was the sort of person who filtered others’ words at least once before accepting them. Even now, he was doubting Mabul’s sincerity.

Had this been yesterday, Mabul would have grown angry at this reaction. He would have thought of the crack that had formed between himself and Geom Muyang.

But he did not. Now, he intended to acknowledge it. This was the essence of the man called Geom Muyang. If he disliked it, then he was the one who had to leave.

Whether he knew Mabul’s thoughts or not, Geom Muyang spoke calmly.

“When I returned to the Cult, I think I was flustered because Mugeuk had grown far more than I expected. The Mugeuk I knew was a young wolf, but when I came back, he had become a great tiger. In my impatience, I was rude to you several times, Mabul. I apologize.”

Geom Muyang bowed his head.

Mabul’s heart eased. This much was enough.

He decided to think of the crack between Geom Muyang and himself not as a fissure that might break apart and send them plummeting, but as a mark for a better relationship in the future. Rather, it would be a crack he could look at whenever he felt hurt or weary, and from which he could draw strength.

“You are a tiger as well, Eldest Young Master. So do not be afraid. Roar to your heart’s content. I will protect you.”

“Thank you.”

Mabul was finding his strength.

Yes, when we approach someone, this is how it must be done. Just as Geom Mugeuk spoke to him without hesitation, he too had to stride toward Geom Muyang like this. Only then, no matter how this matter ended, would he have no regrets.

Mabul’s gaze turned toward the window.

“The moonlight is especially beautiful tonight.”

But Geom Muyang’s eyes could not enjoy that beautiful moonlight at all.

‘This won’t do.’

As he looked at Mabul, whose eyes had grown moist in the moonlight, he thought that this man could not defeat Geom Mugeuk.

‘If you truly care for me, you should not be praising me as a tiger. You should be bringing me a trap to catch the tiger over there.’

Even if they used every method available, it would still not be enough. Even if it meant overreaching, even if it meant schemes, they had to employ everything—yet Mabul was indulging in such leisure.

It was because he was not as desperate as Geom Muyang. After all, if they lost the succession struggle, the only one who would die was Geom Muyang himself.

Because the two men’s hearts were different, the moonlight tonight was different as well. And so that moonlight, soft and warm yet cold and lonely, shone upon them both.

* * *

In the training ground where moonlight fell, I was thrusting my fists alone.

Through my training thus far, I had perfectly mastered even the forms that the Red Fists were learning. They found them difficult, but to me, they still felt as easy as the basics of fist arts. But the basics had to be solid, so from the very first moment I began training, I had invested all my time in practicing fist arts.

At that moment, the Fist Demon walked over.

When shadows fell across his face with the moon at his back, the Fist Demon truly looked like an evil spirit.

“Are you not afraid of me?”

“How could I not be afraid after seeing that face?”

“You are not merely unafraid. You look down on me.”

“Do you like it when others see you as frightening? It must be something you’ve endured your whole life.”

The Fist Demon walked over slowly until he stood right in front of me.

He thrust his face toward mine. This was the first time I had seen his face from such a close distance. It truly was a face that seemed as though a devil had been born into the mortal world.

“How is it, seeing it up close?”

For the first time, as I looked at the face of a Demon Venerable, I felt this.

“You look tired.”

“!”

“What on earth is making a Demon Venerable of the Heavenly Demon Divine Cult so tired? Is it the Iron Fists? Or that cliff? Or is there something else?”

For an instant, the Fist Demon’s eyes trembled slightly. No one had likely ever asked him so directly before. Not only his fellow Demon Venerables, but even Father did not treat the Demon Venerables carelessly.

“It seems you’ve seduced all the Demon Venerables in this manner, but it won’t work on me.”

It seemed my comment that he looked tired had touched his emotions.

“As I told you, I never hoped for that in the first place. What I want is something else.”

“What is it?”

“Please teach me fist arts. Learning fist arts is so enjoyable. It makes my heart race and fills me with excitement, to the point that I cannot sleep.”

Those were words from the heart. Perhaps it was because I had trained in sword arts all my life. Perhaps it was because I had achieved great completion in the Soaring Heaven Sword Art at the twelfth stage. Perhaps it was because I had achieved great completion in the Wind God’s Four Steps. At precisely this time, fist arts had come to me like fate.

“Please teach me. I want to fight you, Fist Demon, with the fist arts you teach me!”

“!”

“I want to defeat you with those fist arts.”

In that instant, waves of energy poured from the Fist Demon’s body. The Fist Demon’s aura was like the wind. It was the kind of aura that made it impossible to open one’s eyes in the fierce gale, that filled the mind with blank terror as though one might be blown away somewhere.

The Heavenly Demon Protective Divine Art activated naturally, and I drew up my protective astral qi to withstand his aura.

Then the Fist Demon’s aura grew even stronger. A wind blew that seemed as though it would sweep away not only my body, but even my soul.

I injected internal energy into my clenched fist and executed a single form.

It was a form I had learned from the Red Fists’ training: One Fist Bearing Heaven.

Paaang!

From the empty air where my fist struck, a refreshing burst of wind rang out.

Staring into the Fist Demon’s eyes, I said,

“You have awakened something inside my body, Fist Demon.”

Even without my saying it, the Fist Demon would know what that something was.

Withdrawing the aura that had been pressing down on me, the Fist Demon asked,

“Why have you only come to me now?”

Among the eight Demon Venerables, he was the seventh connection. It was a question he had every right to wonder about.

“Was I not someone you needed that much?”

“It is the opposite.”

“The opposite?”

“I heard that you, Fist Demon, play the role of two Demon Venerables. That is why I wanted to meet you when I had grown a little stronger.”

“Who said I play the role of two people?”

“Father did.”

Father had said that although the number of Demon Venerables stood at four to three, the balance remained even. Through the Drunken Demon, I had confirmed that he had said it with the Fist Demon in mind.

Father certainly had influence over the Demon Venerables. When Father was mentioned, the Fist Demon too revealed somewhat complicated emotions.

“I cannot believe the Cult Leader said such a thing. Do not overreach in order to draw me to your side.”

“Go and confirm it for yourself.”

The Fist Demon’s face became even more frightening. I could guarantee that if I went out into the Central Plains with that face beside me, every day would bring new incidents, accidents, and a continuous string of entertaining events.

Glaring at me fearsomely, he asked,

“Were you sincere when you said you might become my disciple?”

“I was sincere. This is your only chance to become the master of the Heavenly Demon. Do not miss it.”

“What will you do if I try to manipulate my disciple as I please once he becomes the Heavenly Demon?”

At last, I too released my aura. It was the first aura I had revealed to the Fist Demon.

An endlessly spreading, vast blue sky.

Clear water reflecting that sky. Water that looked shallow, until one dipped a foot into it and realized it was an abyss whose bottom could not be seen. Extending that suffocating fear, I spoke with solemn dignity.

“Is your Heavenly Demon truly such a petty person?”

The Fist Demon looked at me in astonishment.

For a long while, we stared at each other in silence. It was the Fist Demon who broke the long silence.

“Hold out your hand. Hold out that baby hand of yours.”

I held out my hand.

“Make a fist.”

I slowly clenched my hand into a fist.

A strange light flashed through the Fist Demon’s eyes as he examined my fist. Yes, it would not be a hand one could easily see. This hand, which you call a baby hand, was none other than the hand of a Heavenly Martial Body bestowed by the heavens.

To become an expert, the entire body is important, but the hands are especially important. In fist arts, they are even more so. One cannot hide the weaknesses of the body behind weapons.

He wrapped my hand in his enormous hand. Hand met hand, and they explored each other.

At length, without saying anything about my hand, the Fist Demon turned and walked away.

“Come to the Black Fist training starting tomorrow.”

* * *

After leaving the East Fist Gate, I went straight to the Valley of Villains.

It was to ask the Extreme Evil Soma about the Fist Demon. For some reason, I felt that the Extreme Evil Soma would know the Fist Demon well.

As always, the Extreme Evil Soma was sitting silently in that room that was entirely white.

The first thing he said upon seeing me was this.

“Second Young Master, you want to fight the Fist Demon.”

I was startled.

“Have you mastered a martial art that reads minds? Or have we become close enough to understand each other just by looking into each other’s eyes?”

“You are wearing a mask today, are you not?”

“If I am wearing a mask, does that mean I want to fight the Fist Demon?”

“Yes. Then it means you want to fight the Fist Demon.”

Smiling, I lifted the mask up onto my head.

“What if I came like this?”

“Then it would mean you came because you wanted to play with me.”

At least today, I had not come to play, so I lowered the mask again.

“I might deceive ghosts, but I cannot deceive you, Soma.”

I nodded and admitted it honestly.

“Do you miss blood?”

“It seems I do. Since I miss blood, you were the first person who came to mind, Soma.”

“When I heard that you were learning martial arts from the Fist Demon, I knew you would come to me at least once.”

The Extreme Evil Soma and the Fist Demon were utterly different people. Their feeling, their atmosphere, their personalities—everything.

But if one were asked to classify the Demon Venerables without being given any standard, the Extreme Evil Soma and the Fist Demon seemed likely to end up in the same place.

“Do you know why the Fist Demon supports the Eldest Young Master?”

“Because I went to him late?”

The Extreme Evil Soma shook his head.

“Because he supports succession by the eldest son?”

“That likely is not it either.”

“Then why does he support my elder brother?”

Then an answer I had not expected came.

“Because of the Cult Leader, I believe.”

“Because of Father?”

“Until recently, before you began to distinguish yourself, everyone expected that the Eldest Young Master would become the successor. The Cult Leader himself likely had his mind set that way at the time as well.”

“Does that mean the Fist Demon is following Father’s will?”

“That is correct. He likely believes the Cult Leader wants the Eldest Young Master as his successor. He does not know that the situation has changed considerably these days.”

“Because he has been focused only on teaching his subordinates.”

“Exactly. If he learns that the Cult Leader has you in mind, Second Young Master, he will change his mind.”

The Drunken Demon had said that Father would like the Fist Demon, who was purely mad for martial arts, the most. Perhaps the person who liked Father the most was also the Fist Demon.

The Extreme Evil Soma gave me one last piece of advice regarding the Fist Demon.

“The Fist Demon’s title is Undefeated. He is a man who has never once lost. You will learn what kind of person the Fist Demon truly is when he is defeated.”

I nodded silently. Perhaps the person who would deliver that first defeat would be me.

The Extreme Evil Soma read the heat within me.

“Second Young Master, why are you trying to become so strong?”

He knew precisely that the ultimate reason I was trying to learn fist arts from the Fist Demon was not to gain a Demon Venerable, but because of my desire to grow stronger.

“Are you perhaps dreaming of unifying all under heaven?”

“No.”

I spoke firmly.

“Do you wish to become the greatest under heaven?”

“Not that either.”

“Then why are you running forward so breathlessly?”

“Because I have a feeling that one day, a powerful enemy will appear before me. An enemy I will never be able to defeat unless I strive like a madman. It seems I have that sort of anxiety.”

I revealed my honest unease to him. Strangely, it was comfortable to reveal such feelings to the Extreme Evil Soma.

The Extreme Evil Soma, who had been quietly looking at me, suddenly released finger wind.

Ziiiiiiik.

A long line was drawn on the white wall he always gazed at, left behind by the finger wind. I was startled. He had drawn a line on a wall that seemed as though he would never damage it.

“Second Young Master, how far have we come?”

For the first time in a very long while, he drew a line and asked me. The last time he had drawn one, I had said we had reached the line of survival.

I drew the Black Demon Sword and, passing the line of survival he had drawn before, marked a vertical stroke at the very end of the line.

“I have come this far.”

It was not as though I had spent a great deal of time with him. But the two life-and-death battles in which we had stood back to back had made me draw the line here.

“Do you remember? When we first met, I told you that I do not become friends with people who wear masks. Now my principle has changed. Since I am wearing a mask too, I will be friends with people who wear masks as well.”

The Extreme Evil Soma said to me with clear eyes,

“You said it, Second Young Master. That if we reached that line, we would become friends and eat together, drink together, enjoy ourselves together, fight together. If one fell into danger, the other would go to save him. And if one of us died first, the other would pour wine at his grave.”

“That’s right.”

“If an enemy like the one you mentioned earlier appears, and you, Second Young Master, fall into danger…”

After a brief pause, the Extreme Evil Soma added,

“I will go to save you.”

“!”

I could tell. The Extreme Evil Soma’s line had been drawn in the same place as mine.

Father always says this. Do not trust people. That such feelings are cheap sentimentality that will get you killed. That they are naive. Even so, I liked this moment too much.

“I will be waiting for you to save me.”

The Extreme Evil Soma’s eyes shone clearly. No more words were needed.

As I turned to leave, I asked the Extreme Evil Soma worriedly,

“By the way, will that wall be all right? It’s a wall for your training, isn’t it?”

He had said that he had to train while looking at the white wall in order to gain more internal energy. Worried about what he would do now that he had damaged it, I asked, and the Extreme Evil Soma looked at the white wall on the opposite side and said,

“I did not like the color of that wall. Starting today, I will look at this one.”

Chapter 185: You Still Don't Know How Frightening a Sword Is.
-------------------------------------------------------------
The next day, I went to the Black Fist training ground.

Naturally, I had assumed the Black Fists, being the most skilled, would have the smallest numbers.

But when I arrived, the Black Fists were the most numerous. This was the result of the Fist Demon’s efforts. He had continuously unearthed those worthy of becoming Black Fists, forging them from the very basics.

These were ones who could truly be called iron fists. Capable individuals who could easily serve as squad leaders or commanders in the Main Sect’s elite organizations were visible everywhere.

The gazes directed at me were varied—from those looking on with curiosity to those showing hostility. The reason for the hostility was simple. These were Black Fists who had climbed up through training, ranging from as short as seven or eight years to as long as over ten.

And yet, I had broken through three ranks and reached Black Fist in just over ten days. How could they feel good about that? There were likely some among them thinking that the Second Young Master might be aiming to become the Fist Demon.

Among them, one person caught my eye.

A woman with her hair tied tight and bandages wrapped around her hands and wrists. A woman whose fierce, captivating gaze was her greatest charm.

I knew who she was.

Cheon Sohui.

She was the next Fist Demon.

With that slender frame and those small fists, she was the woman who would overcome the competition against every Black Fist here and inherit the seat of the Fist Demon.

After the Main Sect was sealed by Hwamugi, the Fist Demon would pass the Demon Seat to her and go into seclusion.

The Fist Demon ultimately failed to shatter the cliff. Though he had devoted his entire life to martial arts, he could not reach the realm of shattering the cliff with a single fist.

I thought that had he reached a realm capable of shattering the cliff, perhaps he would have gone to find Hwamugi for revenge. I had not known it then, but looking back now, I was certain of it.

Because I had regressed, everyone’s fate and relationships were changing.

Thus, I could not evaluate these people based on past standards. I had to see them through new eyes and new criteria.

That was true even for Hyeolcheon Doma and Geukak Soma. If I died at Hwamugi’s hands, would those two simply stand by? I had a premonition that they would not.

“I heard that the Second Young Master passed through the Red Fists with ease.”

The man who approached and spoke to me was Dogang. Just as I knew Cheon Sohui, I also knew this man.

Dogang was someone who had competed with Cheon Sohui over the position of successor until the very end, then left the sect after losing.

“May I ask one question on behalf of the Black Fists?”

“Ask as many as you wish.”

“Why have you come to the Eastern Fist Gate? Why do you wish to learn fist arts?”

“If you are worried that I might take the position you have devoted your lives to obtaining, you need not be. I have not come to become the Fist Demon.”

I spoke to them calmly.

“I am the one who will become your Heavenly Demon.”

Because of what I had accomplished since regressing, no one showed any sign of looking down on me. If they considered the possibility that I might become the Heavenly Demon, it would not be easy to speak carelessly.

But neither was there anyone who went out of their way to ingratiate themselves with me. For now, the Eastern Fist Gate was still a place that leaned toward the Eldest Successor.

“By the time I become the Heavenly Demon, one among you will have become the Fist Demon. I ask for your cooperation in advance.”

Then Dogang asked again.

“If you are to become the Heavenly Demon, why have you come here?”

“I have come because I wish to grow stronger. I intend to grow stronger by learning fist arts.”

It was also a statement that would bolster the pride of these iron fists, for I was saying I would grow stronger by learning the fist arts they studied.

Among the gazes fixed on me was Cheon Sohui’s. Her extraordinary presence was one thing, but I particularly liked the look in her eyes. Those eyes, filled with a will that seemed unwilling to yield once a goal was set, resembled the Fist Demon.

*I look forward to working with you, future Fist Demon!*

Just then, the Fist Demon appeared. As with the previous training sessions, he did not pay me any special attention.

“Let us begin.”

Training began at the Fist Demon’s word.

The Black Fists also trained centered on form training. The Fist Demon was someone who emphasized fundamentals, valuing the execution of precise forms above all else.

I examined the Black Fists’ movements in detail.

On the first day, I only observed. I could have demonstrated the forms if necessary, but I did not think that would be a very good choice for the Black Fists or for myself.

And so the first day, spent only observing, passed.

The next day as well, I only watched.

The Black Fists displayed an air of pride, as if to say, *See, we’re different from the Red Fists, aren’t we?*

The Fist Demon sent me a voice transmission.

*—You’ve already mastered them all, so why aren’t you following along?*

*—Even if that were true for the Red Fists, I must look after the Black Fists’ pride and morale.*

*—Since when do you concern yourself with such things?*

*—Because they are the people who will become my subordinates in the future. I must look after them.*

From afar, I saw the Fist Demon snort in amusement.

* * *

On the third day, after training finished and everyone had dispersed, Cheon Sohui approached Geom Mugeuk.

When Geom Mugeuk had said on the first day that he would become their Heavenly Demon, something had welled up in her chest.

She had staked her life on martial arts and abandoned romantic affairs entirely. Thus, it was not affection between man and woman. There was some other fateful pull. She had come to find Geom Mugeuk to confirm what it was.

“I am Cheon Sohui.”

“It is a pleasure.”

“I heard that you mastered the Red Fists’ forms merely by watching their training.”

“That is correct.”

“You won’t be able to learn the Black Fists’ forms through observation alone.”

“They are certainly difficult beyond comparison.”

“Then why don’t you ask to be taught?”

To that, Geom Mugeuk gave an unexpected answer.

“I have already learned them all.”

He might not have said so to another, but he spoke honestly to Cheon Sohui. It was because he wished to share a different kind of connection with her.

Cheon Sohui started in surprise, but soon a faint sneer crossed her lips. She was absolutely convinced that they could not be mastered by sight alone.

The words, *Needless pride only makes a person look small,* nearly slipped out.

“I’m curious. Whether you truly have such keen eyes, or if you are merely putting on airs.”

She had phrased it kindly. After all, if he was putting on airs, such advice would not get through, and if by chance she was wrong, it would be her own mistake.

“Then I shall take my leave.”

She had walked a few steps when Geom Mugeuk called out from behind.

“If my words are true, grant me one request.”

She turned around and asked.

“And if it is a bluff?”

“Then I shall grant yours.”

“Very well.”

Seeing that the word *very well* came without hesitation, she was clearly convinced it was empty boasting.

Cheon Sohui left, pondering what request to make. At the same time, she walked away disappointed that it was not some fated pull after all.

* * *

Her disappointment lasted but a day.

The next day, I demonstrated the forms the Black Fists had learned right before everyone’s eyes. When I executed the forms precisely, all were astonished. Cheon Sohui’s surprise was the greatest of all.

*—You... did you really learn them just by watching?*

Her voice transmission, incredulous, flew to me.

*—Did I not tell you so?*

Her pupils trembled. How could she know? That I was someone who had passed the Lesser Heavenly Cave—a feat that took an average of three years, and even her father two months—in a single day. It was not even the Fist Demon’s secret arts; fist arts of the level the Black Fists learned, I could execute merely by watching.

*—Do not forget, you must grant my request.*

*—Of course. I always keep my promises.*

The Fist Demon called me forward.

“Step forward.”

For the first time, the Fist Demon stood me before the iron fists.

“Do you know why I permitted you to train?”

“What is the reason?”

At that, the Fist Demon’s gaze turned toward the Black Fists.

“These children have never fought a true master. Would you show them what the martial world is like?”

Surprisingly, he was asking me to let them experience real combat.

Of course, being Black Fists, they would have had various experiences. Like the other Demon Seats, the Fist Demon would have tasks to perform for the sect, so these Black Fists would mainly have been sent to the Central Plains to handle matters. In that process, they would have experienced real combat several times.

But they had likely never met a true master.

My eyes met the Fist Demon’s. He was sincerely looking out for his subordinates. That assessment was correct. The Fist Demon was smarter than he appeared. The Fist Demon was someone purely obsessed with martial arts.

“They still don’t know to fear the blade.”

It was a difficult thing to hear from the undefeated Fist Demon, but they were words for subordinates swollen with pride. For him to show this much concern…

“Then I shall enlighten them.”

When I readily agreed, the Fist Demon’s expression brightened.

“Shall I select some twenty of them? Or ten?”

He seemed sorry to ask me to let all dozens of them have that experience. This was indeed a difficult request to make lightly, and it would greatly exhaust the stamina of the one accepting it.

“No. Since I am doing this anyway, I will face them all.”

Joy and gratitude flickered across the face of that fearsome Fist Demon. They were emotions that truly did not suit him and looked awkward; it was a sight rarely seen. The tuition fee was more than paid by witnessing that precious expression.

When I looked around at the Black Fists, my presence had already grown cold.

“There are several types of experts. Adequate experts, strong experts, and true experts. Let us start with what it’s like to face an adequate expert. Now, come out one by one.”

The Black Fists came forward one by one. Their faces were filled with confidence. Though they might not defeat me, there was a will not to lose easily.

I sent a voice transmission to the Fist Demon.

*—They are ones you have taught. Is this alright?*

*—I shall make them into a rabble. That was my intention in creating this gathering today.*

I faced them one by one.

“The opponent you just faced possessed the skill to defeat you in thirty moves.”

The opposing Black Fist revealed a resolve that said, *As if that is for you to decide.* He likely wanted to teach me a lesson. He would have wanted to show this uninvited guest the taste of a proper fist, and to do so before the Fist Demon himself.

Because I understood that feeling, I faced him without crushing his pride. Even in victory, I tried not to trample his self-respect.

The first Black Fist charged swiftly, throwing his fists forward. His fundamentals were solid and his confidence high. His footwork was especially good. Martial artists who trained in fist arts were naturally supposed to excel in footwork.

After exactly thirty moves, the first Black Fist bled from his side. It was not a deep cut—just light enough to draw blood.

“I have learned well.”

He cupped his fists respectfully and withdrew. Though he had bled, his expression was one of satisfaction at real combat after so long. Given who his opponent was, how could the exchange not feel substantial?

The second Black Fist was cut in the side after exactly thirty moves as well. The third was the same.

At that, the waiting Black Fists murmured and grew agitated.

They had resolved to endure somehow, but they could not last past thirty moves, nor could they evade the sword flying toward their flanks.

Having seen their comrades fall before them, they should have prepared. But they could not.

It was not that I suddenly raised my strength on the thirtieth move to cut them. I faced them with the same speed and power from the beginning, yet without fail, when the thirtieth move came, their flanks were exposed. That was why they were astonished.

A single emotion dominated them all.

How can this be?

Only one person—Cheon Sohui, who came out last—barely evaded it, and on the thirty-first move, she was cut in the thigh rather than the flank. As expected, she possessed the most remarkable skill among them.

The look in their eyes had already changed. No longer a challenge of *Let me see what you’ve got*, but a question of *What will you show us next?*

“Until now, I was an expert of thirty-move caliber. Now, I am an expert who can kill you in ten techniques.”

The Black Fists came out one by one with even firmer resolve. This time, I faced them much faster than before.

“This is what a ten-technique expert feels like.”

I pressed them fiercely and relentlessly. One might think there would be little difference between thirty moves and ten, but the feeling on the receiving end was on another dimension entirely.

Everyone was shocked and bewildered.

They had been confident that with their skill, they could beat down just about anyone...

After ten moves passed in a daze, the sword would already be aimed at their throats. In an instant, the blade would be pricking their hearts. The chillingly sharp sword would be tapping against their arms. Had I shown no mercy in my hands, their necks would have been severed, their hearts pierced, and their arms cut off.

None of their initial confidence or arrogance could be found in them now. This was surely the very feeling the Fist Demon had wanted his subordinates to experience. Not to swagger about just because they were Black Fists. To know that there were masters who could rip apart those black martial robes they had earned through hardship in a single breath.

In that way, I gave them all the shock of a ten-move adversary.

It was not only the swordsmanship that astonished them. I had been facing this many people one after another, yet not even my breathing had grown rough.

Now it was time to bring things to a close.

“Now, this is a single-move master. They may be difficult to encounter, but such people most certainly exist in this jianghu.”

At the words single-move, everyone tensed.
