They were about to come forward one by one again, but this time I changed the way I dealt with them.

I released my presence completely. My aura seized control of the space and plunged the Black Fists into the depths of a deep sea. Even the Black Fist who had been stepping forward felt his breath being choked off and, without realizing it, retreated several steps.

Srrrk.

The Black Demon Sword drew itself from its sheath.

Chwararararararak.

And then, before my chest, it began to split apart.

The Black Fists were horrified by the sword qi that, in an instant, divided into twenty. It was the first time they had seen it: a sword splitting into sword-shaped sword qi.

Shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik-shwik!

The sword qi shot out in every direction, swift as light.

Screams burst out from all sides.

The sword-shaped sword qi had stopped before the faces of the Black Fists standing in front. They had flown too quickly for anyone to even attempt to dodge. None among them could block my Yucheonsik, which had reached full mastery at the twelve-star level.

Everyone stared in astonishment at the sword-shaped sword qi hovering before their eyes.

They would not know. That stopping them all simultaneously before their faces like this was a far more difficult, higher-level technique than piercing them all at once with sword qi. At this moment, the only one thinking that far was surely the Fist Demon.

Sssssss.

As the brilliant clusters of light scattered, all the sword qi before their eyes disappeared.

The true Black Demon Sword, which had been floating in midair, was in front of Cheon Sohui. To her, the strongest among the Black Fists, I had sent the real sword. It was consideration and respect for her. And into the sword, I placed my heart.

What you are seeing now is reality. The sword that flies toward you in the future will never vanish before your eyes. Please, do not forget this moment.

The Black Demon Sword that had been hovering before her crossed the air and returned to me. The Black Demon Sword sheathed itself.

Listening to the clear metallic click, I spoke calmly to the Black Fists.

“You all just died.”

Chapter 186: Not easy, is it?
-----------------------------
A heavy silence fell.

No one here had died, but something had. The bravado, bluster, and arrogance the Black Fists had carried until now had most certainly just died.

Naturally, the way they looked at me had completely changed. Awe, fear, anger. Each gaze held a different emotion according to its owner’s nature.

The one who broke the silence was the Fist Demon.

“Do you understand now that swords are to be feared?”

“Yes!”

The Black Fists answered thunderously.

“When I first taught you fist arts, I told you not to fear swords. But now you must know to fear them.”

At times, one must not fear them; at times, one must. That was the lesson the Fist Demon had wanted to impart to his subordinates today—that they had to hold both of those states of mind.

“That will be all for today.”

With those words, the Fist Demon was the first to leave. Though he did not show it before his subordinates, I could feel that he, too, had been shaken by the divine might I had displayed.

The remaining Black Fists could not disperse as they usually did and instead stared at me. Gazes filled with awe could be seen here and there. After all, demonfolk were, by nature, people who valued the strong, and their yearning to become strong was especially intense.

Cheon Sohui asked on their behalf.

“If you’re this strong, why are you trying to learn fist arts? Is it some kind of amusement for the powerful?”

It was an extremely provocative question.

She was still shocked by that single move from moments ago. At the same time, she was furious at herself for having been so helplessly beaten. Since she was someone who hated losing to anyone, perhaps she really could rise to the position of the next Fist Demon.

I calmly asked her in return.

“How could learning fist arts be amusement?”

“Then why are you trying to learn them?”

Why? Because a desire had arisen in me to bring down that cliff with these fists.

“When my sword flew toward you just now, both my hands were empty. What if I had been attacked then? What should I do if my opponent is an expert who won’t even give me the chance to retrieve my sword? I want to learn for that moment. Because this fist, driving into an opponent’s carelessness, may save my life.”

Cheon Sohui stared at me intently. She was probably wondering whether I was sincere or not, but it was impossible for her, still young as she was, to read my heart.

After saying that, I too left the training ground.

Just as the fates of everyone connected to me were changing, their fates, too, would change in some way. I only hoped that, if possible, it would be for the better.

* * *

That night, I was training in fist arts.

I slowly and thoroughly practiced every form I had learned, from the White Fists to the Black Fists. The Fist Demon’s exclusive fist art, too, would only be learned properly if these basic fist arts were firmly established.

Papapapang!

I loved the sound of fists striking the air. A yearning rose in me to learn the Fist Demon’s fist art and attain great mastery as soon as possible.

“You train diligently, even though the night is late.”

The person who had come looking for me was the Fist Demon. Each time I heard it, I was surprised by that calm voice, so at odds with his terrifying face. Which resembled his true nature more—his face or his voice?

In truth, I had expected the Fist Demon to come. I had thought he would definitely react to the skill I had shown earlier.

“I started late, so I must work twice as hard.”

“Not twice. Even if you worked ten times as hard, I would not accept you as my disciple.”

“May I ask the reason?”

The Fist Demon did not answer, but the heat in his gaze answered in his stead. He wanted to fight me. Since he could not fight a disciple.

“You want to fight me.”

Just as he had set a fire in my heart, I had also lit one in his.

“I will not fight.”

The moment I fought him, I would be unable to learn fist arts. There was only one way to learn fist arts from the Fist Demon. A succession struggle, political issues—none of that mattered. We had to become bound by martial arts. I had to make him teach me in the end because he wanted to fight me. That was my plan.

“Why won’t you fight?”

Like him, I also gave no answer. The Fist Demon, who had been staring at me, realized the meaning hidden behind my refusal.

“You’re worried you might defeat me.”

A flash of passion crossed the Fist Demon’s face. Ever since taking on the name Unbeaten, he had probably never experienced anyone worrying that they might defeat him. All he would have seen were people struggling not to die at his hands.

“Second Young Master, you need not worry about that. The martial arts you have learned are martial arts taught by the Cult Leader, so even if I lose, it would not be shameful.”

I peered behind his words as well.

“Do you want to fight me? Or do you want to win?”

For an instant, the Fist Demon looked startled.

“Is there a difference between the two?”

Rather than words born of arrogance, it was closer to this:

“You are so accustomed to winning that you can no longer even feel the difference.”

He strode over and thrust his face in front of mine again. Having to see that terrifying face from this close was, truly, more powerful than a fist flying at me.

“What is it you want to say?”

I recalled what the Extreme Evil Little Demon had told me. That I would know what kind of person the Fist Demon was when he lost.

“If you were the Blood Heaven Fist Demon or the Extreme Evil Fist Demon, I do not know, but as long as you are the Unbeaten Fist Demon, accepting defeat will not be easy for you.”

“You think I would break a promise from the shock of defeat? Just what do you take me for!”

The Unbeaten Fist Demon’s expression twisted fiercely. It was so frightening that it seemed as if the ground beside him might split open and evil spirits would burst out from hell.

“Teach me fist arts first. Then I will settle the match with you.”

The Fist Demon snorted, his expression saying that was out of the question.

“You will have to fight me first.”

Leaving those words behind, the Fist Demon turned and walked away.

Sorry, but that is not your card to play. So long as you are mad for martial arts, so long as you have built up the legend of being unbeaten, that card is mine.

Because I can wait, but you will not be able to.

* * *

Cheon Sohui was silently watching Geom Mugeuk.

Despite the uproar that was not quite an uproar yesterday, Geom Mugeuk participated in the Black Fists’ training as usual. He was immersed in training as if yesterday’s events had never happened.

But the Black Fists were different. Having appeared with Golden Sore Medicine applied to their ribs, they displayed a fervor unlike the day before.

“I was reborn yesterday.”

There was even a Black Fist who said such a thing outright.

Whether he knew of this change and agitation or not, Geom Mugeuk focused solely on his training.

His movements were more precise than anyone else there, and he understood the forms better. The suspicion that someone had secretly taught him the forms because he was the Cult Leader’s son had vanished entirely with the skill Geom Mugeuk had displayed yesterday.

“There is something I would like to ask. Would that be all right?”

Just as the warriors of the Red Fists had done, people among the Black Fists began to ask Geom Mugeuk about the forms as well.

“Of course.”

Geom Mugeuk sincerely explained what the man asked.

In just one day, the atmosphere among the Black Fists had changed this much.

After training ended that day, Cheon Sohui went to Geom Mugeuk.

“I lost the wager, so I’ll keep my promise. Tell me what you want to ask of me.”

Then Geom Mugeuk said something she had never expected.

“Make me the Fist Demon’s disciple.”

Cheon Sohui stared at Geom Mugeuk for a moment with an incredulous expression.

“Then please make me the Cult Leader’s successor.”

She said it thinking it was a joke.

But soon, through Geom Mugeuk’s expression, she could tell. He had meant it seriously.

“You’re not joking.”

“Of course I’m not joking.”

“I haven’t even become his disciple. How am I supposed to make you his disciple?”

At this point in time, the Fist Demon had not yet chosen an official disciple. A perfectionist when it came to martial arts, the Fist Demon did not make decisions about taking disciples carelessly.

“That is for you, the loser of the wager, to worry about.”

“I didn’t know you would make such an absurd request.”

“Then why didn’t you say so back then? That there should be limits to the request.”

“Well… I thought anyone with common sense would naturally make a reasonable request.”

“Is that really why? Or did you not bother saying it because you thought you would win?”

She could not refute him. Geom Mugeuk’s words were true. When they had made the wager to grant a request, she had intended to say that demands beyond common sense could not be made. But her instincts had stopped her.

Because it was a wager she would obviously win, she had thought there was no need to say such a thing.

“Fine, I admit it.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled. If she had not been this honest, he would have teased her a bit more.

“Admit what? If someone makes such an absurd request, you should firmly refuse and forget it.”

For a moment, she stared at Geom Mugeuk with a confused heart.

She thought he was truly impossible to grasp.

She turned around. After taking several steps, she stopped. In any case, she had lost the wager, and simply leaving like this wounded her pride. She turned back again.

“Fine, let’s say I have the ability to do that. Even so, if you become his disciple, the odds of me becoming one will decrease. There’s no way I would help you, is there?”

“That is wrong. If I become his disciple, your odds of becoming one will increase.”

“Why is that?”

“Taking the first disciple is difficult. After that, once the channel is opened, he will take a second and third disciple as if the water has begun to flow. And I will strongly recommend you as my junior martial sister.”

“Me? Why?”

Geom Mugeuk looked at her and spoke calmly.

“Because I think you will do better than anyone. Because you seem like the person best suited to be the next Fist Demon.”

She wondered if he was joking again, but Geom Mugeuk’s expression was serious.

A surge of emotion rose in her chest. Since entering Donggwonmun, she had never once heard words like that.

Neither from herself, nor from her peers. Her seniors and juniors, too, had all run while looking only upward. From the White Fists to the Black Fists, they had run with the mindset that if they were eliminated, they would die.

They were all comrades who had suffered together, but she had never sincerely said something kind to anyone. Nor had she ever heard such words.

Rather, deep in her heart, she had secretly wished for it. That others would suffer more than she did. That others would drop out of the competition. That she would become the Fist Demon.

And yet this Geom Mugeuk, whom she had met only a few days ago, said such things without hesitation. During all those long years, they had never once exchanged words like that among themselves.

She was not bitter because she had never heard them. Her heart grew heavy because it felt as if she had confirmed just how narrow-minded she had been, unable to say them to anyone until now.

“You don’t even know me!”

She snapped angrily, then turned and left.

* * *

That night, Cheon Sohui could not fall asleep easily.

Geom Mugeuk’s words kept coming to mind. Was she truly someone suited to become the Fist Demon?

Unable to sleep and tossing and turning, she could not hold back and headed to Geom Mugeuk’s residence.

It was the dead of night, so she did not think she would be able to meet him. She had simply gone because her heart felt stifled, but to her surprise, Geom Mugeuk was training in martial arts.

His training was so serious that she could not even greet him to announce her arrival. Afraid she might disturb him, she stood still.

At last, after finishing a round of form training, Geom Mugeuk sat on a flat rock in the yard and asked,

“Would you like a cup of tea?”

“No, I’m fine.”

Cheon Sohui sat down beside him. She did not particularly dislike the scent of sweat coming from Geom Mugeuk.

“It’s late, but I came because I had to confirm this. If you really become his disciple, will you recommend me as your junior martial sister?”

“I will.”

How much of this should she believe? When the Black Fist comrades gathered to drink, there was something they always talked about. Trust no one. That was the first principle for surviving long in the murim.

“My goal is to learn the Fist Demon’s martial arts. The one to inherit the Fist Demon’s position would be my junior martial sister.”

Now that such a powerful temptation had actually arrived, she could not judge clearly.

“My junior martial sister, whose senior martial brother is the Heavenly Demon.”

Yes, this must be why everyone falls for it. Even knowing full well that they are sweet words, the heart pounds like this, and so one is deceived.

What if he was tempting her like this in order to use her? What if she was used to her fill and then silenced forever? In her mind appeared Geom Mugeuk’s sword. It would pass through her face and say this: Naive bitch.

At that moment, Geom Mugeuk spoke to her in a calm tone.

“Living isn’t easy, is it?”

That single sentence, delivered gently, struck deep into her chest.

For an instant, she wanted to shout. What do you know about me that you keep acting as if you understand? It is easy. I am managing my life just fine. What is there to be difficult about? I just have to keep running and running. I just have to run while looking upward.

But the words that flowed from her mouth now conveyed the will of her heart rather than her head.

“…It isn’t easy.”

She answered that way without realizing it. She had never once shown weakness to another person. She had never told anyone in her life that things were hard. She had endured well until now.

The moment she acknowledged it, tears streamed down from Cheon Sohui’s eyes. She was even more flustered by the tears that suddenly fell. They had flowed without even giving her time to hold them back.

Could tears really flow this suddenly? Her emotions were clearly not sad at all, so why were tears coming? Since she had not shed tears after growing old enough to know better, she was utterly bewildered.

“N-no, this isn’t…”

Just as she was hastily wiping her tears with her sleeve, someone entered and said,

“As expected! Inspector Seo’s words that you’re a peerless womanizer were right. After a fated love, now you’re even making a woman cry.”

Seeing the person who had entered, Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly, and Cheon Sohui was startled. It was the first time in her life she had seen such a beautiful woman.

Chapter 187: Who Says You're My Senior Brother?
-----------------------------------------------
Beneath the moonlight stood a woman clad in pure white robes. Possessed of a noble beauty, she looked as though she had just descended from the heavens.

Cheon So-hui wiped her tears away again with her sleeve. She wondered if the tears that had suddenly come had made her see things. But the beautiful woman smiled brightly before her and introduced herself.

“It’s a pleasure to meet you. My name is Ian. I used to be the young master’s guard.”

Ian greeted her cheerfully. She had always been bright, but of late she had become brighter still. Her every action overflowed with confidence.

“I’m Cheon So-hui of Donggwonmun’s Black Fist.”

“Ah! It’s an honor to meet you.”

“You know me?”

“Of course. You’re Donggwonmun’s foremost Iron Fist, aren’t you?”

Cheon So-hui’s mood lifted. It was not simply because she had been praised. Ian’s praise did not carry that feeling one so often sensed—A woman, using fist arts? How unusual.

“What I said when I came in earlier was just a joke to tease the young master, so if it offended you in any way, please forgive me.”

“It’s all right.”

“Then please continue your conversation. I’ll come again later.”

As Ian bowed and turned to leave, Geom Mugeuk stopped her.

“You’ve come all this way. Stay and talk a while.”

Ian cautiously asked Cheon So-hui,

“Would that be all right?”

“Of course.”

Cheon So-hui had grown curious about this beautiful woman.

The two women gave off quite contrasting impressions. Ian possessed a beauty that carried a noble grace, while Cheon So-hui had a toughness that made it seem she would survive no matter where one threw her.

“Please don’t misunderstand. I wasn’t crying because of the Second Young Master.”

“Really?”

“Pardon?”

Ian’s eyes sparkled as she asked again.

“You really weren’t crying because of the young master? I cry because of him all the time.”

Ian looked at Geom Mugeuk and said,

“He makes me cry every time.”

With a woman’s intuition, Cheon So-hui sensed it. This woman liked Geom Mugeuk as a man.

“You said you were the Second Young Master’s guard?”

“Yes, though I’ve been cast out now.”

“Why were you cast out?”

“He says it was for the sake of my happiness, but surely that’s just an excuse?”

“It is. Be careful. Philanderers are very good with words.”

“He is the best speaker in all the murim, after all.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed and said to the two of them,

“How long has it been since you met, and you’re already getting along?”

The two women felt goodwill toward each other.

Ian had always liked strong women like Cheon So-hui. It was because she herself wanted to become such a person.

Cheon So-hui’s first impression of Ian was also not bad. A woman this beautiful might have had some off-putting quality that made her hard to approach, but Ian had none of that at all.

Ian asked Geom Mugeuk,

“Is your training going well?”

“If even that news has reached your ears when all you do is shut yourself in the training ground and practice, then there must be no one in the Cult who doesn’t know.”

“Yesterday, even the kitchen cooks were making rice while talking about how you passed the Red Fist. I heard there are even people betting on whether or not you’ll become his disciple.”

Geom Mugeuk learning fist arts at Donggwonmun was truly the talk of the Cult.

Ian asked Geom Mugeuk with gentle eyes,

“How is it?”

“It’s fine.”

Cheon So-hui watched Ian smile at Geom Mugeuk’s answer. She felt the deep trust between the two of them, and for a moment, she envied it. She had no one like that—not even anyone who could pretend to be anything close.

“But what brings you here at this late hour?”

“I was training, and suddenly I wanted to see you, young master.”

Geom Mugeuk stared at her, then abruptly asked,

“Has it come?”

At the cryptic question, Ian nodded with a serious expression.

“I think it has.”

At that, Geom Mugeuk sprang up from the rock and walked into the yard.

“Let’s have a spar.”

Ian gave Cheon So-hui a slight bow, then walked out into the yard. It was discourteous to suddenly hold a spar in this situation, but to her, this was something far too important.

Having grasped the mood, Cheon So-hui quickly rose from her seat.

“I’ll be going now.”

Geom Mugeuk stopped her as she tried to leave.

“You may watch. No, watch. This spar will be of great help to you, Miss Cheon.”

It was Geom Mugeuk’s lesson to the person who would become the next Fist Demon.

“Would that really be all right?”

“You shed precious tears, after all. You should earn the worth of those tears before you go.”

At the words precious tears, Cheon So-hui’s eyes grew hot again.

‘Why am I like this?’

Tears kept trying to spill out, as though a tear duct that had been blocked had suddenly opened. Flustered by something she had never experienced before, she was fortunate that Geom Mugeuk and Ian were already facing each other, about to begin their spar.

“I’ll face you with fist arts.”

“Yes!”

“No, Ian! I’m saying I’m fighting without even a sword, and you’re not worried?”

“Worried? If it’s you, young master, even if you fought without either your hands or feet, I still wouldn’t be able to beat you.”

“That’s true. As long as I have this mouth.”

Watching them, Cheon So-hui gave a slight nod as if in agreement.

With his back turned, Geom Mugeuk said to her,

“Don’t nod.”

At that, Cheon So-hui nodded even more emphatically. Since he was a man with eyes on the back of his head, the presence or absence of a sword was clearly not the issue.

The spar began, and at first Ian could not focus. She had said she trusted him, but she was worried that Geom Mugeuk might get hurt fighting barehanded.

But after exchanging a few moves, she concluded that no worry could have been more needless. Geom Mugeuk was fast—far too fast. Even if she swung ten swords at him now, she felt she would not be able to touch a single hair on him.

‘Honestly, who is worrying about whom?’

Geom Mugeuk pressed her fiercely.

A fist with enough force to shatter her jaw flew toward her face.

When she barely dodged it, another fist came for her ribs. This time as well, she twisted her body and narrowly avoided it. Had it landed, it would have broken all her ribs.

Whoosh! Whooosh!

A crisp sound, no less sharp than the wind raised by a blade, rang out every time Geom Mugeuk’s fists flew.

She barely blocked and evaded, trying somehow to put distance between them, but Geom Mugeuk clung to her like a shadow. Once she allowed him to close in, it was not easy to shake him off.

‘Ah, so this is how I die!’

Ian was experiencing something new: a fight against a master of fist arts.

Because she could not secure distance, not even half the power of her swordsmanship could be brought to bear. Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk’s fists flew mercilessly toward her vital points.

She truly had to use everything she had just to evade them.

Thinking that she would be taken down if she did nothing but dodge, Ian tried to counterattack by any means possible.

At the decisive moment, she found an opening in Geom Mugeuk and used it to widen the distance.

‘Now!’

Only one chance!

Her sword embroidered the air. As the Flying Heaven Sword Art’s Second Form, the Changing Heaven Form, was unleashed, her sword brought forth twelve transformations.

An exclamation that Cheon So-hui had held back throughout the fight burst from her lips.

“Ah!”

Geom Mugeuk evaded that attack, which seemed absolutely impossible to avoid, using the footwork of the Black Fist’s fist art.

That footwork could do that?

Only now did she truly realize how remarkable the fist art she had learned was. The entire time she watched, her heart felt as though it would burst. She wanted to jump in and fight with them. She wanted to unfold it herself as Geom Mugeuk did.

That was not the only impact this spar had on her.

Geom Mugeuk, who, despite having the skill of an absolute master and being the Cult Leader’s second son, had come to the Fist Demon to learn martial arts.

Ian, who, despite being beautiful enough to be called the greatest beauty under heaven, had come here in the depths of night to learn swordsmanship.

Shame and respect arose in her at the same time, and the sight of the two of them was engraved upon her heart like a scene from a painting.

“Well done!”

Geom Mugeuk smiled brightly at Ian. He had driven her to an extreme situation while giving her a chance to escape, and she had not missed it; she had found a way through. It was the fruit of the harsh training she had endured.

“I still feel something tickling at me.”

Geom Mugeuk could tell. She had nearly reached the ninth level and was searching for its entrance.

“Starting tomorrow, come to me every night.”

“Thank you, young master.”

Just as his father had forcibly led him to the Great Completion of the Wind God Four Steps, Geom Mugeuk intended to throw her Flying Heaven Sword Art into the ninth level. Once the Flying Heaven Sword Art reached the ninth level, he would be able to feel at ease no matter where she was.

When Geom Mugeuk and Ian finished speaking, Cheon So-hui said in a trembling voice,

“To be honest, the sword the Second Young Master used against me felt too unreal, so it didn’t frighten me. But the sword she wielded tonight, while everyone else slept, frightened me.”

Could she defeat that sword, trained with until late into the night?

Deep down, she had been confident that among women of a similar age, she was the strongest in the murim. But after watching the spar, she realized that had been nothing but arrogance. At her refusal to lose, both her fists clenched tight.

It was the moment a fire caught in her heart as well.

Feeling that burning heat, Cheon So-hui suddenly said,

“Please teach me too. Senior Brother.”

Senior Brother.

She must have known.

If those words reached the Fist Demon, she would be in tremendous trouble. Even if she were expelled, she would have nothing to say. It was a single word spoken with her life staked upon it. Senior Brother.

Perhaps, once she returned to her quarters, she would regret saying those words all night long.

That was why Geom Mugeuk spoke warmly to her.

“All right. You come too, Junior Sister.”

Regret would be regret later; for now, Cheon So-hui’s face was filled with joy.

It would not have been strange for Ian to feel needless resistance, as though her own training were being interfered with, but she gladly welcomed Cheon So-hui.

A person must change for their martial arts to change.

Geom Mugeuk was certain that Ian’s broad heart would lead her to the seat of an absolute master.

And following the drinking gathering, Ian created a second gathering.

“Welcome to the Midnight Training Gathering.”

* * *

My training with the Black Fist continued.

In the meantime, I was locked in a battle of wills with the Fist Demon. It was a fight over who would raise the white flag first.

I was confident in this fight.

After all, my life was one in which I had to prepare and prepare again.

It was not that I had to learn the Fist Demon’s fist arts before moving on to the next stage. His fist arts were merely one part of the process of preparing everything at the same time.

I could learn the Fist Demon’s fist arts now, tomorrow, or a year from now. Why? Because I had much to do, much to learn, and many people to look after. Because I was endlessly preparing other things as well. And you think you can beat me?

From time to time, my eyes met the Fist Demon’s. Each time, we conversed with our gazes. Give up. You give up. I can go to the very end. So can I. I am young. That is why you will not endure.

He probably thought that because training was tedious, I would eventually fail to withstand it. But that was the Fist Demon’s miscalculation. I was no ordinary young man.

* * *

At night, I taught Ian and Cheon So-hui.

On the first day she learned martial arts from me, Cheon So-hui asked,

“Senior Brother, Martial Artist Ian is learning such an important martial art. Is it really all right for me to be beside her?”

Though she had earnestly asked to be taught together that day, once she was actually joining us, she seemed to feel sorry.

I said to her,

“You are more important than that martial art. The current Fist Demon is my father’s Fist Demon, and my brother’s Fist Demon. My Fist Demon is you.”

The future Heavenly Demon and Fist Demon.

Emotion flashed across her face. Soon, she asked about the worst-case scenario.

“Then what if I don’t become the Fist Demon, and you don’t become the Heavenly Demon, Senior Brother?”

“Then the three of us will have to open a gukbap shop in front of the Murim Alliance and live there.”

Perhaps imagining bowls of gukbap flying out from the kitchen through Void Object Seizure and being served with Form-Shift Transposition, Cheon So-hui laughed loudly. It seemed to be the first time I had seen her laugh so brightly since meeting her.

“But why in front of the Murim Alliance?”

Ian answered in my stead.

“Maybe there’s a woman he wants to see.”

“Aha!”

I shouted that it wasn’t true, but the two of them were already nodding and beginning to discuss the characteristics of philanderers.

On the fifth day after we began our midnight training, Ian finally found the door to the ninth level.

Her sword became faster and stronger, bringing forth dazzling transformations.

No words of thanks or congratulations were needed. Ian ran over and threw herself into my arms. I held her tightly. You worked hard, Ian. Now only one hurdle remains until Great Completion.

At that moment, Ian and I were startled by the sound of sniffling and looked at Cheon So-hui.

She was crying.

“I think my tear ducts opened that day. I never cried in my whole life, but tears keep coming.”

When she cried, Ian began shedding tears as well.

“Why are you crying?”

“Because Martial Artist Cheon is crying.”

The two women turned their bodies away from each other and cried.

“What is wrong with you tough women who could beat up every man in the world?”

Wiping her tears, Cheon So-hui said,

“Senior Brother, please keep teaching me.”

She was deeply saddened at the thought of the midnight training ending like this.

Ian stepped forward on her behalf.

“Young master, let’s continue this Midnight Training Gathering.”

It was right then. From behind us, someone spoke coldly.

“Who gave you permission to call him Senior Brother?”

Cheon So-hui was startled out of her wits, her face turning deathly pale.

The Fist Demon was entering the place where we stood, his face terrifying like that of an evil spirit.

I greeted him with a calm expression.

“Welcome.”

In truth, I had known the Fist Demon was there. He had come twice before. He had hidden himself and secretly watched before simply leaving. Just what had he been thinking as he watched?

The Fist Demon stood in the yard and glared at me, Ian, and Cheon So-hui.

Cheon So-hui in particular was utterly shaken. Since the Fist Demon had heard her call me Senior Brother, there was no room for excuses. In terror that she might be expelled from Donggwonmun, she kept her head bowed low and trembled all over. She was surely praying that the words “You are excommunicated” would not come.

With that evil-spirit-like face, the Fist Demon uttered words none of us had imagined.

“Include me in this Midnight Training Gathering as well.”

Chapter 188: What Will You Do After Becoming Stronger?
------------------------------------------------------
We were truly surprised.

That the words “Late-Night Training Group” came from Gwonma’s mouth was surprising enough, but him asking to join that group was almost shocking.

Cheon Sohui, who had been keeping her head down, raised it wondering if she had misheard, but when she met eyes with Gwonma, she quickly lowered it again.

Gwonma looked at me, waiting for an answer.

“I am not the leader of this group.”

My gaze turned to Ian.

The look in her eyes as she saw me was exactly this: No, Young Master! You’re dumping this on me? Despite that urgent gaze, Ian calmly greeted Gwonma.

“Allow me to formally introduce myself. I am Ian.”

Gwonma merely nodded once. Even Ian’s beauty could not soften Gwonma’s fearsome expression.

“Though I am the one who founded this group, I am not someone who acts unilaterally.”

Ian looked at Cheon Sohui. Cheon Sohui’s expression was like this: So you’re dumping this on me too?

Pretending not to see, Ian asked her.

“What do you think, Lady Cheon?”

Without hesitation, Cheon Sohui shouted.

“I absolutely agree!”

Gwonma nodded and asked Ian.

“And you?”

Probably only my father could refuse after seeing that face.

“I agree as well.”

Once Ian agreed too, only then did Gwonma’s gaze turn to me.

“And you?”

“Of course I’m opposed.”

For a moment, Gwonma’s face twitched, but I didn’t bat an eye.

“The reason?”

“It’s uncomfortable. I might be fine, but how uncomfortable would those two be? Especially Lady Cheon—having to see you during the day and then again at night, she must be dying inside.”

Cheon Sohui spoke faster than my sword technique, the Heaven-Piercing Form.

“I am fine. Rather, I am delighted to see you again.”

Then Gwonma said to me.

“She says she’s delighted.”

“As if that could be true? Let me give a simple example. From tomorrow, you must train in martial arts with my father.”

Gwonma’s expression was already stiffening. The discomfort he wanted to hide but could not. As expected of my father.

“After seeing him all day training together, you have to see my father again at night. Now, how would you feel?”

It was an irrefutable example. Father, I’m sorry.

“Then what must I do?”

They were words that normally could not come from Gwonma’s mouth. If he was saying even this much, of course I should open the door for him. Except the admission fee was rather steep.

“Teach me the First Fist of your secret fist technique.”

Gwonma’s secret fist technique was the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

It was Gwonma’s secret martial art, known as a fierce, powerful, and dominating fist technique. When the Thunderbolt Asura Fist reached a high realm and was unleashed, thunder would sound from the fist.

The Thunderbolt Asura Fist consisted of six stances in total, and I had asked him to teach me the first stance, the First Fist.

“Those children are the ones who would be uncomfortable—why do you want to learn martial arts?”

“They’ll understand. I’m paying precisely that much.”

Ian and Cheon Sohui, who were watching, would surely think Gwonma would never accept. Not just them, but anyone would think so, yet I had thrown it out there anyway. Taking one step, one step closer to my goal was what mattered.

Of course, there was a reason I had thrown it out like this. He had already visited this place secretly twice, and he had directly said he wanted to join the group.

Whatever his reason, he too harbored a desire to join this group.

“Do you take me for a fool?”

“As if I would. But I think you’ll pretend to be exactly that much of a fool.”

I conversed with Gwonma through our eyes. What a crazy bastard! Yes, that’s right. I hear that quite often.

Gwonma, who had glared at me for a moment, turned and left just like that.

In truth, he could have insisted. If he had forcefully demanded to be let in, we would have had no choice. If he had pressed us, using the fact that Cheon Sohui called me her Martial Brother as leverage, we would have had to do exactly as he wished.

But when I opposed him, Gwonma simply backed down. He is not a petty man. He is a man befitting the name Gwonma.

The tension drained from Cheon Sohui, and she collapsed into her spot.

“Ah! I thought my heart was going to burst earlier.”

Ian also shook her head.

“I knew he had a scary face, but I didn’t realize he was this frightening up close.”

Once they had calmed down somewhat, Ian asked me.

“But why does Gwonma want to join the group?”

To her, who could not understand at all, I spoke the thought that had suddenly crossed my mind.

“Because he’s lonely?”

I had not said it as a joke, but Ian took it as one.

“Stop joking around.”

“How would I know what’s in that man’s heart?”

However, one thing was certain.

“His ultimate goal is probably to fight me.”

“With you, Young Master?”

“Yes. He probably wants to train together and use that as an excuse to fight me.”

Ian was startled. She had probably never imagined that Gwonma harbored such thoughts.

“If he wants to fight, couldn’t he just fight during the day?”

“His subordinates would be watching then.”

“Couldn’t he just call you separately to fight?”

“I told him I absolutely wouldn’t fight.”

“So he’s joining this group to look for a chance to fight you, Young Master?”

“That’s the only way I can see it right now.”

Ian raised her hand and declared.

“This danger-ridden group is disbanded immediately.”

Of course, she did not mean it. Gwonma had wanted to join and been rejected; if we disbanded right away, there was no telling what might happen.

Meanwhile, Cheon Sohui, sitting slumped at the side, was half out of it. Having been caught by Gwonma for taking the Second Young Master as her Martial Brother, what must her heart be like now? Having fallen out of Gwonma’s favor, she must be thinking that becoming the next Gwonma had permanently slipped through her fingers.

“Should I quit this group even now?”

“Do you think he’d let it go if you quit? I doubt it.”

“Then what should I do? Since it’s already come to this, should I just say to heck with it?”

“You should.”

“What?”

Cheon Sohui looked at me with wide eyes.

“Rather, you should train even harder and make up for your mistake with skill.”

She let out a sigh with an expression that said easier said than done.

After a brief pause, she asked.

“He won’t come again now, right?”

“I suppose not?”

I had said that to comfort her in her shock, but I thought that Gwonma would not give up so easily.

“I-I’ll work twice as hard.”

Just as Cheon Sohui seemed to steel her resolve, she soon clasped her head in her hands.

“I’m ruined!”

* * *

The next day, Black Fist training proceeded as usual.

I tried to gauge Gwonma’s mood, but he acted as if nothing had happened. That only made Cheon Sohui more nervous, and she trained so hard it was pitiful.

Leaving that matter aside, my popularity among the Black Fist members was rising by the day.

And for good reason—I regarded these Black Fists as my direct subordinates. They were people who would someday fight for me, alongside me.

So when they asked questions, I answered them sincerely, and I shared stories unrelated to martial arts as well.

My profound knowledge of the martial world and the Central Plains often surprised them. Before they knew it, they were captivated by my stories, losing track of time.

Gwonma quietly watched me from afar. I wondered what he could be thinking.

That night.

Three people gathered first at the Late-Night Training Group. Me, Ian, and Cheon Sohui.

Cheon Sohui let out a sigh.

“Today Gwonma didn’t look at me even once.”

“When did he ever?”

“…….”

“Since you’re already in his bad books anyway, why not just act comfortable around him?”

“Is that sincere advice? Or a trap to destroy me?”

“Of course it’s advice for my Martial Sister.”

Cheon Sohui was startled.

“Do you still think of me as your Martial Sister?”

“Yes.”

Realizing I was sincere, she shook her head.

“That won’t do. What are you planning to do?”

“Put yourself in his shoes. You’re Gwonma. You have a smart subordinate you want to pass the position to someday. But this kid got caught scheming with the Second Young Master. And after that, she keeps watching your every mood. How would you feel?”

“I think I’d dislike it.”

“So act boldly.”

“Wouldn’t that make him dislike me even more?”

At her words, I laughed. Ian laughed, and Cheon Sohui laughed too.

Cheon Sohui steeled her heart.

“Alright! I’ll do my best as my Martial Brother says! What’s done is done, let come what may. I’ll tell him boldly why I took the Second Young Master as my Martial Brother. If I speak from the heart, it’ll get through. Even though Gwonma looks scary, his heart must be different. Thank you, Martial Brother! I’ll do my best. Let’s keep being Martial Brother and Martial Sister in secret from Gwonma from now on. But why do you look like that? Why? Why do you look so scary…… No, right? Tell me it’s not.”

With her face on the verge of tears, she turned toward the cold air she felt behind her.

Gwonma had somehow come and was standing right behind her.

Cheon Sohui’s face, bearing all the agony in the world, turned pale.

At a loss for words, just then Gwonma suddenly asked.

“What were you thinking when you took the Second Young Master as your Martial Brother?”

Gwonma had heard everything she had said moments ago.

With a trembling voice, Cheon Sohui conveyed her honest feelings.

“I happened to see the Second Young Master duel a martial artist using the fist technique we learned. At that moment, I wanted to become that strong too.”

She had just spoken of arbitrarily taking a Martial Brother and trying to learn martial arts while Gwonma was teaching her his technique. Even if she were beaten to death with a single palm strike, it would not have been a strange situation.

But she spoke honestly. Her mind must have screamed that she shouldn’t, but her mouth once again spoke for her heart.

“What did you want to do by becoming strong?”

“To be honest, I wanted to succeed you and become the next Gwonma. Now…… I think it’s all ruined, though.”

It was a life-risking answer, but it was not the answer Gwonma wanted.

“Yes, that is something like a profession you want. I asked what you wanted to do by becoming strong.”

“!”

In that moment, Cheon Sohui was bewildered.

She had probably never deeply considered the difference between what she wanted to become and what she wanted to do. Having lived with only becoming Gwonma as her goal, she must have been all the more confused.

Listening to their conversation, I admired Gwonma.

Yes, this is the kind of question Gwonma asks. He is not someone who only uses his fists ignorantly—he is a clever person, as you can see from this question.

I stepped in to save her.

“Why have you come today?”

Then astonishing words flowed from Gwonma’s mouth.

“I shall pass on the First Fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist to you.”

I was startled. I had expected Gwonma wouldn’t give up easily, but I never thought he would pass on the First Fist just to join the group.

For what on earth? Did he really want to fight me that badly?

Not only me, but Ian and Cheon Sohui were startled as well. No one would believe it. That Gwonma would pass on the First Fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist in order to join this small Late-Night Training Group.

Gwonma’s and my gazes tangled in the air. We conversed again with our eyes.

Truly impressive.

As if you’re the only one?

You won’t regret this?

Wouldn’t you be the one to regret it? For reeling me in this thoroughly.

I won’t regret it.

“But I have one condition.”

“Please speak.”

“You must keep it a secret that I passed on martial arts to you.”

“Of course.”

Gwonma’s gaze turned to the two women. At a time like this, what words could possibly come out but compliance?

“Yes! Please don’t worry!”

“We were never here!”

Of course, what they truly wanted to say was probably this:

We don’t want to be here in the future either!

The martial arts transmission took place immediately.

Gwonma taught me the mnemonic formula of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist’s First Fist through voice transmission.

First Fist, Black Cloud Asura.

Though it was the first of six stances, it was a fist possessing more powerful might than any other fist technique.

Why this martial art belongs to Gwonma—you will understand once you experience it.

Gwonma even corrected my posture and properly passed on the Black Cloud Asura.

At least when it came to martial arts, he was sincere. He was probably the best teacher among the Demon Lords. And all in a gentle voice that did not match his fearsome face at all.

Ian and Cheon Sohui turned away and did not look at us. Since we were conversing through voice transmission anyway, there was no harm in looking, but they were not so lacking in courtesy as to watch a martial arts transmission take place.

And so I received the transmission of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist’s First Fist.

*Bang!*

Someday, thunder would burst forth from this fist I threw.

Watching the First Fist I performed, Gwonma nodded.

“You will have to continue training to bring out its true power.”

“Thank you.”

As is the rule—if something comes, something must go—I formally spoke to Gwonma.

“Welcome to the Late-Night Training Group.”

Me and Gwonma, Ian and Cheon Sohui. It was the moment four unimaginable people were bound together as one group.

And today, Gwonma seemed determined to surprise us.

“Now, let’s train.”

He took off his upper garment. After coming to Donggwonmun, no one had ever seen his bare body. Yet he began removing his clothes, as if truly intending to train seriously.

Under the bluish moonlight, Gwonma’s upper body was revealed.

Chapter 189: I’ve Been to That Island Too
-----------------------------------------
The Fist Demon’s body was perfect.

His face was like a fiend’s, but his body was a work of art.

The Fist Demon’s muscles looked ready to burst, large and thick, yet they did not seem sluggish. His diamond-hard physique, forged through grueling training and perilous battles, was enough to make a person feel intimidated just by looking at it.

There were fewer scars on his body than I had expected, but one long sword scar that crossed his chest caught the eye. If anything, it made his body look even more magnificent.

His body seemed to be saying this:

This is the body of a fist artist who has reached the ultimate realm.

Could I truly withstand the pressure when that body came surging at me? Could I endure the destructive force when those great fists came flying? Could my sword really protect me from that body?

I was sincerely impressed. Seeing my reaction, a faint trace of pleasure crossed the Fist Demon’s face.

He must have heard countless times that he looked magnificent, and he surely knew it himself. So I asked something else.

“What must one do to build a body like this?”

“If you made your body like this, it would get in the way of your swordsmanship, wouldn’t it?”

After all, the body of a swordsman and the body of a fist artist were clearly different.

“I would have to train so that my swordsmanship grows stronger even as I build my body like this.”

“Then you should move your body rather than your mouth.”

The Fist Demon began his fist training.

To be honest, I had thought that even if he joined the gathering, he would not train. Training could not have been his reason for joining.

But he began training properly. As though telling me to watch and take reference, he started displaying the fist techniques of the Black Fist.

He had taken off his clothes, so I could not just stand there doing nothing. I took off my upper garment as well and stood beside the Fist Demon, beginning to perform the forms.

With the Divine Eye Art, I examined the Fist Demon’s body in detail. When we performed the same movement, I compared his muscles with my own.

How they were different, and why they were different.

Understanding that difference would be the greatest lesson for me.

Ian and Cheon Sohui watched as the Fist Demon and I executed the forms with identical movements.

Though I could not know for certain, Cheon Sohui’s heart alone was probably yearning to throw off her clothes and jump in with us. That was how fiercely the fire in her chest was burning as well.

Ian and Cheon Sohui also began training.

The Midnight Training Gathering. Hearing the name alone, one might think it was some social club for loafers, but the four people gathered there were all aflame. That was why it proceeded more fiercely than any training session.

The sound of the wind stirred by the Fist Demon’s fists was different from mine. Naturally, the Fist Demon’s sound was more pleasant to hear. How could something so heavy produce such a brisk sound? Listening to the sound of the Fist Demon’s fists made my heart grow calm and my mood improve.

I became purely immersed in fist arts. From the moment I heard him say he wanted to collapse the cliff, I no longer looked at the Fist Demon with any other intention or purpose. I only wanted to learn fist arts from him.

Perhaps it was because he sensed that feeling in me that the Fist Demon was here with us at this moment.

After training ended, I told the Fist Demon one piece of news.

“Tomorrow, we will gather at Maga Village.”

“At Maga Village?”

“Tomorrow, we’ll be holding a welcome party for you, Lord Fist Demon. A welcome party for the new member.”

The Fist Demon was startled. Soon, a glare even fiercer than the one he had given me when I said he had to teach us his exclusive martial arts if he wanted to join the gathering came flying my way.

A welcome party for me? And a new member? Are you truly insane?

It was probably something he had never experienced even once in his entire life.

“We’ll assemble in front of Pungryu Tavern at the same time. That is all. Dismissed!”

I withstood the Fist Demon’s glare with a look that said, If you don’t like it, then withdraw.

The Fist Demon snorted and left the place.

I watched his back for a moment, then turned around and flinched in surprise.

Ian and Cheon Sohui were standing side by side, glaring at me frighteningly. It seemed the Fist Demon was not the only one who disliked tomorrow’s welcome party.

Ian raised her voice.

“Are you really going to do this? How are we supposed to drink in that situation?”

“It might be unexpectedly fun.”

Suddenly, Cheon Sohui put a hand to her forehead and said,

“Ah! I think I have a fever. I probably won’t be able to attend tomorrow.”

Ian chimed in.

“Ah! Right. I have plans tomorrow. I’d forgotten.”

Looking at the two women, I smiled and said,

“You think that’ll work? Then I’ll see you tomorrow at Pungryu Tavern!”

And so ended the first day of the Midnight Training Gathering with the Fist Demon.

* * *

The next night.

Jo Chunbae, the owner of Pungryu Tavern, yawned so wide his mouth nearly split.

There were unusually few customers today, and he had been dozing beside the counter, wondering whether he should close early or not.

Just then, he woke at the sound of someone’s presence and was scared out of his wits.

“Aaagh!”

He screamed and fell backward.

A terrifying evil spirit was standing before his eyes. Thinking he must have seen something in his sleep, he rose from the floor and froze stiff.

It was not an evil spirit, but a person. In all his life, this was the first time he had seen anyone look so frightening. The man’s forearms were thicker than his own thighs.

Then came a familiar voice.

“It has been a while, owner.”

Geom Mugeuk appeared from behind the man.

“Pavilion Master!”

Truly, it felt as though he had come back from the dead.

“This is Lord Fist Demon of our cult. Pay your respects.”

At the words Fist Demon, Jo Chunbae’s eyes widened. He had heard many rumors about the Fist Demon, but today was the first time he had seen him in person.

“I did not know such an honored guest had arrived and committed a discourtesy in my drowsiness. Please forgive me!”

Jo Chunbae bent at the waist until he was folded in half.

As of this moment, the record had been broken. Among all the Demon Venerables who had come as guests, the most frightening person was now the Fist Demon.

Ian, who entered after them, greeted him warmly.

“Hello.”

Jo Chunbae’s expression brightened. Seeing Ian, who always greeted him brightly, naturally lifted his spirits. With her standing beside the Fist Demon, it truly felt as though two extremes were standing together.

The three people other than Geom Mugeuk went up first to the second-floor seats where they always sat.

“How has business been lately?”

“It has been going well, thanks to you. I heard you have been training in fist arts lately, Pavilion Master. It seems the rumors were true.”

“You knew as well, owner?”

“If you don’t know that these days, you’re a spy. No, wait, a spy would have known first and reported it to the Murim Alliance.”

At Jo Chunbae’s joke, Geom Mugeuk laughed with him.

Compared to the cheerful atmosphere downstairs, the mood on the second floor was solemn.

Ian and Cheon Sohui were watching the Fist Demon’s mood. Cheon Sohui, in particular, was frozen stiff. Far from leaving the gathering, she had even been caught secretly proposing that they call each other senior brother and junior sister.

‘I really have no chance of becoming the next Fist Demon now!’

To break the awkward atmosphere, Ian asked the Fist Demon,

“Have you been here before?”

At that, the Fist Demon looked at Ian.

‘Was that question really something you had to glare at me so frighteningly for?’

If he had known Ian’s inner thoughts, the Fist Demon would have felt terribly wronged. He had merely looked at her, yet she thought he was glaring frighteningly.

“Tell them to bring liquor.”

“Yes! I’ll bring it!”

At the Fist Demon’s words, Cheon Sohui quickly rose and ran down to the first floor.

Now only the Fist Demon and Ian remained on the second floor.

“It was truly unexpected. That you would join our gathering, Lord Fist Demon.”

In truth, this was what Ian wanted to ask.

Did you really join because you want to fight the young master?

Instead of answering, the Fist Demon asked her,

“Are you not afraid of me?”

“I am afraid.”

“You don’t seem afraid at all.”

“If that is how you feel, it is probably because of the young master.”

“Why the Second Young Master?”

“If you were truly someone dangerous to me, Lord Fist Demon, the young master would not leave me alone with you. To begin with, you would not have been allowed to join the gathering.”

The Fist Demon’s gaze turned toward Geom Mugeuk, who was standing in front of the counter on the first floor.

“The greater the trust, the greater the wound.”

If Ian had heard those words with her eyes closed, she thought she might not have believed they had come from the Fist Demon. Both because of their content and because of the gentle tone. Ian suddenly wondered whether there might be parts of the Fist Demon she had misunderstood.

“I don’t think men know much about women. Women know everything. The only choice is whether to pretend not to know and go along with it, or to run away in advance.”

“So? Are you the type to pretend not to know and go along with it?”

“Originally, I’m more cunning than I look, so I’m the type to run away in advance. The young master, however, would be the one and only exception.”

“Misplaced trust invites misfortune.”

“That’s a shame. Since I won’t be able to experience it.”

Ian’s trust in Geom Mugeuk was absolute.

Just then, Geom Mugeuk and Cheon Sohui came up to the second floor carrying liquor.

“My apologies. I held the owner up for a moment, so the liquor was delayed.”

Geom Mugeuk poured liquor for the Fist Demon first. The cup in the Fist Demon’s large hand looked far too small and cute.

As Geom Mugeuk poured liquor for Ian and Cheon Sohui, he said,

“Anyone who has a bad drinking habit, don’t forget who we’re drinking with right now!”

Ian played along with Geom Mugeuk’s joke.

“As someone with a bad drinking habit, this is a seat where I must risk my life.”

Then she looked at Cheon Sohui.

“Do you perhaps have any bad drinking habits?”

“I don’t have any.”

“It’s not that you’ve never drunk all the way to the point where they come out, is it?”

Cheon Sohui was certain she would never misbehave while drunk. Misbehave while drinking with the Fist Demon? That would never happen.

“Now, we welcome Lord Fist Demon. Cheers!”

Geom Mugeuk raised his cup, and everyone drank a toast together.

Geom Mugeuk worked to make the long-awaited drinking gathering enjoyable and lively. For the Fist Demon as well, who had acted as though he would never come, yet had arrived exactly on time.

Led by Geom Mugeuk and Ian, they talked about all sorts of things. They talked about martial arts, about women, and about men. They also talked about the Murim Alliance and the Sado Alliance, and about the other Demon Venerables.

The Fist Demon did not seem to dislike the gathering either. He drank well, and he answered the questions occasionally asked of him.

But throughout the conversation, Geom Mugeuk felt it. That the Fist Demon was lonely. Seeing the Fist Demon in a place like this, where people laughed and chatted, made it even clearer.

Though the four of them were drinking together, only the Fist Demon was alone in a place impossibly far away.

That remote island was a place Geom Mugeuk had also been to.

Geom Mugeuk had been the same as the Fist Demon. He had been among people, yet he had never been able to fully enjoy that life. He had possessed only the single-minded resolve to gather the materials for the Great Regression Art; he had not had a life of his own.

Perhaps the Fist Demon, too, lived only with the single-minded resolve to collapse the cliff, having already lost the joys of reality.

As I drank, lost in those thoughts, Ian was looking at me. Her eyes, which looked even more beautiful than usual today, asked, What are you thinking so deeply about? Just this and that.

Then Cheon Sohui, slightly tipsy, brought up the words she had been holding back.

“I thought all night about the question you asked me yesterday, Lord Fist Demon. The question about what I would do if I became strong.”

She had truly agonized over it seriously.

“I honestly don’t remember what I used to think before entering Donggwon Sect. At some point, all I thought about was winning, and winning again, so I could become the Fist Demon. But even in the midst of that, I did think this much. If I become the Fist Demon, I’ll be happy. I’ll become happy. As long as I become the Fist Demon, all this effort will be rewarded.”

Her voice trembled.

“If I become strong… I want to be happy.”

Perhaps embarrassed even after saying it, she quickly added,

“Though I suppose it wouldn’t be proper for someone who dreams of becoming the Fist Demon to want something as insignificant as becoming happy, rather than some great undertaking for our cult.”

The one who responded to her words was Geom Mugeuk.

“I think that’s fine.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Fist Demon and asked,

“What do you think, Lord Fist Demon?”

“It is pathetic, but not bad.”

“If it’s pathetic, then it’s pathetic. If it’s not bad, then it’s not bad. Please decide on just one of the two. If you speak so ambiguously, our junior sister will spend the whole night worrying again and won’t sleep a wink.”

“It is not bad.”

Geom Mugeuk smiled and nodded as he looked at Cheon Sohui.

In truth, this was what Cheon Sohui wanted to say.

I want to become like this martial artist.

Ian looked happier than anyone. On top of that, she even had someone she trusted and liked as much as her own life.

‘I envy you.’

We all drank together.

The Fist Demon set down his cup and asked Geom Mugeuk,

“You said you wanted to learn fist arts because you wanted to become strong, did you not?”

“Yes.”

“What will you do once you become strong?”

It was the same question he had asked Cheon Sohui.

“I’ll live freely. If I want to go, I’ll go. If I want to come, I’ll come. I’ll spend time with the Demon Venerables, and when I want to be alone, I’ll shut myself up in the Heavenly Demon Hall for days on end. Then, when I feel stifled, I’ll suddenly head out into the Central Plains. If I meet good people, I might stay in their village for a few days. I’ll drink with herb gatherers on scenic mountains. At the very least, I won’t live cooped up in the Heavenly Demon Hall like my father, feeling stifled. He should go out and enjoy himself a little too. Why are you all making those faces? Why are you looking so scary… No, right?”

Imitating what Cheon Sohui had said yesterday, Geom Mugeuk turned around.

A person whose mere entrance made the surrounding air feel different, a person who made even the Fist Demon, who had remained relaxed the whole time, stiffen, a person whose very existence made everyone tense—the Heavenly Demon, Geom Ujin, was standing behind him.

Chapter 190: Will You Take Me Aboard That Ship Too?
---------------------------------------------------
“You’re still cursing me behind my back, I see?”

At Father’s words, I smiled and replied.

“Cursing you? You misunderstand! Ah, and even if I were, how could I possibly curse the Heavenly Demon to his face? Not even the Martial Alliance Leader could do that. Now, please, have a seat.”

I was probably the first to notice Father entering the tavern. That was why I had said it on purpose, so he would hear. So he wouldn’t just stay cooped up in the Heavenly Demon Hall, and would go out and enjoy himself a little.

“Cult Leader!”

Ian and Cheon Sohee sprang to their feet to greet him, but Father raised a hand to stop them, then sat down.

Even though he had not released any particular aura, the atmosphere in the room changed completely. Before we knew it, the shabby inn’s second floor had become the Heavenly Demon Hall.

“What brings you here?”

“I was passing by and stopped in.”

That was what he always said, but there was no way that was true. Had he really come to see me? Or had he come to see the Fist Demon?

Ian spoke carefully.

“Please speak at your leisure. We will take our leave.”

“There’s no need. I’ll be leaving shortly.”

“Yes! Then we’ll sit quietly as if we aren’t here.”

As Father watched Ian answer so briskly, he suddenly said,

“You’ve grown a lot.”

It was such an unexpected remark that Ian was startled. She was so surprised that I wondered if she had ever been this shocked recently. Not only she, but I was surprised too. I had never imagined Father would acknowledge Ian like that.

“Thank you.”

Ian’s bowed voice trembled.

It was only that one greeting, but Ian was deeply moved. Her gaze turned toward me. With her eyes, she said, Did you see that, Young Master? The Cult Leader remembered me from when I was young. Ah! I’m so touched. The Cult Leader remembered me.

Truly, if this had not been the place it was, she would have screamed in joy and jumped up and down.

This time, Father’s gaze turned to Cheon Sohee.

“They say you fight the best in Donggwon Sect?”

Cheon Sohee, too, looked as if she might faint from shock. The Heavenly Demon knew about her? That, likewise, was something she had never imagined.

“No, sir.”

“Then was I mistaken?”

“What? No, sir!”

She had said no out of modesty, then said no again. Flustered, she had no idea what to do.

I stepped in as the rescue party.

“That’s right. Miss Cheon is the most skilled in Donggwon Sect. I consider her a candidate to become the next Fist Demon.”

Though, since Cheon Sohee became even more flustered, it did not feel much like a rescue.

“No, no! The next Fist Demon? That is far beyond me.”

She waved both hands and denied it strongly.

Father’s gaze turned to the Fist Demon. He seemed to be asking, Is that your opinion as well?

The Fist Demon merely looked silently at Cheon Sohee and said nothing one way or the other.

“The Second Young Master is only teasing me for no reason, so please don’t mind him.”

I did not simply pass it off as a joke.

“I said it because I truly think so. Father, there has never been a woman among our cult’s Fist Demons, has there? Don’t you think it would be impressive?”

I said it deliberately. To let her experience becoming a candidate for the next Fist Demon in front of the Heavenly Demon. Wouldn’t that stimulate her more than telling her a hundred times to work hard?

Father, who understood my intention, said a word to Cheon Sohee.

“Strive to improve further.”

“Yes!”

Cheon Sohee would not be sleeping tonight.

After taking care of the two women like that, Father spoke to the Fist Demon.

“Have you been well?”

“Yes, Cult Leader.”

No matter how frightening he looked, no matter how fine his physique was, no matter how large his fists were, in front of Father, the Heavenly Demon, he became as docile as a baby.

“I hear my son has been bothering you.”

“Lately, I have been the one bothering him.”

“What do you mean?”

“Today’s gathering is a welcome party for a new member. I am the new member.”

Perhaps he had not known that much, because Father looked startled, then soon smiled. This was the part that always made me feel hurt. He never showed that smile to his own child, yet in front of the Demon Venerables, he smiled like that.

Father said to Ian,

“You must be having a hard time, taking in such an imposing new member.”

“It’s all right. Lord Fist Demon is…”

For a moment, she hesitated, unsure what to say. She had never properly thought about how to describe or introduce the Fist Demon. And on top of that, she was in front of the Heavenly Demon.

There was no way I would miss such a good opportunity to tease a flustered Ian.

“Please understand. Ian just can’t lie.”

At that, Ian quickly said,

“No. Lord Fist Demon is very impressive, dignified, gentle, has a wonderful physique, and takes good care of us.”

For a moment, silence fell over the place.

“A wonderful physique?”

The Fist Demon, apparently not having expected Ian to say such a thing, also showed a slight hint of embarrassment. Ian, who had said it, was flustered as well.

“Did I say that?”

“Yes. Among all your lies, that was the only truth.”

At my joke, Ian looked at me with force in her eyes. Please stop teasing me! The Cult Leader is here!

Father poured wine into the Fist Demon’s cup.

“You must be having a hard time because of my mischievous son.”

With an expression that seemed to say, So long as you know, the Fist Demon respectfully accepted the wine.

The two drank.

They were two people who seemed comfortable, yet not comfortable. In any case, the Fist Demon found Father difficult to deal with and was uneasy around him.

Still, if you looked closely, Father and the Fist Demon resembled each other in some way. Apart from their actual personalities, both gave off that extremely hard-line feeling of breaking things first and seeing afterward. And both somehow looked lonely.

“This is your first time joining a gathering like this, isn’t it?”

“It is.”

“How is it, now that you’ve tried?”

Had Father come today because he was curious why the Fist Demon had joined this gathering?

“It’s good. Mingling with young friends makes me feel as if I’m growing younger with them.”

“Are you already old enough to say such things? It feels like only yesterday that you were in the prime of your youth. Do you remember? When we fought those orthodox bastards on Mount Wutai for three days and nights?”

“You still remember that?”

“You were truly impressive back then.”

“It is because I failed to overwrite that memory with something even more impressive.”

Father and the Fist Demon.

I had thought that if the two of them met, the atmosphere would stiffen and they would barely converse, but unexpectedly, the two spoke quite well.

If anything, Father seemed a little more friendly and at ease than when he dealt with the other Demon Venerables.

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had said it before. That among the Demon Venerables, the person Father liked most was the Fist Demon. I found myself thinking that might really be true.

On the other hand, the Fist Demon answered only the questions he was asked and treated Father with difficulty. Perhaps that was why this relationship was maintained so well. Problems always erupted when both people became comfortable.

After the cups had gone around a couple of times, Ian, who had been watching for her chance, spoke to Father.

“If I ever had the chance to meet you in person, I wanted to be sure to thank you. Thank you for what happened last time.”

Ian rose from her seat and offered a deep bow. She did not say what she was grateful for, but Father and I both knew. It was gratitude for his permission to pass down the Flying Heaven Sword Art to her.

Father calmly said one thing.

“A great martial art is always accompanied by a great fate.”

It was not solely because of the Flying Heaven Sword Art, but in any case, she was walking a new path of fate.

“I will keep that in mind and do my utmost not to disgrace the great grace you have bestowed upon me.”

I hoped that this moment today would carry even greater meaning for Ian.

“In that spirit, please transmit one move to Ian.”

At my words, Father’s eyes narrowed slightly.

“Now?”

“When else would there be another occasion like this?”

Ian was about to step forward and say it was all right, but I raised my hand first and stopped her.

“To Father, this may be a single drinking gathering, but to Ian, it could become a moment that changes her life, couldn’t it?”

I sent the rest by sound transmission.

—At her age, she has reached the ninth level. You know how much effort that takes, don’t you? That little child grew up so well, didn’t she?

Was it the last sentence that reached him?

Father rose from his seat and said to Ian,

“Follow me outside for a moment.”

Father took Ian and went out.

As she followed him out, Ian looked at me. Am I going to die like this?

As if bidding farewell, I waved my hand. She would not die, but it would be a killer experience.

Of course, I could guide her to Great Completion myself, but the experience of receiving one move of instruction from Father would, separate from raising her martial realm by one level, become a great change and help in the life of a martial artist of the demonic path.

I apologized to the Fist Demon.

“I’m sorry for ruining the atmosphere.”

“When I am with you, I always end up experiencing things I never expected.”

“That’s why everyone likes me.”

The Fist Demon quietly gazed at me, emptied the cup he was holding, then said,

“I said it to that child earlier. The greater the trust, the greater the wound. That mistaken trust will bring misfortune. Thinking about it, I did not say those words to you.”

It was a question asking what I would do if I took care of others like this, only to be betrayed by them.

“At a glance, it may seem as though I trust all sorts of people, but in truth, that isn’t so. I trust this person this much, that person that much. I have my own standards and lines. Trusting everyone, or trusting no one at all—I actually think that is easier. So I intend to take a slightly harder path. The path of fussing and struggling over whether to trust or not. How much should I trust Lord Fist Demon? How can I make him trust me more? I intend to keep going through that endless series of worries.”

It was the first time I revealed to the Fist Demon what kind of person I was.

“So? Do you think that if you do that, you can take everyone with you?”

“Someday, there will come a moment when I can’t put anyone on my boat. Either because I have grown exhausted, or because the boat is too small.”

“Why are you trying so hard to take them aboard? With your current martial arts alone, you could live while enjoying the freedom you spoke of sufficiently.”

“Because I feel as if the dam is about to collapse.”

“!”

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon asked,

“If the dam collapses and a flood comes, are only the people on your boat going to survive?”

“No. Perhaps it may be the opposite.”

“The opposite?”

“My boat will set off to stop the dam from collapsing. Perhaps they may die because they boarded my boat.”

I spoke to Cheon Sohee, who had been listening.

“So you don’t have to get on my boat.”

The look in her eyes as she stared at me said, I think I’ve already boarded, though?

Of course, at least this time, she represented the will of her head, not her heart.

“Naturally, I have no intention of boarding it. I will board the boat Lord Fist Demon is steering. No, I am already on it.”

“Junior Sister!”

“Junior Sister? Why are you calling me Junior Sister? Second Young Master!”

She drew the line clearly in front of the Fist Demon.

“Yes, Martial Artist Cheon. By all means, please succeed!”

After saying it, I laughed first. Cheon Sohee probably wanted to laugh along, but she did not dare to laugh in front of the Fist Demon.

At that moment, the Fist Demon asked calmly,

“Will you put me on that boat too?”

Silence flowed for a moment. Cheon Sohee, looking even more tense than the Fist Demon, waited for my answer.

“I still don’t know. To be honest, I have yet to properly look at you, Lord Fist Demon. What I have seen is only that fist of yours. Right now, all I can think about is wanting to make these baby hands of mine like those fists.”

After hearing my words, the Fist Demon quietly drank. I drank as well, and Cheon Sohee cautiously drank too.

Fist Demon, you can board my elder brother’s boat. If my brother’s boat is not to your liking, then put my brother on your boat. Instead, someday, I will load the entire boat you are all riding onto my boat. I am going this far because I hope my boat will grow that large.

A short while later, Father and Ian came back inside.

Ian’s face was flushed. I did not know what kind of teaching she had received, but to Ian, it was probably something that could be called a fortuitous encounter.

“How was it? Was today’s welcome ceremony well done?”

Ian nodded.

“How about it? Father, will you join this gathering as well?”

At my words, everyone was horrified. Cheon Sohee actually barely managed to cover her mouth with her hand as a scream burst out.

I had expected Father to say, Do I look like someone with that much free time? But he showed an unexpected reaction.

“If I join, are there any benefits?”

“We would have to give you some. This isn’t just anyone joining, but the Heavenly Demon. There are special benefits.”

“What kind of benefits?”

“First, whenever you wish, the four of us will play with you.”

At that, the Fist Demon suddenly said,

“The four of us?”

“It’s good for you too, Lord Fist Demon. Spending an enjoyable time together with the Cult Leader.”

The Fist Demon bristled with his expression and cursed at me with his eyes.

I pretended not to see and turned my head away, and Father asked again.

“And the second benefit?”

“If Father gives us teachings about martial arts, we will accept them at any time.”

“Isn’t that your benefit, not mine?”

“Young people these days are quick to dismiss the teachings of their elders as nagging and let them flow past their ears. There are few chances to go anywhere and show off. So we will listen to every single word without missing even one. You may tell old stories as much as you like!”

“Men and women, young and old, all listen well to what I say.”

Ian and Cheon Sohee bit their lips. With the awareness that it would be disastrous to get swept up in this joke, they held back their laughter.

“That’s not all. We’ll take Father on trips too. When that happens, goodbye to the Heavenly Demon Hall, goodbye to Strategist Sima, and goodbye to the taishi chair.”

“And?”

“You’re quite greedy. What else do you want?”

Then Father said,

“To play go when I wish, and go hunting as well.”

The instant I heard those words, my heart swelled. What Father had just said was not a benefit for himself, but entirely a benefit for me.

“Even with these outrageous benefits, you still won’t join?”

Father emptied his last cup and rose.

“That’s enough for today.”

Just as he had come suddenly, Father left suddenly as well.

Father exchanged farewells with the Fist Demon.

“Let us meet again soon.”

“I will come to see you.”

We all stood and respectfully bowed to Father.

“Since you came all this way, please buy the drinks before you leave!”

At those words, Father really did buy the drinks before leaving. An excited Jo Chunbae was setting aside the money he had received from Father separately.

“This is precious wine we’re drinking at the Heavenly Demon’s expense.”

At my words, Ian and Cheon Sohee held their cups with both hands and drank.

After emptying another cup, the Fist Demon asked me,

“Do you know why the Cult Leader came here today?”

Chapter 191: You Said You’d Only Say One Word.
----------------------------------------------
Why had Father come?

“Obviously, he came to support me.”

I knew full well he was not that sort of person.

Just as it was not easy to figure out why the Fist Demon had joined the late-night training gathering, it was no simple matter to determine why Father had come here today.

“Do you really think so?”

The Fist Demon acted as if he had some guess about Father’s visit.

As I filled his empty cup, I asked,

“Then why do you think he came?”

The Fist Demon emptied the cup again. He drank without the slightest change in his complexion.

“I think the opposite. He came to tell me to support the Eldest Young Master.”

“Did the two of you exchange voice transmissions or something?”

The Fist Demon shook his head.

“Then how do you know Father’s mind?”

“The very fact that he came here gives the answer. The Cult Leader went out of his way to attend the first drinking party of the gathering I’m in with you. To congratulate us? Is the Cult Leader that kind of person?”

“No.”

“Yes, he is not. Then why did he come? He came to let us know. To let us know that he is watching this situation.”

How could the Fist Demon possibly know everything about Father? Still, he must have sensed something to speak like that.

“So in the end, you’re saying Father is on my brother’s side.”

“That, too, cannot be stated with certainty.”

“Didn’t you just say that?”

“I said he came to tell me to take his side. I never said the Cult Leader was on the Eldest Young Master’s side.”

With those ambiguous words, the Fist Demon rose from his seat.

“I was well welcomed today.”

“I’m glad you joined our gathering, Lord Fist Demon.”

“Glad?”

The Fist Demon’s suspicious gaze shifted from me to Ian and Cheon Sohui.

I spoke on behalf of the two women, whose hearts had frozen stiff.

“Of course those two must find it difficult and uncomfortable, but I’m sure their happiness is just as great.”

The Ian I knew would have wanted to joke, “Not at all!” but she nodded with a resolute expression.

“I’m very glad you joined us, Lord Fist Demon!”

“Of course you are. He is Lord Fist Demon, with such a fine physique.”

At my joke, Ian glared at me again, putting strength into her eyes.

The Fist Demon pretended not to hear. Whether it was a joke or a prank, a woman who could be called the greatest beauty under heaven had said he had a fine physique. At the very least, it would not be something to feel bad about.
