In truth, the person most affected by the Fist Demon’s joining was not Ian, but Cheon Sohui.

She said to the Fist Demon in a trembling voice,

“You may not believe me because I’ve made so many mistakes, but… the reason I chose fist arts instead of swordsmanship was because I admired you, Lord Fist Demon. I… want to become like you.”

Perhaps because of the alcohol, or because she was trembling, her face was flushed red. But in her eyes as she looked at the Fist Demon, she was trying to convey her sincerity.

“I am truly glad that you joined our gathering, Lord Fist Demon.”

After gazing at her for a moment, the Fist Demon went down to the first floor without a word.

Cheon Sohui stood there with a face as if she might collapse at any moment. I nodded to tell her she had done well, then called out to the Fist Demon as he left the tavern.

“Next time, you should treat us to drinks, Lord Fist Demon!”

“There will be no next time.”

“There might be a welcome party for my father, you know?”

The Fist Demon stopped at the tavern entrance, then soon snorted and shook his head.

“The Cult Leader? Don’t even dream of it.”

And so the Fist Demon left the tavern.

Now only Ian, Cheon Sohui, and I remained. Ian and Cheon Sohui sank into their seats as if crumbling.

“Aaaah! It’s over.”

Ian let out a sigh of relief and held out her cup.

“Now we can finally drink comfortably. Let’s have a drink, Young Master.”

I clinked cups with her and drank.

Cheon Sohui’s hands were trembling.

“I’ll drink in a little while. I can’t even hold my cup.”

When everyone had been together, she had been swept up in the situation without time to think, but now that the Heavenly Demon and the Fist Demon had actually left, it seemed like she felt she had been dreaming. Then again, she had drunk with the Heavenly Demon, and had personally told the Fist Demon that she admired him. Things she could never have imagined before had all happened in a single day.

“Ah, I was so tense that my whole body feels stiff.”

“What do you think? If I told you to drink with them again, could you?”

At my question, Cheon Sohui gave an answer that did not match the state of her body.

“It was so hard… but that tension wasn’t bad.”

Though she had looked flustered lately after meeting me and getting caught up in various incidents, she was the foremost Iron Fist of the Eastern Fist School, a woman with more grit than anyone.

“It’s not that I could drink. I want to drink.”

“Stimulating things really are thrilling, aren’t they?”

Cheon Sohui smiled faintly in agreement.

Now my gaze turned to Ian.

“How do you feel?”

“How do you think I feel?”

It was the day she had received martial instruction from none other than the Heavenly Demon.

“It wasn’t simply that I was happy the Cult Leader taught me martial arts.”

“Then?”

“The fact that he taught me martial arts means he formally acknowledged me, doesn’t it?”

Though she could not bring herself to say it, by personally passing on the Flying Heaven Sword Art, Father had acknowledged that she was as close as blood kin. The Flying Heaven Sword Art was a martial art passed down only to blood relatives.

“This will be a day I’ll never forget. Thank you, Young Master.”

“What did I do?”

“You said that to the Cult Leader, didn’t you? That to Father, it might be just one drinking party, but to Ian, it could be a seat that changes her life. If not for those words, it never would have happened.”

Ian remembered exactly what I had said to Father.

“Today, it felt like I was the one being welcomed.”

I smiled brightly and patted her shoulder once. Welcome, Ian.

And so the drinking party at Pungryu Tavern came to an end.

* * *

The welcome party was over, but my day was not.

When I returned to my quarters, someone unexpected was waiting for me.

The person standing in the courtyard of my residence, looking up at the moon in the night sky, was my older brother, Geom Muyang.

“When did you get here?”

“A little while ago.”

Judging by the feeling, he had probably been waiting for quite some time. What had he thought about while he waited?

“I had a drink with Lord Fist Demon. If I’d known this would happen, we could have drunk together.”

Geom Muyang only looked up at the moon in silence.

I stood beside him.

“You look tired.”

At my words, Geom Muyang’s gaze shifted from the moon to me.

“Do I? I’ve had a lot of work lately. What about you?”

He seemed softer than usual. I sensed it. Geom Muyang was making a desperate effort. The more he was driven into a corner, the harder he was trying.

In a way, I felt sorry for him. It was true that his life had become more difficult because I had regressed. This fight had been decided from the start. A young man in his twenties could not possibly stand against me. And after regressing, I had been growing stronger without end.

Still, Brother, we cannot live the same lives as before. If things go as they did, only death awaits us.

“Does it bother you that I’m meeting the Fist Demon?”

“It would be a lie to say it doesn’t.”

“I’ve been completely absorbed in fist arts lately. You won’t believe that, will you?”

“Would you believe it if you were me?”

“You should meet the Demon Supremes comfortably too. Meet the Blood Heaven Blade Demon and talk about books, meet the Drunken Demon and drink with him. I’m fine with it.”

“So you’re confident, is that it? You think they’re already your people? Let me give you one piece of advice as your older brother. Do not take the Demon Supremes lightly. Just because they are smiling in front of you now, if you think they are your people, you are gravely mistaken. In the end, at the final moment, they will choose whatever benefits them.”

I smiled as I looked at him.

“Are you mocking me?”

“No. I was just wondering when the last time was that you gave me advice or counsel. It’s been quite a while, hasn’t it?”

Geom Muyang turned his head and looked at the wall cast in moon-shadow.

Between us stood a wall several times higher and thicker than that one.

“The Fist Demon said this. That he supports you, Brother. And that Father would want that as well.”

For an instant, Geom Muyang flinched in surprise.

“Is that true?”

“Yes.”

“Why are you telling me that?”

“Brother. This is the kind of person I originally am. I’m not someone who throws a wrench in things when the other person comes out kindly.”

I revealed to him what I had not told him for a long time.

“Do you know why I told the Demon Supremes that I would fight you for the succession without shedding blood?”

“Why?”

Geom Muyang looked genuinely curious.

“When we were young, you did things to me you shouldn’t have. You planted fear of the murim in me when I was a child and led me to break down psychologically. Back then, just thinking of the word murim made me shrink back and feel afraid.”

This was the first time I had brought up this matter to him. Geom Muyang’s eyes trembled. It seemed he had thought I did not know. Then again, from his perspective, he had told me all sorts of things while pretending to worry about me.

He did not deny it.

“Then you should take revenge. Why say we shouldn’t shed blood?”

“Because you were only a bad person to that extent.”

“!”

“If you had been a more worthless piece of trash, you would have killed me somehow. You would have destroyed my dantian, or used some other vicious method. But at the very least, you were not someone who wanted to become the successor even at the cost of harming your younger brother. You were bad to a degree I could understand and allow. That’s why. With my generous heart, I decided to forgive and understand up to that point. You were young back then, too.”

Of course, the fact that Father wanted it was also a major reason.

“Crazy bastard. What bullshit.”

A curse he rarely used slipped out. Geom Muyang was flustered. Perhaps, at this very moment, he was being bombarded by questions deep in his heart.

‘If you had not been confident you would become the successor, what would have happened then? Would you still have left your younger brother alone? Wasn’t it because you were confident of becoming the successor that you stopped at that point?’

Just what kind of person was my brother?

In truth, even I did not know that much. It was something that had not happened, and no one could guarantee what choice a desperate person would make in that moment.

“But don’t let your guard down. You and I are still standing on the testing ground. If I say I will fight for the succession without shedding blood and then kill you to become the Cult Leader, not a single Demon Supreme will follow me sincerely. The same goes for you. If you suppress me through some clumsy scheme and ascend to the Cult Leader’s seat, the Demon Supremes will never offer you sincere loyalty. Don’t forget. It may seem like they aren’t watching, but the Demon Supremes are always keeping their eyes on us.”

Geom Muyang, who had been staring at me silently, suddenly said,

“Why have you become so talkative? You weren’t like this when you were young.”

“I guess all my internal energy went to my mouth.”

“That’s not funny at all.”

Had one brick, at least, fallen from the thick wall between him and me because of what I had said for the first time? Hoping that was the case, I nagged him even more. With my brother, I had no choice but to unleash the Divine Art of Nagging.

“Since I’ve become a chatterbox anyway, let me say just one last thing. They say there is no coexistence between brothers in a power struggle? That they will kill each other in the end? Who decided that? Why should we be swayed by the precedent and suspicion of pathetic fools who couldn’t even control their own desires?”

I moved from his side to stand in front of Geom Muyang. Then I looked into his eyes and said,

“So don’t even dream of being swayed. If anxiety that I might kill you starts creeping in, come find me. Once you see me, those thoughts will disappear. Don’t think I looked down on you today! Don’t think you lost. Just think, ‘My younger brother talks a lot and is good with words.’ Think of it that way. Don’t flare up! Don’t decide anything hastily!”

When I finished speaking, Geom Muyang burst out,

“You bastard! I thought my ears were going to bleed. You said you’d say just one thing—was that one thing? If you ever feel like killing me, you come find me too! A young brat lecturing me!”

Geom Muyang strode out with long steps.

Do you know this, Brother? You may find me difficult, but I do too. More than Father, more than the Eight Demon Supremes, you are the one I find most difficult.

For a while, I stood in the courtyard and looked up at the moon he had been looking at. It was a night that brought to mind the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s words: that family can be one’s enemy.

* * *

The next day and the day after that, we trained diligently.

We trained hard during the day, and we trained hard at night. It was truly an unbroken succession of training.

Several days passed like that, and today as well, we were deep in late-night training.

It was when I was practicing forms in the exact same posture as the Fist Demon again today.

Whoooosh!

The Fist Demon’s fist flew toward me.

It looked like a mistake, but it was not one.

Boom!

My fist and the Fist Demon’s fist collided in midair.

At the sound like a drum bursting, Ian and Cheon Sohui looked at us in shock.

We were thrusting our fists out in the same posture, and those fists had met in midair.

“My apologies. My mind wandered for a moment.”

“That can happen.”

“What do you think? Since we have already used our strength like this, shall we have a bout?”

“A fine attempt, but I decline.”

At last, the Fist Demon was beginning to play his little games that were not quite games. He wanted to fight me about ten times more than I wanted to learn his martial arts.

“If you declined, you should turn away. Why are you staring at me like that?”

For him, it was probably an unfamiliar experience. Almost no one would stare at him like this.

Now I intended to truly look at that Fist Demon. I had looked at his fists first, but now I meant to look at him. Straight on, without any prejudice.

“The more I look at your face, Lord Fist Demon, the less frightening you seem.”

The Fist Demon shook his head with an expression of disbelief, then gathered his clothes and put them on as if to say today’s training ended here.

After he left, Ian and Cheon Sohui came to either side of me and said,

“Liar!”

“The more you look, the scarier he gets.”

At their joke, filled with sincerity, I smiled faintly.

My gaze turned to the Fist Demon walking far away. Showing his broad, thick back, the Fist Demon was walking alone into the darkness.

What kept entering my eyes was not his face. In that lonely-looking back, I… saw the image of myself before my regression.

Chapter 192: The Charm of Slacking Off.
---------------------------------------
The next day, after finishing my Black Fist training, I followed after the Fist Demon.

“Have a meal with me today.”

The Fist Demon always ate alone. Of course, the other Demon Supremes were no different, but the Fist Demon would not even occasionally share a meal with his subordinates.

“No need.”

“Why not?”

“It’s uncomfortable.”

“If I’m uncomfortable, that’s one thing, but you, Fist Demon, feeling uncomfortable? Who in the world would believe that?”

“You make me uncomfortable.”

The Fist Demon strode off first. Even with his subordinates around, he openly said he was uncomfortable, and I was trailing after such a Fist Demon asking him to eat with me. The Iron Fists stared at us with startled faces.

Of course, giving up was not an option. The next day, I followed after the Fist Demon again.

“Then how about a cup of tea today?”

“I don’t like tea.”

“They say drinking a lot of tea is good for the body.”

The Fist Demon stopped walking.

“Just what are you doing? Do you want to put poison in that tea?”

“Of course not. If I poison you, I won’t be able to learn the next verse, will I?”

“True. You would have to learn it all before poisoning me.”

“You misunderstand.”

Even at my shout, the Fist Demon strode away.

The next day, when training ended, I followed him again.

“Do you happen to know how to play baduk?”

“I do not. What is a manly man supposed to do with those tiny stones? It’s not a little girls’ game of house.”

“My father happens to be deeply obsessed with that game of house these days.”

“……”

The Fist Demon strode away without even looking back. Perhaps because I had mentioned Father, his steps were even quicker than the day before.

The next day, and the day after that, the Fist Demon remained an iron wall.

This was not going to be easy.

I had succeeded in dunking the Fist Demon into a boiling cauldron, but this large, sturdy opponent showed no sign of softening. Even if I wanted to boil him and eat him, he would not cook.

Whenever I felt this stifled, one person came to mind.

I headed straight for the Southern Blade Sect, where the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was.

* * *

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was passing martial arts down to Seo Daeryong.

Now an official disciple, Seo Daeryong was being taught the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s unique martial art, the Annihilating Heaven Demon Blade Form.

So as not to disturb their training, I went to the backyard, quietly closed my eyes, sat cross-legged, and circulated the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

The Flying Heaven Sword Art had reached the twelfth-star great completion, and the Wind God Four Steps had also reached great completion. Among the martial arts I had learned, the one that absolutely had to reach great completion was the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art.

After I had been training for some time, Seo Daeryong’s cautious voice came from a distance.

“Training is over.”

He had quietly informed me in case he might disturb my training.

When I opened my eyes, Seo Daeryong, drenched in sweat, plopped down beside me.

“Is it hard?”

“I’m dying.”

Now that he had begun training in earnest, it seemed Seo Daeryong was truly going through one hellish session after another.

“I’ll tell him. Our Inspector Seo is having too hard a time. Please go easy on him.”

“Of course. There are many ways to kill a person, after all.”

I quietly looked at Seo Daeryong’s profile.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“You’ve changed. Your aura.”

“How?”

“You look strong.”

At that single sentence, Seo Daeryong’s mood brightened at once. He clenched both fists.

“Just you wait!”

“Evil men of the world! No, women of the world! Isn’t that what you mean?”

“Was it that obvious?”

“Hang in there, my right arm! Without these grinding hours, you’ll be killed by villains and rejected by women.”

“What if I get rejected even after grinding myself down?”

Seo Daeryong’s deer-like eyes were hoping for the answer, “That would never happen.”

“In that case, let’s catch at least one more villain. Ah! The villains of the world might go extinct.”

“No! How could you?”

I laughed loudly, and Seo Daeryong laughed along with me.

He rose from his seat and said his farewell.

“Then I’ll see you tomorrow. Oh, and what you said about me looking strong—please say it again next time too! Whenever I’m having a hard time.”

“Sure.”

After sending him off, I went to the front yard. The Blood Heaven Blade Demon was sitting against the Annihilating Heaven Great Blade, wiping away his sweat.

“You must be busy throwing punches, so why have you come? Already sick of it?”

“Yes. I’m sick of it. I deeply regret saying I wanted to learn fist techniques. I should have just gone around having fun with you, Elder.”

“Who said I would have fun with you?”

I went over and sat beside the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“No matter how much I stoke the fire, he shows no sign of cooking.”

“You’re trying to cook a boulder. Do you think it’ll cook?”

“That’s why I came to see you, Elder.”

“You thought you might try adding some old ginger to the boiling water?”

“This is why I like you, Elder. You understand me at once.”

“What is it you want from the Fist Demon? His martial arts? Or his heart?”

“At first, it was his martial arts.”

“And now you want his heart too?”

“He wants to bring down a cliff. At first, I thought that was impressive, so I wanted to bring it down too. But now I’m curious why the Fist Demon wants so desperately to bring that cliff down.”

“If you find out, it will probably be for some insignificant reason.”

“Isn’t everyone like that? Myself included.”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon looked at me as if I were an old man in a young man’s body.

“I’m sorry you came all this way, but I don’t know much about the Fist Demon.”

“It’s all right. I only came because I felt stifled and wanted to see you, Elder.”

Spicy ginger was used not for cooking, but for healing.

“Listen, Second Young Master.”

“Yes, Elder.”

“Are you all right?”

“!”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon asked me.

If I was all right.

If someone else had asked, I would have said I was fine, but I did not want to do that with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon.

“I’m not sure.”

“Saying you don’t know is more dangerous than saying you’re not all right. When you’re tired, rest.”

It was a moment when I once again felt that the Blood Heaven Blade Demon was sincerely worried about me. These days, in front of him, I found myself disarmed. I had tried so hard to open his door, but perhaps my own door had been thrown wide open first.

“Then, Elder, may I lean against your blade too?”

“No. Seeing you talk nonsense like that, you’re perfectly fine. Perfectly fine.”

“Just once. I’ve truly wanted to lean on it just once for a long time.”

When I pestered him, the Blood Heaven Blade Demon finally gave in, pretending he had no choice.

“Then just once.”

I leaned against the Annihilating Heaven Great Blade.

“Ah! Now I know why you lean here.”

“It feels like something reliable is protecting you, doesn’t it?”

“No. It’s incredibly cool.”

There was a primal chill within the Annihilating Heaven Great Blade.

“So on those sweltering summer days, you were the only one staying cool!”

Perhaps he had not expected me to accuse him like that, because the Blood Heaven Blade Demon chuckled and said, “Why, you little rascal.”

After joking around with Seo Daeryong and laughing like this with the Blood Heaven Blade Demon, much of my frustration eased. And I had obtained a great method of healing as well.

“When I’m tired, I’ll rest.”

“And when it’s hard, come anytime.”

That one word, anytime, pierced my chest.

“Elder, how would I live without you?”

“You’d live just fine, throwing punches with the Fist Demon. Stop saying useless things and go back. You have to do that midnight training or whatever it is.”

“No, seriously. How does the Demonic Cult have no secrets at all?”

The Blood Heaven Blade Demon picked up his blade and went into the house as he said,

“Who do you think they take after, having such loose lips?”

* * *

I accepted the Blood Heaven Blade Demon’s advice.

The next day, I did not go to Black Fist training. At the time training would have been in full swing, I was in the world inside the Space-Time Transposition Art.

A blue sky and sea resembling my sword aura, a sandy beach beneath blazing sunlight, the shade of broad-leaved trees. Even a cool mat. I lay there in absolute comfort.

Today was a day for slacking off.

Ever since returning from the Martial Alliance, I had gotten entangled with the Fist Demon and had not properly rested for even a moment.

So today, I intended to spend time alone. I decided not to think about anyone else.

With drinks, snacks, and even books to read prepared, it was truly the perfect day of idleness.

I looked at the sky, looked at the sea, and then watched a crab crawling across the sand. I read, dozed off, woke up, and then stared blankly at the sea again.

“Ah! This is nice!”

It was so nice it was almost too much.

And so I lazed around all day.

That afternoon, someone came to my residence. It was Cheon Sohi.

“Senior Brother, Senior Brother.”

It was cute how she called me Second Young Master in front of the Fist Demon, but Senior Brother when it was just the two of us.

“Where did he go?”

I could see her peering into my room through the window. Since I had gone to Black Fist training every single day without fail, it seemed she was worried that I had suddenly not shown up.

After pacing around the yard for a while, she left the residence.

Her visit had been entirely expected. But a little later, someone I had never expected came by. To be precise, it was not a visit, but someone passing by.

Amazingly, it was the Fist Demon. As if he had other business and was merely passing in front of my house, he walked past my residence. As he passed, his eyes subtly turned toward my home. Raising his qi to check whether I was inside, he went by.

The way he pretended not to notice was so funny, and at the same time, it moved me.

Truly, I wanted to dispel the Space-Time Transposition Art and run out. I wanted to ask if he had come because he was worried about me, and to see him flustered. But I did not.

You people, learn how precious I am!

I rolled around until late, then replenished my internal energy through breathing exercises, then rolled around again. It seemed like an uneventful day, but to me, it was a precious time of recharging.

And then it was time for midnight training.

The three of them arrived on time, and as soon as she came, Cheon Sohi asked me,

“Why didn’t you come to Black Fist training today?”

When Ian heard that I had not gone to training, she was startled.

“Young Master! Are you sick? How badly are you hurt?”

“Why are you making such a fuss?”

“There’s no way you would miss training, Young Master. What is it? What happened?”

“I’m human too. There are times when I want to slack off and times when I want to rest.”

As I said that, I looked at the Fist Demon.

“Isn’t that right?”

The Fist Demon looked at me for a moment, then shook his head.

“I have never done that.”

“Really?”

“If I had lived such an indolent life, someone else would be in the position of Fist Demon now.”

“Isn’t it hard?”

“Not at all.”

I know. What truly exhausts a person is not that the work itself is difficult, but that no one recognizes that hardship, and even if they do, they pass over it indifferently.

“If it’s hard, come play with me! I’m always on standby.”

Of course, the Fist Demon did not even pretend to hear.

Around the time training was ending that day—

Whoooosh!

An unfamiliar sound of wind came from my fist. It was not thunder, but neither was it the sound my fists had made until now.

“What was that sound?”

At my question, the Fist Demon looked at me in surprise.

“It is the sound of wind and rain rolling in.”

It meant that the time for thunder was not far off.

At my unbelievably rapid progress, fighting spirit rose from the Fist Demon’s entire body. His expression looked as though he wanted to rush at me right then and there.

He instinctively took a step closer, and I stepped back. At the hair-trigger situation that seemed as though a fierce battle might break out at any moment, Ian and Cheon Sohi also grew tense.

I quickly said to him,

“Shall we make a bet?”

“What bet?”

“I don’t want to fight by exchanging blows, so how about a lightness skill contest? If I win, please teach me the second fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.”

“And if I win?”

“I’ll have a match with you, Fist Demon.”

A satisfied smile appeared at the corners of the Fist Demon’s mouth.

“You must have thought that because my body is this large, I would be slow.”

Masters of fist techniques naturally had astonishing footwork, and footwork was connected to lightness skills.

“Good. I accept that bet.”

The Fist Demon was full of confidence. He was confident enough in his lightness skill to wager the second form of his unique martial art.

“To the entrance of Maga Village. How about it?”

“That place isn’t suitable for a duel. If you go about ten li farther from the entrance, there is a bamboo grove. Let’s make it there.”

In the Fist Demon’s mind, he had already won the bet.

Ian drew a line on the ground, and we stood side by side on it.

“Ready, start!”

The two of us shot forward at the same time like arrows loosed from a bow. I could imagine how surprised the two women must have been at the sight of us vanishing from view in an instant.

The Fist Demon truly was fast. I was certain he was the fastest among the Demon Supremes. That was likely why he had accepted the bet, but he could not be faster than my Rapid Step.

I matched my speed to his and ran by the narrowest margin. The Fist Demon’s astonishment was carried to me through his rough breathing.

I arrived at the bamboo grove a few steps ahead of him. I acted as if I had barely won, panting for breath. The Fist Demon, who arrived soon after, looked at me in surprise. It seemed he had never imagined I would be faster than him.

“First, teach me the Second Fist, and if you feel it’s unfair, we can bet the Third Fist and go again.”

When the Fist Demon’s fearsome face contorted into a fearsome expression, it was truly terrifying. But terrifying was terrifying. He was not one to break his word.

“Very well. I shall teach you the Second Fist right here.”

His decision, made without a moment’s hesitation, genuinely surprised me inwardly. He was truly a man among men.

It was too precious a martial art to give away in a qinggong bet. What could the Fist Demon be thinking right now? Could it be that he truly had not considered from the start that he might lose? I was curious about his true intentions.

“Will you be all right?”

“Are you asking if I’m all right after dragging me into a trap?”

Surely you didn’t fall into that trap on purpose, did you?

Second Fist, Thunderbolt Asura.

If the First Fist, Black Cloud Asura, was about focusing all one’s power onto a single opponent to land a strike, Thunderbolt Asura was a technique that threw punches at a speed invisible to the eye. It emphasized speed over power, similar to the Changcheon Form of the Swift Sword within the Bicheon Swordsmanship.

The Fist Demon imparted its mnemonic formulas and stances to me.

I exhausted all my mental strength to make his teachings entirely my own, leaving nothing out.

And so, the Fist Demon’s second fist became mine as well.

“Thank you truly. This fist shall never be misused.”

I bowed deeply to him. We were not yet in a master-disciple relationship, yet I had received instruction up to the second fist.

“Since you’ve already come all the way out here, get some fresh air before heading back in.”

The Fist Demon looked at me with an expression asking what I was talking about.

“It’s been quite a while since you left the main sect, hasn’t it? I skipped training yesterday and had such a wonderful time. Fist Demon, come slack off with me.”

Snorting as if the very idea were absurd, the Fist Demon turned his steps toward the main sect. Watching his large, broad back, I spoke.

“Because you go out every day like that, no one realizes how precious you are.”

The Fist Demon halted his steps dead.

“Your goodwill toward the main sect has continued for far too long.”

I had not described it as goodwill toward his subordinates. I did not wish to belittle the natural sentiments of the subordinates who feared the Fist Demon, nor the Fist Demon’s affection for them.

The Fist Demon turned to face me.

Our gazes intertwined in the empty air. For a long moment, neither of us spoke. He was an intelligent man, and he understood the hidden meaning in my words.

“You look tired.”

This was the second time I had said it to him. Had those words finally struck home?

After a brief pause, the Fist Demon asked.

“Is there somewhere to go?”

I smiled broadly.

“The beauty of slacking off lies in having no plans. Let’s go.”

Before his mind could change, I began to run, and the Fist Demon ran alongside me.

We matched our speed and ran roughly side by side. On the face of the man running beside me, I caught a fleeting glimpse of something—an exhilaration I had never once seen before.

And so, in an instant, we grew distant from the sect. We grew distant from our daily lives.

Chapter 193: Scary but Handsome.
--------------------------------
Gwonma and I stood atop the cliff where the waterfall plunged down.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa!

The enormous spray created by the waterfall was refreshing enough to clear the heart of anyone who saw it.

“Now, absolutely no using internal energy.”

“You’d best not break the promise yourself.”

What we were about to do was jump from a high place. There was nothing at stake, really. It was simply a battle of pride.

When I was young, girls had watched from below as I looked down from up here…but now I was playing that dangerous game with Gwonma in these remote, rugged mountains.

“That thin torso of yours might smash to pieces when it hits the rocks.”

“Your enormous torso will hit the rocks twice as hard, Gwonma.”

The looks we exchanged held the resolve not to lose under any circumstances.

We jumped at the same time.

At my level, why would I be afraid or feel my heart sink merely because I was jumping without using internal energy? Even so, I deliberately let out a refreshing scream.

“Waaaaaaaah!”

Splash!

Kicking up a tremendous spray of water, Gwonma’s figure plunged into the water first.

Gwonma soon raised his head above the surface.

By then, I was floating atop the water using Water-Gliding Steps. I had used my internal energy before falling into the water. I crouched in front of Gwonma, who had raised his head, and said cheekily,

“I lost.”

Gwonma looked dumbfounded for a moment at the mischief on my face, but soon burst into loud laughter that echoed through the valley.

Well, if I’d made him laugh, that was enough.

I also jumped into the water with a splash.

The ice-cold water instantly cleared my head.

While I swam through the water, Gwonma climbed onto the broad rock at the water’s edge on the opposite bank.

Jumping from a waterfall without using inner power.

Had Gwonma ever done such a thing even once in his life? No—he probably had never even thought of it.

“The water is really clear.”

After swimming for quite a while, I also climbed onto the rock where Gwonma was.

I could have dried my clothes by generating heat with my inner power, but instead I took them off and spread them over the rock.

“I’m going to lie down for a bit.”

Then I flopped onto my back and gazed up at the sky.

“You should lie down too, Gwonma.”

“I’m fine.”

“How long has it been since you last lay down in front of other people?”

“I don’t even remember.”

“Then lie down at least now.”

Despite my urging, Gwonma remained seated.

For a while, we gazed silently up at the sky.

If he wanted to work off all the fatigue piled up inside him by loafing around, even crossing the Central Plains from one end to the other would not be enough. That was why I intended to keep moving farther away from the Cult.

“Let’s go now.”

“Let’s stay a little longer.”

Gwonma looked anxious and restless. I understood him. His life in the Cult had been busy from morning to night. He had taught everyone from the White Fists to the Black Fists! When I thought about it, it truly had not been an easy life.

After sitting like that for a long while, Gwonma abruptly stood up.

Unable to bear the restlessness any longer, he seemed ready to perform a fist technique, but I quickly stopped him.

“You’ve been throwing punches your whole life. Just endure it this once.”

“My body is stiff and sore from doing nothing but sitting. I can’t stand it.”

“Now, now, sit down first.”

I made him sit down again. He was having trouble adapting to this idleness.

“That’s right. They say it’s harder for people to remain still. But they also say that spacing out like this is good for your mental health.”

“Where did you hear that?”

“I picked it up somewhere.”

The sky beyond Gwonma, who was looking down at me, was blue.

“Gwonma, look at those clouds drifting behind you.”

Gwonma turned his head and looked up at the sky. As he gazed at the clouds, I could sense distracting thoughts entering his eyes.

“Don’t worry about your subordinates.”

Gwonma looked at me with an expression that asked how I had known what he was thinking.

“They’re probably celebrating right now. Tell your subordinates to relax and loaf around a little too. The leader loafs around, and the subordinates loaf around. How wonderful is that?”

“Then the organization would fall apart.”

“So what if it falls apart a little?”

For an instant, Gwonma flinched.

“You’ve never once thought, ‘So what if it falls apart?’ have you?”

“Of course not.”

“If it falls apart, there are other Demon Lords, aren’t there? If it falls apart, you still have your father, don’t you? What’s wrong with getting scolded a little? What’s wrong with having your pride hurt a little?”

The corner of Gwonma’s mouth curled up slightly. It might have been a derisive smile saying, What do you know? Yet it also looked, in some inexplicable way, like a smile of relief.

“Just forget everything. Forget all those fist-fighters with their colors.”

How long had we remained like that?

Gwonma finally relaxed his posture. He leaned back slightly, supporting himself with both arms behind him. He did not lie all the way down, but he did seem somewhat more comfortable than before.

We continued staring blankly at the sky until our clothes had completely dried.

* * *

After descending the mountain, we walked along a narrow path.

“Are we not going to use lightness kung fu?”

“The scenery is beautiful. Let’s walk until the end of this path.”

As expected, he seemed to be itching to move.

“Wouldn’t it be better to go to our destination and rest there?”

Whenever I heard things like that, I kept thinking of myself before my regression.

I had been like that when traveling to obtain the materials for the Grand Art. No matter what happened, I would rush all the way to my destination. Walking had never even entered my mind.

“They say taking a walk is good for people.”

“Doing nothing is good, walking is good. Is there anything that isn’t good?”

“There are plenty. Coming out to loaf around and worrying about work. Thinking only about someone who isn’t thinking about me at all. Coming out to have fun while thinking about how to collapse a cliff. There are lots of them.”

After a brief pause, he asked,

“Did my life seem that bad to you?”

“Yes.”

At the answer without the slightest hesitation, Gwonma stopped walking and looked at me.

“It didn’t seem bad because it was bad. It seemed bad because it was too perfect.”

A hint of relief passed over his face.

“I know what you’re worried about, but I’m not someone who will collapse easily.”

“Are you sure?”

“What?”

“Are you really someone who won’t collapse easily? How do you know? You’ve never collapsed even once.”

Gwonma could not answer.

“Whenever I look at Father’s grand chair, it reminds me of the platform where you stand and look down at your subordinates. I feel that both of you are trapped there.”

“!”

Gwonma, you made it this far with that unchanging steadfastness of yours. Can’t you rest a little now?

He said nothing. We walked in silence for a long while.

At the end of the narrow path, I asked Gwonma,

“Is there anything you’d like to try?”

Gwonma could not easily find an answer and asked me in return,

“What about you?”

“I’m doing it right now.”

A faint smile appeared around Gwonma’s lips. Ian and Cheon Sohee said they found that expression frightening no matter how often they saw it, but Gwonma’s face did not frighten me. In truth, it had never frightened me from the beginning.

“Then, I’ll leave it to you.”

“Then let’s start by going to eat something delicious. A trip isn’t complete without good food, is it?”

I ran ahead first, and Gwonma ran alongside me. He began taking the lead, pouring out all the speed he had been holding back.

* * *

“Here we are. A famous restaurant with a history of two hundred years!”

We had arrived at a place called Songmyeong Hall, which sold both alcohol and food. It was so popular that customers were lined up outside.

“How did you know about this place?”

“Is that all I know? Their signature dish is bamboo shoot cuisine, which you like, Gwonma.”

“And how did you know that?”

“Don’t look at me like that. I didn’t investigate your favorite foods because I intended to poison them. You’re going to be my future master, so I should at least know that much.”

The man standing in line ahead of us glanced back, then was startled when he saw Gwonma. Trembling, he said,

“W-would you like to go ahead of me?”

“No need.”

The man turned back at Gwonma’s reply and never looked behind him again.

I smiled and asked Gwonma,

“You’ve never stood in line to buy food in your entire life, have you?”

At Gwonma’s expression, which clearly said, Do you think I have?, I asked again,

“Isn’t it interesting?”

Gwonma looked around. The people standing in line, the people newly joining the line behind them, the passersby, and the street vendors hawking their wares.

“It’s not bad.”

Songmyeong Hall was such a large restaurant, with more than ten servers alone, that the line moved quickly.

When our turn came, we took a seat and ordered several dishes and some wine.

“Why order so much?”

“We came all this way after so long. We should eat everything before we leave.”

The wine came first, and as I poured a cup for Gwonma, his displeased gaze turned toward a group of martial artists passing outside, cursing among themselves.

I reached out and blocked his view.

“We’re loafing around! That’s a rock. That’s a chair. Understand?”

“Understood.”

As Gwonma poured me a cup as well, he said,

“At your age, with your martial arts, it must be difficult to restrain your hot blood.”

“That’s why I’m playing with the Demon Lords, isn’t it? So I don’t cause any unnecessary trouble.”

“You may have other reasons for associating with us, but avoiding trouble is commendable.”

“Oh! That’s the first compliment you’ve ever given me, Gwonma.”

I raised my cup, and we toasted.

A little later, the dishes arrived. After tasting a piece of his favorite bamboo shoot dish, Gwonma was astonished.

“It’s truly delicious.”

“There’s always a reason restaurants have lines.”

My gaze shifted toward the kitchen.

“What do you think? Should we kidnap the head chef and take him back to the Cult?”

“You’re saying something you don’t mean.”

“Why are you so certain? If we take him, then we take him.”

After staring at me for a moment, Gwonma unexpectedly said,

“I heard your demonic path is one that doesn’t break tavern tables.”

I was startled. I had not expected Gwonma to know that.

“How did you hear about that?”

“I have ears to listen with.”

This proved that what I had told my elder brother was correct. Even when they seemed not to be watching, the Demon Lords were observing everything.

“Yes. So when others stand in line, we stand in line and eat too. If the chef is kidnapped, we go rescue him. That is my demonic path.”

Gwonma stared intently at me. How would he interpret my demonic path?

He seemed about to say something, but then decided against it and focused on eating again.

We were drinking when one of the two women seated beside us spoke to me.

“Young hero, is this your first time here?”

“Yes, it is.”

“Then there are some places you absolutely must visit.”

“Would you join us and tell me about them in detail?”

The women glanced at Gwonma, then readily joined us.

I ordered new dishes for them.

I could sense Gwonma’s surprise. The natural way in which they had joined our table seemed to be a fresh shock to him.

A woman he had never met had spoken to him first and then joined his table?

That was why he kept his guard up around them. He narrowed his eyes, wondering whether they were plotting something, but the women were simply young girls who liked to have fun.

The reason I had invited them to join us was Gwonma.

Gwonma had lived his entire life as a bachelor. There was no record of him having any dealings with women. Perhaps he had given up on romance from the start because of his appearance.

That was why I wanted to give Gwonma experiences he had never had before.

We drank and chatted with the women about this and that. I made them laugh with amusing stories. Gwonma, who had initially been wary, eventually began enjoying the gathering with interest in his eyes.

Whenever there was something Gwonma could answer better, I asked him. For example:

“Please tell these ladies about the time you broke through the Martial Alliance’s Heavenly Net when you were young.”

Of course, the women laughed uproariously, not believing him. It was a claim that went beyond the usual boasting men did at drinking tables. Gwonma looked at me questioningly. Really? Should I tell them? I smiled and shook my head. No, no one will believe you.

After we had enjoyed ourselves for about half a shichen, the women stood up first.

That was even more astonishing to Gwonma. They were just going to leave after all that? Without even making plans to meet again?

After they left, Gwonma asked,

“Did you send them a secret transmission promising to pay them separately? Or did you threaten to kill them if they refused to join us? Or were they women targeting our money pouches?”

I could not believe he would think of things like that.

“If none of those were the case, why would the women have joined us?”

“That’s because of me, of course.”

“I know that. One of them stared only at your face the entire time.”

“Exactly. That’s why they asked to join us.”

“Even though I was beside you?”

“Would they not have joined us because they were afraid after seeing Gwonma’s face?”

Gwonma nodded.

“There are two things you don’t understand, Gwonma. Young people these days can easily get along and have fun with strangers.”

“What kind of terrible fate are they inviting by doing that?”

“They’re fearless, these youngsters.”

“You’re not?”

“Me included. I’m fearless too, aren’t I?”

“You’re too fearless. And what’s the second thing?”

“That while it’s true you look frightening, Gwonma, you aren’t ugly. In fact, you’re on the handsome side.”

Gwonma was shocked. It was his most astonished reaction since I had met him. He had probably never heard anyone call him handsome in his entire life.

“Even if you’re going to flatter me, at least make it flattery I can accept.”

“I’m good at flattery, but I’m not flattering you now. Women might find your face attractive. You also have a reassuring air, as if you would protect them no matter what happened.”

Gwonma seemed unsure what to say, so I added,

“Today’s conclusion: Gwonma has a frightening but handsome face!”

After emptying his cup, Gwonma reached his conclusion.

“You’re a madman.”

“In the end, I even got to hear that from Gwonma!”

Just then, a young server came walking toward us. He was perhaps only ten years old. His eyes were swollen from crying so much.

“I’ve been watching you for a while. Somehow, I thought it would be all right to ask a great hero like you for help.”

The child pleaded with Gwonma.

“Please, help me.”

From the tears gathered in the child’s eyes, I could feel the weight of suffering too great even for an adult to bear.

I gently patted the child’s head and said to Gwonma,

“And not only is your face handsome, it also looks like you would help when an innocent child asks for it.”

Chapter 194: Because I Chose a Handsome Face.
---------------------------------------------
“Heeya. You mustn’t move yet.”

Mrs. Shim took her daughter Heeya’s hand.

Heeya was getting dressed, her face and entire body covered in wounds. The girl had suffered the greatest misfortune of her life. Three days ago, on her way to work, she had been brutally assaulted. The man had beaten her and raped her.

“You heard him too, didn’t you, Mother? He said that once he was released, he’d kill us all!”

The man who had committed such an unforgivable act against her was named Gong Pae, one of the members of the Beast Faction gang that controlled this area. He had committed such a heinous deed in the morning, no less, rather than at night.

Heeya, her body battered and broken, had personally reported him to the Martial Alliance branch in charge of this region. But before three days had even passed since Gong Pae was arrested, she heard that he had been released. Even after being dragged to the branch, he had openly shouted that he was innocent and that he would not leave Heeya and her family alone once he got out. And the branch had released a man like that.

“We have to go, Mother.”

Heeya staggered to her feet, then felt dizzy and collapsed back down. There was not a single part of her body that did not hurt.

“Where do you think you’re going in your condition?”

“We have to go. He said he’d kill us!”

Tears brimmed in Heeya’s eyes. She truly had never dreamed that they would release the man so easily. What were they supposed to do? What in the world were they supposed to do?

Mrs. Shim pulled her daughter into a tight embrace.

“It’s all my fault. It’s because of me.”

She had raised her son and daughter alone after losing her husband. She had lived stubbornly and worked herself to the bone, but a fall a year ago had badly injured her leg.

Once she could no longer work, her young son, barely ten years old, had begun working as a waiter, while her fifteen-year-old daughter, who had worked at a fabric shop to help her mother, had taken on washing dishes at an inn at night as well.

They were such good children, working to earn their living expenses and pay for their mother’s medicine. And now this misfortune had befallen her daughter.

Tears streamed down Mrs. Shim’s face. She had clenched her teeth, determined not to cry in front of her daughter, but the tears came anyway.

Heeya clenched her teeth again and rose.

“Mother, pack our things. I’ll bring your cart.”

Mrs. Shim grabbed her hand and pulled her back.

“You go. Take your brother and flee first.”

“Mother, there’s no time for this. You know I won’t leave without you.”

“Where could you go with me in tow? Just hide yourself somewhere for a while. Please?”

All kinds of thoughts raced through Heeya’s mind.

Where could she go with her ailing mother and young brother? Or should she simply assume she had been bitten by a mad dog and, if the man came looking for them, beg him over and over to forgive her? She could not think clearly enough to decide which choice was right.

‘Someone, please tell me what to do! Please, help me!’

For a fifteen-year-old girl, the only place left to turn was the heavens.

Just then, her younger brother Seok-i opened the door and entered.

“Mother! Noona!”

Seok-i was flushed with excitement, whatever the reason.

The sight of her younger brother instantly cleared Heeya’s mind. She realized that leaving was the right thing to do if she wanted to save him.

“Pack your things, Seok-i.”

“Why?”

“We’re leaving. Take only what we absolutely need.”

“Noona! Wait! Listen to me first. I asked a martial artist for help. He said he would help us.”

Both Mrs. Shim and Heeya were startled by his words.

“Who is he? Someone you know?”

“No. I met him for the first time today.”

At that, Heeya let out a groan.

“Seok-i, listen carefully to your noona. You mustn’t ask just anyone for something like that. What will you do if that martial artist is killed by the Beast Faction?”

“No. He looked really frightening and strong.”

Heeya sighed again.

“Then where is he? Why did you come alone?”

“He told me to go first.”

“That man isn’t coming. You were a child asking him for help, so he just gave you that answer because he found it troublesome. Hurry up and pack.”

She staggered to her feet. Even walking was difficult, but she was determined to somehow put her mother on the cart and leave.

When she opened the door and stepped outside, she was shocked.

Gong Pae was entering the courtyard, looking absolutely furious.

Heeya was so startled that she collapsed to the ground.

Gong Pae spoke coldly.

“How dare you report me? Because of you, I was locked up in the prison for three whole days.”

For the past three days, Heeya had thought of nothing but killing Gong Pae. In her imagination, she had stabbed him to death with a kitchen knife, beaten him to death with a hammer, and torn out his throat with her teeth. She had killed him dozens, hundreds of times.

But now that she was actually looking at him again, she was so frightened that she could not think at all. Her entire body trembled, and her mind went blank.

Then Mrs. Shim crawled out of the room on her bottom.

“Listen here. I was the one who reported you. If you want to blame someone, blame me.”

Gong Pae snorted.

“Why don’t you take care of your mother, who crawls around like a worm, instead of making reports?”

When he insulted her mother, Heeya screamed as if in hysterics.

“Shut up! I said shut up! Who do you think you are? Why do you—why do you—why do you—Aaaaaah!”

She shouted even louder because she was afraid.

“Has this bitch gone crazy? She needs to be beaten senseless.”

When Gong Pae raised his hand to strike her, Seok-i rushed out of the room.

“Don’t! Don’t touch my noona!”

Seok-i stood in front of Heeya.

Heeya pulled Seok-i behind her. He resisted, refusing to move, and the two of them fell backward together.

“Making such a damn fuss. What’s the point of living like this? I’ll kill every last one of you today. I’ll bury you all in one place.”

As if he truly intended to kill them all, the man drew a dagger. At the sight of its gleaming blue edge, Heeya and Seok-i both froze.

“Please save us! Please, spare my family!”

It was precisely then, as Heeya pleaded, that a quiet voice came from behind them.

“Isn’t that something you should be saying to that man?”

Gong Pae turned around in surprise and saw Geom Mugeuk and Gwonma entering.

At a glance, Gong Pae realized that the two were no ordinary martial artists. In particular, the moment he saw Gwonma looking at him as if he were a mere insect, every hair on his body stood on end.

Seok-i shouted joyfully.

“Martial artist! You came! The martial artist came! He’s here!”

Mrs. Shim and Heeya realized that these two men were the martial artists Seok-i had spoken of earlier.

Gong Pae also realized that the young brat had called these martial artists here.

“Martial artists, I believe there’s been some misunderstanding. I’m actually fond of that woman.”

At Gong Pae’s shameless nonsense, Geom Mugeuk made a hushing gesture.

“Your chance to speak in this life is over. You have exactly one word left—but even that isn’t your right.”

Geom Mugeuk asked the young lady,

“Miss, would you like an apology from that man before he dies?”

Before he died?

Heeya stared at Geom Mugeuk in surprise. He gave a single nod.

Heeya shook her head. She did not even want to exchange words with him.

“Even his final bit of bullshit won’t be permitted.”

The moment Gong Pae tried to open his mouth, Geom Mugeuk’s finger qi sealed his speech acupoint and blood-sealing acupoint. He truly did not grant him even a single word.

Gwonma slowly walked toward Heeya.

Realizing what he intended to do, Geom Mugeuk covered Seok-i’s eyes.

Gwonma stopped in front of Heeya. Looking up at him, Heeya swallowed hard. She had never seen anyone who looked so frightening.

“Hold out your fist.”

Startled, Heeya extended her trembling hand in a fist. Gwonma’s large hand wrapped around her tiny one. Then, enclosing her hand in his, he extended it forward with her.

Very slowly, the two of them thrust a single fist toward Gong Pae.

At the moment a question—What is this?—appeared on Gong Pae’s face—

Whooooooosh!

With an earsplitting roar of wind—

Bam-bam-bam! Bam! Bam-bam-bam! Bam-bam-bam-bam-bam-bam-bam!

Every bone in Gong Pae’s body shattered and burst apart. From his toes to his arms and legs, his ribs and spine, his shoulder blades and neck bones—not a single bone remained unbroken. Every one was crushed and pulverized.

‘Aaaaaaaaagh!’

It was an agony he had never experienced in his life. Since his speech acupoint had been sealed, he could not scream, making the pain all the worse.

Only after enduring all of that torment did his face cave in with a final crack, and he crumpled to the ground.

All of this had been contained in the single technique Gwonma had unleashed while holding Heeya’s hand.

But that was not the end. As if he would not even permit Gong Pae’s corpse to remain in this household, Gwonma crooked a finger, and the body shot into the air.

Gwonma’s other fist thrust into empty space.

Kkwa-reureureung! Boom!

Thunder roared, as though the heavens themselves had passed judgment.

Kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa-kwa!

A tremendous fist force swept over the corpse. Gong Pae’s body vanished from the air without a trace, as if it had never existed.

“Ah!”

Heeya could not believe any of what she had just witnessed. It felt like she was watching an illusion.

Then Gwonma clenched her fist tightly once more.

At that moment, Heeya felt what the fist enclosing her hand was saying.

Live well from now on. Find the strength to endure.

Heeya looked up at Gwonma. He was gazing down at her indifferently.

She was no longer afraid of his face. He did not look like an evil spirit, but like one of the Four Heavenly Guardian Kings standing at the entrance to a temple. Like a guardian deity protecting her from evil spirits.

Seok-i had not seen Gong Pae turn to dust and disappear. But he knew by instinct.

Gong Pae was dead.

Geom Mugeuk felt Seok-i’s tears falling onto his palm.

After removing his hand from the boy’s eyes, Geom Mugeuk turned Seok-i to face him.

“Continue living with the courage you showed today.”

He had possessed the courage to approach that terrifying Gwonma and ask for help. With courage like that, he would surely grow up well.

“Yes.”

Seok-i threw his arms around Geom Mugeuk. The child trembled in his embrace, and Geom Mugeuk slowly stroked his head.

“Benefactor, please accept my bow.”

Heeya tried to bow to Gwonma, but an invisible force stopped her.

“There is no need.”

“Please, accept my bow. It is the only thing I can offer you.”

“I merely granted your brother’s request.”

With those words, Gwonma clasped his hands behind his back and looked up at the sky.

Heeya glanced at Seok-i. Seok-i ran over and embraced his sister.

“Noona.”

“Seok-i.”

This time, Seok-i, nestled in his sister’s arms, looked toward Gwonma. Gwonma could feel the child’s gaze, filled with admiration, respect, and gratitude, but he merely stood there with his hands behind his back, gazing up at the sky.

Mrs. Shim expressed her gratitude through her tears.

“Thank you. I will never forget this kindness, even in death.”

Geom Mugeuk said to Mrs. Shim and Heeya,

“I know that man belongs to a gang. We will take care of everything ourselves, so do not worry from now on.”

And that was not the only piece of good fortune granted to this family.

Perhaps because he had already shown them kindness and now felt inclined to see it through to the end, Gwonma approached Mrs. Shim and suddenly asked,

“When did it begin?”

“Pardon?”

“Your leg. When did it start hurting?”

“Ah, about a year ago.”

“May I take a look?”

Before the dazed Mrs. Shim could answer, Heeya quickly said,

“Please, I beg you!”

Gwonma examined Mrs. Shim’s leg.

“It may hurt, so endure it.”

Before she could say anything—

Crack. Pop. Creak.

The sound of bones being set rang out. As bones that had long since stiffened were shifted into place, Mrs. Shim screamed. But the pain did not last long. Gwonma possessed a more profound understanding of human bones and muscles than even the Medicine Demon.

A moment later, astonishing words came from Gwonma’s mouth.

“If you rest and recuperate for a while, you should be able to stand and walk again.”

Mrs. Shim was so shocked that she could not close her mouth, while Heeya and Seok-i cried out in joy.

“Thank you, benefactor.”

“Thank you. Thank you so much.”

The three of them embraced each other and wept in gratitude.

Finally, Geom Mugeuk handed Heeya an envelope. Inside was enough money for the family of three to open even a small shop and make a living.

Heeya asked in a trembling voice, utterly startled,

“Why are you giving this to us?”

“I am not giving it to you. It is money we will collect from that man’s gang. Take it without feeling burdened.”

How could this money, or Gong Pae’s death, heal the wounds in her heart?

This incident would remain a scar that tormented her for the rest of her life. She would have nightmares, and she would likely harbor a deep distrust of men.

But this was as far as Geom Mugeuk could help. May she overcome it all and live well for the rest of her life.

“Why are you showing us such great kindness?”

Heeya felt as if she had received all the blessings she had been denied throughout her life at once. It almost seemed too much.

Geom Mugeuk looked at Gwonma and said,

“It’s because your brother chose that handsome face over there.”

* * *

After leaving Mrs. Shim’s home, we headed straight for the Beast Faction.

If we did not cleanly deal with what came afterward, Seok-i’s family would eventually suffer the consequences.

Before going, I looked into the Beast Faction. They were the sort who had committed every kind of evil imaginable—frightening and threatening the residents, extorting money, beating people, and killing them. They were black-robed martial outlaws who ruled this region like kings.

Those bastards were dogs, so it was one thing that they behaved like dogs. The real problem was the Martial Alliance branch that had stood by and allowed it.

“The fist technique you used earlier was truly magnificent.”

In truth, it was a fist technique far too profound to use against a lowlife like Gong Pae.

Even so, Gwonma had killed him with his own hands because of Seok-i. Since Seok-i had asked Gwonma for help, he had killed the man personally—so that Seok-i could live with the pride of knowing that his own choice had saved his family.

But what moved me even more was the sight of him taking Heeya’s hand and sending the man flying. I believed that, thanks to the hand Gwonma had held, the girl would live well. When times grew difficult, she would remember that large, thick hand.

“I respect you.”

“You flatter me every time you open your mouth.”

“I mean it, Master.”

I had subtly called him Master, but he remained as unyielding as ever.

“Not a bad attempt, Second Young Master Yi.”

In this manner, we arrived at the manor where the Beast Faction resided.

The man guarding the main gate turned deathly pale at the sight of Gwonma, slammed the gate shut, and ran inside.

“Emergency! Emergency!”

Hearing his cries, Gwonma said self-deprecatingly,

“See? My face alone is enough to cause an emergency.”

If it had been Seo Daeryong, he would have teased him about this for at least three days. But as long as Gwonma was with me, I had no intention of letting him lose confidence because of his face.

“I saw it. The look of admiration in that girl’s eyes as she gazed up at you earlier.”

Perhaps those words put him in a good mood.

When Gwonma placed his palm against the main gate, there was a thunderous crash, and the heavy gate tore free and flew away like a sheet of paper.

Gwonma stepped inside first. From behind, his enormous back seemed to fill the entire wide entrance.

Chapter 195: A Black Path Man Repenting So Cornily?
---------------------------------------------------
Gu Yasu (具冶洙), leader of the Beast Faction, was watching his men count money. It was silver they had collected as tribute from the marketplace.

“Three hundred nyang.”

One of his men brought over a heavy money pouch.

“One crippled bastard is going to ruin our household finances. Where is that son of a bitch Gongpae right now?”

At that, Yang (梁), the right-hand man standing beside him, answered.

“He was released from the Murim Alliance branch, so he’s probably drinking somewhere.”

“When he comes in, tell him to come to me at once.”

“Yes, sir.”

This three hundred nyang was a bribe meant for the Murim Alliance branch chief. If they had been taking money every month as usual, they ought to let things slide at times like this, yet they had demanded as much as three hundred nyang in exchange for releasing Gongpae.

Even so, they could not simply leave Gongpae rotting in a prison cell. Gongpae was necessary to the Beast Faction. Only when they had a dog like him would the merchants be frightened and obediently hand over money.

“What about the wench who reported him? She didn’t run, did she?”

“Her mother is ill, so she won’t be able to flee.”

“You said she had a pretty face? Well, of course. That must be why Gongpae got himself all worked up. Once things quiet down, sell her off to some distant brothel. We should at least make up half of the money we lost because of that bitch.”

“Understood.”

“And make sure that idiot Gongpae can’t touch her.”

“Understood. Ah, and the Jangdo Faction has invaded our territory again.”

“Those bastards!”

The Jangdo Faction was a black-path gang from a nearby area that competed with the Beast Faction. Whenever a dispute over interests arose, they always clashed with them.

“There’s a rumor that they recruited an expert.”

“What kind of expert?”

“I don’t know. They say he’s an unknown expert, and I suspect they’re acting up because they trust in him.”

Gu Yasu was snorting in derision when one of his men came running in from outside and reported urgently.

“There are intruders.”

At the word intruders, Gu Yasu immediately ordered someone to be called.

“Call Martial Artist Cheon.”

The reason he could leisurely snort even after hearing that the Jangdo Faction had recruited a new expert was precisely because of the expert called Cheon. There was a secret regarding Cheon that only Gu Yasu knew. It was that Cheon came from the Demon Cult.

A Demon Cult expert, Cheon!

Because he was here, the Beast Faction had been able to grow like this.

When Gu Yasu and Yang came outside, the intruders were walking across the training yard.

Seeing the two walking toward them from afar, Gu Yasu flinched in surprise.

“What the hell?”

There were two intruders: an immensely muscular man and a slender young man. Their relaxed stride made them look more like visitors than intruders. Of course, they were Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon.

Yang quickly said,

“They seem to be men sent by the Jangdo Faction.”

“The expert those bastards supposedly hired this time?”

“Yes. Who else could they be if not them?”

Gu Yasu nodded. In this area, the only ones who would dare attack them were the Jangdo Faction.

“Where is Martial Artist Cheon?”

“He will be out soon.”

About twenty Beast Faction members sprang out from all sides, drew their weapons, and surrounded the two men.

“Where have you gentlemen come from?”

At Gu Yasu’s question, Geom Mugeuk looked around and said,

“You’ve turned the outside into hell, yet you live quite nicely in here.”

The Beast Faction’s headquarters was a newly built manor. In particular, the building Gu Yasu occupied had been lavishly constructed with no expense spared on costly materials.

“I asked who you were.”

Kill them, or make them kneel. Under normal circumstances, such an order would have left his mouth already, but it did not come out.

It was because of the muscular man beside the young man. Gu Yasu, who had gone through all manner of hardships in the black path, had seen every sort of vicious-looking bastard, but this was the first time he had seen someone who looked this terrifying.

Geom Mugeuk said to Gu Yasu,

“Do you know why you keep asking who I am instead of asking why I came? It’s because you’ve gotten into the habit of deciding first whether this bastard is someone you can kill or not.”

After finishing his words, Geom Mugeuk began striding toward Gu Yasu.

Startled, Gu Yasu shouted to his men,

“Kill him!”

The Beast Faction members rushed at Geom Mugeuk.

Geom Mugeuk dodged the incoming attacks and began swinging his fists.

To Gu Yasu’s eyes, they did not look powerful at all. Just a light thud. Then another light thud.

He dodged well, too. While lightly evading attacks coming from every direction, he struck each man once. One in the face. Another in the stomach.

The attacks were quick, but since they did not seem powerful, Gu Yasu felt relieved inside.

‘He’s nothing much!’

That was truly the thought that came to him. Rather, he felt that the muscular man standing there without joining the fight was the real problem.

“Get up! Get up and attack together! Stab him in the back!”

But the subordinates who had been hit once and fallen did not get up.

“Stop pretending and get up! You useless bastards!”

He understood. If they thought that getting up meant they had to fight that muscular man, even he would probably pretend to be unconscious.

“Damn it. To think I trusted trash like that.”

Meanwhile, all twenty or so men who had stepped forward had fallen.

Yang kicked a subordinate lying nearby to wake him up. Geom Mugeuk simply let him do as he pleased.

“If you don’t want to die, get up already!”

They had been tapped so lightly and lost consciousness? Anyone could see they were pretending to be knocked out.

Even when kicked, he did not move. Feeling that something was strange, Yang carefully examined the fallen subordinate.

Yang flinched in shock. The subordinate was dead. He examined the subordinate fallen beside him, and he too was dead. Yang understood. All of the subordinates were dead.

Yang slowly looked up at Geom Mugeuk.

At that moment, his eyes met Geom Mugeuk’s. The gaze that looked at him indifferently was cold and eerie.

‘He’s a true expert!’

Yang realized that if he said even one wrong word, he would die on the spot.

Geom Mugeuk had shown no mercy in his hands. It was to ensure that no one would survive and harm Seok’s family in the name of revenge.

Just then, Cheon appeared. Perhaps because he had drunk heavily the day before, his hair was disheveled, and with his unshaven beard, he looked extremely haggard. He had also gained a lot of weight compared to when he had first entered the Beast Faction. Still, Gu Yasu was fine with it. Until now, there had never been an enemy Cheon could not deal with.

“Get rid of both of those bastards.”

Cheon strode forward. He paid no attention to the fallen Beast Faction members. The skills of thugs were obvious anyway.

As it happened, he had once been an Iron Fist of Donggwonmun. He had risen as far as Blue Fist, but in the end, he could not endure the harsh training and fled the Demon Cult.

After that, he had been hiding within the Beast Faction. All sorts of people had targeted Gu Yasu until now, but they had all died by his hand. In exchange, he received enough money to indulge in pleasure. In any case, in this third-rate black-path world, with only Blue Fist-level skill, he was the Fist Demon.

Whoosh.

Cheon’s fist, thrust out to smash Geom Mugeuk’s head, stopped in midair.

The problem was not that his attack had been dodged far too easily. The problem was Geom Mugeuk’s next words.

“This is Blue Fist martial arts, isn’t it?”

Cheon was horrified. It was the first time anyone had recognized his martial arts.

It was at that very moment that one person finally entered Cheon’s eyes properly. The large man who had been standing with his back turned, looking up at the sky, turned his head toward him.

The instant he saw that face!

“Aaaaargh!”

A scream truly burst from Cheon’s mouth. He was so shocked that he thought his heart would burst. The hangover that had tormented him all day vanished in an instant.

Gu Yasu, who was looking at Cheon’s back, could not see it, but Yang could.

Cheon’s distorted face. He wondered if such deep despair and fear could dwell in a human face.

The Fist Demon spoke quietly, as if talking to himself.

“You used my martial arts to serve as the fist of a black-path thug?”

There was no emotion in the Fist Demon’s words. The moment his words ended.

Thud! Crack!

Cheon struck his own crown and took his life. He knew. He could not run away, and he could not be forgiven. If he died by the Fist Demon’s hand, he would die in far greater agony.

At that sight, Gu Yasu’s mind went blank. Because something so strange had happened, he could not think at all.

Geom Mugeuk walked past Yang and said,

“Follow me.”

“Yes!”

As if Geom Mugeuk were the leader, Yang respectfully obeyed the command.

When Geom Mugeuk came right up before him, Gu Yasu came to his senses. Now there was only one thing he could rely on.

“Do you know who stands behind us? If you touch us, there will be consequences.”

“Who’s behind you?”

“The Murim Alliance.”

“Does the entire Murim Alliance belong to you?”

“The branch chief here has my back. If you touch me, he won’t stand still.”

Geom Mugeuk said to Yang,

“You can bring the branch chief here, can’t you?”

As if it were not an easy task, Yang hesitated.

“If you just bring him here, you will be the Beast Faction’s leader from now on.”

“I will bring him here by any means necessary.”

Gu Yasu glared murderously at Yang as he ran off without hesitation.

“Damned bastard.”

He had not expected him to betray him so easily. And he had called himself his right-hand man.

After Yang left, Geom Mugeuk said,

“No need to get so angry. That part about making him the leader was a lie.”

Just as Gu Yasu’s expression eased slightly,

“You haven’t lived in the black path for only a day or two. You know everyone dies, don’t you?”

Gu Yasu’s expression stiffened again. He sensed that he too was included in that everyone Geom Mugeuk had mentioned.

“Please spare me.”

Geom Mugeuk said to him,

“If you hand over evidence that you bribed the branch chief.”

Gu Yasu’s eyes shifted.

“If you don’t have any, then just die.”

The moment Geom Mugeuk clenched his fist, Gu Yasu shouted,

“I have it! It’s inside the safe.”

Geom Mugeuk put him in front and went to the safe. His hands trembled as he opened it. The thought of what am I doing right now occurred to him, and belated fear surged in. The fact that Cheon, whom he had trusted so much, had killed himself was a great shock.

“Here it is.”

He took a ledger out of the safe and handed it over. It contained the details of the bribes given to the Murim Alliance branch chief and all sorts of corruption the branch chief had committed.

“You go move the corpses somewhere they can’t be seen.”

Gu Yasu walked toward the corpses.

‘This is my chance!’

He made up his mind to seize an opening and run away, but before he knew it, the internal energy in his dantian had been suppressed and he could no longer use it.

‘When on earth did he do that?’

With no choice, Gu Yasu moved the corpses. Having to move over twenty corpses without internal energy made sweat pour down his body. After living his whole life without lifting a finger, even this kind of work was enough to make him feel like he was dying.

After struggling to move all the corpses, Gu Yasu went to Geom Mugeuk.

“I moved them all.”

“It was hard, wasn’t it? You felt powerless?”

“……Yes.”

“The emotions felt by the people you’ve victimized until now were probably a hundred, a thousand times greater than that. When each of you is taken alone, you’re truly insignificant, yet you gather like a pack of dogs and frighten people, steal from them, beat them, and kill them.”

“I will turn over a new leaf from now on.”

“A black-path man repenting? How unsophisticated. Keep doing evil. But do it in the place I introduce you to. There will be plenty of others in your line of work there too, so it should be fine.”

“Where is that?”

“Here.”

Geom Mugeuk’s fist flew toward him.

Second Fist, Thunderbolt Asura.

Thudthudthudthudthudthudthudthudthud!

Dozens of fists, swift as lightning, struck him, and he died as a bloody pulp without even having time to scream.

Geom Mugeuk threw his corpse into the storage room with the other bodies. He attached no meaning whatsoever to the bastard’s death.

A short while later, Murim Alliance branch chief Baek Chul (伯出) came to the place.

“Why does it smell so much like blood? You bastards, didn’t I tell you to stop killing so many people?”

Baek Chul nagged Yang as he entered. He must have come and gone so often that he treated the place as comfortably as his own home.

“What is so important that you called a busy man all the way here?”

When he saw Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon, he flinched in surprise.

“Who are they?”

“They are warriors we newly recruited this time.”

At Yang’s shameless reply, Baek Chul looked astonished.

“Where did you find a hulking brute like that? Just standing him there would be enough for him to earn his keep. But where did your master go?”

Yang replied calmly.

“It seems he stepped away for a moment.”

Just then, Geom Mugeuk said to Baek Chul,

“How could you simply release a man who beat and even violated a young girl?”

Baek Chul turned to Yang with a look that said, What is this? They’re new, so they don’t know me? Didn’t you train them properly?

But Yang merely watched the situation in silence. At Yang’s reaction, different from usual, Baek Chul instinctively realized that something had gone wrong.

Baek Chul answered calmly.

“Ah, that case lacked evidence, so I released him.”

“Her entire body was left in tatters, and Gongpae was said to have blabbered and boasted of the deed to those dark path bastards—what’s all this talk of evidence? You haven’t even investigated, have you?”

Baekchul was inwardly boiling with rage, but the atmosphere being what it was, he could not retort.

“If this had happened to your family, would you have simply let them go?”

At the mention of family, Baekchul’s face hardened instantly.

“This bastard, the more I see of him...”

“Does talking about family make you feel bad? Your family is this precious to you, while other people’s families are cheap and loathsome things to be preyed upon?”

In that moment, Baekchul debated whether to draw his sword, but endured for now.

‘I’ll throw them all in prison later.’

Surviving this situation was the priority. Baekchul forced a smile and spoke amiably.

“Do try to understand. Even the main alliance must abide by laws.”

“Laws for whose sake? You? The Beast Sect? Who exactly?”

At Geommugeuk’s cold momentum, Baekchul took a step back.

“No matter who you are, I am the Murim Alliance branch chief. Touch me, and you become a public enemy of Murim!”

“That won’t happen. I have someone I know in the Murim Alliance, and I intend to send a letter informing them of your crimes and everything that has transpired here.”

“To whom?”

“There’s a high-ranking figure up there. One who once tried to take me as a son-in-law.”

Baekchul thought this was utter nonsense, but held his tongue for now.

“My crimes? What evidence do you have?”

Geommugeuk produced the ledger he had obtained from the Beast Sect.

“You’ve taken plenty of bribes from the Beast Sect. You’ve even had them kill people over vested interests.”

“What nonsense? I have never done such a thing.”

“Is this something to deny? Those bastards kept diligent records of everything.”

Baekchul’s expression hardened.

“They’re framing me!”

“That’s not it. They were not in a situation to lie.”

Baekchul resolved to obtain that ledger by any means necessary. Eliminating them would come after.

“How much would it take? How much for that ledger...”

Whiiiiish.

Boom!

Baekchul vanished before their eyes.

Baekchul, who had flown far away and been embedded in the wall, died instantly, his entire body shattered.

Now Yang, left alone, spoke while gauging the mood.

“Then from now on, I shall be the leader of the Beast Sect...”

Whiish.

Boom!

Yang also vanished before their eyes.

Sent flying in the opposite direction, he too rolled about as a bloodied pulp.

The one who had sent the two flying was Gwonma. He had dispatched them to the afterlife with a single punch.

“Come.”

It was a gaze asking why Geommugeuk was exchanging words with such trash.

“I was going to ask for about a million nyang for the ledger.”

“Stop your nonsense.”

“Wait a moment.”

Geommugeuk buried the ledger in a corner of the courtyard.

If he reported that it was buried there, the investigator sent by the Alliance Leader would handle it properly. He also intended to include in the letter a request to look after Seok’s family.

Watching Geommugeuk like this, Gwonma spoke.

“You are quite meticulous.”

“You must be meticulous when helping people. Helping others is all well and good, but if you don’t clean up the loose ends properly just because it’s a bother, the one who ultimately suffers is the one you helped. I get to feel good about myself while they suffer the consequences. That wouldn’t do, would it?”

Unexpected words came from Gwonma’s mouth.

“I don’t think you should become the Heavenly Demon.”

“Why is that?”

“How many people must the Heavenly Demon face? Can you endure with such a mindset? If you tend to everything so meticulously, you will be the first to go mad.”

“Now that I hear it, you are right. Very well. Instead of the Heavenly Demon, I shall simply become your disciple, Gwonma.”

Gwonma stared at me quietly and smiled faintly.

“That was the best attempt yet.”

“You almost fell for it. A pity.”

Geommugeuk and Gwonma walked out of the estate side by side. Just as when they had entered, Gwonma’s back filled the entrance as they left.

Fortunately, Gwonma was not suggesting they return to the crossing. At his gaze asking where they would go now, Geommugeuk smiled and said.

“This time, it is a place where you, Gwonma, will enjoy yourself more than I.”

Chapter 196: From Now On, Whenever I See That Scar.
