---------------------------------------------------
A boat carrying passengers was crossing the river.

The Fist Demon and I stood on the windy deck, looking out over the river. We were growing farther and farther from the main cult.

Some people’s deviations make you anxious. What if he causes some kind of accident? Or what if this changes his life for the worse? All because of me?

But the Fist Demon’s deviation did not make me anxious at all. Was it because of the sincerity he had shown in the way he lived? Or was it because I felt that very sincerity had been pressing down on the true nature of the man called the Fist Demon?

It was then.

Rattle, rattle.

A ball made of yarn rolled to the Fist Demon’s feet.

A little child came trotting over, then stopped short. His face showed fear at the sight of the massive Fist Demon. The Fist Demon picked up the ball and handed it to me.

“Give it to him yourself.”

“Do you want me to make the child cry?”

“He won’t cry.”

Even after the Fist Demon held out the ball, the child only hesitated and could not approach, so the Fist Demon rolled the ball to him.

Little one, today is the day you received a ball from the Fist Demon.

The child took the ball, bowed, and ran back toward his parents. Watching him, the Fist Demon suddenly asked,

“You said we were going somewhere I would enjoy?”

“Yes.”

“What makes you think you know what I enjoy?”

“Shall we make a bet?”

“What kind of bet?”

“If you go there and enjoy yourself, I win. If you don’t enjoy yourself, I lose.”

“What if I enjoy myself but say I didn’t?”

“You won’t do that, will you?”

“If it’s a bet, we should wager something.”

“We should wager our pride.”

It had been the same when we jumped down the waterfall.

At that, the Fist Demon looked at me with a strange gaze.

“Why are you looking at me like that?”

“Do you not want to wager the Third Fist?”

“I do.”

“Then why not suggest it?”

“I obtained the First Fist and the Second Fist far too easily because you were generous, Fist Demon. I do have a conscience. I can’t do that again.”

It was sincere, and also something I said to score points with him.

“Now, on a man’s pride!”

I held out my fist to him.

“What is that? Are you asking for a fight?”

“When martial artists make a promise, they clash their weapons, don’t they? We fist practitioners should clash fists.”

“I will not clash fists with a baby hand.”

I looked at the Fist Demon’s hand, then down at my own fist.

“Frustratingly, I can’t deny it.”

The Fist Demon turned toward the river.

Far away over the river, the sunset had begun to fall.

“It’s nice.”

It was the first time he had ever looked at scenery and said it was nice. The Fist Demon was gazing at the river, and I was leaning against the railing, the sunset at my back, looking at him.

Could this moment of playing hooky truly be remembered as one of the heated moments between him and me?

* * *

Two days later, Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon were entering a marketplace where countless people came and went. If the place where the Beast Sect had been last time was the territory of the Murim Alliance, then this place was the territory of the Sado Alliance.

As they walked, the Fist Demon’s expression changed subtly. At times it hardened, and at times it showed intense emotion.

The place the two arrived at was a shabby warehouse building on the outskirts.

Geom Mugeuk handed money to the man guarding the entrance. After receiving the money, he guided the two inside. Between piles of cargo was a passageway leading underground.

After descending the stairs for quite some time, they came upon a large iron door. The moment they opened it and went inside, a rush of hot air swept over them, accompanied by a roar of cheers.

In the center was a large martial arena, and two bare-chested men were fighting, exchanging blows with their naked fists.

“This is an underground fighting arena.”

There were many kinds of underground fighting arenas in the Central Plains. Some allowed internal energy, and some allowed weapons. And there were also pure fighting arenas like the one they had come to today, where men fought only with their fists, without internal energy or weapons.

“You can pay the entrance fee and simply watch the matches, or you can bet on who the winner will be. Most people come mainly to gamble.”

“How did you learn about this place?”

“I’m interested in all sorts of things in the martial world. Since this is a place where people fight purely with their fists, I thought you might like it, Fist Demon, so I brought you here.”

There was a tremor in the Fist Demon’s eyes.

“What is it? Do you not like it?”

Then astonishing words came from the Fist Demon’s mouth.

“When I was young, I was a fighter here too.”

“What?”

Geom Mugeuk was startled. No, he pretended to be startled. In truth, he had known that the Fist Demon came from this place.

“You mean here?”

“That’s right. I was right here.”

The Fist Demon’s life, too, had not been a smooth one.

“Then how did you come to have ties with the main cult?”

“I caught the eye of someone from the main cult here and was selected.”

That much, even Geom Mugeuk had not known. It would have been before he had properly learned martial arts, but even so, where could the skill of someone with the potential to become the Fist Demon go?

“Were you undefeated here as well?”

At Geom Mugeuk’s question, the Fist Demon asked in return,

“Am I undefeated even now?”

“What do you mean?”

“I couldn’t win if I fought the Cult Leader, and the same goes for the leader of the Murim Alliance and the leader of the Sado Alliance, does it not? There’s no need to call forth the countless hidden masters scattered like grains of sand across the world. Even against you right now, I could not be certain I would win.”

“I’m just a baby hand, aren’t I?”

“The Black Demon Sword held by that baby hand is frightening.”

Geom Mugeuk suddenly thought that perhaps the Fist Demon disliked the title of undefeated.

“You never know. If it became a true life-or-death duel, who can say what the result would be? You might be able to defeat my father, Fist Demon.”

The Fist Demon’s gaze returned to the fighting arena. Looking at a martial artist being beaten half to death, he said,

“If I fought the Cult Leader, I would end up like that. Even if the Cult Leader did not draw his sword.”

Geom Mugeuk nodded.

“That’s true. He is someone who would beat even his own child like that.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s words, the Fist Demon nodded with a serious face.

In truth, even Geom Mugeuk did not know what the Fist Demon’s memories of this place were like. Perhaps they were sickening to him, and it was possible this was a place he never wanted to return to again.

But the reason he had brought him here anyway was that he had now become the Fist Demon. He had risen from the very bottom of fists to the very pinnacle of fists.

Would he not have wanted to come here at least once? Would he not have wanted to feel that scorching heat once more?

Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon stood among the crowd and watched the match.

The heat here was so intense that even with the fearsome-looking Fist Demon nearby, no one paid any attention.

Geom Mugeuk could tell. These people, their souls half-gone, were not lost in the fighting but in the gambling. They were shouting only in anticipation of the outcome of the fight.

After watching the fight for a while, the Fist Demon casually muttered,

“The one on the left will win.”

Whose judgment was this? How could it be wrong?

The result was the victory of the martial artist on the left.

The matches continued without pause. As soon as one match ended, three or four men came out and instantly wiped away the sweat and blood spilled on the arena floor.

Meanwhile, other men held up large boards listing the fighters in the next match, their head-to-head records, and their recent results, showing them to the crowd. People placed their bets after looking at those.

This entire process unfolded in an instant. As if time itself were money, as if they would not allow the heat of the spectators to cool, the matches continued one after another without a moment’s rest.

The Fist Demon accurately predicted the next match as well. He was, quite literally, a prophet.

“We might be able to leave this place rich.”

Despite his words, Geom Mugeuk did not bet a single coin.

“Why not wager a little for fun?”

“I don’t want to.”

“Why?”

“To be honest, I’m regretting this. I brought you here so you could feel the heat of the fighting, but it seems the heat of gambling is even hotter.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s honest words, the Fist Demon smiled bitterly.

“It was the same back then. This place… has always been like that.”

At that moment, the Fist Demon fixed his gaze on one young man.

“That young fellow has good eyes.”

Geom Mugeuk could feel it. The Fist Demon was projecting his own youth onto that young man.

“Good. I’ll bet my entire fortune on him.”

At Geom Mugeuk’s joke, the Fist Demon urged him,

“Do it. He will definitely win.”

“No, thank you.”

But the young man the Fist Demon had said would win lost the match. At the result, the Fist Demon’s expression hardened slightly.

“If I had bet as you said, I would have lost my entire fortune. We almost had to sleep outdoors tonight!”

Could the Fist Demon have misjudged their abilities? Of course not. The match had been fixed.

“Shall we ask to see the owner here?”

It would have been entirely like the Fist Demon to meet the owner and give him a stern word.

“The fixing likely has nothing to do with the arena itself. On the contrary, the arena side tries to detect any fixing by any means possible. They make enormous amounts of money just by leaving things be, so they would not deliberately create problems by fixing matches.”

It meant that the fixing was being attempted from the outside.

“Memories should simply be left buried as memories. I’m sorry.”

“What do you have to be sorry for?”

“I brought you here so you could enjoy watching the fights, but I wondered if I had only made you feel worse for no reason.”

“It’s all right.”

“Times have changed a great deal. It seems there are no pure fights like there were back then anymore.”

At that, the Fist Demon smiled strangely and said to him,

“Why do you think there was no fixing back then?”

“There was?”

“Of course there was. It changed my life.”

The Fist Demon’s gaze turned toward the young man leaving the arena, and he naturally recalled the past.

“It was one day when I had been continuing a streak of consecutive victories. The head of the black path in this area came to find me at night with his subordinates. He told me to lose the next match. Since I was on a winning streak, if I lost, he would be able to make an enormous sum of money. He said he would give me money too. It was quite a large amount.”

“What did you do?”

“I refused.”

“They wouldn’t have just let that go.”

The Fist Demon nodded and closed his eyes. The moment from back then came vividly to mind, as if it had happened yesterday.

That moment when he had thrown punches at those black path bastards, dodging this way and that as he fought.

He beat down all the subordinates who had come with him, but he could not handle the leader. The man had formally learned martial arts, while he had not yet learned martial arts at all. In the end, he was subdued by him and rendered helpless.

“If you don’t listen to me, you die.”

“You black path bastard! If you’re a man, don’t use internal energy. Fight me in the arena!”

“Words won’t do, then.”

The leader tore open the Fist Demon’s clothes.

Sssshk!

With a dagger, he drew a long line across the Fist Demon’s chest. The Fist Demon gritted his teeth so he would not scream.

“You have to lose anyway, so it’s fine if it hurts a little, isn’t it?”

In the end, the Fist Demon could not endure the pain and screamed.

“I understand. I’ll lose the next match.”

“You should have done that from the start. Then you wouldn’t have been hurt. Whenever a stupid thought comes to mind, look at that scar.”

The man left that place, laughing.

After hearing the entire story, Geom Mugeuk asked,

“So what happened?”

“What do you think happened? In the next match, I won spectacularly. I made that bastard lose his entire fortune.”

Geom Mugeuk laughed out loud. Yes, this was the kind of person the Fist Demon was.

“That’s amazing!”

“It wasn’t amazing. I was just reckless and fearless then. I was far more fearless than I am now, even though I am a thousand times stronger than I was back then.”

The Fist Demon opened the front of his clothes and showed him again.

“That is how I received the scar on my body. It wasn’t a scar given to me by some grand master, but a shabby, humiliating scar left by a mere black path bastard whose name I no longer even remember. It was a wound I did not receive even when fighting an elder of the Murim Alliance.”

Geom Mugeuk thought that perhaps the Fist Demon had never told anyone about this scar. It was not a wound of the body, but a wound of the heart.

“Isn’t it a more prideworthy scar?”

“Prideworthy?”

“You must have been far more afraid then than when you fought the Murim Alliance elder, but you endured it, didn’t you?”

“You may want to dress it up in a good light, but to me, it is nothing but a shame I wish to erase.”

“Don’t say that. I like that scar even more now. From now on, whenever I see that scar, I think I’ll remember the young Fist Demon who stood proud even without a handful of internal energy. More than a blow left by the leader of the Murim Alliance, more than a wound left by the leader of the Sado Alliance, I think that is a far more magnificent scar.”

Because he had never once thought of it that way, nor had he ever heard anyone say such a thing, the Fist Demon was momentarily bewildered. Whenever he looked at the scar, he had always felt displeased, but he thought that perhaps from today onward, that displeasure might lessen a little.

Geom Mugeuk knew. What was truly necessary to heal wounds of the heart was words spoken by someone else. Rather than thinking this and that alone a hundred times, a thousand times, it was only when someone said something like this once that the wound finally began to heal.

“Please show me that scar often.”

Feeling a heat rising from deep within his heart, the Fist Demon did not know what to say and merely looked silently at Geom Mugeuk.

Whether he knew the Fist Demon’s heart or not, Geom Mugeuk asked again,

“Please tell me the next part. Then that black path bastard must have come to kill you.”

The Fist Demon nodded and recalled the day his fate had changed.

“That night, he came to find me with every subordinate under his command.”

Chapter 197: I Will Become the Heavenly Demon.
----------------------------------------------
The moment he confirmed the match results, the Black Dao leader Qiu Jiawu (秋稼誤) immediately mobilized his men.

He had set up double and triple surveillance, so escape would have been impossible anyway, but the young Fist Demon waiting in his own home had clearly resolved himself to die. Otherwise, he could not have said something like that the moment he saw him.

“Did you make a lot of money?”

Qiu Jiawu’s rage shot to the very top of his head.

“You won’t die an easy death.”

He had suffered far too great a loss this time. It was not only his own money he had lost. Because he had drawn in funds from other organizations as well, one wrong step could put his own life in danger.

And on top of that, this green young fighter had turned his insides upside down.

“I told the arena master here about this too. You know who backs this arena, don’t you?”

Qiu Jiawu’s expression hardened. It was precisely because he feared that backing—the Sado Alliance—that he had only ever fixed matches in secret at truly decisive moments.

He had done well until now, only to get caught in the worst possible way this time. This was the first madman of this sort he had ever met. He knew the brat had a temper, but even after leaving that huge scar on his chest to frighten him, he had never dreamed he would do something like this.

“Young whelp, not knowing how precious your life is.”

“It’s your life that must be precious!”

Through this incident, the Fist Demon had made a decision. No matter what kind of life he lived from now on, he would never make friends or family.

How wonderful was that? Even in the face of the bastard’s filthy threats, he could act according to his own temper like this.

Of course, it was a shame. He was going to die here today, so he would never get to put that decision into practice.

Qiu Jiawu gave his men an order.

“Make that bastard kneel before me.”

Qiu Jiawu had no intention of letting this damned bastard die cleanly.

At the command, dozens of Black Dao men drew their weapons all at once. No matter how many victories the Fist Demon had strung together in the arena, their numbers were far too great for someone without internal energy to face.

Even so, the Fist Demon was not cowed in the slightest.

“I’ll fight with the thought that for every one of you I kill, I save one person.”

The Fist Demon took gloves from his robe and put them on. Sharp, awl-like points were embedded in the front of the gloves.

“They’re coated in deadly poison. Even a scratch, and you’ll spend the rest of your life lying in bed eating gruel.”

It was a lie. But this should at least make their movements shrink back somewhat.

Among the gathered Black Dao men, those who had fought the Fist Demon before flinched. The last time they fought him, they had been injured, knocked unconscious, and thrown into utter chaos—now there was poison on his fists too?

“Despicable bastard!”

At Qiu Jiawu’s words, the Fist Demon shouted.

“Why? You lot can run around waving swords, but I can’t wear something like this? You Black Dao trash! Come at me!”

Qiu Jiawu shouted.

“Kill him! Three hundred taels to the one who kills him first!”

With something that took precedence over anything else placed before them, the Black Dao men rushed in all at once, swinging their swords.

The Fist Demon fled toward the rear courtyard. The Black Dao men at the front, chasing him excitedly, suddenly plunged downward.

“Aaaaargh!”

He had dug a pit trap in the courtyard and set spear blades into the bottom.

The Fist Demon had prepared himself to fight properly today. He had not thought he would fight with only his fists. His goal was to survive, and if he was going to die, he intended to take even one more bastard with him.

Several men fell into the trap and died, but the Black Dao men were also veterans of combat. If their opponent had been a martial artist, that would have been one thing, but he was a green youth without internal energy.

They clung to him viciously, swinging their swords. As long as they planted a blade in his body, the enormous sum of three hundred taels would be theirs. The money-mad bastards charged like lunatics.

The Fist Demon’s movements were truly fast. He dodged the incoming swords and threw his fists.

Those stabbed by the awl fists screamed.

Curses flew from all directions, and the Black Dao men ran wild in their excitement. Even as blood streamed from them, they charged in with monstrous cries.

The Fist Demon rolled across the ground, avoiding the blades flying at him from every side.

Leaping to his feet and running toward the wall, the Fist Demon turned and threw a dagger. The dagger sank into the body of the man following him. He threw stones from the ground as well, and even picked up the bastards’ swords and swung them.

Qiu Jiawu shouted.

“You’ve abandoned a fighter’s honor!”

He had said it deliberately to provoke him and keep him from using other weapons, but it had no effect whatsoever.

“I abandoned it because of you. So it’s you killing your own men!”

The Fist Demon fought truly well. Striking, punching, rolling across the ground, leaping up to kick. He rampaged like a wounded beast.

But there were too many Black Dao bastards. No matter how naturally gifted the Fist Demon’s movements were, he was outnumbered.

Blood sprayed from the Fist Demon’s arm and side. He had no time to check where or how badly he had been wounded. He madly swung his fists while dodging the swords flying in from all sides.

Crack!

At that moment, a kick came flying in, and the Fist Demon was sent hurtling through the air, smashing into the piled-up goods in the courtyard before collapsing.

It was Qiu Jiawu’s strike, delivered as he waited for his chance.

“Take those gloves off him first!”

They rushed in from all sides and stripped off the Fist Demon’s gloves. In the process, the Fist Demon bit into one man’s throat. The blood the man gushed out sprayed everywhere.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

Fists rained down, turning the Fist Demon’s face into a bloody mess.

Qiu Jiawu looked down at the Fist Demon.

“This is the first time I’ve seen such a vicious breed.”

Just as Qiu Jiawu clicked his tongue while looking at his men—

The Fist Demon suddenly flung something from a bottle at him.

Qiu Jiawu waved his hand to block it.

Ssssss.

The liquid that flew at him burned through his sleeve. What had come flying at him was Bone-Dissolving Acid (化骨酸).

Thud! Thud! Thud!

Qiu Jiawu’s eyes rolled with fury as he began trampling the Fist Demon. He had nearly had Bone-Dissolving Acid poured over his face.

Curling his body and trying to avoid his vital points, the Fist Demon sensed that this was the end.

‘This life ends here.’

When he actually thought he was going to die, fear rose in him. It also felt unjust and infuriating to be killed by a bastard like that.

When the beating stopped for a moment, the Fist Demon opened his eyes.

Qiu Jiawu was taking out a dagger.

“I won’t let you die easily.”

The Fist Demon lifted his dazed eyes and looked up at the night sky one last time. He had thought he should at least see the stars or moon before he died.

‘Huh?’

Beyond Qiu Jiawu’s shoulder, farther back on a tree branch, a man was sitting and looking down at him. Thinking he might be seeing things, the Fist Demon closed his eyes and opened them again.

But the man sitting with the bright full moon at his back was still looking down at him. He seemed a few years older than the Fist Demon.

The Fist Demon’s eyes met his. He had never once looked at a person and thought they were frightening, but the moment he saw this man’s cold gaze, fear crossed his mind.

‘Is he the death god come to take me?’

He even had that thought.

But he was not a death god. Someone among the Black Dao bastards had discovered him.

“Huh? There’s someone over there!”

When everyone’s eyes focused on him, the man lightly jumped down from the tree branch.

He had leaped down from quite a height, yet there was not even the sound of his feet landing. At that astonishing movement art, both the Black Dao men and the Fist Demon were startled.

The man slowly walked over and looked at the Fist Demon. He did not give the Black Dao men so much as a glance.

He asked the Fist Demon,

“Can you fight?”

The Fist Demon nodded and raised himself up. His entire body felt as if it would break apart, but he gritted his teeth.

“I can fight.”

Then the man looked at Qiu Jiawu.

“How can you behave so cowardly against a fighter?”

Given that he knew he was a fighter, he was not simply someone passing by. The man already seemed to know why this situation was unfolding.

“Where have you come from?”

Qiu Jiawu was inwardly tense. He was worried that perhaps a master from the Sado Alliance had appeared because of this manipulation.

Then the young man uttered words he had not expected at all.

“Fight fair and square.”

Whoosh.

Before he could even dodge, the man’s finger wind flew in and sealed Qiu Jiawu’s internal energy. A method that could seal an opponent’s internal energy with a single point of a finger was a realm one only heard about over drinks.

Startled, Qiu Jiawu shouted reflexively.

“Kill him! Kill this bastard too!”

But no one dared charge at the man. Even aside from the ability he had already displayed, there was a certain dignity about him that made it impossible to treat him carelessly.

The man looked at the Black Dao bastards and gestured for them to stand in a circle. As if possessed by a ghost, the Black Dao men formed a circle. In that way, a small arena was created.

The Fist Demon, having risen from the ground, clenched his fists. He did not know who the man who had helped him was, but he had only one thought: if he missed this chance, he would die.

Qiu Jiawu likewise realized that if he wanted to survive, he had to kill the Fist Demon. Fortunately, though his internal energy had been sealed, his dagger and sword had not been taken from him.

‘Then I have a chance!’

He had no chance. Qiu Jiawu swung his dagger, lost it, then drew his sword and swung that too, but in the end, even the sword was taken from him.

At last, a fist-to-fist fight began, and there was no way he could handle barehanded an opponent he could not defeat even with a sword.

Thud! Thwack! Crack! Smack!

The Fist Demon’s furious fists drove into every part of his body.

“Why! Why torment someone who was living just fine! Why!”

Qiu Jiawu’s jawbone and ribs broke.

In the end, unable to endure any longer, Qiu Jiawu collapsed and lay there, only panting for breath. The Fist Demon pulled open the front of his upper garment and showed him the wound.

“You told me to look at this scar whenever a stupid thought came to mind, didn’t you? You can look at it all you want and die.”

Boom! Boom! Boom! Boom!

Under the barrage of fists, Qiu Jiawu’s breath was cut off.

The Fist Demon rose, panting, and looked at the man.

At that moment, a sword rose into the air before the man.

Shrrrrrrrrrk.

Before the young man, the sword split apart, forming sword-shaped sword qi, and they all rose into the sky at once. Just as the Black Dao men looked up at it with fascinated expressions—

Swishswishswishswishswishswishswishswishswish!

The sword qi floating in the air rained down toward the Black Dao men.

Their heads, faces, and chests pierced by sword qi, the Black Dao men collapsed all at once.

The man did not leave a single Black Dao member alive.

Geom Mugeuk, who had been listening to the Fist Demon’s story, cried out.

“The Flying Heaven Sword Art, Flowing Heaven Form! It was Father!”

“That’s right. It was the Cult Leader.”

Geom Mugeuk was shocked. He had not known that the person who had discovered the Fist Demon was his father.

“He was the Cult Leader at your age. The Cult Leader before he had even been designated successor.”

For some reason, Geom Mugeuk was deeply moved by the fact that his father had saved the Fist Demon.

“Standing there in that place strewn with corpses, the Cult Leader said this to me: ‘Would you like to turn those baby fists into the fists of a real man?’”

“Ah!”

Only now could Geom Mugeuk understand where the words “baby hands” the Fist Demon had said to him had originated.

“So what did you say?”

“I said no. How could I follow him when I didn’t know who he was? What if he was an assassin? What if he was an unparalleled villain? I said, ‘I am truly grateful that you saved me, but I refuse.’”

Geom Mugeuk thought that the Fist Demon, who had said no even in that situation, was remarkable. The Fist Demon of his youth felt vividly alive, pulsing with energy. That liveliness was hidden within this man’s heart. Who could have known that the Fist Demon, who silently watched over his subordinates’ training every day and seemed stiflingly rigid and old-fashioned, had such a youth?

“So what did Father say?”

“He turned away without a word, with a look that said, ‘Suit yourself.’ I was watching the Cult Leader’s back when it suddenly occurred to me that this wasn’t right. If I didn’t follow him, I would never escape this arena for the rest of my life. I had survived this time by luck, but someday I would die while suffering another filthy situation like this.”

The Fist Demon recalled that moment. His fate had changed because of the decision he made in that brief instant.

“Perhaps if the Cult Leader had tried to persuade me to come with him, I might have stubbornly refused to the very end. Back then, I thought youthful recklessness and stubbornness were something admirable. But as I watched the Cult Leader’s back grow farther away, my body moved instinctively. I ran after him. I said I was sorry, that I had changed my mind. Then the Cult Leader looked at me and smiled. You know it too, don’t you? That unique smile of his…”

“That sneer that looks like he’s looking down on people?”

“That’s right, that smile.”

“My father is probably the person in the world best suited to that smile.”

“To be honest, the Cult Leader that day was impressive. He had a different kind of charm from you.”

“Am I impressive too?”

The Fist Demon pretended not to hear me and continued his story.

“The Cult Leader brought me to the main cult and, without saying one thing or another, placed me in the Eastern Fist Gate. And after that, he never once came to see me.”

“That sounds like Father.”

“On my own, I climbed from White Fist to Black Fist. Back then, I truly made blood-soaked efforts.”

With his innate sense and talent for fist arts, and effort on top of that, the Fist Demon of those days must have drawn everyone’s attention.

“On the day I became a Black Fist, the Cult Leader came to see me. As if he had been watching me all along, he came exactly on that day. And do you know what he said to me?”

“What did he say?”

As he recalled that day, the Fist Demon’s voice trembled slightly.

“I will become the Heavenly Demon, so you become my Fist Demon.”

The relationship between the two of them was special not merely because they were people mad for martial arts. It was because they had this hidden past.

Imitating his father, Geom Mugeuk said,

“I will become the Heavenly Demon too, so…”

“You’re too late. I am already the Fist Demon.”

“Ah! That’s right. This is something I should say to Cheon Sohee. Please wait a moment. I’ll find words that suit us.”

The Fist Demon gazed at Geom Mugeuk.

Over Geom Mugeuk’s figure, the image of the young Heavenly Demon Geom Woojin overlapped.

Two people similar yet different. If Geom Mugeuk was light, Geom Woojin was darkness. If one seemed so light he might fly away, the other seemed so heavy he might sink forever.

But the Fist Demon also felt it. That this light and darkness were not so different. That they were dark yet bright, and bright yet dark.

“The Cult Leader took me away from this place, while you brought me here.”

The Fist Demon had met two young candidates for Cult Leader, connected by blood, in this place. He felt this was some great destiny.

Then Geom Mugeuk calmly looked at the Fist Demon and spoke evenly.

“I will become your disciple, so please become my master!”

Chapter 198: Do You Think That Would Be Easy?
---------------------------------------------
The words the Fist Demon always said did not come.

A good attempt.

Had the attempt finally worked? Or did it mean he would no longer even mention it?

Without a word, the Fist Demon turned his gaze back to the arena. Two men were fighting fiercely, blood flying. And the crowd was going wild for them.

“This place will look the same even after several decades pass.”

“My son may one day come here with you, Fist Demon, when you’ve become a grandfather.”

At that, the Fist Demon looked at me in surprise.

“Why are you so surprised?”

“Do you intend to marry?”

“If there is someone good.”

Of course, I had no intention of marrying at all, but I gave the ordinary answer.

The Fist Demon spoke firmly.

“If it is not someone you truly love, I recommend you do not.”

Since he had told me of his youth and said he had resolved to go through life without family or friends, I thought it was only natural for him to give such advice. But there was another reason behind it.

“Even for someone as outstanding as you, a succession struggle without bloodshed is this difficult. Do you think your children could accomplish it as you have? It is not something ordinary people can bear.”

It was both a statement that valued me highly and a concern for future generations. Above all, it was based on the assumption that I became the Heavenly Demon.

“I will judge carefully.”

“Come to think of it, I have spoken of cutting off the Heavenly Demon bloodline.”

“It doesn’t matter. It will be after I’m dead.”

The Fist Demon’s expression, upon hearing my words, said this: If it were truly you, you might genuinely be thinking so carefreely.

“Let us leave.”

“Yes.”

At the exit, the Fist Demon looked back at the arena one last time. Would he ever come to this arena again in his lifetime? The future was unknowable, but the Fist Demon was probably looking at it with the heart of someone who would never return. When he looked around, his gaze was slow and filled with complicated emotions, but once he turned away, he strode out with great steps.

After leaving the arena, we walked without deciding on a destination. The Fist Demon no longer asked where we were going. I too walked along the road without a thought, as if having no plan was the plan.

“Have you ever lost your way?”

“No.”

“Neither have I.”

Perhaps that was why this loafing journey was all the more precious and meaningful.

Neither he nor I had ever lived so aimlessly before. The pleasure of losing one’s way in life had been something unimaginable.

“Right, I lost the bet. I admit it.”

“No. I lost this bet.”

That was unexpected. Though he had said it was fine, I had thought he could not have felt entirely good watching the match-fixing happen.

“It was enjoyable after so long. Thanks to you, I also recalled old memories.”

Wasn’t this what he wanted to say? Thanks to you, I showed you what kind of person I am.

“Then our battle of pride is one to one.”

“We must settle it.”

“Of course.”

At that moment, the Fist Demon said,

“Before that, there is somewhere I want to go.”

For the first time, the Fist Demon said he wanted to go somewhere.

“If it is with you, Fist Demon, I would go even to hell!”

“The place we are going is not so hot. Rather, it is a place where we should bring thick cotton clothes.”

And so, leaving behind the arena filled with the Fist Demon’s old memories, we began walking along the lengthening sunset.

* * *

After traveling a long road, the Fist Demon and I stood together at the summit of a snowy mountain.

Whoooooosh.

The wind that had driven the snow swept up the snow piled around us and lifted it into the air.

“Why did you want to come here?”

The place the Fist Demon had said he wanted to go was this snowy mountain.

“This is the place where the previous Fist Demon took me as his successor.”

“Ah, is that so?”

It was a fact even I had not known.

“In truth, my master and I did not suit each other well. When he chose his final successor, my master wanted to choose someone else. His martial arts were inferior to mine, but he was far more amiable toward my master. Do you know why my master chose me?”

“Because of my father?”

“That is right. Because of the Cult Leader.”

When the young Fist Demon had been placed in the Eastern Fist Sect, my father had not yet been the Cult Leader. But by the time the Fist Demon formally inherited the position of Demon Supreme, my father had ascended to the seat of the Heavenly Demon.

The previous Fist Demon could not have ignored the gaze of my father, who had become the Heavenly Demon.

“I became the Fist Demon because of your father.”

“Is that not an even greater thing for you, Fist Demon?”

“Greater?”

“Because my father’s eye for people must have been far superior to the previous Fist Demon’s. My father chose a young fighter throwing punches in an arena without a shred of internal energy. That fighter went alone from White Fist to Black Fist, and in the end became the Fist Demon. When it comes to judging people, trust my father.”

“There was a time when I was full of confidence. If not me, who could become the Fist Demon? But now, I myself do not know. Was the Cult Leader’s judgment truly correct?”

“If that judgment had been wrong, the other Demon Supremes would not have named you as the Demon Supreme they find most difficult to face. No matter who sees it, the person suited to that position is you, Fist Demon.”

The Fist Demon silently gazed at the snowy landscape. It was a rapturous scene that could only be enjoyed from this summit, standing tall in a world entirely white.

I held in high regard the Fist Demon’s way of looking back upon himself. Without such a heart, he would harden like a fossil into a stubborn old man. Because he was someone who looked within himself, I believed the Fist Demon could bring down the cliff.

I conveyed my honest feelings to him.

“At first, I was curious what kind of person you were, Fist Demon. Then I became enamored with your goal of bringing down the cliff, and I wanted to bring it down too. Now, I like your true self even more.”

“My true self?”

“I think the way you were in your youth, fighting against the black path, is your true self. When we return, I want to gather all the Iron Fists and tell them that story. Hey! This is the kind of person your Fist Demon is! Like that.”

“Did you say only such pleasant words to the other Demon Supremes as well?”

“They were not pleasant words, but honest words. Words others would not say because of the position of Demon Supreme. Ah, of course, I admit I flattered them to some degree. I wanted to see them favorably if possible, and I wanted to become close to them if possible. But I did not fabricate things that did not exist. I said the kinds of words that made me think, I hope someone says this about my life someday as well.”

Then I looked at the Fist Demon and added,

“People don’t say them much, do they?”

Even if one teaches hundreds, even if one resides where tens of thousands of cultists come and go.

“Such words... you don’t hear them often, do you?”

Would there be someone to say such words to me later? I had said them all for others.

The Fist Demon silently stared at me. After gazing at me for a long while, he then looked for another long while at the mountain across from us.

“What do you intend to do if you become the Heavenly Demon? Is your dream perhaps the unification of murim?”

“The opposite.”

“The opposite?”

“I will stop anyone from unifying murim. Even if that person is my father.”

The Fist Demon’s eyes shone.

“So you knew as well.”

Those words meant the Fist Demon knew too.

“I know that my father dreams of unifying murim.”

“Yes, there is no way someone as clever as you would not have noticed.”

“What do you think, Fist Demon?”

“I will follow as your father wishes. If he starts a war and commands me to kill them all, I will kill them all without hesitation. No matter who they are.”

Only now could I know for certain. My father’s right arm was the Fist Demon. Then was his left arm the Blood Heaven Blade Demon? The Blood Heaven Blade Demon had said the elixir he gave me was something he had received from my father.

“I will stop him. My father, and you as well, Fist Demon.”

After that, we stood there in silence for a long time.

Whoooooooosh.

When the blowing snowstorm swept coolly past us, the Fist Demon’s decision came to me on the wind.

“Mugeuk, I will take you as my first disciple.”

I was startled. Though becoming his disciple had been my goal, I had not expected him to take me as his disciple like this, in this place. Moreover, this was the very moment we had confirmed that we held opposing thoughts regarding the unification of murim.

His large, thick body turned toward me. That frightening face I had now grown used to—yet the look in his eyes now was different from usual. With a deep and serious gaze, the Fist Demon said,

“I will become your master, so become my disciple.”

The moment I heard those words, emotion surged within me. Joy welled up. In the end, I had succeeded in moving this man’s heart. It was not because I had gained a Demon Supreme called the Fist Demon, nor because I could obtain his fist art. I was simply so happy that this person had become my master.

“Waaaaaaaaah!”

I was so happy that I leaped on the spot. Like a puppy excited by falling snow, I expressed my joy to my heart’s content.

When I rejoiced sincerely, the Fist Demon’s expression softened. To the point that I wondered whether such softness had been hidden in that frightening face.

Because I was so happy, I unleashed the First Fist. Since it could cause an avalanche, I released it into empty air where there was nothing. I poured out the Second Fist in an instant as well.

“Thank you. Thank you so much.”

Right there, I performed the Nine Bows Ceremony (九拜之禮).

“Disciple Geom Mugeuk formally greets Master. From now on, please guide this foolish disciple well with your great teachings.”

The Fist Demon’s large hand rested on my bowed shoulder. With that rough, thick hand, he lightly patted my shoulder. Come to think of it, this was the first time I had felt the Fist Demon’s gentle touch.

“Rise.”

I stood up.

“Master.”

The Fist Demon was someone I wanted as my master. He was a person fully qualified for it.

“My disciple.”

It seemed the Fist Demon did not dislike taking on his first disciple either.

“From now on, I will transmit the complete Thunderbolt Asura Fist to you.”

The Fist Demon did not delay the transmission of his martial art. Nor did he demand anything from me in exchange for it. The Fist Demon was that kind of man.

At the summit of the snowy mountain, the Fist Demon began transmitting the martial arts from the Third Fist to the Sixth Fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

The Third Fist, Heavenly Thunder Asura (天雷修羅).

It was the heaviest of the Fist Demon’s fists. It was not like Black Cloud Asura, which struck enemies at a distance, nor was it an attack like Thunderbolt Asura, which sent the fist flying swiftly. It was a form that struck directly with the fist, and the power contained in each and every blow was tremendous.

The Fourth Fist, Iron Leg Asura (鐵脚修羅).

The Thunderbolt Asura Fist did not consist solely of forms that used the fists. There was also a form that smashed the opponent with powerful kicks. The movements themselves were so splendid and impressive that I was certain admiration would always follow whenever Iron Leg Asura was displayed.

The Fifth Fist, Vajra Asura (金剛修羅).

It was a form that temporarily turned the body as hard as steel to protect it. Though it consumed an extreme amount of internal energy, it was a form that could save one’s life in a moment of crisis.

If I used the Fifth Fist together with the Heavenly Demon Body Protection Art, I wondered if I might temporarily achieve the effect of the Vajra Indestructible Body.

And the final Sixth Fist, Yama Thunder Asura (閻雷修羅).

The last fist of the Thunderbolt Asura Fist. It bore the name of the lightning struck down by the King of Hell, and it was the final form that poured all the internal energy within the body into a single strike. It was also the form meant to bring down the cliff, but if this move did not work, one would be killed by the opponent.

After all, if the enemy was powerful enough that I had to use the final Sixth Fist, then should this form fail, I would have to face him without a shred of internal energy.

At the summit of the snowy mountain, I received instruction in the Thunderbolt Asura Fist for seven days. From the First Fist to the final fist, I learned it all again in meticulous detail.

In that process, I also received all the various insights into fist arts that the Fist Demon had gained throughout his life. Once he had made up his mind to take me as his disciple, the Fist Demon truly gave without holding anything back. Perhaps I, too, had fallen for that refreshing decisiveness of his.

What remained now was for me to train diligently and achieve great mastery.

From now on, even if I lost my sword in battle, that situation would not become an opportunity for my opponent. Rather, that moment would become my chance to strike a careless opponent.

“When we return to the cult, I intend to make two announcements. The first is that I have accepted you as my disciple. The second is that the Eastern Fist Sect will officially support the Eldest Young Master.”

It was as I had expected. With this announcement, the cult would once again be thrown into an uproar. How would the other Demon Supremes or my elder brother react? And what about my father?

“Everyone will see me as a greedy man with a foot in both your camp and the Eldest Young Master’s.”

“What does it matter if they see you that way? You are going to live that way, Master. If they envy you, you can tell them, ‘Then you do the same. Do you think it’s easy? You cowards!’”

The Fist Demon laughed. I could feel that his laughter toward me had now become comfortable.

“Let us descend, my disciple.”

“Yes, Master.”

Together with the Fist Demon, I walked down the snowy mountain. We descended, leaving footprints in the snow no one else had trodden. Both he and I had already reached the realm of Treading Snow Without Trace (踏雪無痕), but we deliberately pressed our footprints deeply into the snow as we came down.

If I could have, I wanted to stamp footprints into the stone ground and set up a sign beside them that read:

The path where the Fist Demon and Geom Mugeuk formed the bond of master and disciple and walked down together.

After descending the mountain, we naturally set our direction toward the main cult.

Before departing, I stopped by the West Lake Inn near the snowy mountain to check whether any message had arrived for me.

“Ah! Master. There is somewhere I must stop by.”

“Where?”

“My people have sent word asking to meet me.”

Gowol and Cult Leader Pungcheon, who were out in the Central Plains building an intelligence organization, had sent me a message asking to meet.

“So? You mean to abandon me?”

“Abandon you?”

“Are you not telling me to go back alone? Now that you have learned all my martial arts, you are saying, ‘You, whose sweetness has been sucked dry, go away.’ Is that not it? Ah! This is why, since ancient times, they have said one must never give everything at once.”

Because I had never expected such words to come from the Fist Demon’s mouth, I burst into loud laughter.

“Well, if you come with me, I do not know when we will return.”

Gowol would not have contacted me over something trivial.

“Will the Iron Fists’ lessons be all right?”

Even now, he had been away for quite a number of days. I had asked out of consideration, thinking the Fist Demon must be worrying about it inwardly.

“What is there to worry about? I have a disciple now.”

“You have learned words that make a person feel good very quickly.”

The Fist Demon strode ahead and said,

“Of course. Whose master do you think I am?”

Chapter 199: Do I Look Like Someone Who’d Be Robbed?
----------------------------------------------------
Go Wol, who had been sitting at his desk reading documents, asked without lifting his gaze.

“Cult Leader, have you had something on your mind lately?”

The Leader of the Pungcheon Cult was leaning against the window, looking outside.

“What do you mean, something on my mind? Why ask all of a sudden?”

The two of them had been moving from place to place throughout the Central Plains, devoting themselves to building an intelligence organization. Go Wol was always busy, and since the Pungcheon Cult Leader served as both guard and assistant, he had comparatively more leisure.

“It seems like you do.”

“Who in the world has a life as easy as mine? I have no worries.”

“Originally, it’s busy people who don’t have worries.”

“I said I don’t.”

At that firm reply, Go Wol lifted his eyes from the documents and looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader. As if he didn’t. The Pungcheon Cult Leader, staring out the window, was clearly in low spirits, and lately he had the look of someone troubled by something.

“Is it hard?”

“Hard, my foot.”

“It’s hard for me.”

At Go Wol’s words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader turned to look at him. As if he meant it, Go Wol added,

“It’s hard. If you hadn’t been beside me helping, I wouldn’t have endured it.”

“Then who told you to work so hard? Since when were you overflowing with such loyalty?”

“Should I start doing things halfheartedly from now on?”

“As if you could.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader turned his head back toward the window.

“Usually, saying it’s hard and you’re dying is what you do, Cult Leader. This isn’t like you.”

“What exactly is like me? No. You’ll just start spouting things that look down on me again. Don’t!”

But Go Wol was not someone who would stop just because he was told to.

“Someone who may look petty on the outside, but has a heart as vast as the sea within; someone who seems to live carelessly, but is drawing a grand design in his heart. Someone who snaps and squawks, but is deep inside. That sort of person is you, Cult Leader.”

“Hmph! You meant the opposite, didn’t you? That I’m petty, live carelessly, and squawk?”

“And someone quicker on the uptake than anyone!”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader wore an expression that said, I knew it. Normally, a stream of retorts would have poured out of him. But the Pungcheon Cult Leader still kept his eyes outside the window. Go Wol rose from his chair and walked forward.

“Could it be homesickness?”

“Since you mention homesickness, let me say this: this time I realized for certain that I’m not someone who likes his hometown. I don’t think of it at all. I don’t feel like going back at all, either. Maybe I’ll go once before I die. I don’t think I’ll have any reason to go there again.”

“It’s not homesickness either. Then what could it be?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s lips twitched. He looked as if there was something he wanted to say, but then he changed the subject.

“Why isn’t the Second Young Master coming? You did contact him properly, didn’t you?”

“He’s busy, isn’t he?”

“Busy, my foot. We’ve been pushed down his list of priorities. He does everything he wants first, and then, huh? That shackle from back then sent word asking to see me. Should I meet him once? Or not?”

“If that’s the case, aren’t you investing far too much money in that shackle from back then?”

An enormous amount of funding was being poured into this undertaking. The intelligence network had not yet been laid across the entire Central Plains, and already the funds were running low again.

“Maybe he’s got money rotting away.”

“Cult Leader. What are you angry about? Are you depressed because you aren’t losing weight?”

“Hmph! If I put my mind to it, I could lose it all in a month.”

“You said that a month ago too, didn’t you?”

“When a person gets older, if he’s too thin, he looks like he has no presence.”

“You have too much presence, Cult Leader.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader whipped around and frowned, and Go Wol made a gesture to show he had been joking.

“What’s troubling you?”

After a brief pause, the Pungcheon Cult Leader said,

“If I tell you the reason, you’ll make fun of me again, won’t you?”

“I’ll hear it first.”

“You won’t say you absolutely won’t make fun of me.”

In the end, the Pungcheon Cult Leader began to speak.

“Do you remember Seok Chung, whom we met not long ago?”

“I remember.”

The process of establishing an intelligence organization in the Central Plains involved a tremendous amount of work. They truly had to meet countless people. And in the course of doing so, they would inevitably meet people like Seok Chung.

“That bastard made slips of the tongue to me that day, and acted rudely too.”

Go Wol nodded. Would he have done so if he had known the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s true identity or skill? Since he had merely been introduced as some wealthy man with money, Seok Chung had trusted in his paltry martial arts, put on airs, and behaved rudely.

“You endured it well, Cult Leader.”

“I keep thinking about that bastard. I should have smashed him to pieces then. No, at the very least, I should have lectured him that he shouldn’t act that way. At the time, I held back because I wanted the matter to be handled smoothly. But now, after the fact, I keep thinking about it.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could not count how many times he had killed the man in his imagination. In truth, it had not been a mistake serious enough to warrant killing him, yet anger kept surging up, making him want to kill him.

“You know that guy before, the one with the filthy expression? I held back well then. But this time, it won’t go away so easily. Ah, I know. Don’t say it. I know very well how narrow-minded I am. But what am I supposed to do when I keep thinking about it?”

“I understand.”

“You understand?”

“He was a piece of shit. Even if you had beaten him up, Cult Leader, I wouldn’t have stopped you.”

“Really?”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader brightened.

“Then maybe I should have beaten him up. Should I go find him now and beat him up?”

Go Wol merely watched the Pungcheon Cult Leader in silence. Only then did the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s excitement subside.

“Say what you want to say already.”

“I have nothing.”

“I said say it!”

“Everyone lives like this. Because you’re a cult leader, you’ve lived until now without having to see much filth. Everyone lives while enduring things like this. Beating someone because you’re angry, beating someone because you feel bad, beating someone because he’s impolite—there aren’t many people who can live that kind of life.”

“You mean you want to say only someone like the Second Young Master lives like that, right?”

“You’re the same, Cult Leader. You lived that way too. You spent your whole life living comfortably, and it hasn’t been long since you started living while enduring things, has it?”

As if he acknowledged at least that part, the Pungcheon Cult Leader nodded.

Go Wol understood why the Pungcheon Cult Leader was struggling. How hard must it be? He had lived a life that would make anyone envious, and now he was following Go Wol around without even a single attendant, acting as his guard. That was why Go Wol always thought this.

‘Cult Leader, you must have committed a great sin against me in a past life.’

Go Wol walked over beside the Pungcheon Cult Leader and looked outside alongside him.

“It’s not because you’re narrow-minded that you keep thinking about it. It’s because resentment has built up in your chest.”

“Resentment?”

“Traveling the Central Plains and doing this work—this is your first time doing something like it, isn’t it? Meeting people, and then meeting more people. It may not seem like much, but it’s work that accumulates anger without you realizing it. Until now, you’ve been enduring it for my sake, Cult Leader.”

“I’m glad you know. Still, now that I’ve said it, I feel a bit relieved.”

“From now on, don’t endure it. Just beat them up.”

“No. I’ll still endure it. If I was born narrow-minded, I should at least make an effort. Whether I live this way or that way, I’m going to die anyway. I won’t end it pathetically.”

Go Wol looked at him. The Pungcheon Cult Leader was sincere.

“Would you like to curse the Second Young Master once and clear your chest out?”

“Can I?”

“It’s been a while. Go ahead!”

As if he had been waiting for it, the Pungcheon Cult Leader shouted loudly.

“You lazy Second Young Master! If we call, you should hurry up and come. What are you doing, being this late? Have you forgotten us? Just try forgetting us! Have you ever been eaten by a fish in your own pond? I’ll just—chomp! I’ll swallow you from the head down! No matter how much you treat me like that cult leader from back then, that shackle from back then, we’re living just fine like this! You wicked Second Young Master! You ugly Second Young Master! You Second Young Master who cheats with lightness arts! Ah! Even shouting like this makes me feel better. Strategist Go, you do it too! All that pent-up indigestion will vanish at once. As expected, cursing the Second Young Master is the most refreshing.”

Then Go Wol said with a solemn expression,

“Cult Leader, how dare you utter such insolent nonsense?”

“Hm?”

At Go Wol’s sudden change, the Pungcheon Cult Leader blinked with an expression that asked what he was doing. Then he sensed a presence behind him.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader slowly turned around.

Geom Mugeuk was standing behind him, having arrived at some point.

“You were insulting me again.”

Why now of all times! He had been caught cursing him before too. The Pungcheon Cult Leader was flustered, but he was not one to simply back down.

“Second Young Master! First fix that habit of appearing whenever someone curses you! Of course, I’m not someone who wouldn’t say something just because you were here, but……”

Just then, the Fist Demon entered after him.

The moment he saw him, the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s expression became serious in an instant. The playfulness vanished from his face, and he returned to being the Pungcheon Cult Leader. Lowering his voice, he greeted the Fist Demon.

“It has been a long time.”

“It is good to see you.”

The two greeted each other with clasped fists. The Fist Demon likewise revealed an aura different from when he was alone with Geom Mugeuk. Right now, they were a cult leader and a Demon Venerable. This was what they had originally been like, but after meeting Geom Mugeuk, everyone had been changing.

Geom Mugeuk introduced Go Wol to the Fist Demon.

“This is my strategist, Go Wol, who would be perfect if only he were good at baduk.”

Go Wol said with a smile,

“I am holding myself back from learning because I do not wish to clash with the Chief Strategist already.”

He said this because the person teaching baduk to the Heavenly Demon was the Chief Strategist. In other words, it was also confidence that if he learned and taught Geom Mugeuk, he could make him defeat the Cult Leader.

“Now, please come in.”

Geom Mugeuk and the Fist Demon entered and sat down.

Go Wol brought out tea.

“This is all I have to serve you.”

“You have been working hard, coming all this way.”

“I am working happily, thinking of it as a tour of the Central Plains.”

Geom Mugeuk spoke respectfully to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“You have suffered much. Because you are protecting him, Cult Leader, I do not worry about Strategist Go at all.”

“I do it because I want to.”

Go Wol added credit to the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s efforts.

“If not for the Cult Leader, I would already have died several times over.”

In truth, danger had come upon them several times, but they had overcome the crises thanks to the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

Geom Mugeuk looked at the Pungcheon Cult Leader with gentle eyes. He knew very well how great a sacrifice the man had made to come to him. Of course, the reason was Go Wol, but in any case, Geom Mugeuk was the one enjoying the fruit of it. He always called him “that cult leader from back then,” but he had not the slightest intention of truly making him into that.

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said to the Fist Demon,

“I heard the news that you left the cult together with the Second Young Master.”

“I was enjoying some leisure for the first time in a long while.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader asked casually,

“By any chance, has the Second Young Master taken anything from you?”

“Taken anything?”

“If you let your guard down for even a moment, what is mine ends up in the Second Young Master’s hands. I wondered whether our Fist Demon had experienced such a thing as well.”

Because the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s eyes seemed to be saying, You must have had something taken too, the Fist Demon firmly shook his head.

“No such thing has happened.”

At the same time, the Fist Demon sent back a look that said, Do I look like someone who would have something taken from him?

Amid the invisible war of nerves, the Pungcheon Cult Leader felt upset inside.

‘Am I the only one who had everything taken by the Second Young Master?’

Even now, he sometimes thought of the sacred token he had given Geom Mugeuk. Since he had left the cult anyway, it was not that he had any lingering attachment to or greed for the sacred token itself.

It was just that sometimes he felt doubt—whether he was the kind of person who foolishly gave away everything he did not need to give, whether he was the only one living at a loss. Those feelings were also playing a part in the melancholy he had been feeling lately.

Then Geom Mugeuk said,

“Ah, that’s right. I have come to take the Fist Demon as my master.”

At those words, the Pungcheon Cult Leader was startled.

“Truly?”

“Yes. From now on, the Fist Demon is my master.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader could not hide his surprise. Soon, his mood improved as he looked at the Fist Demon.

‘You had something taken too! You had your entire self taken!’

What made the Pungcheon Cult Leader happiest of all was this.

‘I wasn’t the only one being taken advantage of!’

It was truly a great comfort to him. Of course, the Fist Demon would never acknowledge it at all.

Go Wol clasped his fists and offered congratulations.

“Congratulations on forming the bond of master and disciple.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader also congratulated the Fist Demon.

“Congratulations.”

“Thank you.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader’s mouth itched.

‘I hope you do not become that master from back then.’

If he had been even a little closer to the Fist Demon, it was something he surely would have ended up saying.

“I will go speak with Strategist Go for a moment.”

Geom Mugeuk and Go Wol went outside.

Now only the Fist Demon and the Pungcheon Cult Leader remained there. The one who broke the awkward silence and spoke first was the Pungcheon Cult Leader.

“You made a great decision.”

“It somehow turned out that way.”

Of course. When you come to your senses, things have turned out this way. A smile formed at the corner of the Pungcheon Cult Leader’s mouth.

This time, the Fist Demon asked,

“You are the one who made a great decision, Cult Leader.”

“Looking at me, Demon Venerable, I may seem somewhat ridiculous.”

“Ridiculous? Why would you say such a thing?”

“I mean, seeing me abandon the Pungcheon Cult and come into the Central Plains to be here alone like this.”

Originally, the two of them were people who would never have exchanged even a single word. But because of the central figure that was Geom Mugeuk, conversations they would never have had before were passing between them.

“Why are you alone? That strategist from earlier is here. My disciple is here too, is he not?”

“According to Strategist Go, you support the Eldest Young Master.”

“That is correct.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader said something unexpected.

“Do not treat him carelessly just because he is your disciple.”

The Pungcheon Cult Leader was not afraid of the Fist Demon at all.

“You have only staked half, but we have staked everything on your disciple.”

The Fist Demon gave no answer and drank his tea.

Meanwhile, Geom Mugeuk and Go Wol came out into the yard. Once the two of them were alone, Geom Mugeuk comforted him for his efforts.

“You have suffered much.”

“It must have been less hardship than becoming the Fist Demon’s disciple.”

Geom Mugeuk did not deny it and laughed.

“Before I tell you why I left word asking to see you, there is one thing I would like to ask of you separately.”

“Tell me.”

“The Cult Leader has been struggling lately.”

How? For what reason? How much?

Geom Mugeuk did not ask such questions. The fact that Go Wol was speaking to him separately like this was enough for him to infer all of it.

“I still dream of the time when I was bound in shackles. At times, I even converse with the Yin Thunder Sect. It seems truly difficult for a person to break free from the past. The Cult Leader must be the same.”

Geom Mugeuk looked at him and smiled.

“I will speak to him separately.”

“Thank you.”

Having told Geom Mugeuk, Go Wol no longer worried about it.

“What is the reason you asked to see me?”

Then Go Wol said something unexpected.

“Have you perhaps heard of the Immortal Debt?”

Chapter 200: Are You Ready to Borrow Money?
-------------------------------------------
Immortal Loan.

It was a term born as the opposite of the vicious usurious loan known as Yama Debt (閻王債). Money lent without interest—money given by immortals—was called Immortal Loan.

Before my regression, a major incident related to Immortal Loan had occurred, so of course I knew about it. And the moment I heard the name, I thought to myself: So those bastards had already been moving from this point onward. The Immortal Loan incident would not be revealed to the world until much later.

Gowol explained Immortal Loan.

“It’s a new term that has appeared recently. It refers to lending money without taking interest. It’s spreading in secret, mainly among young martial artists.”

Immortal Loan.

It was also a term that heralded the beginning of evils so vile even demons would shake their heads.

“When I first heard in Guizhou that someone was lending money to young martial artists without interest, I thought some wealthy magnate was acting out of goodwill. But afterward I heard about it in Chongqing as well, and in Hubei too. Ah, of course, I wouldn’t have known if I weren’t in the business of handling information. It’s spreading quite discreetly.”

And Gowol’s suspicion began right there.

“Who on earth is lending money without even taking interest? And why only to young martial artists, of all people?”

Gowol already seemed to have sensed that there was something behind this matter.

“When I looked into it, a mysterious organization called Hyeopgye (協契) was the one lending the money.”

The name carried the meaning of helping one another, but their real name, later revealed, was the Dark Merchant Realm (暗黑商界).

“There was one decisively strange point.”

“What was it?”

“They say there is no repayment date written on the promissory notes.”

“No interest, and no repayment date? Then the borrowers would lose any sense of responsibility and have no intention of paying it back, wouldn’t they?”

“That’s right. So people began borrowing again and again without repaying.”

It was a method to make the other party keep borrowing more money. They were all young and clever martial artists, but they still lacked the experience to withstand the despicable scheme of hiding a knife behind a smile, designed with deliberate intent.

“They must have misunderstood. No repayment date doesn’t mean you can put it off forever. It means they can come at any time and demand that you repay the principal.”

What if they suddenly came, placed all the accumulated promissory notes before you, and demanded that you repay the entire principal at once?

If you said you couldn’t, that would be when they started dropping all pretense. Depending on the target’s disposition and personality, they might use force or threaten to kill their family. They might appeal to their conscience, or soothe them with kind words. In the meantime, they would already have investigated every weakness there was to exploit, and because the borrower had freely taken money that carried no interest, the justification in the eyes of the world would also belong to them.

“They’re using money to completely seize control of a person’s life. From the start, they probably didn’t lend that money with any intention of recovering it. They’ll use the debt as an excuse to make them do other things. The moment someone commits even a single evil deed on the condition that their debt will be forgiven, they become those bastards’ slave for life.”

Gowol looked at me with admiration.

There was no need to admire me like that. I hadn’t figured it out because I was clever; it was something that had actually happened. You’re the impressive one for catching this conspiracy without letting it slip by.

Those Dark Realm bastards used indebted youths to commit countless atrocities. Because of them, many innocent people lost their lives. There were also many young people who could not endure the suffering and took their own lives.

The fact that the Dark Merchant Realm was behind all of it was only revealed after a great many years had passed. Their wicked atrocities, with which they had thoroughly toyed with people using money.

At least when dealing with those who used Yama Debt, one could be wary from the start. These people approached with smiling faces and sucked the marrow from the bones of the young.

By the time they were later branded public enemies of the murim, the leaders, including the head of the Dark Realm, had already earned an immense fortune and gone into hiding. In the end, they were never caught.

“I want to confirm just how much money they have, for them to be playing such tricks.”

I could understand Gowol’s intention. Since we were suffering from a shortage of funds right now, he meant to deal with these villains and recover the money. Since he was in the position of spending money directly to build an organization, he must have felt apologetic toward me as well.

“We’re short on money anyway. Shall we get a little help from the immortals?”

When I readily went along with it, Gowol’s expression brightened.

“May I activate the intelligence organization?”

“This organization isn’t mine. It’s yours. You can judge for yourself, move first, and report afterward.”

I gave him full authority and infinite trust when it came to his work.

Gowol bowed his head in gratitude.

“Show me what you can do.”

“Yes!”

It was the moment Gowol moved against an external enemy for the first time.

Gowol would take care of devising the plan from here on. As for me, all I had to do was swing my fists on the stage Gowol prepared. Wasn’t being able to feel this at ease the privilege of a leader with a capable strategist?

When I finished the conversation and returned to the room, silence hung in the air. The Fist Demon and Cult Leader Pungcheon were drinking tea without a word. The awkwardness was suffocating.

“What were you two talking about?”

At my question, Cult Leader Pungcheon answered.

“I told him to take good care of our Second Young Master.”

“Even though you curse me behind my back all the time?”

“You have to keep rising in triumph for me to keep cursing you, don’t you?”

“That is true.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon seemed to be in a much better mood. Seeing his bright expression, I looked at Gowol. Gowol also wore an expression that said he had no idea why.

The reason was revealed that night.

I was inside the Spacetime Transposition Art created by Cult Leader Pungcheon. When I said I wanted to talk for a moment, he had unfolded the Spacetime Transposition Art. A field stretched out beneath a blue sky; it was an exceedingly beautiful and refreshing sight.

“Master!”

“Master?”

“Have you forgotten? Did we not agree that, at least in here, I would take you as my master?”

The one who had passed down the Spacetime Transposition Art to me was none other than Cult Leader Pungcheon. That was why we had once spoken about how I would regard him as my master, at least within this place.

“Ah! That’s right.”

Instead of being pleased, Cult Leader Pungcheon sighed.

“What is the matter?”

He let out a sigh and said,

“Since it’s you, I’ll speak honestly. When I heard that the Fist Demon had become your master, I was truly happy. Not because the two of you had become master and disciple, but because I thought, Ah, the Fist Demon got completely stripped bare by you too. I’d been depressed, thinking I was the only one who had been thoroughly wrung dry by you.”

Truly, the only person who could say something like this so openly was Cult Leader Pungcheon.

“But then I thought about it, and I’m your master too, aren’t I? The one who was stripped bare first wasn’t the Fist Demon. It was me. Me, I tell you.”

At his self-reproach, I ended up laughing.

“Are you mocking me?”

“Mocking you? How could I? You are my master.”

“Yes, mock me all you like.”

“Cult Leader.”

“What is it?”

I gazed at him for a moment, then spoke with sincerity.

“Thank you.”

“For what?”

“For staying by my side. For staying by Gowol’s side.”

“Why are you suddenly acting like this? I don’t have any money!”

Why was I doing this? Something I had been trying hard to uphold lately in my relationships with people. Something as important as looking at people properly.

Saying it.

Do not keep it saved up. Say it when it comes to mind, without putting it off. I was putting that into practice now.

“Thank you, truly.”

When I conveyed my sincere gratitude once more, Cult Leader Pungcheon’s eyes grew deep.

“I’m doing fine, so you don’t need to look after me like this.”

“No, I will look after you. I’m human too, so I’ll make mistakes. If I ever neglect you, please keep cursing me. Don’t treat me like that cult leader from back then! Don’t treat me like a fish you’ve already caught! Please keep cursing me.”

The wind that blew in made the hems of our clothes flutter.

Looking at the grass that surrendered to the wind and lay down all at once, Cult Leader Pungcheon asked,

“Gowol told you to look after me, didn’t he?”

“Yes.”

“Why are you young ones looking after an old person so much? You’re hurting my pride.”

“Strategist Go said this. That he still dreams of the time when he was bound in shackles. That it truly does not seem easy for someone to escape from the past. He said you would be the same.”

“He should look after himself. Listen, Second Young Master. Instead of me, look after that strategist of yours. He’s having a hard time these days.”

“Can’t you look after him yourself?”

At that, Cult Leader Pungcheon abruptly revealed what was in his heart.

“This was the place Gowol liked best. He truly loved the fields and sky here. But after he became a strategist, I became reluctant to bring him here.”

“Why is that?”

After a brief pause, Cult Leader Pungcheon said,

“I’m afraid. I’m afraid that if he comes here, he’ll think this: Back then, I was so oppressed that I even longed for this fake world, wasn’t I?”

I understood Cult Leader Pungcheon’s concern. Yes, the past was a disgustingly difficult thing to shake off.

“Bring Strategist Go here.”

“Bring him?”

“The memories won’t disappear from this place just because he doesn’t come. Do you think someone with a mind as sharp as his would forget this place simply because he avoids coming here?”

“!”

“If anything, if he is to forget, he will have to forget here.”

Whooooosh.

Was it reflecting Cult Leader Pungcheon’s heart? An even fiercer wind blew in.

“Then I’ll be going now.”

Even after I left, Cult Leader Pungcheon did not emerge from the Spacetime Transposition Art for a long while.

* * *

The next day as well, Cult Leader Pungcheon was inside the Spacetime Transposition Art. In the same place, with another person. Gowol stood where Geom Mugeuk had been.

“It’s been a long time since you came here, hasn’t it?”

At Cult Leader Pungcheon’s words, Gowol nodded with a gloomy expression. Watching Gowol’s mood, Cult Leader Pungcheon asked,

“What’s wrong?”

“What do you think? Coming here makes me remember the old days. There isn’t a single good memory, is there?”

Hearing those words directly from Gowol made Cult Leader Pungcheon’s heart ache.

“Why did you bring me here? To tell me, You are still wearing my invisible shackles?”

At Gowol’s cold reaction, Cult Leader Pungcheon grew flustered.

“No. I just, after so long…”

“Let me out.”

“Very well.”

Cult Leader Pungcheon was about to undo the Spacetime Transposition Art, then suddenly flared up. Now that he thought about it, anger was surging within him.

“Hey! Why are you getting so serious and angry over this? Do our old memories mean nothing at all? I apologized over and over for what happened back then, didn’t I? Do you really have to react so sharply? I just wanted to, with you…”

As he spoke, Cult Leader Pungcheon found himself at a loss for words. Just what was he trying to do with him? Did he want to recall old memories? Because Geom Mugeuk had told him to do it? Or because he wanted to hear Gowol say from his own mouth that he was all right now? Damn it. This was a place he should never have opened.

It was then. Gowol’s gentle voice reached him.

“Cult Leader. You were worried I might act like I did just now, weren’t you?”

Startled, Cult Leader Pungcheon raised his head. Gowol, who had been angry just moments before, was gone, and he had returned to his usual self. Cult Leader Pungcheon understood. Gowol had deliberately acted that way.

“What do you take me for, worrying about something like that? You’ve watched me for so long and still don’t know?”

“Honestly, the more I watch, the less I know. I don’t know you, and I don’t know myself either.”

Looking around, Gowol said,

“I wanted to come here. There was no time when I was happier than when I was in this place. I may be wandering around the Central Plains now, but I liked standing here with you more. That’s why I was waiting, wondering when you would suggest coming here.”

At that, Cult Leader Pungcheon shouted.

“What kind of insane nonsense is that? Take that back right now! You liked this paltry fabricated world more? Are you mad? This place is just… Forget it! Forget it, I said! It was nothing but a miserable little airhole in that painful life. What are we supposed to do if you like this wretched place more? Is your life now worth no more than that? If you’re that unhappy, what’s the point of an intelligence organization? Forget the Second Young Master and everything else—quit it all!”

At the fervor in Cult Leader Pungcheon’s eyes, hot emotion surged up within Gowol. To have even one person like this in one’s life—what more could he ask for?

“Aaaaah! This is good!”

Gowol began running across the field as he had in the past. Then he lay spread-eagled on the grass.

“This is nice.”

“What’s so nice about it?”

“You finally seem like that damned Cult Leader again. Your anger’s eased a bit today, hasn’t it?”

“Eased? The resentment that had eased has piled up again.”

But unlike his words, Cult Leader Pungcheon’s expression had become more relaxed than before he entered the Spacetime Transposition Art.

Gowol, who had been lying down, raised himself up.

“Cult Leader, let’s go out. I’m busy.”

“Stay a little longer. Rest a bit more. Forget that trivial work. Forget the Second Young Master too.”

At that, Gowol’s gaze turned toward the sky again.

“It really is better. The sky here is better.”

At least in that moment, they felt the same way, but Cult Leader Pungcheon said something different from what was in his heart.

“I’ll never open this place again. Look and roll around to your heart’s content.”

* * *

While Gowol was setting the stage regarding Hyeopgye, I trained in martial arts with the Fist Demon.

This time was truly precious. We had finally begun exchanging pointers through fist techniques.

Thunderbolt Asura Fist against Thunderbolt Asura Fist.

The Fist Demon had now begun teaching me with his body.

Now that he had formally become my master, simply fighting was out of the question. Instead, when it came to the Thunderbolt Asura Fist, we could exchange blows as much as we liked.

Even if I were to defeat him later, I would be defeating him with his own martial art, so there was no great need to worry.

And a few days later.

Gowol produced results faster than expected. He had succeeded in establishing a connection with Hyeopgye.

“Are you ready to borrow money from the immortals?”

I smiled broadly as I looked at Gowol. I intended to return to them exactly what they had done to the youths. Those demon-like bastards—I would scrape them clean away, not leaving a single one behind.

“Shall we go and see just how much money our dear immortals have?”
